# Tinfirm engineering conventions

This file is the shared contract for everyone who works on the repo, human or agent.
The design is in [PLATFORM.md](PLATFORM.md). The HTTP contract is [`api/openapi.yaml`](../api/openapi.yaml), the runner protocol [`protocol/runner-v1.schema.json`](../protocol/runner-v1.schema.json) and [`protocol/runner-v2.schema.json`](../protocol/runner-v2.schema.json).
If this file and the code disagree, fix the code or change this file on purpose. Never let them drift apart.

## 1. Scope

Sections 2 to 13 are the engineering rules of the whole repository: layout, names, the runtime contract, authentication, authorization, the database, the audit log, the vault, code conventions, the web app, the image and the rules for contributors. Section 15 holds the domain of the platform redesign of 2026-10-12 ([ADR-0058](adr/0058-authority-from-the-audience.md), [PLATFORM.md](PLATFORM.md)), which `main` runs: its head says which subsection holds which package of [PLATFORM.md §12](PLATFORM.md#12-build-order) and what came after them, and links each package's contract. Section 14 holds the domain rules of the restart of 2026-10-05 ([ADR-0037](adr/0037-restart-on-the-platform-design.md)), which `main` does not run (the note at its head says which parts still hold): the outbound guard of §14.4, the negotiation and the frames of runner protocol 2 in §14.10, the MCP server for people's clients in §14.11 ([ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md)), and §14.13's groups; its other rules are history, in [archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md), and §14.12 says where the rules of companies and their sign-in went when [ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md) removed companies' own identity providers. The rules of the v1 domain are history: [archive/v1/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v1/CONVENTIONS.md).

## 2. Repository layout and ownership

| Path | Contents | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| `api/openapi.yaml` | HTTP contract (OpenAPI 3.0.3) | The maintainer. Others, agents included, may change it in their own pull request, where a human reviews the change (§13). |
| `server/` | Spring Boot app (single Maven module, artifactId `tinfirm-server`) | backend |
| `web/` | Angular app (npm package `tinfirm-web`) | web |
| `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `Makefile`, `deploy/`, `.github/`, `.pr_agent.toml`, `.semgrepignore`, `.trivyignore` | Image; Compose (`deploy/compose`); Helm (`deploy/helm/tinfirm`); the runner host's Ansible playbook and role (`deploy/ansible`); the release assets, the runner host binaries and the Compose tarball (`deploy/release`); CI; PR-Agent's settings (`.pr_agent.toml`) | deploy |
| `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `NOTICE`, `PRODUCT.md`, `docs/` except `PLATFORM.md` and `CONVENTIONS.md` | Documentation, the security policy, the code of conduct, the licence and third-party notices, what the product is and for whom, threat model, ADRs | docs |
| `docs/PLATFORM.md`, `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` | The platform design; this engineering contract | The maintainer, as their code owner (`.github/CODEOWNERS`). Others, agents included, may change them in their own pull request; the code owner reviews every change (§13). |
| `docs-site/` | The docs site, public at <https://docs.tinfirm.com>: the manuals for members, for a company's Owners and Admins, and for operators (Zensical), with its settings, its lock, its `nginx.conf` and its `Dockerfile` (§12). Never the engineering record, which stays in `docs/`, and never a link into this repository | docs; its `Dockerfile` and `nginx.conf` with deploy |
| `DESIGN.md`, `.impeccable/` | The visual design system, and the design tool's record of it (`design.json`) and of the web app's surfaces | web |
| `.editorconfig` | The editor settings of every file type | The maintainer |
| `protocol/runner-v1.schema.json`, `protocol/runner-v2.schema.json` | Runner WebSocket protocol, versions 1 and 2 (JSON Schema 2020-12; the server's `RunnerMessages` and the runner's `tinfirm-protocol` crate implement both) | The maintainer, as their code owner (`.github/CODEOWNERS`). Others, agents included, may change them in their own pull request; the code owner reviews every change (§13). |
| `runner/` | Rust Cargo workspace: runner host, protocol types, runner images | runner |
| `libs/jira/` | The shared Jira library, package `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.shared.jira` (inside the server's open `shared` module): the Atlassian Document Format and wiki markup to and from Markdown, Jira's timestamps, one reading of issues, comments and users for Data Center and Cloud, the search's URLs and pages, Jira's error messages. No HTTP: the server sends with its guarded outbound client (§14.4). Not a Maven module of its own: the server's build compiles its sources and runs its tests (build-helper `add-source`, `add-test-source`) | backend |
| `libs/net/` | The shared bounded reading of HTTP answers, package `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.shared.net` (beside the server's guard of the same package, inside its open `shared` module): `BoundedBodies`, body handlers of the JDK's HTTP client that read at most a number of bytes of an answer (§14.4); `InflightBytes`, the cap of request bodies a process holds at once, which the server's model relay (§15.4) and the model port take a share of per request. Built as `libs/jira` is, into the server and the model port | backend |
| `libs/litellm/` | The shared filter of LiteLLM's own request fields, package `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.shared.litellm`: `LiteLlmRequests`, which removes from a model request's body the fields LiteLLM takes as its own (an upstream URL, credentials, metadata, routing, canned answers; v1.106.6's `all_litellm_params`, listed in its test resources) and, for the server's relay, turns `drop_params` on (§15.4). Built as `libs/jira` is, into the server and the model port | backend |
| `model-api/` | The model port (ADR-0069): Spring Boot app (single Maven module, artifactId `tinfirm-model-api`) beside the bundled LiteLLM that serves its model routes alone to the company's services, its Dockerfile | backend |
| `ee/` | Tinfirm's commercial part under its own licence ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md)): the licence, module `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.ee`, and the paid features' code, in the packages of the modules it belongs to; compiled into the server's build and every image as `libs/*` are, its tests run with the server's; the licence's public keys; FLOWTIVO's key tool (`ee/tools/`). §15.24 | backend |

## 3. Names and versions

- Product name: **Tinfirm**. Keep it in few places: package root, image names and the Helm chart name.
- Java package root: `com.flowtivo.tinfirm`. Maven groupId `com.flowtivo.tinfirm`, artifactId `tinfirm-server`, version `0.1.0-SNAPSHOT`.
- Images: `ghcr.io/flowtivo/tinfirm-server`; `ghcr.io/flowtivo/tinfirm-model-api` for the model port (artifactId `tinfirm-model-api`, package `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.modelapi`; ADR-0069), released with the server under the same version. `ghcr.io/flowtivo/tinfirm-docs` is the docs site's (`docs-site/`, §12), published from `main` and never released. The Helm chart is `tinfirm`.
- Versions: take the latest patch release of each line at implementation time.

| Component | Version line |
|---|---|
| Java | 25 |
| Spring Boot | 4.1.x (manages Spring Security, Spring Session, Flyway, Testcontainers, JUnit) |
| Spring Modulith | the 2.x release that matches Boot 4.1 |
| PostgreSQL | 18 |
| Node | 24 LTS |
| Angular | 22.x |
| spartan/ui | 1.x |
| Tailwind | 4.x |
| Spring AI | 2.0.x, for the MCP server (`spring-ai-starter-mcp-server-webmvc`; 2.0.2 in `server/pom.xml`) |
| Rust | 1.89 or later, edition 2024 (`runner/Cargo.toml`: `rust-version`, `edition`) |
| Runner host | 0.3.0, the version of `runner/`'s workspace (`runner/Cargo.toml`), which the server reads from `hello.runnerVersion`: a run paid by a budget needs 0.2.0 or later (`RunnerVersions.MODEL_PROXY_ALWAYS_ON`), and 0.3.0 sends its own token to the model relay (`HOST_TOKEN_TO_RELAY`, §15.4) |
| LiteLLM | v1.106.6, pinned by tag and digest, not a line: Compose's `litellm` image, Helm's `litellm.image`, and the list of its own request fields in `libs/litellm`'s test resources; bump them together |

## 4. Runtime contract of the server

### Ports
- `8080`: the application (API, OIDC endpoints, SPA).
- `8081`: management, with `/actuator/health/liveness`, `/actuator/health/readiness` and `/actuator/prometheus`. Never expose 8081 through ingress. Liveness is the application's own state only (`livenessState`), so a database outage never restarts a pod; readiness is `readinessState` and the database (`db`, `management.endpoint.health.group.readiness.include` in `application.yaml`), so a replica that cannot reach PostgreSQL takes no traffic (`ReadinessProbeTest`).

### Paths
- `/api/**`: JSON API, as defined in `api/openapi.yaml`.
- `/oauth2/authorization/{registrationId}` and `/login/oauth2/code/{registrationId}`: the Spring Security OIDC flow, in which Tinfirm is the client of one of the instance's providers, the registrations of the environment (§5); `GET /api/auth/providers` lists them.
- `/api/companies/{companyId}/mcp`: the MCP endpoint of a company for people's clients (§14.11). It is JSON-RPC, not part of `api/openapi.yaml`, and takes bearer tokens only.
- `/api/companies/{companyId}/mcp/files`: a file a person's client holds for its next start (§14.11, [ADR-0084](adr/0084-email-attachments-and-gmail-drafts.md)), one multipart `file`, behind the MCP endpoint's chain and not part of `api/openapi.yaml`.
- Tinfirm's own OAuth 2.1 authorization server (§14.11, [ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md)): `/oauth2/authorize`, `/oauth2/token`, `/oauth2/jwks`, `/oauth2/revoke`, `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`, and the protected-resource metadata of each company's MCP endpoint, `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/api/companies/{companyId}/mcp`.
- Tinfirm as an OAuth client of the company's systems ([ADR-0058](adr/0058-authority-from-the-audience.md), §15.3): `GET /api/oauth/callback`, the one callback of every system's app, where a system's authorization server sends the browser back after a sign-in (a redirect that needs the session cookie, in the JSON API's namespace), and `GET /oauth/client-metadata.json`, Tinfirm's own Client ID Metadata Document, which names that callback: public, no session, cacheable. Not to be confused with `/oauth2/**`, Tinfirm's authorization server.
- `/api/runner/v1/**`: reserved for runner hosts. Its own security chain matches the whole prefix before the BFF chain: no session, no CSRF, no request cache, every request let through to the endpoint, which authenticates its own bearer. Not part of `api/openapi.yaml`. The deployment routes the prefix to the server as a whole, so a new endpoint under it needs no route of its own. Today four kinds of endpoint: the runner WebSocket `/api/runner/v1/connect` (the runner host's token, §14.10); from package 2 the model relay (§15.4), which takes a run's one-run token as `Authorization: Bearer`, with the runner host's own token in `X-Tinfirm-Runner-Token` (required from runner host 0.3.0 on), and speaks the providers' APIs: `POST /api/runner/v1/model/anthropic/v1/messages`, `POST …/model/anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens`, `GET …/model/anthropic/v1/models`, `POST /api/runner/v1/model/openai/v1/responses`, `POST …/model/openai/v1/chat/completions` and `GET …/model/openai/v1/models`; any other path below `/api/runner/v1/model` is 404, another method 405. And from package 3c's part 3 the files of a run's conversation (§15.7), which take the runner host's token as `Authorization: Bearer` (a missing or unknown one is `401`): `GET /api/runner/v1/runs/{runId}/attachments/{attachmentId}?epoch=` downloads a file for a run assigned to this host with this epoch while it is assigned (awaiting `run.accepted`) or running, and `POST /api/runner/v1/runs/{runId}/attachments?epoch=&artifact=&source=` uploads the agent's file (`multipart/form-data`: `file`, optional `note`) for a run `RUNNING` on this host with this epoch. And from the skills library ([ADR-0064](adr/0064-skills-library.md), §15.19) a run's skills, with the runner host's token as `Authorization: Bearer` (a missing or unknown one is `401`): `GET /api/runner/v1/runs/{runId}/skills/{skillId}/versions/{version}?epoch=` downloads the canonical zip of a skill at a version the run was assigned (`run_skill`), for a run assigned to this host with this epoch while it awaits `run.accepted` or runs (`RunnerSkillsController`; `application/zip`, `Cache-Control: no-store`). Anything else is 404, another company's run included.
- `/api/chat/**`: the chat platforms' own requests (package 5a, §15.16): `POST /api/chat/slack/{systemId}/events` (the Events API), `POST /api/chat/slack/{systemId}/interactions` (Slack's interactivity, form-encoded `payload=`) and `POST /api/chat/teams/{systemId}/messages` (the Bot Framework). Its own security chain (`ChatConfiguration`) matches the whole prefix before the BFF chain, as the runner's does: no session, no CSRF, no request cache, no security context, every request let through to the endpoint. Not part of `api/openapi.yaml`, since a generated interface would parse a body before its signature is checked. Each endpoint reads at most `TINFIRM_CHAT_MAX_BODY` raw (`413`), finds its system by the path through `chat_system_by_id` (an unknown id or another kind is `404`), verifies the request (Slack's signature, the Bot Framework's token and the activity's `serviceUrl` and tenant; `401` without a body, nothing stored), and only then parses it, at most 32 levels deep. A Slack event of another workspace is `200` with `X-Slack-No-Retry: 1` and stored nowhere; a stored or duplicate event is `200` at once; `503` while the database fails.
- `/api/hooks/**`: a webhook system's deliveries (package 5b, §15.17): `POST /api/hooks/{systemId}`. Its own security chain (`WebhookConfiguration`), as the chats' (no session, CSRF, request cache or security context), not part of `api/openapi.yaml` for the same reason. In this order, so that nobody without the secret spends the sender's rate: `application/json` (`415`); a client address with more than 60 refusals for the system in the last minute (`429`), keyed by the path's system id as given, before the system is looked up; the system through `webhook_system_by_id` (`404`); at most 1 MiB raw (`413`); the scheme verified (`401` without a body, nothing stored); the company's stop (`503`, `Retry-After: 300`, nothing stored); the system paused (`200 {"paused": true}`, nothing stored, never fired later); the system's deliveries a minute (`429` with `Retry-After`); the body parsed (an object, at most 32 levels and 10 000 values, no `U+0000` in a name or a string, which `jsonb` refuses: `400`); then `200 {"duplicate": true}` for a known delivery id, else `202 {deliveryId, firings}`; a stop or a pause committed after those checks, which a trigger's gate sees in the delivery's transaction, answers as they do, nothing stored; `503` while the database fails.
- `/scim/v2/**`: SCIM 2.0 provisioning of a company's members by its identity provider ([ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md), §15.25): `Users`, `ServiceProviderConfig`, `ResourceTypes` and `Schemas`, in `api/openapi.yaml` under the tag `Scim`. Its own stateless chain (`ScimSecurityConfiguration`), before the BFF chain: no session, no CSRF, no request cache, the session cookie ignored; every request authenticated by the company's SCIM token (`ScimRequestFilter`), whose refusals are SCIM's errors, never `problem+json`. Any other path below `/scim` is `404`, after the token.
- SAML 2.0 sign-in ([ADR-0086](adr/0086-saml-sign-in.md), §15.26), on the BFF chain: `GET /saml2/authenticate/{id}` starts a sign-in (a `302` to the IdP, HTTP-Redirect binding, signed); `POST /login/saml2/sso/{id}` is the assertion consumer service (HTTP-POST only, at most 256 KiB, exempt from the CSRF token as Spring exempts it); `GET /saml2/service-provider-metadata/{id}` is the service provider's metadata, public. Each is served for a declared SAML provider only; another id ends on the login page.
- `/outlook/**`: the Outlook add-in's pages (ADR-0074), routes of the SPA (§11) with headers of their own (§5); its icons are files of the web app (`web/public/outlook/`), served from `classpath:/static/outlook/`.
- `/api/gmail/**`: Google's requests to the Gmail add-on ([ADR-0083](adr/0083-gmail-add-on.md), §14.11): `POST /api/gmail/{companyId}/home`, `/open` and `/act`. Its own security chain (`GmailConfiguration`), as the chats' (no session, CSRF, request cache or security context), not part of `api/openapi.yaml`. In this order: `404` while `TINFIRM_GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` or `TINFIRM_GMAIL_CLIENT_ID` is unset, for a company id that is no UUID or another trigger; `413` beyond 256 KiB (bytes, read raw and never further); `400` for a body that is not a JSON object; `401` without a body when Google's two ID tokens do not verify (logged with why); `404` for an unknown company; else `200` with a card.
- `/gmail/link/{companyId}`: the Gmail add-on's sign-in link, a route of the SPA (§11) with the strict headers.
- Everything else is the SPA. The server serves `classpath:/static/`, and any GET that does not match a file, `/api/**`, `/oauth/client-metadata.json`, `/oauth2/**`, `/login/oauth2/**`, `/saml2/**`, `/login/saml2/**`, `/.well-known/**`, `/actuator/**` or `/scim/**` returns `index.html`. The consent page of the authorization server is the SPA route `/authorize`. Open dynamic client registration (`/oauth2/register`) and OpenID Connect discovery (`/.well-known/openid-configuration`) are not served: 404 for any method, with or without a session.

### Configuration

Configuration uses environment variables. Spring's standard names are used where they exist.

| Variable | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `SPRING_DATASOURCE_URL` | JDBC URL | `jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/tinfirm` |
| `SPRING_DATASOURCE_USERNAME` / `_PASSWORD` | Runtime DB role `tinfirm_app`. It does not own tables, so RLS applies to it. | — |
| `SPRING_FLYWAY_USER` / `SPRING_FLYWAY_PASSWORD` | Migration DB role `tinfirm_owner`, which owns the schema | — |
| `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL` | External base URL, e.g. `https://tinfirm.example.com`. It drives OIDC redirects, the `Secure` cookie flag, the issuer of Tinfirm's authorization server and the canonical URI of each company's MCP endpoint (§14.11). | `http://localhost:8080` |
| `TINFIRM_VAULT_KEYS` | Master keys, as comma-separated `keyId:base64(32 bytes)` | **required**; the server fails at startup without it |
| `TINFIRM_VAULT_ACTIVE_KEY` | The keyId used for new wraps | **required** |
| `TINFIRM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `_PASSWORD` | Creates the first instance admin when the user table is empty | unset |
| `TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_NAME` | A company the deployment declares ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md), §5): created at the server's start when no company has the key `<KEY>`, and matched by name when a company from before keys is adopted; 1 to 100 characters. The app may rename the company later; the name is not applied again. `<KEY>` is lower-case letters and digits, starting with a letter, at most 40 (`tinfirm.companies.<key>`); the server does not start with another | none: no company is declared |
| `TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_OWNER` | The email of the user who becomes the declared company's OWNER while it has none, at every start and at that user's every sign-in (§5) | `TINFIRM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAIL` |
| `TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_ID` | Adopts this existing company, one from before keys, as the declared company `<KEY>` (§5) | unset |
| `TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_SCIM_ENABLED` | The company serves SCIM ([ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md), §15.25); its OWNER then issues the token in the app | `false` |
| `TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_SCIM_ROLE` | A new member's role when SCIM's request names none: `ADMIN`, `MEMBER` or `VIEWER`, never `OWNER` | `MEMBER` |
| `TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_SCIM_ROLE_SOURCE` | Who decides the role of the members SCIM manages ([ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md) decision 8): `IDP`, SCIM whenever a request carries it; `APP`, SCIM gives it once at creation | `IDP` |
| `TINFIRM_SCIM_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE` / `_REFUSALS_PER_MINUTE` | A company's SCIM requests a minute, and refused tokens a minute from one client address, in memory per replica (`tinfirm.scim.*`, §15.25) | `600` / `20` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_LOCAL_ENABLED` | Enables local accounts | `true` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_AUTO_PROVISION` | Creates a new OIDC user on first login (see §5 for the linking rules) | `true` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_EMAIL_TRUST` | How the email of a new user from registration `<ID>` is trusted (§5): `REQUIRE_VERIFIED_CLAIM` or `TRUST_ISSUER` | `REQUIRE_VERIFIED_CLAIM` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_EMAIL_CLAIMS` | Claims that may carry that email, comma-separated, tried in order (§5). Claims other than `email` need `TRUST_ISSUER`. | `email` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_COMPANY` | The key of a declared company that a new user from registration `<ID>` joins (§5); the server does not start when no company of that key is declared | the declared company when exactly one is declared; otherwise none |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_ROLE` | Under `_ROLE_SOURCE=APP`, a new member's role when the role claim maps none or no claim is set: `MEMBER`, `ADMIN` or `VIEWER`, never `OWNER` | `MEMBER` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_ROLE_SOURCE` | Who decides the role of the members of registration `<ID>`'s company ([ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md), §5): `IDP`, the provider at every sign-in; `APP`, the provider gives a new user a role once and the app decides after. `IDP` without `_ROLE_CLAIM` stops the start, and so do two `IDP` providers of one company | `IDP` when `_ROLE_CLAIM` is set; otherwise `APP` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_ROLE_CLAIM` | The claim of the ID token or the userinfo response that carries the role's values, a string or an array, such as `roles` or `groups` | none |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_ROLE_MAP_ADMIN`, `_ROLE_MAP_MEMBER`, `_ROLE_MAP_VIEWER` | The claim's values, comma-separated and matched exactly, that give each role in the provider's own company; the highest wins. The server does not start on a map without `_ROLE_CLAIM`, a claim without any map, an empty value, or a value under two roles | none |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_COMPANIES_<KEY>_ROLE_MAP_ADMIN`, `_ROLE_MAP_MEMBER`, `_ROLE_MAP_VIEWER` | The claim's values that give each role in the further declared company `<KEY>` ([ADR-0089](adr/0089-companies-from-the-role-claim.md), §5): the same provider fills several companies. The server does not start without `_ROLE_CLAIM`, on an undeclared key, on the provider's own company's key, on a company without any map, or on a deciding provider whose own company has no `_ROLE_MAP_*` | none |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_EMAIL_DOMAINS` | Comma-separated email domains: when set, only an email in one of them gets a new user through registration `<ID>` (§5) | empty: any email the email trust accepts |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_AUTO_PROVISION` | Whether an unknown identity of registration `<ID>` that binds to no SCIM user gets a new user ([ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md) decision 6) | `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_AUTO_PROVISION` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_SCIM_MATCH` | How a first sign-in through registration `<ID>` finds the user its company's SCIM created (§5): `EXTERNAL_ID`, or `EXTERNAL_ID_OR_EMAIL` | `EXTERNAL_ID` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_METADATA_LOCATION` | A SAML provider's IdP metadata ([ADR-0086](adr/0086-saml-sign-in.md), §15.26): an `https://` URL or a `file:` path; `http:` stops the start. The id is unique across the SAML and OIDC providers, `^[a-z0-9]+$` | required |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_NAME`, `_ENTITY_ID` | The login page's button; the IdP to take from a document of several | the id; the only IdP |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_COMPANY`, `_ROLE`, `_EMAIL_DOMAINS`, `_ROLE_SOURCE`, `_ROLE_CLAIM`, `_ROLE_MAP_*`, `_COMPANIES_<KEY>_ROLE_MAP_*`, `_SCIM_MATCH` | As an OIDC provider's; `_ROLE_CLAIM` names an attribute | as OIDC |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_SUBJECT` | `NAMEID`, or the attribute that identifies the person | `NAMEID` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_EMAIL_ATTRIBUTES`, `_NAME_ATTRIBUTES` | The attributes read for the email and the name, in order (§15.26) | ADR-0086 decision 5 |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_AUTO_PROVISION` | Whether an unknown person gets a new user | `true` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_REQUIRE_ENCRYPTED_ASSERTIONS` | Refuse a plain assertion | `false` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_PROVIDERS_<ID>_METADATA_REFRESH` | How often a worker reads the metadata again, at least `PT5M` | `PT12H` |
| `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_SP_CREDENTIALS_<KID>_KEY`, `_CERT`, `TINFIRM_AUTH_SAML_SP_ACTIVE_CREDENTIAL` | The service provider's PKCS#8 PEM keys (RSA ≥ 3072 or EC P-256) and certificates, all published and tried to decrypt; the active one signs. Required with SAML providers; an ended active certificate stops the start | none |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT` | Session idle timeout (ISO-8601 duration) | `PT8H` |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_MAX_AGE` | Absolute session lifetime | `P7D` |
| `TINFIRM_AUDIT_CHECK_INTERVAL` / `_TICK` / `_INITIAL_DELAY` | How often each audit chain is checked, how often `worker` replicas look for chains due, and the first look after start (§15.11) | `P1D` / `PT1H` / `PT10M` |
| `TINFIRM_AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX_EVENTS` | The most events one export of the audit chain holds (§15.11) | `1000000` |
| `TINFIRM_RETENTION_SWEEP_INTERVAL` / `_TICK` / `_INITIAL_DELAY` | How often each company is swept for retention, how often `worker` replicas look for companies due, and the first look after start (§15.11) | `P1D` / `PT1H` / `PT15M` |
| `SERVER_TOMCAT_REMOTEIP_INTERNAL_PROXIES` | Regex of trusted proxy IPs whose `X-Forwarded-For` is honoured (used by the per-IP login throttle) | Tomcat default (private ranges) |
| `SPRING_SECURITY_OAUTH2_CLIENT_REGISTRATION_<ID>_*` / `..._PROVIDER_<ID>_ISSUER_URI` | OIDC providers; standard Spring properties. Each registration appears in `GET /api/auth/providers`. `<ID>` is the registration id in lower case; use letters and digits only. The `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_*` variables use the same `<ID>`, and the server does not start when one names no registration. | none |
| `TINFIRM_ROLES` | Server roles in this process: `api`, `worker`. Only a replica with `worker` runs the background work, each job checking `TinfirmProperties.hasRole(WORKER_ROLE)`: the run dispatcher and reaper; the triggers' polls, schedules and write-back (`TriggerPolls`, `ScheduleFirings`, `OutboxDelivery`); the chats' events, Socket Mode connections and silent subscriptions (`ChatInbox`, `SlackSockets`, `ChatSilence`); the recovery of calls, the watch of change requests and the sweeps of the call journal and the submission keys; the model keys' reconciliation and rotation (`KeyReconciliation`); the spend sync, the retention sweep (with the memories' compaction), the sweep of expired held files (`StagedFileStore`, ADR-0084) and the daily check of the audit chains; error reports' flush and delivery. Every replica, whatever its roles, keeps its runner connections alive, extends the leases at its start (`ReconnectGrace`), sweeps expired sign-ins (`SignInSweep`) and forgets the relay's idle keys. `api` gates nothing today: every replica serves the API. Compose and Helm (`serverRoles`) run both in every replica | `api,worker` |
| `TINFIRM_REVISION` | The build's git revision, which `GET /api/system/info` answers as `commit` and the error reports name (`BuildInfo`, property `tinfirm.revision`); runner hosts on auto-deploy compare it with a release's `REVISION`. The image sets it from its build argument `REVISION` (`Dockerfile`). Only a full revision counts (40 lower-case hex digits); otherwise the short id of a `git.properties` when the build has one, else none | the image's build argument (`unknown` without one) |
| `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOW_HTTP` | Accept `http` base URLs of systems (the token then travels in clear text); §14.4. The name keeps the word of the design of 2026-10-05 | `false` |
| `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOW_PRIVATE_ADDRESSES` | Accept base URLs of company systems that point at loopback, link-local or private addresses (a member's personal MCP server never reaches them); §14.4 | `false` |
| `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_INTERNAL_HOSTS` | The operator's internal hosts, comma-separated host names or IPv4 literals without a scheme, port or wildcard (ADR-0066): a company system whose base URL names one may resolve to private addresses only (never public, loopback, link-local, unspecified or multicast ones) and may use `http`. A personal MCP server never honours it; every other host keeps the guard. Use it for a service on the deployment's own network, instead of allowing every private address; §14.4 | empty |
| `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS` | Error reports (ADR-0070, §15.21): `off` (nothing is captured), `local` (captured and shown to instance admins, never sent) or `automatic` (also delivered to `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS_DSN`); case is ignored. Set, the page *Errors* shows it and refuses a change (`409 ERROR_REPORTING_SET_BY_OPERATOR`); unset, an instance admin chooses `local` or `automatic` there | unset (`local` until an admin chooses) |
| `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS_DSN` | The destination of automatic delivery: a Sentry DSN, `https://<public key>@<host>[/<path>]/<project id>`, from any tracker that speaks Sentry's protocol (Sentry, GlitchTip, Bugsink); `https`, or `http` to a loopback address. A wrong one stops the server with the reason, never the key; unset, nothing is delivered | unset |
| `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS_LABEL` | The installation's name in its reports, 1 to 64 letters, digits, dots, dashes or underscores; unset, only its random id names it | unset |
| `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS_PROXY` | `http://host:port` of an HTTP proxy for the delivery; unset, the JVM's proxy settings apply | unset |
| `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT` | Connect and request timeout of one request to a company's system (ISO-8601 duration); a tool's call in all is bounded by `TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT` | `PT20S` |
| `TINFIRM_RUNNER_LEASE_TTL` | The lease of a running run, renewed by its heartbeats and the other frames of its host (§15.6) | `PT30S` |
| `TINFIRM_RUNNER_RECONNECT_GRACE` | When a host disconnects, and at the server's start, the runs that hold a slot keep a lease of at least this long (§15.6) | `PT2M` |
| `TINFIRM_RUNNER_LOST_RESUME_WINDOW` | A run lost because its lease expired is resumed when its host reports it within this time; `PT0S` turns it off (§15.6) | `PT10M` |
| `TINFIRM_RUNNER_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL` | The heartbeat interval announced in `welcome`; also the keep-alive ping of runner connections | `PT10S` |
| `TINFIRM_RUNNER_POOL_TICK` | How often every replica evaluates the pool schedules of the hosts with ready sandboxes connected to it, and sends a host whose count changed its `host.config` (`tinfirm.runner.pool-tick`, §15.4, ADR-0099); between two ticks the host follows the next change it was sent | `PT1M` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_WALL_CLOCK` | The wall-clock limit of a run, sent in every `run.assign` of an agent that sets no time limit of its own (`agent.turn_time_limit_minutes`, 5 to 1440 minutes); at least a minute (§15.6) | `PT60M` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_TURNS` | The tool-call limit of a run, sent in every `run.assign` | `200` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_ACCEPT_TIMEOUT` | An assigned run without `run.accepted` this long after it was sent goes back to the queue (§15.6) | `PT10S` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_REJECT_BACKOFF` | A run its host rejected, or whose preparation failed, is not dispatched again before this | `PT10S` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_EVENTS_PER_RUN` | Events stored per run, at least 100; `LOG` events are dropped first | `20000` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_STREAM_TIMEOUT` | The lifetime of one live stream of a run's events, and of a conversation's; the client reconnects, with `Last-Event-ID` to a run's | `PT30M` |
| `TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT` | Read twice. As `tinfirm.run.tool-timeout`: how long a server-executed tool call of a run (runner protocol v2) may take before it is answered: an `UNSAFE` call `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`, a `SAFE` one `TOOL_FAILED` (§15.6, §15.14; the call is cut off 5 seconds before it, or a quarter of a shorter timeout, so that its record fits; a request sent again waits for its call running elsewhere until `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.answer-within`, below); and, with 15 seconds more, when a call left `RUNNING` counts as lost. As `tinfirm.connection.tool-timeout`: how long one call of `SystemTools` may take in all, its requests together: a tool's call, and opening a change request or reading its state or body, or rewriting its body (§15.2). At most `PT60S` each, since the runner host waits 5 s longer | `PT60S` |
| `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_FILE_SIZE` | Read from package 3c's part 3 (§15.7). The largest file of a conversation, a person's or its agent's (`tinfirm.attachment.max-file-size`): larger is `413 ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE`, or a `REJECTED` answer to a runner host's upload. Also Spring's multipart limit (`spring.servlet.multipart.max-file-size`), so that a larger upload is refused before it is read completely (`413 ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE`) | `25MB` |
| `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_REQUEST_SIZE` | Read from package 3c's part 3. The largest multipart request, the file with its other parts (`spring.servlet.multipart.max-request-size`) | `26MB` |
| `SERVER_TOMCAT_MAXPARTHEADERSIZE` | The largest header of one part of a multipart request (`server.tomcat.max-part-header-size`), which carries a file's name. A runner host sends a path of `/output` of up to 1024 characters (§15.7) twice, as an ASCII fallback and percent-encoded UTF-8 (`filename`, `filename*`): up to about 14 KB. Tomcat's default, 512 bytes, refused names of a few hundred bytes as `413 ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE` | `16KB` |
| `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_FILES_PER_CONVERSATION` | Read from package 3c's part 3. The most files one conversation holds, its outputs' documents included (`tinfirm.attachment.max-files-per-conversation`, at least 1); more is `409 TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS` | `50` |
| `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_CONVERSATION_SIZE` | Read from package 3c's part 3. The most bytes of all files of one conversation (`tinfirm.attachment.max-conversation-size`); more is `409 TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS` | `250MB` |
| `TINFIRM_CHANGE_REQUEST_STATE_INTERVAL` | Read from package 3c's part 3. A watched change request's state is read again this long after its last read (`tinfirm.conversation.change-requests.state-interval`); at least `PT30S`, else the server does not start (§15.7) | `PT5M` |
| `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_SUFFIX_ENABLED` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)**: the server reads only the bundled list (§15.5). Was: whether the server downloads the Public Suffix List (ADR-0044, §14.10). Off for an air-gapped installation: it then keeps the stored or the bundled list. Property `tinfirm.public-suffix.enabled` | `true` |
| `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_SUFFIX_URL` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)** (above). Was: where the list is fetched (https; a mirror may be on a private address; redirects are not followed, so give the final address). `tinfirm.public-suffix.url`. `tinfirm.public-suffix.allow-http` (no variable) accepts http, for tests | `https://publicsuffix.org/list/public_suffix_list.dat` |
| `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_SUFFIX_REFRESH_INTERVAL` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)** (above). Was: how long after the last check the list is asked for again (conditional GET); at least `PT1M`. `tinfirm.public-suffix.refresh-interval` | `PT24H` |
| `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_SUFFIX_TIMEOUT` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)** (above). Was: connect and request timeout of the download. `tinfirm.public-suffix.timeout` | `PT30S` |
| `TINFIRM_SYNC_POLL_TICK` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)**: the sync module went with the old domain (§14.9), and `application.yaml` binds none of its variables; package 4's polls read `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_POLL_TICK` (below). Was: how often a `worker` replica looks for connections due for a poll and writes due to the source; each connection is polled at its own interval | `PT15S` |
| `TINFIRM_SYNC_SCHEDULE_TICK` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)** (above); package 4 reads `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_SCHEDULE_TICK`. Was: how often a `worker` replica looks for due SCHEDULE triggers | `PT30S` |
| `TINFIRM_SYNC_MISSED_AFTER` | **Not read since the redesign's package 1 (2026-10-12)** (above); package 4 reads `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_MISSED_AFTER`. Was: a schedule's firing due longer ago than this was missed; its catch-up decides | `PT1H` |
| `TINFIRM_MCP_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` | Requests per minute that one credential (a personal access token or an OAuth grant; else the user) may send to the MCP endpoints, as a token bucket with a burst of the same size; over it `429 RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After`. In memory per replica, as the login throttle: behind N replicas a credential gets up to N times the limit. `0` turns it off (§14.11) | `120` |
| `TINFIRM_TOOL_JOURNAL_RETENTION` | How long after its run ended a tool call's row of the call journal is kept (`tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.retention`, package R, §15.14); its answer is cleared at the run's end already. Positive, else the server does not start | `P7D` |
| `TINFIRM_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL` | How long a submission's idempotency key (`Idempotency-Key` on REST, `requestId` on MCP) is kept (`tinfirm.conversation.idempotency-key-ttl`, package R, §15.14): an older key counts as gone, and `worker` replicas delete it. Positive, else the server does not start | `PT24H` |
| `TINFIRM_OUTLOOK_FRAME_ANCESTORS` | The hosts that may frame the Outlook add-in's task pane beside Tinfirm itself (ADR-0074; `tinfirm.outlook.frame-ancestors`): https origins, comma-separated, each optionally with a leading `*.` on its host, else the server does not start. They go into the add-in's `frame-ancestors` (§5). Outlook on the web and the new Outlook for Windows; a national cloud's Outlook needs its hosts here | `https://outlook.office.com,https://outlook.office365.com,https://outlook.live.com,https://outlook.cloud.microsoft` |
| `TINFIRM_GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` | The Gmail add-on's Google Cloud project (ADR-0083; `tinfirm.gmail.service-account`): the service account Google signs the add-on's requests as, `serviceAccountEmail` of `gcloud workspace-add-ons get-authorization`. With `TINFIRM_GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`; without both, `/api/gmail/**` answers `404` | unset |
| `TINFIRM_GMAIL_CLIENT_ID` | The same project's OAuth client (`tinfirm.gmail.client-id`), `oauthClientId` of `gcloud workspace-add-ons get-authorization`: the audience of the people's ID tokens | unset |
| `TINFIRM_MCP_STARTS_PER_HOUR` | Conversations one member may start through AI clients (the MCP endpoint's `start_conversation`) per hour in a company, counted from the `conversation` table (origin `CLIENT`) under a lock per member, so the limit holds across replicas; over it the tool error `RATE_LIMITED` with `retryAfter` (§15.10). `0` turns it off | `30` |
| `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_FIRINGS_PER_HOUR_DEFAULT` / `_MAX` | The instance's firing limits of triggers (§15.15): a new trigger's `max_firings_per_hour`, and the highest a company's admins may set, until a company sets its own (`PUT /settings`, `trigger_setting`); 1 to 10000 | `30` / `300` |
| `TINFIRM_MEMORY_MAX_CHARACTERS` | Agent memory (§15.18, ADR-0063; `tinfirm.memory.max-characters`): the most characters a memory's active notes hold in all, 1000 to 100000 (else the server does not start). Only what a conversation touches is sent to its agent, so it bounds storage and a compaction's request, not each prompt | `20000` |
| `TINFIRM_WEBHOOK_DELIVERIES_PER_MINUTE_DEFAULT` / `_MAX` | Read from package 5b (`tinfirm.webhook.deliveries-per-minute-default`, `-max`; §15.17). The instance's seed of a company's webhook rates: a webhook system's deliveries a minute when it names none, and the most one may name, until the company's admins set their own (`PUT /settings`, `webhookDeliveriesPerMinute`); 1 to 6000, counted per replica after the verification | `60` / `600` |
| `TINFIRM_CHAT_MAX_BODY` | Read from package 5a (`tinfirm.chat.max-body`, §15.16). The largest request the chat platforms' endpoints read (§4 Paths); larger is `413`. Also the longest Socket Mode message, in characters; a longer one is dropped unacknowledged. The operator's: it protects the server, not a company's budget | `1MB` |
| `TINFIRM_CHAT_MESSAGES_PER_USER_MINUTE` | Read from package 5a (`tinfirm.chat.messages-per-user-minute`). The instance's seed of a company's chat rate, messages a minute per chat user, until the company's admins set their own (`PUT /chat-settings`); counted per replica, after the verification | `20` |
| `TINFIRM_CHAT_MESSAGES_PER_USER_DAY` | Read from package 5a (`tinfirm.chat.messages-per-user-day`). The seed of messages a day per chat user | `200` |
| `TINFIRM_CHAT_EVENTS_PER_SYSTEM_MINUTE` | Read from package 5a (`tinfirm.chat.events-per-system-minute`). The seed of verified events a minute per chat system | `600` |
| `TINFIRM_CHAT_SOCKETS_PER_SYSTEM` | Read from package 5a (`tinfirm.chat.sockets-per-system`). Socket Mode connections per Slack system across the `worker` replicas, each a leased slot of `chat_socket` (Slack allows 10 per app) | `2` |
| `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_POLL_TICK` | Package 4's part b (§15.15): how often a `worker` replica looks for the polls that are due (`trigger_poll_due`); each poll is due at its system's `pollIntervalSeconds` (30 to 3600, 60 when its settings name none; its admins set it on a tracker through the Systems API, from each poll's next pass), doubled while less than a tenth of the system's rate limit is left | `PT15S` |
| `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_SCHEDULE_TICK` | How often a `worker` replica looks for the schedules that are due (`trigger_schedule_due`) | `PT30S` |
| `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_SETTLE` | A subject changed more recently than this is left for a poll's next pass, so that the transaction recording a write of Tinfirm's (`system_write`) has committed before the change is read; longer than the tool timeout | `PT90S` |
| `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_CATCH_UP` | A change older than this when a poll first reads it updates the snapshot and fires nothing (`missed_count`) | `PT24H` |
| `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_MISSED_AFTER` | A schedule's firing due longer ago than this was missed: catch-up `NONE` skips it (`SKIPPED_MISSED`), `ONE` fires once | `PT1H` |
| `TINFIRM_LITELLM_ADMIN_URL` | Read from package 2 on (`tinfirm.model.litellm-admin-url`, §15.4). The LiteLLM bundled with the deployment ([ADR-0059](adr/0059-litellm-budgets-and-the-model-proxy.md)), inside the deployment's network: Compose `http://litellm:4000`, Helm `http://<release>-litellm.<namespace>.svc:4000`. The server reaches a company's `BUNDLED` gateway here for its admin API, and for the runs' model requests it relays when `TINFIRM_LITELLM_MODEL_URL` is unset; only the server reaches it, and runner hosts never do. It is the operator's address, so the outbound guard does not apply to it. Unset, or without `TINFIRM_LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`, no gateway is bundled, and companies configure their own (`EXTERNAL`) | unset |
| `TINFIRM_LITELLM_MODEL_URL` | `tinfirm.model.litellm-model-url` ([ADR-0069](adr/0069-company-model-gateway.md)). The bundled LiteLLM's model port, which serves its model routes alone: Compose `http://model-api:4001`, Helm `http://<release>-litellm.<namespace>.svc:4001`. The model requests the server relays for runs and its own (a memory's compaction) go here instead of the admin URL; its admin API stays on `TINFIRM_LITELLM_ADMIN_URL`. The operator's address, so the outbound guard does not apply to it. Unset: the model requests go to the admin URL, as with an `EXTERNAL` gateway | unset |
| `TINFIRM_LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` | Read from package 2 on (`tinfirm.model.litellm-master-key`). The bundled LiteLLM's master key (its `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`), for its admin API. A secret, never logged: Compose takes it from `.env`, Helm from the Secret `litellm.existingSecret` | unset |
| `TINFIRM_MODEL_KEY_ROTATION_INTERVAL` | Read from the key rotation on (`tinfirm.model.key-rotation-interval`, §15.4; security review row 2). How long after its creation each principal's LiteLLM key is rotated, as an ISO-8601 duration; at least `P1D`, else the server does not start. The reconciliation (`tinfirm.model.reconcile-interval`) finds the keys that are due | `P30D` |
| `TINFIRM_MODEL_KEY_ROTATION_GRACE` | Read from the key rotation on (`tinfirm.model.key-rotation-grace`, §15.4). How long a key replaced on schedule still serves the relayed requests that already hold it before LiteLLM blocks it; `PT0S` to `PT1H`, else the server does not start. A rotation an admin asks for gives none | `PT10M` |
| `TINFIRM_MODEL_RELAY_REQUIRE_HOST_TOKEN` | `tinfirm.model.relay-require-host-token`. The model relay refuses a run's token without its runner host's own token (`X-Tinfirm-Runner-Token`) from every host (§15.4). Off, it refuses it only from hosts whose latest `hello.runnerVersion` is 0.3.0 or later, which always send it, and accepts an older host's, warning once a day per host and replica. Runner host 0.3.0 is the minimum: turn it off only while an older host is upgraded | `true` |
| `TINFIRM_MODEL_RELAY_MAX_INFLIGHT_BYTES` | `tinfirm.model.relay-max-inflight-bytes`, in bytes. The most bytes of request bodies the model relay holds at once on a replica, each body counted twice while it is read and written anew, then once until its answer ends; a body beyond it is `429` with `Retry-After` (§15.4). In memory per replica. At least twice `tinfirm.model.relay-max-request-bytes`, else the server does not start | `268435456` (256 MiB) |
| `TINFIRM_MODEL_RELAY_BODY_TIMEOUT` | `tinfirm.model.relay-body-timeout`. The longest the model relay waits for a request's whole body; a body still arriving then is `408`. Each wait for its next part is bounded by Tomcat's `server.tomcat.connection-timeout` besides | `PT30S` |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_BUNDLES_STORE` | Session bundles (ADR-0097, §15.27; `tinfirm.session-bundles.store`): `none` (off: no `run.assign` carries `sessionBundle`, and the hosts keep their homes), `filesystem` or `s3`; any other value, `filesystem` without a directory, or `s3` without a bucket and its key, stops the server | `none` |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_BUNDLES_DIRECTORY` | The `filesystem` store's directory (`tinfirm.session-bundles.directory`), for Compose and the tests; one file of mode `0600` per object | empty |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_BUNDLES_S3_ENDPOINT` / `_REGION` / `_BUCKET` / `_PATH_STYLE` / `_PREFIX` | The `s3` store (`tinfirm.session-bundles.s3.*`): the endpoint of any S3-compatible storage (empty: AWS's own), its region, a private bucket that holds nothing else, path-style addressing (MinIO, Ceph), and the objects' prefix | empty, `us-east-1`, empty, `false`, `session-bundles/` |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_BUNDLES_S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` | The server's key for that bucket only, from a Secret; required for `s3` | empty |
| `TINFIRM_SESSION_BUNDLES_RETENTION` | A bundle goes this long after its conversation's last turn, and when the conversation ends (`tinfirm.session-bundles.retention`, at least an hour); `worker` replicas sweep hourly | `P30D` |

The model module's tuning (`ModelProperties`, package 2, §15.4) beyond the eight variables above has no variable of its own in `application.yaml`: set it as the property, or as the variable Spring's relaxed binding derives from it (`TINFIRM_MODEL_ADMIN_TIMEOUT` and so on). The defaults are the code's:

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.model.admin-timeout` | Connect and request timeout of one call to a company's LiteLLM admin API; also the connect timeout of the relay's requests, which share its client | `PT10S` |
| `tinfirm.model.reconcile-interval` | The delay between two reconciliations of every company's keys with LiteLLM, on a `worker` replica (§15.4) | `PT5M` |
| `tinfirm.model.reconcile-initial-delay` | How long after startup the first reconciliation runs. Read by the job's schedule only, not a field of `ModelProperties` | `PT1M` |
| `tinfirm.model.relay-idle-timeout` | The relay's longest wait for LiteLLM's answer headers (else `504`) and between two parts of an answer (then the answer is cut off); a streaming answer has no total limit | `PT10M` |
| `tinfirm.model.relay-max-request-bytes` | The largest request body the relay passes; a larger one is `413` | `67108864` (64 MiB) |
| `tinfirm.model.relay-run-concurrency` | Relayed requests of one run at a time; more are `429`. In memory per replica | `16` |
| `tinfirm.model.relay-host-concurrency-per-slot` | Relayed requests of one runner host at a time, per slot of its ceiling (`max_concurrent_runs_ceiling`, at least 1); more are `429`. In memory per replica | `16` |
| `tinfirm.model.key-rotation-settle` | How long after LiteLLM blocked a rotated-out key it is deleted and its late spend moved to the new key (§15.4): LiteLLM writes requests' spend every 10 to 15 seconds (`proxy_batch_write_at`), so a gateway that writes less often needs longer; `PT0S` to `PT1H` | `PT1M` |

The test profile sets both reconciliation values to `PT1H`, and the tests reconcile on demand; it sets `key-rotation-settle` to `PT1H` as well, so that no in-process wake runs a rotation's step during a test, which moves the row's times back instead.

The run module's tuning (`RunProperties` and its schedules, package 3b, §15.6) beyond the variables above has none of its own either, and is set the same way:

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.run.prepare-timeout` | How long the assignment of a run may take to prepare its `run.assign` (the turn's content, its git tokens, the relay's token) before the accept timeout may take it back; at least a second | `PT2M` |
| `tinfirm.run.stream-keep-alive` | An idle live stream of a run's events, or of a conversation's, gets a comment after this, so that proxies keep it open; a conversation's stream checks its caller again then | `PT15S` |
| `tinfirm.run.dispatch-interval` | The delay between two dispatcher passes on a `worker` replica. Read by the schedule only | `PT2S` |
| `tinfirm.run.reaper-interval` | The delay between two reaper passes on a `worker` replica. Read by the schedule only | `PT5S` |

The test profile sets `tinfirm.run.stream-keep-alive` to `PT1S` and `tinfirm.run.tool-timeout` to `PT4S`, and `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.answer-within` to `PT3S`.

The chat module's tuning (`ChatProperties`, package 5a, §15.16) beyond the variables above is set the same way: `tinfirm.chat.tick` (`PT1S`, how often a `worker` replica handles the due events), `tinfirm.chat.silence-after` (`PT30M`, a Slack system answered `503` and silent this long turns `ERROR`), `tinfirm.chat.link-lifetime` (`PT10M`, a sign-in link's), `tinfirm.chat.agent-hold` (`PT1H`, a direct message waiting for the agent picker), `tinfirm.chat.socket-tick` (`PT5S`) and `tinfirm.chat.socket-lease` (`PT30S`). `tinfirm.chat.endpoints.*` (`ChatEndpoints`: Slack's API, file host and Socket Mode hosts, the Bot Framework's connector, login and OpenID metadata) has no variables: the platforms' endpoints are fixed, and only the tests point them at their stubs. So are `tinfirm.gmail.endpoints.*` (`GmailEndpoints`: Google's keys and the Gmail API, ADR-0083). The server refuses to start with any of them changed outside the `test` and `e2e` profiles, also through a variable (`FixedEndpoints`): changed, Google's keys or the Bot Framework's OpenID metadata would make it trust tokens another party signs. The test profile sets `tinfirm.chat.socket-tick` to `PT1S`.

The conversation module's tuning (`AttachmentProperties` and `ChangeRequestProperties`, package 3c's part 3, and the recovery of calls, part 2, §15.7) has properties without a variable of their own, set the same way:

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.conversation.change-requests.state-tick` | The delay between two passes of the watch of change requests on a `worker` replica, each of which reads the states that are due | `PT15S` |
| `tinfirm.conversation.call-recovery.initial-delay` | How long after the start a `worker` replica first looks for the calls a restart left behind (an approved call, a parked one). Read by the schedule only | `PT20S` |
| `tinfirm.conversation.call-recovery.interval` | The delay between two such passes. Read by the schedule only | `PT2M` |
| `tinfirm.conversation.staged-file-sweep` | The delay between two passes of the sweep of expired held files (ADR-0084) on a `worker` replica, and the first after the start. Read by the schedule only | `PT1M` |
| `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.sweep-interval` | The delay between two passes of the call journal's sweep and of the submission keys' on a `worker` replica, and the first after the start (package R, §15.14). Read by the schedules only | `PT1M` |
| `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.answer-within` | How long after a call's claim a request sent again, which waits for the call running elsewhere, is answered at the latest: `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (a `SAFE` call `TOOL_FAILED`) while it still runs (package R, §15.14). Within the runner host's wait of 65 s from its first send. Positive, else the server does not start | `PT55S` |

The test profile sets the files' limits to 64 KB a file, 5 files and 160 KB a conversation (the multipart limit stays 25 MB), the tick to `PT1H` (the tests read the states on demand), both delays of the recovery to `PT1H` (the tests run its pass on demand) and the journal's sweep interval to `PT1H` (the tests sweep on demand, as of a time they choose).

The governance module's spend sync (`SpendProperties`, package 6, §15.12) has no variable of its own either, and is set the same way:

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.spend.sync-interval` | The delay between two passes of the spend sync on a `worker` replica, each of which settles the runs that are due. Read by the schedule only | `PT1M` |
| `tinfirm.spend.sync-initial-delay` | How long after startup the first pass runs. Read by the schedule only | `PT1M` |
| `tinfirm.spend.source` | `LITELLM`: a run settles from LiteLLM's spend logs, else from the relay's count after `relay-after`; `RELAY`: from the relay's count as soon as it is due, without reading the logs (the contract's open point 1, should the pinned LiteLLM not keep the request tags) | `LITELLM` |
| `tinfirm.spend.settle-delay` | How long after a run's end its logs are first read: LiteLLM writes them in batches | `PT2M` |
| `tinfirm.spend.relay-after` | How long after a run's end its logs are waited for before the relay's count settles it | `PT1H` |

The test profile sets both schedule values to `PT1H`: the tests settle on demand (`SpendSync.syncDue`).

The trigger module's polls (`PollProperties`, package 4's part b, §15.15) have four more properties without a variable of their own, set the same way:

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.trigger.lease` | How long a pass holds its poll (`trigger_poll.lease_until`): another replica does not poll it meanwhile | `PT10M` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.overlap` | How far a window's bound reads back, for changes a system lists late | `PT2M` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.max-per-pass` | The most subjects one pass reads (1 to 1000); a pass that stops at it goes on from its offset | `200` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.unreachable-after` | After failing this long, a poll's triggers wait `SYSTEM_UNREACHABLE` | `PT1H` |

The test profile sets both trigger ticks to `PT1H`: the tests run a poll's pass and a schedule's firing on demand (`TriggerPolls.poll`, `ScheduleFirings.fireOne`).

The trigger module's write-back (`OutboxProperties`, package 4's part c, §15.15) has no variable of its own in `application.yaml` either, and is set the same way:

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.tick` | The delay between two passes of the write-back's delivery on a `worker` replica, each of which delivers the rows that are due; a transaction that queues rows also starts a pass after its commit. Read by the schedule only | `PT15S` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.deliver-after-commit` | Whether a transaction that queued rows, or made them due, starts a pass after its commit on a thread of its own; else the tick delivers them | `true` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.claim` | How long a pass holds a row it claimed (`claimed_until`) | `PT5M` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.batch` | The due rows one round claims, each the oldest undelivered row of its conversation (`outbox_due`), 1 to 500 | `20` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.rounds` | Rounds per pass while they make progress, 1 to 100 | `10` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.max-attempts` | Attempts before a row that keeps failing is dropped, 1 to 10 (`outbox_attempts_ck`) | `10` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.first-retry` / `last-retry` | The wait after a first failure, doubling after each, at most the last (a system's longer `Retry-After` up to the last); the first is also how long a claim puts a row off, without an attempt, while the company's runs are stopped or its system is paused | `PT1M` / `PT1H` |
| `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.binding-wait` | How long a row waits for its binding, from the binding's `waiting_since`, before it is dropped | `P7D` |

The identity module's limits (`AuthProperties`, `OAuthProperties`) and the runner module's frame size (`RunnerProperties`) have no variable of their own either, and are set the same way. The login throttle's defaults are `application.yaml`'s, the others the classes':

| Property | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `tinfirm.auth.login-throttle.per-email.capacity` / `.period` | The failed logins one email may have, refilled gradually over the period (§5); in memory per replica | `5` / `1m` |
| `tinfirm.auth.login-throttle.per-ip.capacity` / `.period` | Every login attempt of one client address (§5); in memory per replica | `30` / `1m` |
| `tinfirm.oauth.cimd.timeout` | Connect and request timeout of one fetch of a client's Client ID Metadata Document (§14.11) | `PT5S` |
| `tinfirm.oauth.cimd.max-document-bytes` | The largest such document read | `16384` |
| `tinfirm.oauth.cimd.cache-ceiling` | The longest a document is cached, whatever its `Cache-Control` says | `PT1H` |
| `tinfirm.oauth.cimd.cache-size` | The most documents cached at once | `1000` |
| `tinfirm.runner.max-message-bytes` | The largest inbound text frame of a runner connection; a larger one closes the connection (`1009`) | `1048576` (1 MiB) |

The lifetimes of the authorization server's tokens are fixed, not configured: access token 1 hour, refresh token 30 days, authorization code 5 minutes (§14.11). `application.yaml` also sets Spring AI's own MCP server properties (`spring.ai.mcp.server.protocol: STATELESS`, `type: SYNC`, `request-timeout: PT60S`, tools only); they are not part of the deployment contract. Three properties exist for the tests only and are deliberately not in the tables: `tinfirm.oauth.cimd.allow-http` and `tinfirm.oauth.cimd.allowed-private-hosts` let the integration tests serve a client metadata document from a local stub, and `tinfirm.connection.allowed-private-hosts` (`OutboundProperties`, read by `OutboundHttp`) lets them reach an authorization server or a company system's stub on a local address. They must never be set in production. Spring's relaxed binding would take the last from a variable (`TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOWEDPRIVATEHOSTS`, or `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOWED_PRIVATE_HOSTS` as the model gateway's `@Value` reads it), so the server refuses to start with it set outside the `test` profile (`OutboundHttp.refuseTestSettingsOutsideTests`), and a personal MCP server never honours it, in the tests either: the tests' personal servers reach their stubs on `localhost` through a bean only the tests define (`PersonalStubHosts`, `support.TestOutbound`), which the server refuses outside the `test` profile too (`OutboundTestSettingsTest`).

The server sends no product telemetry. The only thing it sends the vendor is error reports, and only while their delivery is automatic with a destination (ADR-0070, §15.21); `SystemInfo.telemetryEnabled` is true exactly then.
Logs are structured JSON on stdout (Spring Boot structured logging).
The server never logs secret values, passwords, session IDs or CSRF tokens.

## 5. Authentication

- **BFF pattern.** The server keeps a session. The browser holds only an HttpOnly cookie.
  - Cookie `TINFIRM_SESSION`: HttpOnly, `SameSite=Lax`, and `Secure` when `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL` is https.
  - Sessions live in Postgres via Spring Session JDBC, so replicas need no sticky sessions. Flyway creates the session tables; Spring must not auto-initialize them.
- **CSRF.** Cookie `XSRF-TOKEN`, readable by JS, plus the header `X-XSRF-TOKEN`. These are the Angular `HttpClient` defaults.
  - `GET /api/auth/providers` must set the cookie, because the SPA calls it before `POST /api/auth/login`.
  - Every mutating request needs the token, including login and logout.
- **Local accounts.** Passwords are hashed with Argon2id. Login is throttled per email and per client IP; the throttle returns 429 with problem code `RATE_LIMITED`. Failed and successful logins are audited in the instance chain. The REST API has no general per-principal limit; the other limits belong to what they protect: the MCP endpoint's per credential and its starts per hour, a webhook system's deliveries a minute, the chats' rates (§4), and the model relay's (§15.4).
  - The throttle is in memory per replica: behind N replicas a client gets up to N times the limits.
  - Per IP, every attempt counts (default 30 per minute, `tinfirm.auth.login-throttle.per-ip`, §4).
  - Per email, only failures count (default 5 per minute, `tinfirm.auth.login-throttle.per-email`).
  - Both limits are checked before the password is verified.
  - A password change or reset ends the user's other sessions.
- **Session lifecycle.**
  - Login, local or OIDC, rotates the session ID.
  - Idle timeout is `TINFIRM_SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, and the absolute lifetime is `TINFIRM_SESSION_MAX_AGE`. The absolute lifetime is checked against the session creation time on every request.
  - Logout deletes the session in the database and clears the session and XSRF cookies.
- **OIDC.** Spring Security `oauth2Login` against any provider, with PKCE enabled for every registration, including confidential clients. After login the server redirects to `/`, or back to an authorization request the login interrupted (below).
  - An OIDC user is identified by `(issuer, subject)`, never by email. A known `(issuer, subject)` logs in whatever its email claims say.
  - On the first login with an unknown `(issuer, subject)`, the server creates a new user with `authSource=OIDC`, if auto-provisioning is on and the email can be trusted. The registration's email trust decides (`TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_EMAIL_TRUST`, §4; [ADR-0026](adr/0026-per-provider-oidc-email-trust.md)):

    | Email trust | Email from | `email_verified` |
    |---|---|---|
    | `REQUIRE_VERIFIED_CLAIM` (default) | the `email` claim | must be `true` |
    | `TRUST_ISSUER` | the first claim in `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_EMAIL_CLAIMS` (default `email`) whose value looks like an email address; an array claim gives its first such value | may be missing; an explicit `false` still refuses |

    `TRUST_ISSUER` is for single-tenant issuers that the operator controls, such as one Entra ID tenant, which sends no `email_verified`. Its claim order for Entra ID is `email,preferred_username,verified_primary_email` ([operations/entra-id.md](operations/entra-id.md)). The server does not start when a registration names claims other than `email` without `TRUST_ISSUER`, because `email_verified` speaks only for `email`.
  - Without a usable email the first login fails with code `EMAIL_MISSING`, and the SPA shows `/login?error=EMAIL_MISSING`. When the email trust does not accept the email (no `email_verified=true` under `REQUIRE_VERIFIED_CLAIM`, or an explicit `email_verified=false`), it fails with code `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`, and the SPA shows `/login?error=EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`. A provider that lists email domains refuses an email outside them with `EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED` (below). A provider that decides roles refuses a person its claim maps to no role with `ROLE_NOT_MAPPED` (below). Every other refusal except `EMAIL_IN_USE` gives `OIDC_LOGIN_FAILED`; the `auth.login.failed` event records the reason (§8).
  - It **never** links automatically to an existing user. If the email already belongs to another user, login fails with problem code `EMAIL_IN_USE`, and the SPA shows `/login?error=EMAIL_IN_USE`. This holds for every email trust.
  - OIDC users cannot use `/api/me/password`, which returns `409 NOT_LOCAL_USER`.
- **Security headers** on every response:
  - `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; font-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; object-src 'none'`
  - `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`
  - `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`
  - `X-Frame-Options: DENY`
  - HSTS when `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL` is https.

  The SPA must work under this CSP, so it uses no inline scripts. Angular's critical-CSS inlining is off, because it injects an `onload` handler.

  The Outlook add-in's pages, `/outlook/**` (ADR-0074), are the one exception, on the web app's chain only: their CSP adds `https://appsforoffice.microsoft.com` (Office.js, which Microsoft requires add-ins to load from its CDN) to `script-src` and `connect-src`, and `https://ajax.aspnetcdn.com` (MicrosoftAjax.js, which Office.js loads for Outlook before it starts) to `script-src`, and has `frame-ancestors 'self'` followed by `TINFIRM_OUTLOOK_FRAME_ANCESTORS` (§4); they carry no `X-Frame-Options`. The other headers are the same.
- **Bootstrap.** On startup, if no user exists and both bootstrap variables are set, the server creates an instance admin, audited with actor `SYSTEM`. The operation is idempotent. It creates no company: the declared companies follow at every start, and the bootstrap admin is the owner of each that names no other (below). `TINFIRM_BOOTSTRAP_COMPANY_NAME` is no longer read.
- **Deactivated users.** A user with `active=false` cannot log in, and their existing sessions stop working.
- **Bearer credentials** ([ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md)). The MCP endpoint of a company, and only it, takes `Authorization: Bearer` with one of two credentials of a user:
  - an **OAuth access token** from Tinfirm's authorization server (§14.11);
  - a **personal access token**: `tfp_` and 43 base64url characters (32 random bytes), shown once at creation; the server keeps `hex(sha256(token))`, the name, the scope, an optional expiry, `last_used_at` (at most a minute stale) and the revocation. A user holds at most 25 active tokens (`409 TOKEN_LIMIT`).

  A `tfp_` token is resolved by its format, anything else through the JWT decoder. A missing token is 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/api/companies/{companyId}/mcp", scope="tinfirm:read"`; an unknown, malformed, revoked or expired token, a token for another company's endpoint, or a token of a deactivated user, is 401 `TOKEN_INVALID` with the same header after `error="invalid_token", `. An OAuth access token is accepted only while its authorization still holds it unrevoked, so revoking a grant on `/me` or the token at `/oauth2/revoke` takes effect at once. The MCP chain is stateless, has no CSRF, ignores the session cookie, and refuses with 403 every request with an `Origin` header other than the origin of `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL` (scheme, host, and a port other than the scheme's default), which only Tinfirm's own page sends: the Outlook add-in's task pane (ADR-0074). The REST API stays session-only: it accepts no bearer token.
- **SCIM tokens** ([ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md), §15.25). The SCIM endpoint, and only it, takes `Authorization: Bearer` with a company's SCIM token: `tfs_` and 43 base64url characters, shown once; `hex(sha256(token))` in `scim_token`, resolved before any tenant is known (`scim_token_by_hash`). Its requests run as the token (actor `SCIM`), in its company's tenant context; it reaches no other path.
- **A SCIM user's first sign-in** ([ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md) decision 6). An unknown `(issuer, subject)` of a registration bound to a company is bound to the member that company's SCIM created whose `externalId` is the sign-in's `sub`, or for an Entra ID v2.0 issuer the ID token's `oid`; with `_SCIM_MATCH=EXTERNAL_ID_OR_EMAIL`, else by the email the sign-in asserts under the registration's email trust and domains: the one link by email the server makes. Only a user with `authSource` `SCIM` (no password, no identity), active, and never an OWNER of any company. Audited `user.identity_bound`; the user becomes `OIDC`, or `SAML` through a SAML provider. What does not bind falls through: a new user under the registration's `_AUTO_PROVISION`, `EMAIL_IN_USE` for a SCIM user's email.
- **SAML 2.0** ([ADR-0086](adr/0086-saml-sign-in.md), §15.26), Enterprise, served after an Enterprise key expired too. A SAML user is identified by `(the IdP's entity ID, subject)` in `oidc_issuer` and `oidc_subject`, `authSource` `SAML`; an identity of one kind never signs in through the other (`identity_in_use`). The subject is the NameID, never transient, or the attribute `_SUBJECT` names. A SAML provider is trusted for the email as an OIDC one under `TRUST_ISSUER`; the email, the name, the role, the SCIM binding, the declared owner and the seat fallback are the OIDC rules, through the same `ExternalProvisioning`. Sign-in starts at Tinfirm only: no IdP-initiated sign-in, no single logout. The AuthnRequest is saved server-side for 5 minutes, used once, and bound to the browser by the `TINFIRM_SAML` cookie (`HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=None`, `Path=/login/saml2/sso/`), since the IdP's cross-site POST carries no `Lax` session cookie; the session cookie stays `Lax`, and SAML needs an https public URL, `localhost` aside. A sign-in ends on a new session as an OIDC login does (§5, ADR-0045), ending at the earlier of `TINFIRM_SESSION_MAX_AGE` and the assertion's `SessionNotOnOrAfter`. A refusal redirects to `/login?error=` with `EMAIL_MISSING`, `EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, `EMAIL_IN_USE`, `ROLE_NOT_MAPPED`, `EDITION_REQUIRED`, else `SAML_LOGIN_FAILED`, whose reason is only in the audit and the log.
- **Authorization requests survive the login** ([ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md)). `/oauth2/authorize` without a session saves the request in the session (Spring's request cache, for that endpoint only) and redirects to `/login?returnTo=<path and query of the request>`. `returnTo` is honoured only when it is a same-origin path to `/oauth2/authorize`: after a local login the SPA navigates there; after an OIDC login the server's success handler redirects there from the request cache, once. Every other login still ends on `/`.
- **The consent.** The authorization server's chain shares the web app's session, security context repository, CSRF cookie and security headers. Its user is loaded fresh on every request, as on the web app's chain, and a deactivated user loses the session. The consent page posts the decision to `/oauth2/authorize` form-encoded (`client_id`, `state`, one `scope` per approved scope; none denies), with the `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header, which the server checks although Spring exempts its endpoints from CSRF. Sent with `Accept: application/json`, the answer is `200 {"redirectUri": "..."}` and the page navigates there: a form would be stopped by `form-action 'self'` when its answer redirects to the client's loopback address. Every authorization response, a code or an error, carries `iss` (RFC 9207).
- **Companies and their sign-in come from the deployment** ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md)). Companies' own OIDC providers configured in the app are gone; every provider is a registration of the environment.
  - **Declared companies.** `tinfirm.companies.<key>` (`TINFIRM_COMPANIES_<KEY>_NAME`, `_OWNER`, `_ID`, §4). At every start, after the bootstrap above, on one replica at a time (an advisory lock of their own, in one transaction), as actor `SYSTEM`, each declared company in key order: a company with that key (`company.key`) is left as it is; else the company `_ID` names, which must have no key, gets the key (`company.updated` with `key`); else the one company without a key whose name is exactly the declared name is adopted the same way; else the company is created (`company.created` with `name` and `key`), without members. The server does not start with an invalid key, a blank name, a malformed id, an id no company has or whose company has another key, or two companies without a key that both have the declared name. Companies that no declaration names stay as they are; when at least one company is declared, the start logs a warning that lists them. Nothing is deleted. No API creates a company (§6); `Company.key` in the API names the declaration, null for a company no declaration adopted.
  - **The declared owner.** While a declared company has no member with role OWNER, the active user whose email (case-insensitive) is the declared owner (`_OWNER`, else `TINFIRM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAIL`) becomes its OWNER: `member.added`, or `member.role.changed` for a member with another role, by `SYSTEM`, with `declaredOwner: true`. At every start this is a local user only (`UserDirectory.findLocalByEmail`): an instance admin made that account. At every successful login of a user with that email (the identity event `UserEvents.UserSignedIn`, after the login's commit), it is a local login, or an OIDC login through a provider whose company is this one and whose claims in that login assert the user's email under its email trust and in its domains (`providerCompanyId`; a known identity's stored email may come from another registration of the same issuer); another company's provider, or a provider of none, claims nothing, since an OIDC user's email is what the provider asserted. A company that has an OWNER is never changed by it. On an instance with several companies, give each provider its `_EMAIL_DOMAINS` too: a provider without them can create the account of another company's owner, who then meets `EMAIL_IN_USE` at their own provider.
  - **A provider's company.** `TINFIRM_AUTH_OIDC_PROVIDERS_<ID>_COMPANY` names the declared company a new user from that registration joins; unset, the declared company when exactly one is declared, otherwise none. `_ROLE` is the new member's role, `MEMBER` (default), `ADMIN` or `VIEWER` ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md)); a role that holds a seat becomes `VIEWER` when no seat is free, recorded as `member.added` with `seatFallback: true` and `providerRole`, and the company's Owners and Admins get a `SEAT_FALLBACK` inbox item (§15.24): signing in never fails for want of a seat. `_EMAIL_DOMAINS`, when set, limits new users to emails whose domain (the part after the last `@`, lower case, matched exactly) is listed: any other email of an unknown identity fails with code `EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED` (`/login?error=EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, audit reason `email_domain_not_allowed`), checked after the email trust and before `EMAIL_IN_USE`, so the refusal does not tell whether the address is taken. The server does not start when a registration names a company that is not declared, a role other than `MEMBER`, `ADMIN` or `VIEWER`, or a domain that is not a domain name. A user the provider creates becomes a member of its company with its role, `member.added` by `SYSTEM` with `userId`, `role` and `provider` (the registration id), in the transaction that creates the user. Under `APP` (below) a known identity only logs in, whichever provider it comes through: no membership is granted again, so a member the company removed stays removed. Nothing ties a user to a company: any user can be added to any company.
  - **A provider's companies** ([ADR-0089](adr/0089-companies-from-the-role-claim.md)). `_COMPANIES_<KEY>_ROLE_MAP_ADMIN`, `_ROLE_MAP_MEMBER` and `_ROLE_MAP_VIEWER` (§4) name, for a further declared company `<KEY>`, the role claim's values that give each role there, so that one provider fills several companies: its own (`_COMPANY`, with `_ROLE_MAP_*`) and each of `_COMPANIES_*`. The server resolves a provider's companies from the deployment before the sign-in (`ProviderCompanies`), never from the request; a declared company that does not exist yet (it waits for a licence, §15.24) is left out until it does. The sign-in runs as `SYSTEM` in the tenant context of the provider's own company, else its first, and `ProvisionedMembershipsImpl` enters each company's context (`TenantScope.enterCompany`) before it writes that company's membership, so each write and each audit event is that company's. A new user joins each company whose map names them, with the role mapped there (`member.added` in each company's chain; `user.created` carries `companyId` and `role` for one company, `companyIds` for several). Settings → Sign-in of a company lists every provider that fills it, as its own or through the claim.
  - **A provider's role** ([ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md)). `_ROLE_SOURCE`, `_ROLE_CLAIM` and `_ROLE_MAP_ADMIN`, `_ROLE_MAP_MEMBER`, `_ROLE_MAP_VIEWER` (§4) say who decides a member's role. The claim is read from the ID token and the userinfo response, as the email claims are; a string or an array of strings, each value matched exactly; when it holds values of several roles the highest wins (ADMIN, MEMBER, VIEWER); there is no value for OWNER. A provider without `_ROLE_CLAIM` runs as `APP`, which the start logs once.
    - **`IDP`, the provider decides.** At every sign-in, of a new user or a known identity, the mapped role is applied to the membership of the provider's company in the sign-in's transaction, as actor `SYSTEM`: `member.role.changed` with `roleSource: IDP`, `provider`, `previousRole` and `role` when it changes, a promotion and a demotion alike, with the seat fallback (a seat role for a person without a seat while none is free gives VIEWER, `seatFallback: true` and `providerRole`, and the `SEAT_FALLBACK` item, one per person while it lasts; each later sign-in asks again). The membership's role source (`company_membership.role_source`) becomes `IDP:<registration id>`; a new member joins with it. A claim that is missing or maps to no role in any of the provider's companies refuses the sign-in: `/login?error=ROLE_NOT_MAPPED`, audited `auth.login.failed` with reason `role_not_mapped` and the user when known, checked for a new user after the email domains and before `EMAIL_IN_USE`, so the refusal does not tell whether the address is taken; no user is created, and a known user's memberships, roles and content stay.
