An OWNER is never changed, refused or removed, nor is a membership whose role source is `SCIM` ([ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md): SCIM wins over sign-in); either keeps a person whose claim maps nothing signed in as they are. **Membership follows the claim** ([ADR-0089](adr/0089-companies-from-the-role-claim.md)): in each of the provider's companies, a role mapped for a person who is no member grants the membership (`member.added` with `provider` and `roleSource: IDP`, the seat fallback, a principal of their own), and no role mapped for a member is a departure, as a member an admin removes (`ProvisionedMemberships.leave`, under the company's lock as the member operations take it, then `MemberService.leave`: the principal `LEFT`, the content kept, the departure's checklist to the Owners and Admins, `member.removed` with `provider` and `roleSource: IDP`), but only when the claim still maps a role in some company of the provider: a claim that maps none refuses and ends nothing. A member the company removed in the app comes back at their next sign-in while the claim names them; under `IDP` people are removed at the IdP. One provider decides per company, counting every company a provider fills: the server does not start when two `IDP` providers share one.
    - **`APP`, the app decides.** A new user gets the mapped role, else `_ROLE`, in the provider's own company, and the role mapped there in each further company of `_COMPANIES_*` whose map names them, once, with the seat fallback; a known identity only signs in. The membership's role source is `APP`.
    - **A switch to `APP`**, the removal of a provider, or its move to another company is a restart: once the server is ready, every membership whose role source names a provider that no longer decides that company's roles (the provider's own company or one of its `_COMPANIES_*`) becomes `APP`, keeping its role, audited once per company and provider as `member.role_source.changed` with `count` (`ProvisionedMemberships.releaseProviderRoles`). A switch to `IDP`, or a company added to a provider's `_COMPANIES_*`, takes each role at the person's next sign-in.
  - **The direct link.** The SPA's `/login?provider=<registrationId>`, for any registration of the instance (`^[a-z0-9]+$`), offers only that provider and the password form: the link an operator hands a company's people. An unknown registration at `/oauth2/authorization/{registrationId}` redirects to `/login?error=OIDC_LOGIN_FAILED`, unaudited.

## 6. Authorization matrix

Roles apply per company: **OWNER**, **ADMIN**, **MEMBER** and **VIEWER** ([ADR-0058](adr/0058-authority-from-the-audience.md), VIEWER from [ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md); `CompanyRole`, `CompanyPermission`). **Instance admin** is a flag on the user, separate from company roles. The rows of the package 1 to package 3 contracts (§15) name their permission; the triggers' rights are the trigger module's own (`TriggerCallers`, §15.15). A **service account** is a principal of kind `SERVICE`; the API, the code and the tables call it a service identity (`/service-identities`, `serviceIdentityId`, `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES`), and this file names those as they are ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md), decision 1). A **credential** is a company credential that a system holds; a member's own credential on a system is their **account**.

| Operation | OWNER | ADMIN | MEMBER | VIEWER | Instance admin who is not a member |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| List or get the company | yes | yes | yes (also while suspended) | yes (also while suspended) | yes (getting it audited `admin.company.accessed`) |
| Create a company | — | — | — | — | — (only the deployment declares companies, §5; [ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md)) |
| Rename the company | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List members | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Add a member, or change a role to or from OWNER | yes | only non-owner roles | no | no | non-owner roles, and appointing an OWNER (package 6); never demoting one |
| Remove a member | yes | only non-owners | no | no | only non-owners |
| Suspend or resume a member (`MANAGE_MEMBERS`; an OWNER's needs `MANAGE_OWNERS`; nobody themselves, never the last active OWNER; §15.13) | yes | only non-owners | no | no | yes |
| Read the company's sign-in providers (`GET /sign-in`; `MANAGE_MEMBERS`, §15.26) | yes | yes | no | no | yes (ADMIN's view) |
| See the company's SCIM and its tokens (`GET /scim`, `GET /scim/tokens`; `VIEW_PROVISIONING`, §15.25) | yes | yes | no | no | yes (ADMIN's view) |
| Issue or revoke a SCIM token (`POST /scim/tokens`, Enterprise; `DELETE /scim/tokens/{tokenId}`, every edition; `MANAGE_PROVISIONING`) | yes | no | no | no | no |
| See the kill switches that are on (`GET /kill-switches`, §15.13) | all | all | the company's stop and their own suspension | the company's stop and their own suspension | all |
| Stop or lift the company's runs (`STOP_RUNS`, §15.13) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Pause or resume a company system (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`, §15.13) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| A departure's preview and checklist, closing it (`MANAGE_MEMBERS`, §15.13) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Give an agent a new owner (`MANAGE_AGENTS`, §15.13) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| See on which company system a member holds an account, and its status (`MANAGE_MEMBERS`, I4, §15.3) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List the groups' names (`VIEW_GROUPS`) | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Create, rename, fill or delete a group; add or take out one of its members; list a group's members; read what its members may use through it (`MANAGE_GROUPS`) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List or get the service accounts, with whether the caller may use each (`VIEW_SERVICE_IDENTITIES`, §15.1) | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Create, rename, describe, suspend, resume or delete a service account; set the groups that may use it; list the credentials it borrows (`GET /service-identities/{id}/credentials`) (`MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES`) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Use a service account: act with the credentials it borrows and pay with its budget in what one sets up (a trigger's accounts table and payer, §15.15; `Principals.mayUse`) | every one | through a group | through a group | no | — |
| List or get the company's systems, their repositories and tools; the company's switch of personal MCP servers (`VIEW_SYSTEMS`, §15.2) | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Register, change, test or remove a company system; set or remove its app; add or remove its repositories; read an MCP server's catalogue, confirm a tool's effect, switch a tool; switch personal MCP servers on or off (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Register, change, test or remove one's own personal MCP server; set or remove its app; read its catalogue and confirm its tools' effects (`VIEW_SYSTEMS`, the owner only, while the company allows them) | yes | yes | yes | no | — |
| List a company system's credentials; add one (a token, or a sign-in with the system's app), give it a new token or sign it in again, rename, disable, enable or remove it; lend it to a service account, change the lending's access or withdraw it (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`, §15.3, [ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md); a chat system's bot changes only with the bot) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Connect, list or disconnect one's own accounts (`VIEW_SYSTEMS`, §15.3) | yes | yes | yes | list only | — |
| List grants; lend a system's credential to a group, change or withdraw a grant (`MANAGE_GRANTS`) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Set or remove a chat system's bot, set a Slack system's secrets, read its app manifest; read and change the company's chat rates (`GET`, `PUT /chat-settings`) (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`, §15.16) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Remove a member's chat account (`DELETE /members/{userId}/accounts/{systemId}`, `MANAGE_MEMBERS`, §15.16) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Read and confirm one's own chat sign-in link (`POST /chat-links/read`, `…/confirm`); read and set one's default agent of direct messages (`VIEW_COMPANY`; the caller's principal, §15.16) | yes | yes | yes | read the link and the default agent only | — |
| List a chat system's channels, each with the triggers on it the caller sees (`VIEW_COMPANY`, §15.17; OWNER and ADMIN also an author's channels, `authorPrincipalId`) | every trigger | every trigger | their triggers' | their triggers' (none) | every trigger |
| Read a webhook system's deliveries and their counts, try a body against its mapping (nothing stored), rotate its secret (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`, §15.17) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List secret metadata (`VIEW_SECRETS`) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Create, update or delete a secret (`MANAGE_SECRETS`; a managed secret is neither updated nor, while kept, deleted: §9) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List the company's runner hosts with their live status; register a company host, change its capacity or remove it (`MANAGE_RUNNER_HOSTS`, §15.4) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Register a personal runner host of one's own, which then serves one in every company one belongs to (ADR-0092); list one's own personal hosts, change their capacity or remove them (`VIEW_RUNNER_HOSTS`, `REGISTER_RUNNER_HOSTS`, the owner only: another member's personal host is `404`, to OWNER and ADMIN too) | yes | yes | yes | list only | — |
| Read, configure or test the company's model gateway and the defaults of its keys; read any principal's model access and set or clear its budget and models (`MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS`, §15.4) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List the models the company's gateway serves; read one's own model access with the subscriptions on one's personal hosts (`VIEW_MODEL_ACCESS`) | yes | yes | yes | yes | the models only (one's own access: `404`) |
| Read spend (`GET /spend`, `VIEW_MODEL_ACCESS`, §15.12; its CSV audited): the runs one paid and those of the triggers whose author or team one is; OWNER and ADMIN every run | every run; another's personal conversation by key, untitled | every run; another's personal conversations folded per person, without keys | own and their triggers' | own and their triggers' | as ADMIN |
| List or get the agents one sees (`VIEW_AGENTS`, §15.5): one's own, those shared with one's groups, and with `MANAGE_AGENTS` every agent | every agent | every agent | own and shared | own and shared | every agent |
| Create an agent, which the caller then owns (`VIEW_AGENTS`; the caller needs a principal in the company) | yes | yes | yes | no | — (`404`) |
| Edit an agent (its name, handle, description, instructions and runtimes; its systems with their tools, its repositories and network hosts), share it with groups, pause, resume or delete it (`MANAGE_AGENTS` for every agent, else its owner) | yes | yes | only own agents (`403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE` for one shared with them) | no | yes |
| Use an agent: read, save and reset one's own settings of it, accept its widening, read its readiness (its owner, a member of a group it is shared with, or a member it is shared with; `MANAGE_AGENTS` adds no use) | own or through a share | own or through a share | own or through a share | read only (`403 VIEWER_READ_ONLY` for a change) | — (`403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE`) |
| Read one's own memory of an agent, delete a note of it or clear it (`…/agents/{agentId}/settings/me/memory`; `VIEW_AGENTS` and the use of the agent, §15.18) | own | own | own | read only | — (`403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE`) |
| Read and set the company's memory settings, the model that tidies memories (`GET`, `PUT /memory-settings`; `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS`, §15.18) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| List or get the skills one sees, read their versions and download one (`VIEW_AGENTS`, §15.19, `SkillCallers`): one's own, those shared with one's groups or with one on one's own, and with `MANAGE_AGENTS` every live skill; the deleted ones with `?deleted=true` | every skill, and the deleted | every skill, and the deleted | own and shared (`?deleted=true`: `403`) | own and shared (`?deleted=true`: `403`) | every skill, and the deleted |
| Create a skill, which the caller then owns (`VIEW_AGENTS`; the caller needs a principal in the company) | yes | yes | yes | no | — (`404`) |
| Add a version to a skill, change its shares, delete it (`MANAGE_AGENTS` for every skill, else its owner; who sees it but may not edit it gets `403 SKILL_NOT_EDITABLE`) | yes | yes | only own skills | no | yes |
| List or get the triggers one sees, their firings, settings and accounts; read and review a trigger's memory (accept, delete, clear); run it now; pause it (`VIEW_COMPANY` and `TriggerCallers`, §15.15: its author, a member of its team, OWNER and ADMIN; to anyone else a trigger is `404`) | every trigger | every trigger | own and their teams' | own and their teams', to read (running or pausing one: `403 VIEWER_READ_ONLY`) | every trigger |
| Create a trigger, which the caller then authors, with an agent they use (`VIEW_COMPANY`; the caller needs a principal in the company) | yes | yes | yes | no | — (`404`) |
| Change a trigger, its settings and its accounts table, accept its widening, resume or delete it (its author; OWNER and ADMIN on the author's behalf; a member of its team gets `403 TRIGGER_NOT_EDITABLE`) | every trigger | every trigger | own only | no | every trigger |
| Reassign a trigger to another author and payer; list a system's polls (`TriggerCallers`: OWNER and ADMIN; anyone else who sees the trigger `403`) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Read the company's settings of triggers and webhooks (`GET /settings`, `VIEW_COMPANY`); change its firing limits and webhook rates (`PUT /settings`, OWNER and ADMIN, else `403`; §15.15, §15.17) | read and change | read and change | read | read | read and change |
| List and get the conversations one sees, and their subjects; list and download a conversation's files (`VIEW_COMPANY`, §15.7): a personal conversation is its person's, and OWNER sees it in full | every conversation (another person's reads audited, `conversation.read`) | own only (another's is `404`) | own only (another's is `404`) | own only (another's is `404`) | none (another's is `404`: ADMIN's view, package 6) |
| Start a conversation with an agent one uses, and be its person (`VIEW_COMPANY`; §15.5 for using the agent); start it with files of one's own conversations | yes | yes | yes | no (`403 VIEWER_READ_ONLY`) | — (`404`) |
| Message in a conversation, rename it, choose or continue one of its bindings, add or delete its files (its person only) | own only (another's is `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`) | own only (`404`) | own only (`404`) | no | — (`404`) |
| Store a file that looks like a credential in one's own conversation, acknowledging the match (`MANAGE_SECRETS`, and its person, §15.7) | yes | yes | no (`403`) | no (`403`) | — |
| Cancel, retry or end a conversation (its person, OWNER) | every conversation | own only (`404`) | own only (`404`) | no | none (`404`) |
| List and get the decisions of the conversations one sees (`VIEW_COMPANY`, §15.7: `GET /decisions` with `?open` and `?mine`, `GET /decisions/{decisionId}`) | every conversation's (another person's reads audited) | own conversations' only (another's is `404`) | own conversations' only (another's is `404`) | own conversations' only (another's is `404`) | none (`404`) |
| Decide a decision: answer a question, approve or deny an approval (`POST /decisions/{decisionId}/decide`; its approver, the conversation's person; scope `AGENT` also needs the right to edit the agent, else `403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE`) | own only (another's is `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`) | own only (`404`) | own only (`404`) | no (`403 VIEWER_READ_ONLY`) | — (`404`) |
| Read one's inbox (`VIEW_COMPANY`, §15.7: `GET /inbox`): what one acts on in one's own open conversations, and for OWNER and ADMIN the company's credentials and service accounts that keep conversations waiting (`RECONNECT_COMPANY`, `SUSPENDED`), with counts only | own, and the credentials | own, and the credentials | own | own | the credentials only |
| Dismiss a notice of one's own inbox for oneself, and show it again (`VIEW_COMPANY`, §15.7: `PUT`, `DELETE /inbox/dismissals/{noticeKey}`; the caller's principal; not audited) | own | own | own | own | — (`404`) |
| See a run, its events and their live stream, and cancel it (`VIEW_COMPANY` and the port `RunVisibility`, §15.6: a run follows its conversation, §15.7) | every run (another person's reads audited) | runs of own conversations only (`404`) | runs of own conversations only (`404`) | runs of own conversations only, to see (`404` otherwise) | none (`404`) |
| Download the company's Outlook add-in manifest (`GET /outlook/manifest`, `VIEW_COMPANY` and `OUTLOOK_ADDIN`, ADR-0077; ADR-0074; the web app offers it on the Settings pages, which only OWNER and ADMIN open) | yes | yes | yes | yes | — (`404`) |
| Download the company's Gmail add-on deployment file (`GET /gmail/deployment`, `VIEW_COMPANY` and `GMAIL_ADDON`, ADR-0077; ADR-0083; the web app offers it on the Settings pages, which only OWNER and ADMIN open) | yes | yes | yes | yes | — (`404`) |
| Read and confirm a Gmail sign-in link (`POST /gmail/links/read`, `/gmail/links/confirm`, and `GMAIL_ADDON`), and disconnect one's own Google account in every edition (`DELETE /gmail/links/{linkId}`); an active member, never an instance admin's break-glass; ADR-0083 | yes | yes | yes | yes | — (`404`) |
| Filter, verify and export the company's audit log, list its daily checks (`VIEW_AUDIT`, §15.11; `AUDIT_TOOLS`, ADR-0077, §15.24) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Acknowledge a failed daily check of the audit chain (`MANAGE_RETENTION`, §15.11) | yes | no (`403`) | no | no | no (`403`: ADMIN's view) |
| See who read a personal conversation (`GET /conversations/{id}/reads`, `VIEW_COMPANY`, §15.11): its person and whoever sees it | own, and every conversation | own only (another's is `404`) | own only (another's is `404`) | own only (another's is `404`) | none (`404`) |
| Read the retention periods (`VIEW_AUDIT`, §15.11) | yes | yes | no | no | yes |
| Change the retention periods (`RETENTION_SETTINGS`); list the open conversations older than them; set (`LEGAL_HOLDS`), list and lift legal holds; erase a person's or an outside identity's content (`MANAGE_RETENTION`, §15.11; the editions, ADR-0077, §15.24) | yes | no (`403`) | no | no | no (`403`: ADMIN's view) |
| Preview proposed retention periods or an erasure, changing nothing (`POST /retention/preview`, `POST …/erase/preview`; `MANAGE_RETENTION`, §15.11) | yes | no (`403`) | no | no | no (`403`: ADMIN's view) |
| `/api/admin/**` | — | — | — | — | instance admin only |

Rules:
- A non-member without instance admin gets **404** for every company-scoped path, never 403.
- Break-glass (package 6, §15.13; the package 6 contract, §5.10 and open point 12): an instance admin's access to a company where they are not a member is audited as `admin.company.accessed` and gives **ADMIN's view**: the permissions ADMIN holds, and `MANAGE_OWNERS` only to appoint an OWNER (`member.role.changed`), the remedy for a company without a reachable owner; demoting or removing an OWNER, `MANAGE_RETENTION` and every other OWNER-only permission are `403`; another person's personal conversation, its runs, decisions and files are `404`, as for ADMIN (so no `conversation.read` of an instance admin arises). A mandatory reason and an hour's access are planned (the package 6 contract, §5.10; threat model 1.15 and Q5).
- **A suspended member** (package 6, §15.13; open points 5 and 24): every company path answers `403 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` for a read and `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` for a write (`CompanyAccessGuard` tells them apart by the request's method), but the company's page and `GET /kill-switches`, which show the suspension (`requireAllowingSuspended`). Their bearer credentials (personal tokens, OAuth grants) and the authorization server's consent treat them as a non-member: `404` and `access_denied`.
- A member without the needed role gets **403**, an instance admin who is a member included: the break-glass is only for an instance admin who is **not** a member, and a member's role answers for them in every module, as it does for any member (`CompanyAccessGuard.require` takes the instance-admin branch only for a caller without a role in the company, so `CompanyAccess.viaInstanceAdmin` and the blanket `covers` it gives mean "not a member"). An instance admin who needs more in a company where they are a member asks its OWNER for the role.
- **Groups** ([ADR-0049](adr/0049-agent-kinds-groups-and-personal-accounts.md)) are a unit of permission beside the roles, not a role. A group may use service accounts (§15.1), receives grants of the systems' credentials (§15.3) and, from package 3, may use the agents shared with it (§15.5), and the skills shared with it (§15.19); package 4 adds a trigger's team. A group's right adds to a role and never replaces it, and a specific permission is a group's, never a role's: OWNER uses every service account, nobody else without a group, ADMIN included. What a member may not see because of a right they lack is 404, as for another company's id.
- **Runner hosts by scope** (package 2, §15.4). A registration names its `scope`, `PERSONAL` by default. A `COMPANY` host is registered by OWNER and ADMIN (`403` for a member) and belongs to no one; OWNER, ADMIN and an instance admin see it in `GET /runner-hosts`, change its capacity and remove it, and a member neither sees it there nor changes it (`403`). A `PERSONAL` host is registered by a member for themselves, with their principal as its owner (a caller without a principal in the company, an instance admin who is not a member, gets `403`); only its owner sees, changes and removes it, and to anyone else, OWNER and ADMIN included, it is `404`, as an unknown id is and as a personal MCP server is. So a member lists only their own personal hosts, and OWNER and ADMIN list the company hosts and their own personal hosts. The scope and the owner never change. A personal host is a machine of its owner's, present in every company they belong to (ADR-0092, §15.4): registered in one company, it is listed in each of theirs, and a member who leaves a company takes their presences there along, keeping the machine for their other companies.
- **Agents by owner and group** (package 3, §15.5; `Agents.access`, the contract's §5.1). Every member creates agents (`VIEW_AGENTS`) and owns those they create; an agent is private to its owner until shared with groups or with members on their own. Its owner sees, uses and edits it; a member of a group it is shared with, and a member it is shared with, see and use it; OWNER, ADMIN and an instance admin (`MANAGE_AGENTS`) see, edit, share, pause, resume and delete every agent, and use one only as its owner or through a share. To anyone else an agent is `404` on every path, as an unknown or deleted one is. Who sees an agent but may not edit it gets `403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE`, who sees it but may not use it `403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE`. A caller without a principal in the company (an instance admin who is not a member) creates no agent (`404`) and uses none. When its owner leaves, an agent passes to the admins.
- **Conversations by person** (package 3c, §15.7; the contract's §5.10, I15, I17). Every path of a conversation needs `VIEW_COMPANY` and nothing more; who may act then follows from the conversation. A personal conversation belongs to its person: they see it and act in it. OWNER sees every conversation in full (an instance admin who is not a member no longer does: break-glass gives ADMIN's view, package 6, §15.13), with its messages, runs, run events and bindings, which `seenByOwner` tells the person; ADMIN and every other member get `404` on every path, as for an unknown conversation, and lists leave it out. Every read by OWNER of a conversation of another person, or of what belongs to it, is audited as `conversation.read` (§8, §15.7; the security review of the redesign, row 18), and the app shows the person a standing notice of it (`seenByOwner`). Messaging, renaming, choosing a grant and continuing a binding are the person's alone: OWNER, who sees the conversation but is not its person, gets `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`. Cancelling, retrying and ending are the person's or OWNER's. Starting one needs a principal in the company (`404` without, as for an instance admin who is not a member) and the use of the agent (§15.5). Its files (package 3c's part 3) follow it: whoever sees it lists and downloads them, its outputs' documents included; only its person adds and deletes them (OWNER: `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`); ADMIN and every other member get `404`. A file that looks like a credential is stored only when its person holds `MANAGE_SECRETS` (OWNER, ADMIN) and acknowledges the match (a MEMBER gets `403`); a vault secret's value is never stored. Its decisions (package 3c's part 2) follow it too: whoever sees it lists and reads them, and `404` answers anyone else; only its person, the approver of each, decides them (`mayDecide`), and OWNER gets `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`. The inbox is each member's own: what they act on in their own open conversations, never another member's, OWNER's included; OWNER, ADMIN and an instance admin also see the company's credentials and service accounts that keep conversations waiting, with how many and nothing of the conversations.
- **Runs by conversation** (package 3b, §15.6; package 3c, §15.7). A run follows its conversation, through the port `RunVisibility`, which the conversation module implements: who sees the conversation (its person, OWNER) sees the run, its events and their stream, and cancels it; ADMIN and every other member get `404` on every path, as for an unknown run. OWNER's reads of another person's run, its events and its stream are audited through the port (`RunVisibility.read`, `conversation.read`): the stream once, when it opens, and its re-checks at every poll below record nothing. There is no permission of its own: every path needs `VIEW_COMPANY` first. `mayCancel` is `maySee`, so the Runs API's `403` for a run one sees but may not cancel (§15.6) answers nobody today. The live stream of a run's events checks both again, as its caller, at every read of the database (`CompanyAccessGuard.recheck`, with the user read again, and `RunVisibility`): a member removed, a role lowered (an OWNER who becomes ADMIN no longer sees another's run), a user deactivated or an instance admin demoted ends the stream without another frame; a break-glass audited when the stream began is not audited again.
- Through the MCP endpoint (§14.11, §15.10) the caller is the user of the bearer token, as a member, in their personal space; the token's scope can only narrow what the role allows, never widen it. An instance admin who is not a member gets `NOT_FOUND` from every tool and no break-glass (the package 6 contract, G9); one who is a member acts by their role. Through a client OWNER sees only their own conversations, and nobody decides a team's approval (`DECIDE_IN_APP`). A suspended member's bearer credentials reach nothing in the company either: `404`, as a non-member's (§15.13).
- The authorization server asks, as the user, whether the user is a member of the company of the request's `resource` and may act there (`VIEW_COMPANY` through the access guard): before the consent page and again when the consent is posted. A non-member gets `access_denied`, which names no company; so does an instance admin who is not a member, refused before the guard so that no break-glass is recorded (the package 6 contract, §5.10). A suspended member is refused the same way, `access_denied` with nothing recorded (`requireActiveMember`, §15.13).
- Adding a member by email finds any user, local or from any of the instance's providers; an unknown email answers `404 USER_NOT_FOUND`. Nothing ties a user to one company ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md) removed `provisioned_by_company_id`).
- **VIEWER** ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), decision 5; §15.24) holds no seat and reads what a member reads: the company, its members, groups, systems, agents and skills shared with them, their own conversations, decisions, runs and inbox. It holds every permission that every role holds (the `VIEW_*` ones) and none that starts or continues work: `WORK` (OWNER, ADMIN, MEMBER) guards starting, messaging, renaming, cancelling, retrying, ending and binding a conversation and deciding a decision, in the app and through the MCP endpoint, and `REGISTER_RUNNER_HOSTS` holds no VIEWER. Beyond them the access guard refuses every write of a Viewer's session under a reading permission (any method but `GET`, `HEAD` and `OPTIONS`), but their inbox's dismissals and `POST /chat-links/read`: creating or editing an agent or a skill, saving their settings of an agent, a trigger, a personal MCP server or runner host, connecting an account. Through the MCP endpoint, where every call is a `POST`, the tool invoker refuses a Viewer every tool that writes or submits (`delete_memory_note`, `test_system` beside those of `WORK`). Each answers `403 VIEWER_READ_ONLY` (a tool's error `VIEWER_READ_ONLY`), and so does a permission a Viewer lacks for a write or for `WORK`; a read they lack is the plain `403`. A linked Viewer's direct message in a chat starts and continues nothing (refused `VIEWER_READ_ONLY`, with a reply), and a press of theirs decides nothing. Who decides a request never depends on the role but here: a Viewer decides nothing, and a request whose only candidates are Viewers goes to the company's Owners and Admins (§15.24).
- **Seats** (§15.24). Under a licence that counts seats, giving OWNER, ADMIN or MEMBER to a person who holds no seat while every seat is taken is `409 SEATS_EXHAUSTED` (`details.licensed`, `details.used`): adding a member, changing a role, and making a deactivated user active again. A provider's new user falls back to VIEWER instead (§5).
- The last active OWNER of a company cannot be removed, demoted or suspended: `409 LAST_OWNER` (a suspended OWNER counts for nothing, package 6).
- **A managed role** ([ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md), §5). While a membership's role source is a provider or SCIM (`Member.roleManagedBy`), changing its role in the app (`updateMember`, the instance admin's break-glass included) is `409 ROLE_MANAGED` with `details.provider` (the registration id, or `scim`). Making the member OWNER is allowed, with the OWNER rules above: the role source becomes `APP`, and stays so while they are OWNER; an OWNER demoted in the app is the provider's again at their next sign-in. Everything else stays the app's: adding the user to another company, suspending, removing, erasing, groups and shares. The members page shows the role as "managed by <provider's name>", and offers only Owner.
- The last active instance admin cannot be demoted or deactivated: `409 LAST_INSTANCE_ADMIN`.
- `VALIDATION_FAILED` names the fields it refuses in `errors` (`[{field, message}]`), whether the request's schema refused them or a service's own check did (`ApiException.invalid`, as the model settings and a principal's model access do; `INVALID_BASE_URL` for the gateway's admin URL carries its field the same way), so that a form marks the field. A check that refuses no one field (a combination the request cannot hold) may answer without it.
- Problem `code` values of the instance and company core: `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `DUPLICATE_EMAIL`, `DUPLICATE_SECRET_NAME`, `DUPLICATE_MEMBER`, `USER_NOT_FOUND` (add member with an unknown email; returns 404), `LAST_OWNER`, `ROLE_MANAGED` (409), `LAST_INSTANCE_ADMIN`, `NOT_LOCAL_USER` (409), `WRONG_PASSWORD` (400), `EMAIL_IN_USE`, `EMAIL_MISSING`, `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`, `EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, `ROLE_NOT_MAPPED`, `OIDC_LOGIN_FAILED` and `SAML_LOGIN_FAILED` (OIDC and SAML sign-in failures, sent as `/login?error=` redirects; §5; no JSON problem uses `EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED`).
- Runner hosts (kept from v1, [ADR-0016](adr/0016-runner-capacity-settings.md)): `DUPLICATE_RUNNER_HOST_NAME`, `RUNNER_HOST_HAS_ACTIVE_RUNS` (409); `RUNNER_HOST_POOL_UNSUPPORTED` (409, pool settings on a personal host or on a company host that keeps no ready sandboxes; §15.4, [ADR-0099](adr/0099-pool-settings.md)). The vault: `SECRET_MANAGED` (409, §9).
- Agents and settings (package 3, the contract's §7; §15.5 says which request answers which): `DUPLICATE_AGENT_NAME`, `DUPLICATE_AGENT_HANDLE` (409): a live agent of the company has this name, ignoring case, or this handle. `SYSTEM_NOT_ON_AGENT` (400): a repository of the agent, or in settings a system switched off, a tool's mode or a default project, on a system the agent does not list; `details.repositoryId` or `details.systemId` names it. `UNKNOWN_TOOL` (400): a tool its system does not serve or switched off, or in settings a tool the agent does not list or one's own server does not serve; `details.systemId` and `details.tool` name it, so that the editor points at the field. `RUNTIME_NOT_LISTED` (400): settings name a runtime the agent does not list. `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED` (400): settings name a model the person's model access does not allow. `MODEL_REQUIRED` (400, `V035`): settings paid by a budget name no model, and the company's gateway serves only the models it lists. `SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_SERVING` (400): settings name a subscription that is not one on the person's own runner host serving the runtime. `SETTINGS_WIDEN` (400): settings would widen the agent (I4 of the package 3 contract). `AGENT_NOT_USABLE` (403): the caller sees the agent but may not use it. `AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE` (403): the caller sees the agent but may not edit it. `AGENT_HAS_ACTIVE_RUNS` (409): an agent is not deleted while a run of it is queued or running. `NETWORK_HOST_LIMIT` (409): an approval for good would give an agent a 101st network host (a `PUT …/hosts` of more than 100 is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). An agent's ceiling also answers package 1's `PERSONAL_SYSTEM` and `SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID` (400). These replace the meanings the archived §14.2 gave the same codes ([archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md#142-problem-codes-added-to-6)).
- Conversations (package 3c's part 1, the contract's §7; §15.7 says which request answers which): `MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE` (409): the conversation can never be paid as the person's settings stand, with `details.reason` package 2's refusal (`GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE`, `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`, `PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY`, and for a budget's settings `MODEL_REQUIRED` or `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`). `NO_HOST_FOR_CONVERSATION` (409): no registered runner host may run it, with `details.reason` `NO_RUNNER_HOST`, `NO_HOST_FOR_CREDENTIALS` or `CANNOT_RUN_ANYWHERE`. `TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES` (409): more than 20 repositories would start the workspace without a choice of them. `CONVERSATION_ENDED` (409): the conversation ended and takes nothing more. `CONVERSATION_BOUND` (409): only the conversation's person messages, renames and binds in it. `TURN_ACTIVE` (409): a retry while a turn is queued or running. `NOTHING_TO_RETRY` (409): a retry when the last turn neither failed nor was cancelled. `BINDING_NOT_WAITING` (409): a choice or a continue on a binding that does not wait for it. `GRANT_NOT_OFFERED` (400): a grant that is not among the binding's choices, or that cannot be used now. They replace the archived §14.2's `TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES` and the threads' `THREAD_BOUND`, `JOB_CLOSED` and `WORK_ITEM_CLOSED`.
- Conversations' files (package 3c's part 3, the contract's §3.13; ADR-0014; §15.7 says which request answers which): `ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE` (413): a file larger than `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_FILE_SIZE` (the service's check, or Spring's multipart limit before the file is read). `TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS` (409): the conversation would hold more files or more bytes than its limits. `DUPLICATE_ATTACHMENT_NAME` (409): the conversation has a file of this name, or two files carried into a new one have one name. `ATTACHMENT_IS_OUTPUT` (409): the file is an output's document, which is not deleted. `CREDENTIAL_FOUND` (422): the file holds the value of a vault secret of the company (never stored), or a known credential format that was not acknowledged; `credentialMatches` names each match (`VAULT_SECRET` or `FORMAT`, with its name), never the value. `ATTACHMENT_NOT_SCANNABLE` (422): the file cannot be scanned completely (an encrypted or damaged archive). An acknowledgement without `MANAGE_SECRETS` is `403` without a code. They replace the archived §14.7's codes of attachments, `ATTACHMENT_IS_ARTIFACT` by `ATTACHMENT_IS_OUTPUT`. The same codes are the `code` of a `REJECTED` answer to a runner host's upload (§15.7), where they are not problems.
- Decisions (package 3c's part 2, the contract's §7; §15.7 says which request answers which): `AGENT_PAUSED` (409): approving while the conversation's agent is paused (I18); an answer and a denial are taken. `DECISION_DECIDED` (409): the decision was decided or cancelled already (I16). A decision is also answered `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND` (anyone but its person who sees it), `409 CONVERSATION_ENDED`, `403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE` (scope `AGENT` without the right to edit the agent), `409 NETWORK_HOST_LIMIT` (the conversation's 101st granted host, or the agent's), `409 TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES` (a full workspace) and `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` (a field of the other kind, an option not the question's, `scope` or `access` where they do not apply, the agent's 101st repository); each leaves the decision open. `DECISION_DECIDED` replaces the archived §14.2's code of that name. The `code` of a `REJECTED` answer to a run's request (`run.request.ack`) is a protocol code, not a problem, and the protocol schema lists the codes only as examples: part 2 answers `TOO_MANY_QUESTIONS`, `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS`, `UNKNOWN_REPOSITORY`, `ALREADY_PENDING`, `ALREADY_IN_WORKSPACE`, `ALREADY_ALLOWED`, `REQUEST_UNAVAILABLE`, `TOOL_DENIED`, `UNKNOWN_TOOL` and `ACCOUNT_GONE`, beside `INVALID_REQUEST`, `CONVERSATION_ENDED`, `RUN_NOT_ACTIVE` and the system module's refusals of a call (`RESULT_TOO_LARGE`, `TOOL_FAILED`; §15.2).
- The inbox's notices (§15.7): `NOTICE_NOT_DISMISSIBLE` (409): a dismissal of a notice the caller is the one to act on (`DEPARTURE`, `CHAT_SUBSCRIPTION_SILENT`, a `TRIGGER` whose widening waits for acceptance, whose author left or whose account is broken), which leaves once handled. A key that is none of the caller's notices is `404`.
- The audit, retention, holds and erasure (package 6, the package 6 contract, §7; §15.11 says which request answers which): `AUDIT_EXPORT_TOO_LARGE` (400): an export of more than 1 000 000 events. `RETENTION_INVALID` (400): a period out of its range, or chats' content kept longer than other content. `ERASURE_NOT_ALLOWED` (409): an open personal conversation of a member still in the company named for erasure, or a service account's principal. `ON_HOLD` (409): an open legal hold covers what the erasure would purge, `holdIds` naming the holds. `CONTENT_PURGED` (410): a file's content was removed under retention or by an erasure.
- Spend (package 6, §15.12): `SPEND_RANGE_INVALID` (400): a spend query over more than 366 days, `to` not after `from`, more than two groupings (or one twice), or an unknown zone. An unknown `groupBy` value or `bucket` is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. `PUT /model-settings`' alert settings out of range are `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`.
- The kill switches (package 6, §15.13): `COMPANY_STOPPED` (409: starting a conversation or approving while the company's runs are stopped), `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (409: testing, refreshing or connecting on a paused system), `CANNOT_SUSPEND_SELF` (409), `DEPARTURE_OPEN` (409: closing a leaver's checklist with open items without `force`); package 1's `PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` (409 on a suspended member's writes, 403 on their reads) and `LAST_OWNER` (also on suspending the last active OWNER).
- Idempotent submissions (package R, the package R contract, §3.5; §15.14): `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED` (422): the caller sent this `Idempotency-Key` (REST) or `requestId` (MCP) before with another operation or another request; a malformed key (not 1 to 200 visible ASCII characters) is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. A replay is no problem: it answers the first request's status with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`. A run's `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (§15.14) is no code of `run.request.ack` either: it is a tool error result (`DONE`, `isError`, `structuredContent.code`).
- The licence ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), §15.24): `SEATS_EXHAUSTED` (409, `details.licensed`, `details.used`), `EDITION_REQUIRED` (403, `details.feature`, `details.edition`: the gates of decision 4, §15.24), `VIEWER_READ_ONLY` (403), and the keys' `LICENCE_KEY_INVALID` (400), `LICENCE_KEY_EXPIRED`, `LICENCE_KEY_SUPERSEDED` and `LICENCE_SEATS_TOO_FEW` (409, `details.required`, `details.licensed`).
- Credentials on systems ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md), §15.3): `DUPLICATE_CREDENTIAL_NAME` (409): the system has a credential of this name, ignoring case (`account_name_uq`, §7).
- Skills ([ADR-0064](adr/0064-skills-library.md), §15.19, which names the module's other codes): `DUPLICATE_SKILL_NAME` (409): a live skill of the company has this name (`skill_name_uq`). `SKILL_NAME_CHANGED` (400): a new version's `SKILL.md` names another skill than the one it is added to.
- The codes of the redesign's domain are in §15; §14.2 lists the codes of the MCP endpoint and groups, which still hold. The codes of the removed modules are in [archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md#142-problem-codes-added-to-6).

## 7. Database

- **Migrations:** Flyway files in `server/src/main/resources/db/migration`, named `V001__short_name.sql`, `V002__...`. Never edit a released migration.
- **Identifiers:** UUIDv7, generated in the application.
- **Timestamps:** `timestamptz`, stored in UTC.
- **Roles:** `tinfirm_owner` owns all objects and runs Flyway. `tinfirm_app` is the runtime role; migrations `GRANT` it only what it needs.
  - Compose creates both roles in `deploy/compose/postgres/init.sql`.
  - For Helm with an external database, the operator creates them; `docs/` explains how.
- **Tenant tables:** every table that belongs to a company has `company_id uuid NOT NULL`, and runs `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` plus `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`.
- **Instance-level tables of [ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md):** `api_token` (`V001__baseline.sql`), Spring's `oauth2_registered_client`, `oauth2_authorization` and `oauth2_authorization_consent` (`V001__baseline.sql`: Spring Security 7.1's schema in PostgreSQL types, `text` for `blob`, `timestamptz` for `timestamp`, client id columns widened to 500 characters for metadata document URLs, `redirect_uris` as `text` for ten URIs of 200 characters, hash indexes on the token columns), and the vault's `instance_key` (also `V001`). No `company_id`, no row-level security; the runtime role has no `DELETE` on `oauth2_registered_client`, `api_token` and `instance_key`. Tokens are revoked, never deleted.
- **Instance-level tables of error reports** (ADR-0070, §15.21): `error_report` and `error_reporting` (`V046__error_reports.sql`), no `company_id` and no row-level security, as `api_token`: they hold no company's rows and, by construction, no personal data. The runtime role has every right on `error_report` and no `DELETE` on `error_reporting`, whose one row (`id = 1`) is never deleted.
- **ShedLock's locks** (§15.21): `shedlock` (`V047__shedlock.sql`), ShedLock's own table for PostgreSQL (`name`, `lock_until`, `locked_at`, `locked_by`; `timestamp` in UTC, as ShedLock writes the database's clock), one row per lock of a scheduled job that one `worker` replica at a time runs, today only the delivery of error reports (`error-reports.delivery`). Instance-level, without row-level security. The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE`: a lock's row is taken, extended and released in place, never deleted. The lock provider is `shared.config.SchedulingConfiguration`'s (`JdbcTemplateLockProvider`, `usingDbTime`).
- **Instance-level table of the redesign** (§15.3): `oauth_authorization_request` (`V001__baseline.sql`), a pending sign-in with a system's app: `state_hash` (the primary key, the SHA-256 of the 32 random bytes of its `state`, in hex; the state itself is never stored), `company_id`, `system_id`, what the grant becomes (from `V049`, exactly one by `oauth_authorization_request_subject_ck`: `principal_id`, the member whose own account it becomes; or for a system's credential, ADR-0072, `credential_id`, the credential signed in again, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, or `credential_name`, the name of a new one), `user_id` and `session_hash` (the person in the browser and the SHA-256 of their session's id), `return_to` (a same-origin path, checked), `created_at` and `expires_at` (10 minutes). The one callback finds it by its state before any company context exists, so it has a `company_id` but no row-level security, like `api_token`: the callback consumes only a request of the caller from the same session, in one `DELETE … RETURNING`, and then checks the company. The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE`; a request is written once, consumed once, deleted with its system, principal or credential (foreign keys `ON DELETE CASCADE`), and swept every 10 minutes once expired; `V049` dropped the sign-ins pending when it ran. It keeps no PKCE verifier: `V025__drop_pkce_verifier.sql` dropped `code_verifier` (and the sign-ins pending then, whose verifiers cannot be derived), since the callback derives the verifier from the state (§15.3); the same migration lets `instance_key.algorithm` be `HMAC` beside `RSA` (§9).
- **Company keys** ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md), `V052__companies_from_the_deployment.sql`): `company.key varchar(40) NULL`, unique, checked against `^[a-z][a-z0-9]{0,39}$`: the key the deployment declares the company by (§5), set when the declaration creates or adopts the company and never changed; NULL for a company from before that no declaration adopted. The same migration drops companies' own OIDC providers: their client secrets in the vault (with their versions), `company_identity_provider_domain`, `company_identity_provider`, the lookups `company_identity_provider_of_company` and `company_identity_provider_of_domain`, and `app_user.provisioned_by_company_id` with its check and index.
- **The licence** ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), `V053__licence.sql`, §15.24):
  - `licence_key`, instance-level as `api_token` (no `company_id`, no row-level security: one key covers the instance and it is read before any company context), one row per `slot` (the primary key): `CURRENT`, the key in force or the one that ended, and `NEXT`, a renewal held until its start. Each: `licence_id`, `rev` (from 1), `customer`, `edition` (`PRO` or `ENTERPRISE`), `seats` (null only for an Enterprise key), `companies`, `starts_at`, `ends_at` (dates, both included), `key_text` (the key as entered, verified again at every start and every hour, never returned), `applied_at`, `applied_by`. The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE`: a key is applied in place of its slot's, and the next one moves into `CURRENT` on its start date.
  - `company_membership_role_ck` admits `VIEWER`.
  - `decision.approver_admins`: a request whose only candidates were Viewers, for the company's Owners and Admins, with neither an approver principal nor a group (`decision_approver_ck` and `decision_conversation_ck()` again, with it).
  - `seat_fallback`, a tenant table: a provider's new user who joined as VIEWER for want of a seat (`company_id`, `user_id`, `role` the provider gives, `provider`, `joined_at`), going with its membership (`ON DELETE CASCADE`); the runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE`.
  - The SECURITY DEFINER counts over every company's memberships, for the seat check before or outside any company context: `licence_seats_used()` (the distinct active users with OWNER, ADMIN or MEMBER anywhere), `licence_holds_seat(uuid)` and `licence_has_seat_role(uuid)` (the same, active or not). They answer a count and flags, never ids.
- **Community's retention** ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), decision 8, `V054__retention_keeps_everything.sql`): `retention_settings.run_events_days` and `chat_content_days` may be NULL (keep); `retention_chat_content_ck` keeps a chat's content only while all content is (`content_days` NULL). §15.24 says which periods are in force.
- **Requests of Viewers** ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), decision 5, `V055__decisions_of_viewers.sql`): the open decisions whose only candidates had become Viewers go to the company's Owners and Admins (`approver_admins`), and those with them whose candidates hold a seat again go back, company by company in its context (§15.24).
- **Tenant context:** each transaction sets these values with `set_config(..., true)` (transaction-local):
  - `app.company_id`: the company of the request, or empty.
  - `app.user_id`: the caller.
  - `app.instance_admin`: `true` or `false`, the caller's flag, set for the whole request of an instance admin's session.
  - `app.system`: `true` only in the SYSTEM tenant context (`TenantContext.SYSTEM`, `SystemContext.call`): trusted server-side work across companies, today the bootstrap and the declared companies (§5), the daily check of the instance's audit chain, the lookups of companies' names and owners, the vault's rewrap (`POST /api/admin/vault/rewrap` runs its batches there and audits the admin as the actor; it rewraps the session bundles' data keys too, through `vault.StoredDataKeys`, ADR-0101), and error reports' capture, settings and delivery (`SystemTransactions.instanceWide`, §15.21). A caller's request never sets it.

  Policies use `current_setting('app.company_id', true)` and must deny when it is empty, except for explicitly cross-company reads. Examples:
  - a user's own memberships (`user_id = app.user_id`);
  - companies where the user is a member;
  - every company (`company`: `GET /api/companies`, the access guard's lookup) and the instance chain of the audit log (`/api/admin/audit-events`) when `app.instance_admin = 'true'`;
  - any row of `company`, `company_membership`, `secret`, `secret_version`, `session_bundle` (V070: the rewrap of its keys), `audit_event` and `audit_chain_head` when `app.system = 'true'`.

  Within a transaction the context changes only when the access guard enters a company (`TenantScope.enterCompany`, after its check), and at the commit for the audit log's appends: each event is appended under the context it was recorded in, and the transaction's own is set back after (`TenantScope.eachIn`, §8). No append runs with a wider context than its call had.

  The instance admin's flag opens nothing else (`V016__rls_system_bypass.sql`): a company's secrets and their versions, its audit events and chain head, and its memberships are read and written in its own context, which the access guard sets after its check (for an instance admin who is not a member, the audited break-glass, §6). Renaming a company also needs its context. `RowLevelSecurityTest` shows both: an instance admin's session without a company context sees the companies and the instance chain and none of a company's other rows, and the system context sees them all.
- **Audit tables:**
  - `tinfirm_app` has only `INSERT` and `SELECT` on `audit_event`.
  - It has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on the chain-head table only. That is needed for the `FOR UPDATE` lock and for advancing the head.
  - The instance-chain head row is readable and updatable in every context, because logins advance it before any admin context exists. It holds only `seq` and a hash.
  - It never has DELETE on either table.
- **Audit RLS:**
  - `audit_event` and the chain-head table have RLS, but `company_id` is nullable (null means the instance chain).
  - Company rows are visible when `company_id = app.company_id` or `app.system = 'true'` (never through `app.instance_admin` alone: the company's audit log is read in its context, `V016`).
  - Instance-chain rows are visible only when `app.instance_admin = 'true'` or `app.system = 'true'`.
  - Inserts are allowed when `company_id IS NULL OR company_id = app.company_id`, because instance events such as logins are written with no company context.
- **Pagination:** cursor-based and keyset. Never unbounded queries or OFFSET. The one exception is SCIM's `GET /scim/v2/Users` (RFC 7644 §3.4.2.4 is index-based; §15.25): an offset by the user's id over `company_membership_scim_page_idx`, inside one company, bounded at a `startIndex` of 10 000 and a page of 100.
- **Baseline.** The redesign of 2026-10-12 started the schema afresh at `V001__baseline.sql`, as the restart of 2026-10-05 ([ADR-0037](adr/0037-restart-on-the-platform-design.md)) had: no earlier migration is carried, and no data is migrated (an installation's database is replaced). The baseline holds the kept modules' tables (shared, identity, company, audit, vault, bootstrap, runner, mcp) in their final shape and package 1's tables (below); the old domain's tables (connections, agents, work items, jobs, runs, decisions, model and runner credentials, groups' rights on agents) are not created, and each later package creates its own. The migrations are numbered from `V001`; the numbers `V006`, `V007` and `V022` are unused.
- **Tenant tables of the domain** carry composite `UNIQUE (id, company_id)` keys and composite foreign keys with the company id, so a row never points at another tenant's row. Cross-company candidates for background work come from `SECURITY DEFINER` lookups that return ids only; the caller re-locks each row in its tenant context (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`).
- **Tenant tables of the redesign** (package 1, `V001__baseline.sql`; the package 1 contract, §3 and §4), each with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `principal` | Whom work acts for: `kind` `MEMBER` (with `user_id`) or `SERVICE` (with `name`, `description`); `status` `ACTIVE`, `SUSPENDED` or `LEFT` | One principal per member that has not left (`principal_member_uq`); a service account's name unique ignoring case (`principal_service_name_uq`); a member's principal that has not left has its membership, and a membership ends only after its principal left (two triggers, I8); only members leave |
  | `service_identity_user` | The groups whose members may use a service account | Only `SERVICE` principals (a foreign key on the principal's kind); goes with the principal and with the group |
  | `external_system` | A system: `name`, `handle`, `kind`, `base_url`, `owner_principal_id` (a personal MCP server's member), its app (`oauth_client_id`, `oauth_client_secret_id`, `oauth_scopes`, `oauth_metadata`), `bot_account_id` (package 5), `settings`, `status`, `last_error` | Names unique ignoring case and handles unique (`external_system_name_uq`, `external_system_handle_uq`); a base URL for every kind but `GIT` and `WEBHOOK`; a personal system is an `MCP` server owned by a member's principal (I6); no client secret without a client id; a bot is a credential on that system (I9: from `V049` `external_system_bot_kind_ck` requires `bot_principal_kind` `SYSTEM`, and `external_system_bot_fk` on `(bot_account_id, id, company_id, bot_principal_kind)`, `ON DELETE SET NULL (bot_account_id)`), which a service account borrows (`service_account_credential`, below) |
  | `system_repository` | A code host's repository: `url`, `name`, `default_branch` | Unique `(system_id, url)`; goes with its system |
  | `system_tool` | An MCP server's catalogue: `name`, `title`, `description`, `input_schema`, `effect` (`UNKNOWN` until confirmed), the server's hints, who confirmed it, `enabled` | Primary key `(system_id, name)`; `confirmed_at` set exactly when the effect is not `UNKNOWN`; goes with its system |
  | `system_setting` | The company's switch of personal MCP servers, on without a row | One row per company |
  | `account` | A member's own account on a system, or from `V049` a credential the system holds ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md)): `principal_id` (nullable from `V049`) with `principal_kind` (`MEMBER`; `SYSTEM`, without a principal, for a credential), a credential's `name` (1 to 100 characters) and `enabled`, `kind` `TOKEN`, `OAUTH`, `CLIENT` (from `V065`, a credential only) or `LINK`, `secret_id`, the identity there (`external_id`, `external_login`), `api_base_url`, `scopes`, `access_expires_at`, `status` `OK`, `RECONNECT` or `ERROR`, `last_error`, `connected_at`, `connected_by`, `last_used_at` | I1 (`account_principal_uq`: one account per member per system; a system holds any number of credentials); `account_holder_ck` (a principal exactly when not `SYSTEM`, a name exactly for a credential, only a credential disabled, a credential a `TOKEN`, an `OAUTH` or a `CLIENT`, and a `CLIENT` only a credential, `V065`); a credential's name unique on its system ignoring case (`account_name_uq`: `DUPLICATE_CREDENTIAL_NAME`); I2 among members' own accounts (`account_identity_uq` where `external_id` and `principal_id` are set: `IDENTITY_IN_USE`), and between them and the credentials in `account_ck()` (an identity on a system is members' own accounts' or the system's credentials', never both, checked under an advisory lock per identity; two credentials may share one); a secret exactly when it is not a `LINK`; a member's secret owned by the member, a credential's a company secret, and on a personal system only its owner's account and no credential (`account_ck_trg`: I3, I6); goes with its secret (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), which only the account's own removal deletes (§9) |
  | `account_grant` | A system's credential lent to a group, `access` `READ` or `WRITE` | From `V049` only a credential (I5: `account_grant_credential_ck` keeps `account_principal_kind` `SYSTEM`, and `account_grant_credential_fk` on the account's `(id, company_id, principal_kind)` cascades); unique `(account_id, group_id)`; never a chat system's bot (`account_grant_bot_ck`, a trigger of `V017`); goes with its credential and its group |
  | `service_account_credential` (`V049`) | The credential a service account borrows on a system: `principal_id` (`principal_kind` always `SERVICE`), `system_id`, `account_id` (`account_principal_kind` always `SYSTEM`), `access` `READ` or `WRITE`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by` | Primary key `(principal_id, system_id)`: at most one credential per service account and system, while a credential may be lent to several; only a service account (`service_account_credential_service_ck`, and the foreign key on the principal's `(id, company_id, kind)`) and only a credential of that system (`service_account_credential_system_ck`, and the foreign key on the account's `(id, system_id, company_id, principal_kind)`); `READ` or `WRITE` (`service_account_credential_access_ck`); goes with its service account and its credential (`ON DELETE CASCADE`). Lending another credential on the system replaces the row |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on them, but no `UPDATE` on `service_identity_user` and `system_repository` (replaced or removed, never changed) and no `DELETE` on `system_setting`. A service account, a system, an account or a credential, a grant and a lending are removed by their services after what hangs from them, each with its audit (§15), never by a silent cascade of a parent row; the cascades above are the database's last word. `V049` made every service identity's account a credential on its system, named after the service identity and lent to it `WRITE`; its grants, the triggers' rows, the conversations' bindings and a chat system's bot kept naming it, and its secret kept its name (`SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, §9). What `V049` and `V050` changed in the tables of later packages is below (Credentials on systems).
- **Tables of package 2** (`V002__model_access.sql`; the package 2 contract, §2 and §3; §15.4), tenant tables as above:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `runner_host` (changed) | `scope` `COMPANY` or `PERSONAL`, and `owner_principal_id`, the member's principal for a personal host and `NULL` for a company host, in place of `owner_user_id`; `owner_kind`, always `MEMBER`. From V064 (ADR-0092) a `PERSONAL` row is a machine's *presence* in the company: `personal_host_id` names its `personal_runner_host`, and the row carries no `token_hash` and no facts of its own (the machine's are read through a join); a `COMPANY` row keeps its token and facts | An owner exactly on a personal host (`runner_host_owner_ck`), and only a `MEMBER` principal of the company (`runner_host_owner_fk` on `(owner_principal_id, company_id, owner_kind)`, so no service account owns a host); scope and owner never change (`runner_host_scope_fixed_trg`, a check violation); a machine exactly on a personal host (`runner_host_personal_ck`), a token exactly on a company host (`runner_host_token_ck`), one presence per machine and company (`runner_host_presence_uq`); a company host's name unique among the company's company hosts, a personal host's among its owner's in the company (two partial unique indexes). A host registered before V002 became its member's personal host; one whose member no longer had a principal in the company was removed; V064 turned every personal host into a machine with a presence in each company its owner was a member of |
  | `personal_runner_host` (V064, ADR-0092) | A member's personal runner host as a machine, outside any company (an instance-level table without row-level security, like `app_user`): `owner_user_id` (`app_user`, `ON DELETE CASCADE`), `name`, `token_hash`, the capacity (`max_concurrent_runs`, `max_concurrent_runs_ceiling`, `desired_max_concurrent_runs`, `draining`, `config_version`, `applied_config_version`) and the facts of its last `hello` (`runtimes`, `runner_version`, `hostname`, `os`, `arch`, `last_seen_at`) | `UNIQUE (owner_user_id, name)`, `UNIQUE (token_hash)`; the runtime role has all four rights (a machine is removed with its presences) |
  | `personal_runner_credential` (V064, in place of `runner_credential`) | A credential a machine reported in `hello`, by `label` (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$`): `kind` (`CLAUDE_LOGIN`, `CODEX_CHATGPT_AUTH`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`; ADR-0085), `runtimes` (at least one of `CLAUDE_CODE`, `CODEX`, `PI`, `OPENCODE`), `pool_size` (1 to 16), `status` as last reported (`AVAILABLE`, `COOLDOWN`, `INVALID`; `OFFLINE` is derived from the connection when read), `cooldown_until`, `detail`, `reported` (false once a `hello` no longer lists it). Never its value | M5: a machine's, so a personal host's only, shown in each company through its presence; one per machine and label; goes with its machine (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) |
  | `model_settings` | The company's LiteLLM, one row per company (`company_id` the primary key): `gateway` `BUNDLED` or `EXTERNAL`, `admin_url` and `admin_key_secret_id` (an `EXTERNAL` gateway's), `relay_enabled`, `default_budget_usd`, `budget_period` (`30d` by default), `default_models` (a JSON array; empty allows every model the gateway serves), `tested_at` and `last_error` of the last test, `updated_at`, `updated_by`. No row: no gateway configured | An admin URL exactly for `EXTERNAL`, no admin key for `BUNDLED`; the key's secret by composite foreign key (`ON DELETE SET NULL (admin_key_secret_id)`); a budget of at least 0; a period of LiteLLM's form (`^[1-9][0-9]{0,3}(h\|d\|w\|mo)$`) |
  | `principal_model_key` | A principal's LiteLLM key, a member's or a service account's (`principal_id` the primary key): `secret_id` (the key, an internal secret, §9; `NULL` while the key does not exist), `litellm_key_id` (LiteLLM's id of the key, its SHA-256), the overrides `budget_usd` and `models` (`NULL` takes the company's default), `status` (`OK`, `BUDGET_SPENT`, `BLOCKED`, or `ERROR` with `last_error`, which is also the status of a row whose key is not created yet), `spent_usd`, `budget_resets_at` and `synced_at` as LiteLLM last said, `sync_lease_until` (the replica that talks to LiteLLM about the key, or rotates it). From `V014__model_key_rotation.sql` (the key rotation, §15.4), its rotation: `key_generation` (the current key's, which names its alias; 0 until the first rotation), `key_created_at` (the current key's creation: its age), `key_rotated_at` (when a rotation made it; `NULL` for a key never rotated), `rotation_requested_at` (an admin's request to rotate it at once, until a newer key answers it), `rotation_error` (the last failed attempt's reason), and while the key a rotation replaced is still in LiteLLM, `retiring_key_id` (its LiteLLM id; its secret is gone), `retiring_until` (the end of its grace), `retiring_blocked_at`, and `retiring_spend_usd` and `retiring_resets_at` (its spend and reset time when the current key was created with that spend). The keys that existed took their row's `created_at` as their creation | M1: one key per principal, a secret and a LiteLLM id never another principal's, its previous key's neither (`principal_model_key_secret_uq`, `principal_model_key_litellm_uq`, `principal_model_key_retiring_uq`); `OK` only with a secret and an id; a creation time with every key (`principal_model_key_created_ck`); a previous key with the end of its grace, never the current one (`principal_model_key_retiring_ck`, `_retiring_other_ck`), blocked only while named (`_retiring_blocked_ck`); goes with its principal (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), and loses its secret (`ON DELETE SET NULL (secret_id)`) |
  | `company_model_key` (`V045`) | The company's own LiteLLM key (ADR-0065, §15.4), no principal's (`company_id` the primary key): the columns of `principal_model_key` but `principal_id`, with the same meaning | The same checks under its own names (`company_model_key_*`); its secret never another key's (`company_model_key_secret_uq`); goes with its company only |
  | `run_model_token` | A run's one-run token for the model relay: `token_hash` (the primary key, `hex(sha256(token))`; the token is never stored), `run_id`, `epoch`, `runner_host_id`, `paying_principal_id`, `format` (`ANTHROPIC` or `OPENAI`), `conversation_id`, `agent_id`, the counters `request_count`, `request_bytes` and `response_bytes`, `created_at`, `revoked_at`; from `V035` `model_not_served`, the model the gateway last answered it does not serve for a request with this token, which the run's end reads (failure class `MODEL_NOT_SERVED`) | Goes with its host and its paying principal (`ON DELETE CASCADE`); `run_id` has no foreign key: package 3b's `run` table (V004) adds none |

  `secret.internal` (`boolean`, default false) marks a secret Tinfirm keeps for itself (§9), never one a person owns (`secret_internal_ck`). The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `personal_runner_credential` (a credential goes with its machine only) and `model_settings` (changed in place, never removed), and `DELETE` as well on `principal_model_key` and `run_model_token` (revoked tokens are swept a day after their revocation); `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `company_model_key` (synced, rotated and forgotten in place). Beside `runner_host_for_token` (from V064 a row per presence for a machine, with `personal_host_id`), V064 adds the lookups of ADR-0092, ids and counts only: `personal_runner_host_presences(uuid)`, `member_principals(uuid)` (a user's `(company_id, principal_id)` where their `MEMBER` principal has not `LEFT`), `run_company(uuid)`, `personal_runner_host_occupying(uuid[])`, `personal_runner_host_occupying_subscription(uuid, text)` and `personal_runner_host_active(uuid)`. Two more return ids only, as `runner_host_for_token` does: `model_settings_companies()`, the companies whose gateway passed its test, for the reconciliation of their keys; `run_model_token_company(text)`, the company of a token by its hash, for the relay before any company context exists. The row is then read in that company's tenant context.
- **Tables of package 3a** (`V003__agents.sql`; the package 3 contract, §3.1 to §3.7, §4 and §11; §15.5), tenant tables as above. No column of an agent's own tables (`agent` to `agent_share`, and `agent_member_share`) names a secret, an account, a model key, a runner credential or a principal the agent acts as (the contract's I1; the only principals they name are the owner who edits it, once it passed to the admins its former owner (`V021`), and the members it is shared with (`V034`), which `AgentConstraintTest` reads from the schema). I1 to I5 here are the package 3 contract's, not package 1's.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `agent` | A specialist's definition: `name`, `handle`, `description` (at most 1000 characters), `instructions` (at most 100 000, `''` for none), `runtimes` (`varchar(16)[]`, the first the default), `owner_principal_id` (the member who owns it; `NULL` once it passed to the admins) with `owner_kind` (always `MEMBER`), `status` `ACTIVE` or `PAUSED` with `paused_at` and `paused_by`, `version` (the current one), `deleted_at` and `deleted_by` (the soft delete), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by` (the user, kept after they leave); from `V021` `former_owner_principal_id` (the member it passed to the admins from, until it gets a new owner), from `V037` `turn_time_limit_minutes` (5 to 1440, `agent_turn_time_limit_ck`; `NULL` takes `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_WALL_CLOCK`, §4), from `V039` `memory_allowed` (§15.18) | Names unique among the live agents ignoring case, handles unique among them (`agent_name_uq`, `agent_handle_uq`, partial on `deleted_at IS NULL`: a deleted agent frees both); the handle's form (`agent_handle_ck`); one to four known runtimes without duplicates (`agent_runtimes_ck`, through the function `agent_runtimes_valid(varchar(16)[])`); an owner only a `MEMBER` principal of the company (`agent_owner_fk` on `(owner_principal_id, company_id, owner_kind)`); `paused_at` exactly while `PAUSED` |
  | `agent_version` | Every version of the definition (§15.5): `version`, `definition` (jsonb: name, handle, runtimes, the systems with each tool's name, mode and the effect the mode was chosen for, the repositories with their access, the hosts; never the description or the instructions, which follow the current version), `widens`, `decision_id` (the approval for good that made it; no foreign key, decisions come with package 3c), `created_at`, `created_by` | Primary key `(agent_id, version)`; version 1 widens nothing (`agent_version_first_ck`); written once: the runtime role has no `UPDATE` |
  | `agent_system` | The ceiling of systems | I2 (the trigger `agent_system_ck_trg`, function `agent_system_ck()`, raising check violations): only a company system (`agent_system_personal_ck`), of a kind with tools or repositories, `JIRA_DC`, `JIRA_CLOUD`, `GITHUB`, `GITLAB`, `GIT` or `MCP` (`agent_system_kind_ck`); goes with its system (`ON DELETE CASCADE`); `UNIQUE (agent_id, system_id, company_id)`, the target of the next two tables' keys |
  | `agent_tool` | A tool of a listed system, by the system's name for it (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`), its `mode` and `chosen_for_effect` (`READ` or `WRITE`) | I2: only on a system the agent lists (`agent_tool_system_fk`, cascading); I3: `ALLOW` or `ASK`, no `DENY` (`agent_tool_mode_ck`) |
  | `agent_repository` | A repository of a listed code host, `access` `READ` or `WRITE` | I2: its system on the agent (`agent_repository_system_fk`) and the repository that system's (`agent_repository_repository_fk`, on `system_repository_id_system_uq`, the `UNIQUE (id, system_id, company_id)` that V003 adds to `system_repository`); goes with either |
  | `agent_host` | A network host its runs may reach: `host`, normalized (§15.5), `reason` (at most 500 characters), `added_by`, `decision_id` (the approval for good that added it; no foreign key), `added_at` | Unique `(agent_id, host)`; the host's characters (`agent_host_host_ck`; the grammar is `NetworkHosts`') |
  | `agent_share` | A group whose members may use the agent: `group_id`, `shared_by`, `shared_at` | Primary key `(agent_id, group_id)`; goes with its group (`ON DELETE CASCADE`, after the agent module removed the share itself, §15.5) |
  | `agent_member_share` (`V034`) | A member who may use the agent on their own: `principal_id`, `shared_by`, `shared_at` | Primary key `(agent_id, principal_id)`; a member's principal only (`principal_kind` `MEMBER` in the foreign key); goes when its member leaves (the agent module, §15.5), the cascade a safety net, since a member's principal is never deleted |
  | `agent_settings` | One person's settings of one agent: `principal_id` with `principal_kind` (always `MEMBER`), `runtime` (`NULL`: the agent's first), `model`, `effort`, `model_access` `BUDGET` or `SUBSCRIPTION` with `subscription_host_id` and `subscription_label`, `instructions` (at most 20 000 characters; always `NULL` on a trigger's settings, `agent_settings_trigger_instructions_ck`, V033), `default_repository_ids` (at most 100), `default_projects` (jsonb `[{systemId, key}]`, at most 20), `accepted_version`, `auto_retry` (V051, ADR-0073: the owner's opt-in to automatic retries, false by default), `memory_next_number`, `memory_changed_at` and `memory_compacted_at` (V039, §15.18), `created_at`, `updated_at` | I5: one row per person per agent (`agent_settings_person_uq`); only a member's principal (`agent_settings_principal_fk`); a subscription only with `SUBSCRIPTION`, and its label whenever its host is named (`agent_settings_subscription_ck`); the model's pattern, the effort, `accepted_version` at least 1. The host's removal sets `subscription_host_id` to `NULL` and keeps the label (`ON DELETE SET NULL (subscription_host_id)`): the subscription is gone |
  | `agent_settings_system` | A system of the agent switched off (`OFF`), or the person's own MCP server added (`ADDED`) | I4 (the trigger `agent_settings_system_ck_trg`, function `agent_settings_system_ck()`, check violation `agent_settings_widen_ck`): `OFF` only on a system the agent lists, `ADDED` only on an `MCP` server the settings' principal owns; goes with its settings and its system |
  | `agent_settings_tool` | A tool's mode in the settings, with `chosen_for_effect`: `ASK` or `OFF` on a system of the agent, `ALLOW` or `ASK` on an added server | I4 (the trigger `agent_settings_tool_ck_trg`, function `agent_settings_tool_ck()`, `agent_settings_widen_ck`): `ALLOW` only on an `ADDED` server, where `OFF` is refused; on a system the agent lists `ASK` or `OFF`; nothing on any other system; goes with its settings and its system |
  | `agent_memory_note` (`V039`) | A note of a memory (§15.18): its `settings_id` (the person's or the trigger's settings row it belongs to), `number` (`m{number}`, never reused in the row: `agent_settings.memory_next_number`), `state` (`ACTIVE`, `PROPOSED`), `about` (`GENERAL`, `SYSTEM` with `system_id`, `REPOSITORY` with `repository_id`), `body`, a proposal's `replaces_number`, `conversation_id` and `learned_in` (where it was learned, a merged note's sources' included), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `used_at`, and `search` (its words, `to_tsvector('simple', body)`, generated). `V044` dropped `request_id`, which a later replace overwrote and a delete took away: a write's request is kept in the call journal (`tool_call`, §15.14) | One line of 1 to 300 characters, trimmed, without a control character (`agent_memory_note_body_ck`); `about` and its id agree (`agent_memory_note_about_ck`); a number unique in its row; `replaces_number` only on a proposal; goes with its settings row, its system and its repository (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), keeps its conversation's id until that is gone (`SET NULL`) |
  | `memory_settings` (`V045`) | The company's settings of agents' memory (§15.18, ADR-0065), one row per company (`company_id` the primary key): `compaction_model` (the model that tidies memories on the company's own key; `NULL` for none), `updated_at`, `updated_by`. No row: none | A model name of the gateway's form (`memory_settings_model_ck`); changed in place, never removed |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `agent` (soft-deleted, never removed: its row, ceiling and versions stay for its conversations' history), `SELECT` and `INSERT` on `agent_version`, `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on `agent_system`, `agent_share`, `agent_member_share`, `agent_settings_system` and `agent_settings_tool` (replaced, never changed), and all four on `agent_tool`, `agent_repository`, `agent_host`, `agent_settings` and `agent_memory_note` (`V039`), and `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `memory_settings` (`V045`). The services check every rule above before the database does, with their own problem codes (§15.5); a violation that reaches the database is its last word, not an answer of the API, but for I4 on a person's settings: when `agent_settings_widen_ck` refuses a save that the agent's change meanwhile made a widening, the save answers `400 SETTINGS_WIDEN` (§15.5). A removed system leaves every agent and every person's settings, and a removed repository every agent, by the cascades, which narrow them at once (§15.5).
- **Tables of package 3b** (`V004__runs.sql`; the package 3 contract, §3.11; §15.6), tenant tables as above: the restart's run tables, with the conversation as the parent in place of the job and its work item, and the payer's model access in place of the agent's runner or model credential.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
