  | `run` | A turn of a conversation: `conversation_id`, `conversation_key` (a snapshot, 1 to 93 characters), `agent_id`, `runtime`, `turn_reason` (`FIRST`, `MESSAGE`, `DECIDED`, `CONNECTED`, `RETRY`; `V011` adds `EVENT` and `REVIEW_RETURNED`) and `turn_number`, `acting_principal_id`, `payer_principal_id`, `model_access` (`BUDGET`, or `SUBSCRIPTION` with `subscription_host_id` and `subscription_label`), the host it was assigned to (`runner_host_id`, with `runner_host_name` and `runner_host_kind` as snapshots), `status` (`QUEUED`, `RUNNING`, `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELLED`, `LOST`), `epoch`, `dispatched_at`, `dispatch_after`, `lease_expires_at`, `failure_class` and `failure_message`, `summary` and the `finish` result, `session_id`, `branch`, the usage, the event counters, the queue reason (`queue_reason`, `queue_reason_details`, `queue_reason_since`), `lost_cause`, the counts of tool calls, messages and thoughts, `resumed` and `resume_outcome`, and `relay_*` (what the model relay passed for it) | I11: one `QUEUED` or `RUNNING` run per conversation (`run_one_active_per_conversation_uq`, partial on those statuses); the agent of the run's company (`run_agent_fk` on `agent (id, company_id)`; an agent is deleted only softly, so its runs keep it); the acting and the paying principal of the company; I10: `run_payer_ck`, the payer is the acting person, which package 4 relaxes for a trigger's runs; a subscription's host and label only with `SUBSCRIPTION`, which always has its label; the known runtimes, reasons, statuses, failure classes (the runner's, with `TIME_LIMIT` from `V037`; `LOST`, `RUNTIME_REMOVED`, `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, and from `V035` `MODEL_REQUIRED` and `MODEL_NOT_SERVED`), queue reasons (package 6's `V021` adds `COMPANY_STOPPED` and `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, `V040` `NO_HOST_FOR_SKILLS`, `V051` `RETRY_SCHEDULED`) and lost causes; `dispatched_at` only while `QUEUED`, a lease while `RUNNING`, `finished_at` exactly once ended, a queue reason only while `QUEUED`. A removed host sets `runner_host_id` or `subscription_host_id` to `NULL` (`ON DELETE SET NULL (…)`) and keeps the snapshots |
  | `run_event` | The transcript: `seq`, `type`, `at`, `payload` as the host sent it | Primary key `(run_id, seq)`; the event types; the payload a JSON object |
  | `run_command` | A durable command to the run's host, `CANCEL` only: `status` `PENDING` or `DONE`, `created_by`, `last_sent_at`, `completed_at` | One pending command per run and type (`run_command_pending_uq`) |
  | `run_repository` | The workspace a run was assigned with, in order (`position` 0 to 19): `repository_id` and `system_id` (`NULL` once removed), `binding_id`, `name`, `dir` (unique in the run), `read_only` with `read_only_cause` (`AGENT`, `GRANT`, `REQUEST`), and the git result `pushed`, `head_sha`, `commits` | A cause exactly when read-only; the directory's form; a repository once per run |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `run` and `run_command` (a run and its commands are never deleted), `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on `run_event` (an event is deleted only to keep a run under its cap), and all four on `run_repository` (its rows are replaced by each assignment). Five `SECURITY DEFINER` lookups return ids only, as `runner_host_for_token` does, for the work across companies, which then locks each row in its company's tenant context: `run_dispatch_queue` (the queued runs that are neither assigned nor backing off, oldest first, after a position), `run_accept_overdue`, `run_lease_expired`, `run_lost_unrevoked` (runs lost by lease expiry before a cutoff whose model tokens are still unrevoked) and `run_occupying_companies` (the companies whose runs hold a slot). They read `run` through the owner's SELECT-only policy `run_owner_lookup`, which the runtime role never matches. `run.conversation_id` and `run_repository.binding_id` got their foreign keys with package 3c's `V005` (below).
- **Package 3d** adds no table and no migration: the fixed catalogues are code (`FixedTools`, §15.2), and a tool's call stores nothing.
- **Tables of package 3c** (`V005__conversations.sql`; the package 3 contract, §3.8 to §3.13 and §4; §15.7), tenant tables as above. Part 1 created all of them; it writes `conversation`, `conversation_key_counter`, `conversation_binding` and `message`; part 2 writes `decision` and a binding's `waiting_calls`, with what `V009` adds (below); part 3 writes `output` and `attachment`, with what `V008` adds (below). I6 to I16 here are the package 3 contract's.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `conversation` | One agent's work for one person in one place: `key`, `title`, `place` (`TINFIRM_THREAD`; `CHAT_THREAD`, `TRACKER_ISSUE` and `SCHEDULE` reserved, with `place_ref`), `origin` (`APP`; `CLIENT`, `DIRECT_MESSAGE` and `TRIGGER` reserved), `person_principal_id` with `person_kind` (always `MEMBER`), the subject (`subject_system_id`, `subject_key`, `subject_url`, `subject_title`), `runtime`, `payer_principal_id`, `model_access` (`BUDGET`, or `SUBSCRIPTION` with `subscription_host_id` and `subscription_label`), `workspace` (jsonb `[{repositoryId, readOnly, source}]`, `source` `AGENT` or `REQUESTED`), `granted_hosts`, `state` (`OPEN`, `ENDED`) with `ended_reason` (`USER`, `AGENT_DELETED`, `PERSON_LEFT`), `ended_at` and `ended_by`, `turn_status`, `waiting_on` with `waiting_reason` and `waiting_since`, `turn_count`, `active_run_id`, `last_run_id`, `last_turn_at`, the latest turn's `result_*`, `failure_class` and `failure_cause`, `auto_retries` (V051, ADR-0073: how many automatic retries in a row the latest turn is, 0 for any other turn; at least 0), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by` | I12: a key unique in the company (`conversation_key_uq`), 1 to 93 lower-case letters, digits and inner hyphens (`conversation_key_ck`); the agent of the company (`conversation_agent_fk`); the person only a `MEMBER` principal (`conversation_person_fk` on `(person_principal_id, company_id, person_kind)`); I10: the payer is the person (`conversation_payer_ck`); a label exactly with `SUBSCRIPTION`, neither host nor label with `BUDGET` (`conversation_model_access_ck`); a subject's system, link and title only with its key, the link http(s) without user info; at most 20 workspace entries and 100 granted hosts; `ENDED` exactly with its reason and time; a waiting reason and `waiting_since` exactly while `waiting_on` is not `NONE`. The trigger `conversation_fixed_ck_trg` (function `conversation_fixed_ck()`): the agent, person, key, runtime, payer, model access and subscription label never change and the subscription's host only goes (`conversation_fixed_ck`), and an ended conversation stays ended (`conversation_ended_final_ck`). A removed subscription host sets `subscription_host_id` to `NULL` and keeps the label; a removed subject system sets `subject_system_id` to `NULL` and keeps the key, link and title |
  | `conversation_key_counter` | The `n` of `{base}-{n}`: `company_id`, `base` (at most 80 characters), `next` | Primary key `(company_id, base)`; advanced under its row lock (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`), so a key is never reused |
  | `conversation_binding` | The account a conversation uses on one system: `system_id`, `state` (`BOUND`, `WAITING`, `GONE`), `how` (`OWN`, `GRANTED`, `CHOSEN`), `account_id`, `holder_principal_id`, `external_id`, `grant_id`, `reason`, `choices` (grant ids), `waiting_calls` (the calls part 2 parks, at most 10), `waiting_since`, `bound_at`, `bound_by` (the person, when they chose); from V032 `run_id`, the run whose tool call or workspace first asked for it (set once, at its insert or, for a workspace's, once the turn's run exists; `NULL` for a trigger's `FIXED` bindings and those before V032; no foreign key) | I6: one per system per conversation (`conversation_binding_system_uq`); `how` exactly with an account, and a grant exactly when not `OWN`; a reason exactly when not `BOUND`, from its state's list (`conversation_binding_reason_ck`); choices only for `PICK_GRANT` and `CHOOSE`; `waiting_since` exactly while `WAITING`. **No foreign key** on the system, the account, the grant or the holder: a binding survives their deletion as the conversation's history. The trigger `conversation_binding_ck_trg` (function `conversation_binding_ck()`) checks them when the binding binds: the system is the company's at insert (`conversation_binding_system_ck`); the conversation and the system never change (`conversation_binding_fixed_ck`); I7: `GONE` is final (`conversation_binding_gone_ck`); I6: once bound, the holder, the identity, the grant and `how` never change, and the account is replaced only by the holder's account on the same system with the same identity (`conversation_binding_fixed_account_ck`); I8: the account is the holder's on the system with the bound identity, and the holder is the person (without a grant) or a principal whose account the bound grant lends, which only a service identity's can be (`conversation_binding_holder_ck`; `V049` and `V050` changed I6 and I8 for the systems' credentials: Credentials on systems, below) |
  | `message` | What is said in a Tinfirm thread: `kind` (`PERSON` with `author_principal_id`, `AGENT`, `SYSTEM`), `body` (1 to 100 000 characters), `answers_open_questions`, `run_id` (the turn that took a `PERSON` message, or produced an `AGENT` or `SYSTEM` one), `unanswered`, `decision_id`, `created_at`; from `V036` `steering` ([ADR-0060](adr/0060-steering-a-running-turn.md), §15.7): `SENDING` while a message written during a turn is sent to that turn's runner host (it still waits, `run_id` `NULL`, and the next turn takes it if the running one does not), `DELIVERED` once the running turn took it (`run_id` that turn's run), `NULL` for every other message | An author exactly on a `PERSON` message, and `answers_open_questions` only there; `message.unanswered` only on a `PERSON` message without a run (`message_unanswered_ck`): one that waited when its turn was cancelled or its conversation ended, which no later turn takes; `steering` only on a `PERSON` message, `SENDING` only without a run and not unanswered, `DELIVERED` only with a run (`message_steering_ck`); the run, the author and the decision of the company |
  | `decision` | Part 2's questions and approvals: `kind` (`QUESTION`, `APPROVAL`), `run_id` and `request_id`, the question and its options or the subject and reason, `approver_principal_id`, `status` (`OPEN`, `DECIDED`, `CANCELLED`), the outcome with its answer, option, note, scope and access, `result`, `decided_by_principal_id`, `decided_at`, `cancel_cause` | One decision per request id per conversation (`decision_request_uq`); each kind's shape and outcomes; an outcome exactly when `DECIDED`, a cause exactly when `CANCELLED`; I16: decided or cancelled once (`decision_final_ck`, trigger `decision_final_ck_trg`) |
  | `output` | Part 3's: `CHANGE_REQUEST` (`url`, `repository_id`, `external_number`, `change_request_state`), `DOCUMENT` (`attachment_id`), `LINK` (`url`), with `title`, `run_id` and `request_id` | Each kind's shape; one output per request id per conversation; an attachment the document of one output at most (`output_attachment_uq`), and not deleted while it is (`output_attachment_fk`); a removed repository sets `repository_id` to `NULL` |
  | `attachment` | Part 3's files: `file_name`, `content_type`, `size_bytes`, `sha256`, `content` (bytea, stored out of line uncompressed), `note`, the uploading user, or the agent and its run | A name unique in the conversation, without `/` or `\`, not `.` or `..` (`V008` admits the paths of `/output`, `V010` up to 1024 characters and keys the name's uniqueness on its MD5, below); the size the content's; an agent's file with its run and no user, a person's with neither |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on every table but `attachment`, which it may `DELETE` as well (a person removes a file): a conversation, its bindings, messages, decisions and outputs are its history, changed in place and never deleted. `V005` also adds the foreign keys `V004` left: `run.conversation_id` to `conversation` (`run_conversation_fk`) and `run_repository.binding_id` to `conversation_binding` (`run_repository_binding_fk`), and the conversation's `active_run_id` and `last_run_id` to `run`. The subscription is stored as `subscription_host_id` plus `subscription_label`, as package 2 names a subscription and `agent_settings` keeps it (§15.5), not as the contract's `subscription_id`.
- **Tables of package 3c's part 3** (`V008__outputs.sql`; the package 3 contract, §3.11, §3.13, §5.4 and open point 5; §15.7), tenant tables as above:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `attachment` (changed) | `file_name` up to 500 characters (1024 from `V010`, below): a document from a run's `/output` keeps its path relative to the folder as its name (`reports/q3.md`) | `attachment_name_ck` anew: 1 to 500 characters, no `\`, no leading or trailing `/` and no `//`, no segment `.` or `..` or longer than 255 characters. A person's file always has a name of one segment (the server checks it) |
  | `output` (changed) | | `output_id_kind_uq`, `UNIQUE (id, company_id, kind)`: the target of `run_change_request`'s key |
  | `run_change_request` | A change request the server opened after a turn pushed, keyed by its `CHANGE_REQUEST` output (`output_id`, with `output_kind`): `conversation_id`, `run_id` (the turn whose push it was opened for), `repository_id`, `binding_id` (the conversation's binding on the code host, the only account that opens it and reads its state), `number` (a pull request's number, a merge request's iid), `url`, `state` (`OPEN`, `DRAFT`, `MERGED`, `CLOSED`; `NULL` before a first read), `state_checked_at` (`NULL`: due now), `watched` (false once `MERGED`, the conversation ended or its binding is gone), `created_at`; from V038 `head_sha` (its head commit as last read, for a `MERGED` one the commit it was merged at; `NULL` before a read that had one) | At most one per conversation and repository that is not `MERGED` (V038's partial `run_change_request_current_uq`, which replaced V008's `run_change_request_repository_uq`): a later push goes to it, and after a merge a push of another commit gets the next one; a head commit that is a commit's hex name; only a `CHANGE_REQUEST` output (`output_kind` always `CHANGE_REQUEST`, and `run_change_request_output_fk` on `(output_id, company_id, output_kind)`); the conversation, the run and the binding of the company; a number above 0, an http(s) link without user info, the known states. A removed repository takes the row along (`ON DELETE CASCADE`); its output keeps the link |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `run_change_request`: its state and `watched` change in place, and it goes only with its repository. One `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup returns ids only, as `run_dispatch_queue` does: `run_change_request_state_due(timestamptz, integer)`, the watched change requests never read or last read before the cutoff, least recently read first, at most 500, for the watch on `worker` replicas, which claims each row again in its company's tenant context. It reads the table through the owner's SELECT-only policy `run_change_request_owner_lookup`, which the runtime role never matches.
- **Tables of package 3c's part 2** (`V009__conversation_tools.sql`; the package 3 contract, §3.12, §5.4, §5.8, §5.9 and §11; §15.7), tenant tables as above. Part 2's migration is `V009`, so that it runs after part 3's `V008`. `V006` and `V007` are unused.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `conversation_tool_name` | The name each tool was listed by in a conversation, kept for the conversation's life and mapped back before a call (§15.7): `conversation_id`, `name` (the protocol's lower-case name), `system_id`, `tool` (the system's name for it), `created_at` | Primary key `(conversation_id, name)`; one name per tool (`conversation_tool_name_tool_uq` on `(conversation_id, system_id, tool)`); the name `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`, the tool `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`; the conversation of the company (`conversation_tool_name_conversation_fk`). **No foreign key** on the system: a removed system's names stay, and a call by one is `UNKNOWN_TOOL` |
  | `conversation_tool_denial` | The tools a run was refused, once per run and tool, so that `tool.denied` is audited once (§8): `run_id`, `system_id`, `tool`, `step` (`AGENT`, `SETTINGS`, `BINDING`), `created_at` | Primary key `(run_id, system_id, tool)`; the run of the company (`conversation_tool_denial_run_fk`); the steps (`conversation_tool_denial_step_ck`) |
  | `decision` (changed) | `reported_run_id`: the turn whose prompt told the agent a decided or cancelled decision (`# Answers`), so that each is told once | The run of the company (`decision_reported_run_fk` on `(reported_run_id, company_id)`); only a decision that is not `OPEN` is told (`decision_reported_ck`); the partial index `decision_unreported_idx` on `conversation_id` for the decided and cancelled ones not told yet, and `decision_reported_run_idx` on `reported_run_id` |

  Both new tables have `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`. The runtime role has `SELECT` and `INSERT` on them: a conversation's names and a run's denials are written once and kept with its history. `reported_run_id` is set with the runtime role's `UPDATE` on `decision` (`V005`).

- **Tables of package 6's audit and retention** (`V023__retention.sql`; the package 6 contract, §3.6, §3.7; §15.11). `V011` to `V021` are other work, package 6's spend and switches (`V020`, `V021`) among them; the indexes the contract also planned for its `V022`, a number left unused, are created `IF NOT EXISTS`.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `audit_check` | A daily check of one chain (`company_id` `NULL` for the instance's): `checked_at`, `ok`, `from_seq` (the anchor's head), `failure` (`BROKEN` with `first_invalid_seq`, `SHORTER`, `HEAD_CHANGED`), `head_seq` and `head_hash` (the chain's last event as found), `events_checked`, `acknowledged_at`, `acknowledged_by`, `acknowledged_note` | A failure exactly when not `ok`, `first_invalid_seq` exactly for `BROKEN`; only a failure acknowledged. Row-level security as `audit_event`'s since V016: a company's in its context, the instance's for instance admins, every chain's for server-side work (`app.system`) |
  | `retention_settings` | The OWNER's periods, one row per company: `run_events_days` (7 to 3650), `chat_content_days` (1 to 3650, at most `content_days`), `content_days` and `files_days` (30 or 7 to 3650, `NULL`: kept), from `V039` `memory_days` (how long a note not used is kept, 30 to 3650, `NULL`: kept; 730 by default, §15.18), `updated_at`, `updated_by`. No row: the defaults 90, 30, 365, 365 and 730 | The ranges (`retention_*_ck`, `retention_memory_ck`) |
  | `retention_sweep` | When the daily sweep last ran in the company, and the open conversations it found older than their period | |
  | `legal_hold` | A hold on a `conversation_id` or a `principal_id` (exactly one), `reason` (1 to 500 characters, on the row only), `set_at`, `set_by`, `lifted_at`, `lifted_by` | One target (`legal_hold_target_ck`), of the company (composite foreign keys) |
  | Purge markers | `message.purged_at` (`body`), `decision.purged_at` (question, options, reason, answer, option, note, result, and a tool call's `arguments` and `what` and a result's `summary`, `writes` and `writeItems` in `subject`), `output.purged_at` (`title`), `attachment.content_purged_at` (`content`, `note`), `run.texts_purged_at` (summary, failure message, the finish result's steps and questions) and `run.events_purged_at` (its `run_event` rows deleted), `conversation.title_purged_at`, `content_purged_at` and `erased_at` | Each text `NULL` once its row's marker is set (and, for a message's body and an output's and a conversation's title, only then), every check naming `IS NOT NULL` itself (a `CHECK` that yields `NULL` passes); a purged file keeps its size, hash, type and name (`attachment_size_ck`); a conversation marked purged or erased has no title, latest result or subject title (`conversation_content_purged_ck`) |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `audit_check` (the last 400 per chain are kept, and always the anchor) and `legal_hold` (a lifted hold is deleted 400 days after its lift), `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `retention_settings` and `retention_sweep`, and `DELETE` on `run_event` as before. `audit_event` gains the filters' indexes, each ending in `seq`: `(company_id, action)`, `(company_id, target_type, target_id)`, `(company_id, actor_type, actor_id)`, `(company_id, occurred_at)`, and on `details ->> 'conversationId'` and `details ->> 'principalId'`; nothing hashed changes. Two `SECURITY DEFINER` lookups return ids only: `audit_check_due(timestamptz)`, the chains (with `NULL` for the instance's) not checked since the cutoff, through the owner's SELECT-only policies on `audit_chain_head` and `audit_check`; `retention_due(timestamptz, integer)`, the companies not swept since the cutoff, through the owner's policies on `company` and `retention_sweep`.

  `V042__retention_preview_indexes.sql` adds indexes only, for the previews of retention and erasure (§15.11), which run the sweep's and the erasure's selections on every pause in the Owner's typing: `decision_decided_by_principal_idx` on `decision (decided_by_principal_id)` and `attachment_uploaded_by_idx` on `attachment (uploaded_by_user_id)`, both partial on the column being set (what a member who left decided and added in others' conversations, which an erasure and its preview read by the person; a user's deletion, `ON DELETE SET NULL`, reads the files by the user too), and `conversation_ended_idx` on `conversation (company_id, ended_at, id)` where `state = 'ENDED'` (the sweep's selection of each kind by the conversation's end, which V023's `conversation_content_kept_idx` serves only while the content is kept).
- **The names of `/output`** (`V010__artifact_names.sql`; runner protocol v2's `ArtifactResult.name`; §15.7):

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `attachment` (changed) | `file_name` up to 1024 characters, as the protocol allows a path of `/output` (`V008` kept 500, and a longer path was refused at its upload) | `attachment_name_ck` anew: 1 to 1024 characters, with `V008`'s other rules. `attachment_name_uq` anew, a unique index on `(conversation_id, md5(file_name))`: a B-tree entry holds at most 2704 bytes and 1024 characters may take 4096 in UTF-8, so the name's uniqueness is kept on its MD5, a key and not a protection. The server checks the name under the conversation's lock before it inserts, as before |

- **The recovery of calls** (`V015__call_recovery.sql`; §15.7, Work after the commit). `V011` to `V014` belong to package 4 and the model keys.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `decision` (changed) | `call_started_at`: when an approved tool call was taken to run, under the decision's lock; null before, and again after a passing failure that called nothing. One taken long ago and still without a `result` was running when the server stopped | Only an approved decision's (`decision_call_started_ck`); the partial index `decision_call_pending_idx` on `decided_at` for the approved tool calls without a result |
  | `conversation_binding` (unchanged columns) | A parked call's `claimedAt` in `waiting_calls`: when it last turned `ALLOWED` or `RUNNING` | The partial index `conversation_binding_calls_idx` on the bindings that hold calls |

  One `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup returns ids only, as `run_dispatch_queue` does: `conversation_calls_due(timestamptz, timestamptz, integer)`, at most 500 of each kind: `DECISION`, an approved tool call without a result, never taken and approved before the first cutoff, or taken before the second; `BINDING`, a binding with a call `ALLOWED` before the first cutoff, `RUNNING` since before the second, or `PARKED` while the binding is `BOUND`. The recovery on `worker` replicas claims each again under its locks in its company's tenant context. It reads the tables through the owner's SELECT-only policies `decision_owner_lookup` and `conversation_binding_owner_lookup`, which the runtime role never matches.

- **Tables of package 6's spend** (`V020__spend.sql`; the package 6 contract, §3.1, §3.2; §15.12), tenant tables as above. Package 6's migrations are `V020`, `V021` and `V023` (with `V028` in the chats, §15.16); Flyway tolerates the gaps between the packages' migrations.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `run` (changed) | `model`: the settings' model the run was assigned with (`NULL`: the runtime's default), written by the run module at the assignment | 1 to 200 characters (`run_model_ck`) |
  | `relay_usage` | The model module's: what the relay saw of a run's answers, per `(run_id, model, key_id)`: `requests`, `input_tokens` (neither read from nor written to the cache), `output_tokens`, `cached_input_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`, `updated_at`. `key_id` is LiteLLM's id of the key forwarded with | Counts at least 0; no foreign key on the run, as `run_model_token`; deleted 30 days after its last answer, with the reconciliation |
  | `model_settings` (changed) | `budget_alert_thresholds` (`smallint[]`, `{80,100}`), `monthly_alert_usd` (`NULL`: none) | 1 to 3 thresholds from 1 to 100, none `NULL` (`model_settings_alert_thresholds_ck`); an amount of at least 0 |
  | `budget_alert` | The model module's: one threshold crossed in one budget period, `(principal_id, resets_at, threshold)` (`resets_at` `'infinity'` when LiteLLM named no reset), with the spend and budget then and `crossed_at` | A threshold from 1 to 100; goes with its principal; deleted 400 days after it was crossed |
  | `spend_settlement` | The governance module's: an ended run to settle, `PENDING` until `SETTLED`: `payer_principal_id`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `attempts`, `due_at`, `lease_until` (the worker that reads its logs), `last_error` and `failing_since` (LiteLLM's logs could not be read), `settled_at` | One per run (the primary key); the run of the company; `settled_at` exactly when `SETTLED`; `failing_since` exactly with `last_error`; deleted 30 days after it settled |
  | `run_spend` | The governance module's: what one run spent on one model, `(run_id, model)`: `conversation_id`, `agent_id`, `trigger_id` (no foreign key), `payer_principal_id`, `acting_principal_id`, `personal`, `day` and `ended_at`, `requests`, the four token counts, `cost_usd` (`numeric(14,6)`), `source` (`GATEWAY`, `RELAY`, `SUBSCRIPTION`), `recorded_at` | G3: one row per run and model (the primary key), written once (the runtime role has `SELECT` and `INSERT` only); the run of the company (`run_spend_run_fk`); a cost for `GATEWAY`, none for `SUBSCRIPTION`, either for `RELAY` (a model without a price) (`run_spend_cost_ck`); `day` the UTC date of `ended_at`; kept for good |
  | `company_spend_alert` | The governance module's: the company's monthly alert amount crossed in a calendar month (UTC), `(company_id, month)`, with the total and the amount | `month` a first day; deleted 400 days after it was crossed |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `relay_usage` and `spend_settlement`, `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on `budget_alert` and `company_spend_alert`, and `SELECT` and `INSERT` on `run_spend`. One `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup returns ids only, as `run_dispatch_queue` does: `spend_due(timestamptz, integer)`, the due `PENDING` settlements that no lease holds, oldest first, at most 500, through the owner's SELECT-only policy `spend_settlement_owner_lookup`; the sync claims them again in each company's tenant context.
- **Tables of package 5a** (`V017__chat_systems.sql`, `V018__chat_conversations.sql`, `V028__chat_switches.sql`; the package 5 contract, §3; §15.16), tenant tables as above, each with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`. `V018` runs after package 4's `V011`, which adds `message.external_id`, `external_author` and `decision.decided_by_name` and fixes every conversation's place and origin (`conversation_fixed_ck`): it adds only what is missing, and redefines the checks both packages share as the union of their rules. `V028` brings package 6's switches to the chats: `chat_event`'s reasons gain `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, `COMPANY_STOPPED` and `SUSPENDED`, and `chat_user` gains `stop_told_at`.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `external_system` (changed) | A chat system's `bot_account_id` (a `TOKEN` account of its service identity until `V049`; since then a `TOKEN` credential of the system, which a service account borrows, §9), `chat_signing_secret_id` and `chat_app_token_secret_id` (a Slack system's own managed secrets, §9); its settings (`transport`, `teamId`, `teamName`, `botUserId`; `tenantId`, `appId`) in `settings` | The secrets on a `SLACK` system only (`external_system_chat_secrets_ck`), each `ON DELETE SET NULL`; one system per workspace or tenant in a company (`external_system_chat_workspace_uq` on `coalesce(settings->>'teamId', settings->>'tenantId')`); a bot's account is never granted (`account_grant_bot_ck`, a trigger on `account_grant`) |
  | `app_user` (changed) | `oidc_tenant_id`, `oidc_object_id`: the Entra tenant and object of the user's last login through an Entra v2.0 issuer, read from the ID token itself | Both or neither, an `OIDC` user's only (`app_user_oidc_entra_ck`); unique together (`app_user_oidc_entra_uq`) |
  | `chat_user` | What Tinfirm knows of a chat user: primary key `(system_id, external_id)` (Slack's user id; Teams' `29:` id), `aad_object_id`, `team_id`, `audience` (`MEMBER`, `GUEST`, `EXTERNAL`, `BOT`, `DELETED`), `display_name`, `dm_ref`, `checked_at`, `last_seen_at`, `notice_shown_at`, `link_offered_at`, `refusal_told_at`, `stop_told_at` (V028: the company's stop told, at most once an hour) | Goes with its system |
  | `chat_channel` | A channel the bot is in or was in: primary key `(system_id, channel_id)`, `team_id`, `name`, `kind` (`PUBLIC`, `PRIVATE`, `SHARED`), `bot_member`, `archived`, `deleted` | Kept by the lifecycle events (`channel_*`, `group_*`, `channel_shared`, `channel_unshared`, Teams' `conversationUpdate`); goes with its system |
  | `chat_link_request` | A sign-in link: `token_hash` (the primary key, hex SHA-256; the token is never stored), the chat user (`external_id`, `display_name`, `team_id`, `team_name`), `held_event_ids` (at most 5), `created_at`, `expires_at`, `consumed_at`, `consumed_by_user_id`, `outcome` (`LINKED`, `REFUSED` with `refused_reason`) | The hash's shape; at most 5 held events; expiry after creation; a refusal's reason with it only |
  | `chat_preference` | A member's default agent of direct messages: `principal_id` (the primary key), `default_agent_id` | A member's principal only (`chat_preference_principal_kind_ck`), and goes with it; the agent the company's |
  | `chat_event` | What arrived, verified: `event_id` (Slack's `event_id`; a press `{user}:{action_ts}`; Teams `{conversation}:{activity}`), `kind` (`MESSAGE`, `MENTION`, `ACTION`, `LIFECYCLE`), `sender`, `thread_key`, `platform_ts`, `retry_num`, `payload` (cleared once handled), `status` (`RECEIVED`, `AWAITING_LINK`, `AWAITING_AGENT`, `HANDLED`, `IGNORED`, `REFUSED`, `FAILED`) with `reason`, `attempts`, `next_attempt_at`, `claimed_until`, `held_until` | Unique `(system_id, event_id)` (C3); the kinds, statuses and reasons; a hold exactly while held (`chat_event_held_ck`), `handled_at` exactly once handled; rows go a day after they arrived |
  | `chat_setting` | The company's chat rates (`messages_per_user_minute` 1 to 600, `messages_per_user_day` 1 to 10 000, `events_per_system_minute` 1 to 6000), `updated_by` | One row per company; none: the instance's |
  | `chat_notice` | The chat module's inbox items: `kind` `LINKED` (a member's, `principal_id`, until `expires_at`, 7 days) or `SUBSCRIPTION_SILENT` (the admins'), `details` | A member's exactly when `LINKED` (`chat_notice_audience_ck`); one silent item per system (`chat_notice_silent_uq`); goes with its system and principal |
  | `chat_post` | What the bot posted that a later step names: `purpose` (`ANSWER`, `FAILURE`, `QUESTION`, `APPROVAL`, `CONNECT`, `NOTICE`, `PICKER`, `LINK`, `SYSTEM`), `conversation_id`, `decision_id`, `binding_id`, `channel_id`, `thread_id`, `external_id` (the message's `ts` or activity id) | Goes with its system |
  | `chat_socket` | A Socket Mode slot: primary key `(system_id, slot)` (0 to 9), `holder` (a replica), `lease_until` | Taken or renewed by its holder, or by another once its lease ran out (one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … WHERE`) |
  | `conversation` (changed) | A direct message's conversation: `place` `CHAT_THREAD`, `origin` `DIRECT_MESSAGE`, `place_ref` `{systemId, kind DM, channelId, threadId, serviceUrl?}` | A chat thread is one conversation for good (`conversation_chat_thread_uq`, C8); a chat thread's reference names its system, channel and thread (`conversation_place_ref_chat_ck`); a direct message is a `DM` chat thread's, and a chat thread a direct message's or a trigger's (`conversation_chat_origin_ck`); a chat thread's reference never changes (`conversation_place_fixed_ck`, a trigger beside package 4's `conversation_fixed_ck`, which fixes every conversation's place and origin); `ended_reason` gains `SYSTEM_REMOVED` beside package 4's reasons |
  | `message` (changed) | A person's message from the chat: `external_id` (the platform's message id) and `external_author` (the chat user's id and name) | The author's rules as the union of packages 4 and 5 (`message_author_ck`) |
  | `decision` (changed) | `decided_via` (`APP` or `CHAT`), `decided_by_external_id` (the chat user who pressed), `decided_by_name` | `decided_via` only on a decided decision, a chat user only with `CHAT` (`decision_decided_via_ck`) |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on the chat tables, but no `DELETE` on `chat_setting` (changed in place) and no `UPDATE` on `chat_post` (a post is recorded once). Four `SECURITY DEFINER` lookups return ids only, as `run_dispatch_queue` does: `chat_system_by_id(uuid)` (a chat system's id, company and kind, for the platforms' endpoints before any company context exists), `chat_systems_with_bot()` (the chat systems with a bot, for the Socket Mode connections and the silent subscriptions), `chat_event_due(timestamptz, integer)` (each thread's oldest `RECEIVED` event, in the platform's order, when its next attempt is due and no replica holds it) and `chat_event_expired(timestamptz, integer)` (held events whose hold ran out, rows older than a day). They read through the owner's SELECT-only policies `external_system_owner_lookup` and `chat_event_owner_lookup`, which the runtime role never matches; the work then claims each row again in its company's tenant context (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`).

- **Tables of package 5b** (`V019__webhooks.sql`, `V029__chat_outbox.sql`, `V030__channel_triggers.sql`, `V031__slack_history.sql`; the package 5 contract, §3.9 to §3.12; §15.17), tenant tables as above. `V019` is the number the contract reserved for the webhook: it runs before `V020` and touches nothing they change; the trigger's source `WEBHOOK`, the place `WEBHOOK` and the event `DELIVERED` are `V030`'s, with the channel triggers' checks they share. `V029` and `V030` follow package 6's `V028`.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `outbox` (changed, V029) | A row's `writer`: `BINDING` (package 4's, a binding's account) or `BOT` (a chat thread's, its system's bot), `binding_id` null for a bot's row; `target_outbox_id` (a `CHAT_UPDATE`'s earlier `CHAT_POST`); kinds `CHAT_POST`, `CHAT_UPDATE`, `CHAT_FILE`, `CHAT_REACTION`; causes `RECEIVED`, `APPROVAL`, `DECIDED`, `CONNECT`, `CONNECTED`, `NOTICE` | A bot's row is a chat kind of a `CHAT_THREAD` conversation of the system it names (`outbox_chat_ck`, `outbox_binding_ck()`), a binding's a tracker's; a target is an earlier `CHAT_POST` of the conversation (`outbox_binding_ck()`) and never changes (`outbox_final_ck()`); a chat post's body at most 40 000 characters |
  | `system_write` (changed, V029) | `kind` gains `CHAT_MESSAGE`: a bot's delivered post | As package 4's |
  | `trigger` (changed, V030, V019) | `source` `CHANNEL` (a company `SLACK` or `TEAMS` system: one event `MENTIONED`, no scope, subject `ANY`, `EVERY_TIME`) and `WEBHOOK` (a company `WEBHOOK` system: 1 to 20 `DELIVERED` events); `waiting_reason` gains `BOT_MISSING`, `BOT_RECONNECT`, `BOT_SUSPENDED`, `CHANNEL_UNAVAILABLE`; `webhook_conditions` (V019: at most 5 `{pointer, op, values}`) | `trigger_shape_ck` per source; `trigger_ck()` the source's system and kind at the insert; webhook conditions only on a `WEBHOOK` trigger (`trigger_webhook_conditions_ck`) |
  | `trigger_channel` (V030) | A channel trigger's channels: primary key `(trigger_id, channel_id)`, `system_id`, `agent_id` (the trigger's), `is_default`, `state` (`ACTIVE`, `UNAVAILABLE`), `added_at`, `added_by` | One trigger per agent per channel and one default per channel (`trigger_channel_agent_uq`, `trigger_channel_default_uq`); a row of a live channel trigger on its system, naming its trigger, system, channel and agent for good (`trigger_channel_ck()`); goes with its system, and with its trigger's soft delete (the service) |
  | `trigger_firing` (changed, V030) | `actor_principal_id` (the member linked to a chat asker); `event` gains `DELIVERED` | `ON DELETE SET NULL` |
  | `trigger_setting` (changed, V019) | `webhook_per_minute_default`, `webhook_per_minute_max`: the company's webhook deliveries a minute; null: the instance's | Both or neither; 1 to 6000, the default at most the maximum |
  | `trigger_setting` (changed, V059) | `subscriptions`: whether a trigger may pay with its author's subscription (ADR-0085, decision 7) | `false` by default; a row without the limits takes the instance's |
  | `chat_channel` (changed, V030) | `service_url`: a Teams channel's last `serviceUrl`, for its roster | |
  | `conversation` (changed, V030, V019) | `place` `WEBHOOK` (`place_ref` `{systemId, deliveryId, subjectKey?}`, with the mapped `subjectTitle?` and `subjectUrl?`, http(s) only; the API's `subject` is read from it); a trigger's conversation in a `CHAT_THREAD` (`kind` `CHANNEL`) or `WEBHOOK` | `conversation_payer_ck` and `conversation_place_ref_trigger_ck` take both; a channel's thread is a trigger's by V018's `conversation_chat_origin_ck`; a webhook's reference never changes (`conversation_place_fixed_ck()`); one open conversation per webhook trigger and subject key (`conversation_trigger_webhook_open_uq`) |
  | `message` (changed, V019) | | A `WEBHOOK` conversation's `EXTERNAL` message has no member as its author (`message_place_ck`, a trigger, C13) |
  | `external_system` (changed, V019) | A `WEBHOOK` system's `webhook_secret_id`, `webhook_previous_secret_id`, `webhook_secret_set_at`, `webhook_previous_until` (its managed secrets, §9); its settings (`scheme`, `secretHeader`, `mapping`, `maxPerMinute`) in `settings` | The secrets on a `WEBHOOK` system only (`external_system_webhook_secret_ck`), each `ON DELETE SET NULL` |
  | `webhook_delivery` (V019) | Each verified delivery once: `delivery_id` (1 to 200 printable characters), `signed_at`, `received_at`, `status` (`FIRED`, `NO_TRIGGER`, `MAPPING_FAILED` with `failure`), the mapped `event` and `subject_key`, `firings`, `body` (jsonb, cleared after 24 hours), `body_bytes` | Unique `(system_id, delivery_id)` (C3); `FIRED` exactly with firings; a failure exactly when `MAPPING_FAILED`; goes with its system; rows go after 7 days (`WebhookRetention`, the daily sweep) |
  | `agent_system` (changed, V031) | | An agent lists a `SLACK` system too, for Slack's `history` (`agent_system_ck()`, open point 13); `TEAMS` and `WEBHOOK` stay refused (`agent_system_kind_ck`) |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `trigger_channel` and `webhook_delivery`. One more `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup returns ids only: `webhook_system_by_id(uuid)` (a webhook system's id, company and kind, for its inbound endpoint before any company context exists).

- **Part b's use of package 4's `V012`** (`V012__trigger_polls.sql`, written with package 4's part a; the package 4 contract, §3.9 to §3.12; §15.15):

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `trigger_poll` | One read of a system per account, shared by the triggers whose source row names it: `since`, `offset_keys` (the keys at the bound already handled; their number is the listing's offset), `cursor` (Jira Cloud's page token), `etags` (GitHub's per listing), `status` (`ACTIVE`, `IDLE`, `WAITING`), `next_poll_at`, `lease_until`, the backoff (`failures`, `backoff_until`, `failing_since`, `last_error`) and the rate limit as the system said it | One per `(system_id, account_id)`; goes with its account and its system; at most 500 offset keys |
  | `trigger_subject` | A poll's snapshot of a subject, the baseline of its edges: status, type, labels, assignees' identities, the text's hash, a change request's head commit, `created_by_tinfirm_depth`, the newest comment, `source_updated_at`, `seen_at` (when the page was read) | Primary key `(poll_id, subject_id)`: a subject's transaction inserts it (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) or locks it (`FOR UPDATE`), so that a second handling of one change waits and finds no edge (T10); swept after 90 days unread |
  | `trigger_firing` | Each firing, or a skip worth showing: event, value, actor, comment, `once`, `manual_by`, outcome and reason, depth, the `# Request` (at most 20 000 characters) and a started conversation's `# Subject` (at most 16 KiB), the conversation and the turn that took it | `trigger_firing_once_uq` (`ONCE` once per trigger and subject, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); run now never `once` |
  | `system_write` | Tinfirm's record of every write to a system, personal conversations' included: `kind`, `subject_key`, `external_id`, `written_values` (the status entered, the labels added and removed), the conversation, `chain_depth` for a change request or an issue | Written only by the trigger module's recorder (`SystemWriteRecorder`, on `ConversationEvents.SystemWritten`); swept after 90 days |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `trigger_poll` and `trigger_subject`, `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `trigger_firing`, `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on `system_write`. Two `SECURITY DEFINER` lookups return ids only: `trigger_poll_due(now, limit)` (`ACTIVE`, unleased, due, not backing off) and `trigger_schedule_due(now, limit)` (live, active schedules whose `next_run_at` passed); each row is claimed again in its company's context (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and a lease; a schedule's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, due checked again).
- **The write-back's delivery** (`V027__outbox_delivery.sql`; the package 4 contract, §5.8, §5.9 and T14; §15.15, part c), a tenant table as above, beside `V013`'s `outbox`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `outbox_review` | The writes of a result under review, exactly as its decision's `writes` show them: `decision_id` (the primary key, the review's decision), `conversation_id`, `run_id`, `writes` (jsonb, the outbox rows its approval queues: the `RESULT` comment, the `ON_SUCCESS` actions, the conversation's link), `created_at` | The decision, conversation and run of the company (composite foreign keys); 1 to 100 rows; nothing of a result under review is in `outbox` (T14) |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on it: a review's rows are written once and taken once (its approval queues them, its return or its conversation's end deletes them). `outbox` itself is changed in place and never deleted: `PENDING` rows are delivered in order per conversation (`outbox_due` answers each conversation's oldest undelivered row only), `HELD` while their trigger is paused, then `DELIVERED` with their `external_id` or `DROPPED` with their reason, for good (`outbox_final_ck`).

- **The call journal and the submission keys** (`V026__call_journal.sql`; the package R contract, §3.2, §3.5; §15.14), tenant tables with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`. Package R's migration is `V026`; `V044__memory_calls_in_journal.sql` let the journal hold agent memory's writes (§15.18), whose rows have no system.

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `tool_call` | One row per `TOOL` request of a run that reached its call, claimed before the call runs: `run_id`, `request_id`, `conversation_id`, what is called as checked (`system_id`, `tool` as the system names it, `name` as the run lists it, `effect`, `replay`), `arguments_sha256` (of the canonical arguments: keys sorted, no whitespace), `state` (`RUNNING`, `DONE`, `INTERRUPTED`), `attempt` (1, one more for each claim again of a `SAFE` call), `started_at` and `finished_at` (the current attempt's claim and end), `result` (the answer as it went out: the ack's status with its payload, or its code and message; cleared when the run ends), `run_ended_at` (from the run's end; the row is deleted `TINFIRM_TOOL_JOURNAL_RETENTION` later). `run_ended_at` is beside the contract's columns, so that the conversation module, which never reads `run`, knows when to delete. From `V044` a memory tool's write (`remember`, `forget`; §15.18) has a row too, with no `system_id`, claimed and answered in the transaction that writes the memory | Primary key `(run_id, request_id)`; the run and the conversation of the company (composite foreign keys); **no foreign key** on the system, as `conversation_tool_name`; a system on every row but a memory tool's (`tool_call_system_ck`: no system only for `remember` and `forget`, `name` the same as `tool`); the name's and the tool's forms, the effects, classes and states; `finished_at` exactly when not `RUNNING`, a `result` only then, an object; the partial indexes `tool_call_running_idx` (the calls under way, by claim) and `tool_call_run_ended_idx`, and `tool_call_conversation_idx` for a purge |
  | `submission_key` | A caller's `Idempotency-Key` or `requestId`: `principal_id` (the caller's), `key` (1 to 200 visible ASCII characters), `operation` (its operationId: `startConversation`, `postConversationMessage`, `uploadAttachment`, `retryConversation`, `decideDecision`), `request_sha256` (of the method, the path and the canonical body; an upload's of the file's SHA-256, its name and its note), `resource_type` (`conversation`, `message`, `attachment`, `decision`) and `resource_id` (what the first request made), `created_at` | Primary key `(company_id, principal_id, key)`: a key is the caller's own; the principal of the company (`ON DELETE CASCADE`); the key's characters, the operations, the resource both or neither (`submission_key_resource_ck`: the key is inserted first and its resource written in the same transaction, so a committed key always has one); `submission_key_created_idx` for the sweep |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on both: a call's row changes with its attempts and goes after its run's retention or with its conversation's purge, a key goes after its lifetime. Two `SECURITY DEFINER` lookups return ids only, as `conversation_calls_due` does: `tool_call_due(timestamptz, timestamptz, integer)`, at most 500 of each kind, `RUNNING` (a call claimed before the first cutoff and still running: its replica stopped) and `EXPIRED` (a company with rows of runs that ended before the second); `submission_key_expired(timestamptz, integer)`, the companies with keys created before the cutoff. They read the tables through the owner's SELECT-only policies `tool_call_owner_lookup` and `submission_key_owner_lookup`, which the runtime role never matches.
- **The inbox's dismissals** (`V041__inbox_notice_dismissals.sql`; §15.7), a tenant table with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `inbox_dismissal` | A notice a member dismissed from their own inbox: `principal_id` with `principal_kind` (always `MEMBER`), `notice_key` (the notice's `noticeKey`, which holds its condition), `dismissed_at` | Primary key `(principal_id, notice_key)`: once per member and key; a member's principal of the company only (`inbox_dismissal_principal_fk` on `(principal_id, company_id, principal_kind)`, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, a safety net: a member's principal is never deleted); the key's characters (`inbox_dismissal_key_ck`, the API's pattern); `inbox_dismissal_company_idx` for the sweep |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on it: a dismissal is written once and removed (an undo, the member's leaving, their erasure, the daily sweep 400 days after it), never changed.

- **A system's last test** (`V043__system_last_test.sql`; §15.2, §15.17), columns and an index of existing tenant tables:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `external_system` (changed, V043) | Its last test, which the next one replaces: `last_tested_at`, `last_test_ok`, `last_test_error` (at most 500 characters), `last_test_with_account`, `last_tested_by_principal_id` (who ran it, never their name), `last_test_account_id` (the account it used: the tester's own, or from ADR-0072 a credential of the system, which the system's page names to whoever manages it) | All of the test's columns or none; the error exactly when it failed; an account only with `last_test_with_account`; each foreign key with the company id, `ON DELETE SET NULL` of its own column |
  | `audit_event` (changed, V043) | | `audit_event_webhook_refused_idx` on `(company_id, target_id, occurred_at)` where `action = 'webhook.refused'`: a webhook system's refusals of a week without reading the company's chain |

  The runtime role's grants stay as they were.

- **MCP servers that need no account** (`V048__mcp_without_accounts.sql`; [ADR-0071](adr/0071-mcp-servers-without-accounts.md), §15.2, §15.3, §15.7), columns and checks of existing tenant tables:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `external_system` (changed, V048) | `accounts_required`: whether a conversation needs an account on it, true unless whoever manages an MCP server set it false | False only for an `MCP` system (`external_system_accounts_required_ck`) |
  | `conversation_binding` (changed, V048) | `how` gains `OPEN`: `BOUND` without an account, holder, identity or grant, on a system that needs none; it keeps `how` and `bound_at` while it waits or goes | `OPEN` exactly without an account (`conversation_binding_how_ck`, `_account_ck`, `_reason_ck`); `BOUND OPEN` only on a system that needs no account, checked when the binding becomes so (`conversation_binding_open_ck`, in `conversation_binding_ck()`); a trigger's conversation binds `FIXED` or `OPEN` (`conversation_binding_fixed_how_ck`); `conversation_rebind` leaves an `OPEN` binding without a row as it is |

  The runtime role's grants stay as they were.

- **The kill switches** (`V021__switches.sql`; the package 6 contract, §3.3, §5.4 and §5.5; §15.13), columns of existing tenant tables and one tenant table with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `company` (changed) | The company's stop: `runs_stopped_at` (set while its runs are stopped), `runs_stopped_by`, `runs_stopped_reason` (1 to 500 characters, kept on this row, never in the audit chain), `runs_stopped_access_id` (the break-glass access of an instance admin who stopped them; no foreign key) | All of them or none, a reason exactly while stopped (`company_runs_stopped_ck`) |
  | `external_system` (changed) | A company system's pause: `paused_at`, `paused_by`, `paused_reason` | Only a company system (`external_system_paused_ck`: a personal MCP server has the company's switch instead); a reason of 1 to 500 characters exactly while paused (`external_system_paused_reason_ck`) |
  | `principal` (changed) | A suspension: `suspended_at`, `suspended_by`, `suspended_reason`, `suspended_cause` (`ADMIN`, or `USER_DEACTIVATED` when an instance admin deactivated the user); a closed departure checklist: `departure_closed_at`, `departure_closed_by` | `suspended_at` exactly while `SUSPENDED` (`principal_suspended_ck`), with a cause (`principal_suspended_cause_ck`) and a reason of 1 to 500 characters when one is given (`principal_suspended_reason_ck`); a checklist closed only for a member who left (`principal_departure_ck`) |
  | `principal_departure_group` | The groups a member was in when they left (`principal_id`, `group_id`), for the checklist's groups that have no member now | Primary key `(principal_id, group_id)`; goes with its principal and its group (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) |
  | `agent` (changed) | `former_owner_principal_id` (package 3a's table, above) | Only while the agent has no owner (`agent_former_owner_ck`) |

  `trigger.paused_reason` gains `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, `trigger.waiting_reason` `COMPANY_STOPPED`, and `run.queue_reason` both `COMPANY_STOPPED` and `SYSTEM_PAUSED`. The runtime role has `SELECT` and `INSERT` on `principal_departure_group`: its rows are written once, when the member leaves. One `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup returns ids only: `member_companies(uuid)`, the companies a user is a member of, for an instance admin's deactivation or reactivation of the user, which suspends or resumes their principal in each company's own tenant context.

- **The skills library** (`V040__skills.sql`; [ADR-0064](adr/0064-skills-library.md), [the skills contract](reviews/2026-10-15-skills-contract.md), §3; §15.19), tenant tables with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, a policy on `app_company_id()` and composite foreign keys with the company id:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `skill` | A skill of the library: `name` (the Agent Skills name, its folder's in a sandbox), `owner_principal_id` with `owner_kind` (always `MEMBER`; `NULL` once it passed to the admins), `latest_version`, `deleted_at` and `deleted_by` (the soft delete), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by` | The name's form, lower-case letters and digits in hyphen-separated runs of at most 64 characters (`skill_name_ck`); unique among the company's live skills (`skill_name_uq`, partial on `deleted_at IS NULL`: `DUPLICATE_SKILL_NAME`); an owner only a member's principal (`skill_owner_fk`); 1 to 100 versions (`skill_latest_version_ck`) |
  | `skill_version` | A version of the skill's folder, never changed: `version`, `description` (1 to 1024 characters), `sha256` of `content` (the canonical zip, stored out of line uncompressed), `size_bytes`, `file_count`, `files` (`[{path, sizeBytes}]`), `note`, `created_at`, `created_by` | Primary key `(skill_id, version)`; version 1 to 100; the hash's form; at most 5 MiB, the size the content's; 1 to 200 files, as many as `files` lists |
  | `skill_share`, `skill_member_share` | The groups, and the members on their own (a member's principal, `principal_kind` always `MEMBER`), that see and use the skill: `shared_by`, `shared_at` | Primary keys `(skill_id, group_id)` and `(skill_id, principal_id)`; go with the skill, the group or the principal (`ON DELETE CASCADE`, after the skill module removed them, §15.19) |
  | `agent_skill` | A skill of an agent at the version it pins: `version`, `added_at`, `added_by`, `changed_at`, `changed_by` | Primary key `(agent_id, skill_id)`; the version a row of `skill_version` (`agent_skill_version_fk`), which is never deleted, so a deleted skill stays in the agents that pin it; goes with its agent |
  | `agent_settings_skill` | A skill of the agent switched off in a person's or a trigger's settings (`skillsOff`) | Primary key `(settings_id, skill_id)`; the foreign key to `agent_skill` refuses a switch of a skill the agent does not have (I4: `SETTINGS_WIDEN`) and takes the switch away with the pin; goes with its settings |
  | `run_skill` | The skills a run was assigned, at their versions: what its runner host may download (§4) | Primary key `(run_id, skill_id)`; the version a row of `skill_version`; goes with its run |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `skill` (soft-deleted, never removed), `SELECT` and `INSERT` on `skill_version` (written once, never removed), `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `DELETE` on `skill_share`, `skill_member_share`, `agent_settings_skill` and `run_skill` (replaced as a whole, or written once), and all four on `agent_skill` (a pin moves in place). `V040` also adds `NO_HOST_FOR_SKILLS` to `run.queue_reason`.

- **Credentials on systems** (`V049__system_credentials.sql`, `V050__triggers_follow_lendings.sql`; [ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md); §15.3, §15.15), beside the changes to package 1's tables above (`account`, `account_grant`, `service_account_credential`, the bot of `external_system`) and to `oauth_authorization_request`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `conversation_binding` (changed, V049, V050) | A binding through a grant has no holder (`holder_principal_id` `NULL`; `V049` cleared it on the bindings it found): the credential is the company's, lent to the person's group. A trigger's binding holds its author's own account, or a service account and the credential it borrows | A holder exactly when no grant lends the account (`conversation_binding_account_ck`); in `conversation_binding_ck()` (I8): a personal conversation binds its person's own account or a credential a grant lends, never another member's; a trigger's binds its author's own account or the credential its service account borrows (`service_account_credential`). From `V050` a trigger's `FIXED` binding through a service account may be bound again, holder and kind unchanged, to the credential the service account borrows on the system now, with that one's identity, which is how it follows a replaced lending; any other bound account stays fixed for the binding's life (`conversation_binding_fixed_account_ck`) |
  | `trigger_account` (changed, V049, V050) | Package 4's table of the accounts a trigger acts with (`V011`, §15.15): one row per system, `account_id` (`NULL` only while the trigger is paused, a deferred check), `holder_principal_id`, `external_id`, `filled_from` (`CHOICE`, `ROW`), `saved_at`, `saved_by`. From `V049` a row names its author's own account, or a service account and the credential it borrows on the row's system | `trigger_account_ck()` (`trigger_account_holder_ck`): the holder the author or a service account, never another member, and the account the holder's own or the credential its service account borrows (`service_account_credential`); a service account's row refused while the trigger's settings add the author's own MCP servers (`agent_settings_widen_ck`). `V050` moved every live trigger's row whose service account borrowed another credential on the system to that credential (not a chat system's bot, which acts for no trigger, and not a tracker trigger's source row to a credential without an identity); since then the trigger module moves the rows in the lending's transaction (`TriggerLendings`, §15.15) |
  | `trigger_poll` (changed, V049) | A credential's poll has no holder (`holder_principal_id` nullable, `NULL` for a credential): it serves the triggers of every service account that borrows the credential, and deleting one of them leaves it; a member's own account's poll keeps its member | |

  `conversation_rebind` (`V049`) binds a reassigned trigger's conversation again also when only the holder changed: the row may name another service account that borrows the same credential. The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `service_account_credential` (a lending's access changes in place; lending another credential on the system replaces the row); the other grants stay as they were.

- **The Gmail add-on** (`V056__gmail_add_on.sql`; [ADR-0083](adr/0083-gmail-add-on.md), §14.11), the `mcp` module's tenant tables with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `gmail_link` | A Google account linked to a person: `user_id`, `google_subject` (`sub` of Google's ID token), `google_email` (shown, never matched), `last_agent_id` (offered first), `linked_at`, `last_used_at` | Unique `(company_id, google_subject)`: an account is one person's per company; goes with its user. A link unused for 30 days, or whose person is no longer an active member, is deleted when it is next used |
  | `gmail_link_request` | A sign-in link: `token_hash` (the primary key, hex SHA-256; the token is never stored), `google_subject`, `google_email`, `created_at`, `expires_at` (10 minutes), `consumed_at`, `consumed_by_user_id` | The hash's shape; expiry after creation. An account's unused request is deleted when it is offered another, and the company's requests a day after they expired |
  | `gmail_email_conversation` | The conversation a person started about an email: `user_id`, `email_key` and `thread_key` (the first 32 hex digits of a SHA-256 of Gmail's message and thread ids, never the ids), `conversation_id`, `action`, `started_at` | Primary key `(company_id, user_id, email_key)`; the action one of the add-on's five; the person's last 100; goes with its conversation and its user |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on the three.

- **Held files** (`V057__staged_files.sql`; [ADR-0084](adr/0084-email-attachments-and-gmail-drafts.md), §15.7), the `conversation` module's tenant table with `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on `app_company_id()`:

  | Table | Holds | The database keeps |
  |---|---|---|
  | `staged_file` | A file a member's client holds for their next start: `principal_id` (a `MEMBER`), `user_id`, `file_name`, `content_type`, `size_bytes`, `sha256`, `content` (`bytea`), `created_at`, `expires_at` (an hour) | The name's rules (1 to 255 characters, no `/`, `\`, `.` or `..`), the size equal to the content's, the hash's shape, expiry after creation; goes with its principal and its user. A start moves it into the conversation (`INSERT … SELECT` into `attachment`, then `DELETE`); the person's next hold and a sweep of the worker each minute delete the expired ones |

  The runtime role has `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` (for the lock a start takes on the files it moves, never to change one) and `DELETE`. The sweep finds the companies with expired files through `staged_file_expired(timestamptz, integer)` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, ids only), and the owner's `SELECT` policy `staged_file_owner_lookup`, which the runtime role never matches.

- **Tables described elsewhere.** Spring Session's `spring_session` and `spring_session_attributes` (`V001__baseline.sql`; §5: Flyway creates them, Spring does not); the groups, `user_group` and `user_group_member` (`V001__baseline.sql`, tenant tables of the `company` module), in §14.13 (Groups), which still holds; package 4's part a, `trigger` (from `V024` with `admin_changed_at`, `admin_changed_by` and `admin_changed_what`, the author's inbox item after an admin's change), `trigger_account`, `trigger_setting`, `outbox` and what `V011` adds to the conversation, binding, message, run and decision tables, in §15.15 (Tables); agent memory's columns and `agent_memory_note`'s rules in §15.18.

## 8. Audit log

- Each company has its own chain, and there is one instance chain (`company_id` null) for instance events such as users, logins and the vault.
- Each chain has a head row, locked with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`. The head gives the next `seq` and `prevHash`.
- **A transaction's events are appended when it commits** (`JdbcAuditLog`, from package 4's part c): `AuditLog.record` keeps them, each with the time and details of its call, and a listener of the transaction manager (`TransactionExecutionListener.beforeCommit`, which runs after every synchronization's `beforeCommit` and `beforeCompletion`, right before the commit) flushes the transaction, so that JPA writes its changed entities first, and then appends them, in their order, each under the tenant context of its call (§7): a transaction that entered several companies (an instance admin's deactivation of a user suspends them in each, with an event in the instance chain first) appends each company's events in that company's context, as their chains' row-level security requires, and then sets its own back (`TenantScope.eachIn`). So a chain's head is the last row any transaction locks, whatever the order of its calls (§10's lock order), and a transaction that rolls back appends nothing. An event recorded in a synchronization, a `BEFORE_COMMIT` listener's included, is appended with the others, in the transaction; one recorded after the commit (an `afterCommit` or `afterCompletion` callback on the same thread, whose transaction-local tenant context is gone) is appended in a transaction of its own (`REQUIRES_NEW`). A transaction does not read its own events before it commits: they are not in `audit_event` yet. Within a chain, `seq` follows the commits and `occurredAt` the calls, so `occurredAt` may go back from one `seq` to the next: a transaction that recorded an event early and committed late follows one that recorded later and committed sooner. An export by time is the contiguous range of `seq` from the first event at or after `from` to the last one before `to` (§15.11), and may hold such an event from outside the times at either end.
- `hash = hex(sha256(prevHash + "\n" + canonical))`.
  - `canonical` is JSON with keys sorted recursively and no whitespace.
  - It contains `companyId`, `seq`, `occurredAt` (UTC with microseconds: `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss.SSSSSS'Z'`), `actor`, `action`, `targetType`, `targetId` and `details`.
  - The first `prevHash` is 64 zeros.
- **Actions of the instance and the company core** (kept from v1's first milestone):
  - `auth.login.succeeded`, `auth.login.failed`, `auth.logout`
  - `user.created`, `user.updated`, `user.password.set`, `user.password.changed`
  - `company.created`, `company.updated`, `admin.company.accessed`
  - `member.added`, `member.role.changed`, `member.removed`, `member.role_source.changed`
  - A provider that decides roles ([ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md), §5) changes a role as `SYSTEM`: `member.role.changed` with `roleSource: IDP`, `provider`, `previousRole`, `role`, and `seatFallback` with `providerRole` when no seat was free; its new member's `member.added` carries `roleSource: IDP`, for a new user and for a known identity its claim names in a company they are no member of ([ADR-0089](adr/0089-companies-from-the-role-claim.md)); a member it names no more leaves with `member.removed` by `SYSTEM` carrying `provider` and `roleSource: IDP`. `member.role_source.changed`, by `SYSTEM` in the company's chain, says who decides a role changed while the role stayed: for one member whose same role a provider or SCIM takes (target the user, `userId`, `role`, `previousRoleSource`, `roleSource`, `previousProvider` and `provider` where a provider is named), or for a provider that stopped deciding (`previousRoleSource`, `provider`, `roleSource: APP` and the `count` of memberships released).
  - Companies the deployment declares ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md), §5) are audited by `SYSTEM` in their own chain: `company.created` with `name` and `key`, `company.updated` with `key` when a company from before is adopted, and `member.added` or `member.role.changed` with `declaredOwner: true` when the declared owner becomes OWNER. A provider's new member is `member.added` by `SYSTEM` with `userId`, `role` and `provider`.
  - `secret.created`, `secret.updated`, `secret.deleted`
  - `secret.accessed` (emitted by the internal reveal, §9)
  - `vault.rewrapped`
- **Actions of the licence** ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), §15.24), in the instance chain: `licence.key.applied` by the instance admin (target `licence` and the licence id, `details` `licenceId`, `rev`, `edition`, `seats`, `companies`, `startsAt`, `endsAt`, `next` (held as the next key) and `replaced` (`{licenceId, rev}` of the key it replaced, or null); never the key), and `licence.key.activated` by `SYSTEM` when the next key takes over on its start date (`licenceId`, `rev`, `replaced`).
- **Actions of error reports** (ADR-0070, §15.21), in the instance chain, by the instance admin: `error_reporting.changed` (`details.mode`, `LOCAL` or `AUTOMATIC`; only when it changes) and `error_report.deleted` (target `error_report`, `details.source`).
- **Actions kept from v1**, in the company chain: `runner_host.updated` (a host's desired capacity), with the details v1 recorded ([archive/v1/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v1/CONVENTIONS.md) §8); a change of a company host's pool settings adds `changed` `pool`, `oldPool` and `newPool` (the zone, `readyDefault` and the windows; null for none), in the same event as a change of the slots in the same request ([ADR-0099](adr/0099-pool-settings.md), §15.4). Package 2 changed the other two of runner hosts: `runner_host.registered` records the scope and owner, and `runner_host.deleted` became `runner_host.removed` (below). A personal host's credentials are stored without audit; company model credentials are gone, replaced by the principals' keys (below). The actions of runs went with their module and returned with package 3b's (below), which supersede the `run.*` rows of §14.3; those of agents are package 3a's (below), and supersede the `agent.*` rows of §14.3.
- **Actions of ADR-0045 and of groups:** §14.3 (personal tokens, OAuth; `group.created`, `group.updated`, `group.deleted`); the rows of the removed modules are in [archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md#143-audit-actions-added-to-8). `identity_provider.*` of companies' own providers is no longer recorded ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md)); events in a chain stay.
- **Actions of the Gmail add-on** ([ADR-0083](adr/0083-gmail-add-on.md), §14.11), in the company chain, target `gmail_link`: `gmail.linked` (USER, the confirming member; `details.googleSubject`, the account's id at Google, never its email) and `gmail.unlinked` (USER, the person, or SYSTEM; `details.googleSubject`, `reason`: `SIGN_OUT` in the add-on, `DISCONNECTED` on the profile, `UNUSED` after 30 days, `NOT_A_MEMBER`). A write the add-on makes as its person carries `details.gmailLinkId`, as a client's carries `grantId`.
- **Actions of the skills library** ([ADR-0064](adr/0064-skills-library.md)): §15.19 (`skill.created`, `skill.version_added`, `skill.shares_changed`, `skill.deleted`, `skill.passed_to_admins`, `agent.skills_changed`; `settings.changed` names `skillsOff`, below). Never a skill's content.
- **Actions of package 6's kill switches, suspensions and departures:** §15.13 (`company.runs_stopped`, `company.runs_resumed`, `system.paused`, `system.resumed`, `principal.departure_closed`, `agent.owner_changed`, and what they change in earlier actions).
- **Actions of the redesign's domain** (the package 1 contract, §8), in the company chain. `details` holds ids and the names of Tinfirm's own objects; never a token, a secret, an account's login at a system or a person's name. A request that changes nothing is not audited.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `principal.created` | The actor that created the membership (USER, or SYSTEM for the bootstrap and a provisioning login), or the admin who created a service account | `principal` | `kind`; `userId` for a member's, `name` for a service account's |
  | `principal.changed` | USER | `principal` | `changed` (`name`, `description`), `name` |
  | `principal.deleted` | USER | `principal` | `name`; after the credentials the service account borrowed went back to their systems, each `credential.withdrawn` with `cause` `principal.deleted` (the credentials stay, ADR-0072) |
  | `principal.suspended`, `principal.resumed` | USER (the admin, or the instance admin who deactivated the user) | `principal` | `kind`, `cause` (`ADMIN`, `USER_DEACTIVATED`, `SCIM`); `name` for a service account, `userId` for a member (§15.13) |
  | `principal.left` | The actor who removed the member | `principal` | `userId`; before its accounts, owned secrets and personal systems, each audited (I8) |
  | `service_identity.users_changed` | USER, or the deleting actor with `cause` `group.deleted` | `principal` | `name`, `groupIds` (the whole list after the change), `added`, `removed`, `cause` |
  | `system.created` | USER | `system` | `kind`, `name`, `handle` (the one Tinfirm made from the name, §15.2); `ownerPrincipalId` for a personal MCP server; a tracker's `pollIntervalSeconds` when given |
  | `system.changed` | USER | `system` | `changed` (`name`, `authHeader`, a tracker's `pollIntervalSeconds`, an MCP server's `accountsRequired` and `baseUrl`; never `handle`, which never changes), `name`, `handle`; `pollIntervalSeconds` and `accountsRequired` when they changed; for a new endpoint (ADR-0079) `baseUrl`, `previousBaseUrl`, `accountsRemoved` and `appRemoved` |
  | `system.deleted` | USER, or the actor who removed its owner with `cause` `principal.left` | `system` | `kind`, `name`, `cause`; after its accounts, each audited |
  | `system.app_changed` | USER | `system` | `clientId`, `clientSecret` (whether one is kept), `scopes`, `registered` when Tinfirm registered itself at the server (RFC 7591), `automatic` when Tinfirm set it up as an MCP server was registered; for a removal `removed` with the `clientId` removed. Never the secret |
  | `system.tools_refreshed` | USER | `system` | `added`, `removed`, `unconfirmed` (tool names); only when the catalogue changed |
  | `system.tool_effect_confirmed` | USER | `system` | `tool`, `effect` |
  | `system.tool_switched` | USER | `system` | `tool`, `enabled` |
  | `system.settings_changed` | USER | none | `personalMcpServers` |
  | `repository.added`, `repository.removed` | USER | `repository` | `systemId`, `url` |
  | `account.connected`, `account.reconnected` | USER; for `account.reconnected` also the actor of the call whose refresh proved the grant of an account that had turned `RECONNECT` (§15.3) | `account` | `systemId`; `principalId` for a member's own account, or `credential` (its name) for a system's credential (`AccountService.holderDetails`); `kind` (`TOKEN`, `OAUTH`); `userId` for a member's own account; `refreshed` true when a refresh made the account `OK` again |
  | `account.disconnected` | USER, or the actor of what removed it, with `cause` | `account` | `systemId`, `principalId` or `credential` as above, `userId` for a member's; after a credential's grants (`grant.deleted`) and lendings (`credential.withdrawn`), each with the same `cause` (`account.disconnected` when it was removed on its system's page); `revoked` (whether the system confirmed revoking its OAuth grant: false for a token, a system without revocation and a removed system's accounts) or, for an account that went with its principal, `revocation` `after_commit` (its grant is revoked after the commit, best effort, §15.3); `reason` `SECRET_UNREADABLE` beside `revoked` false for a grant whose secret the vault could not decrypt, so nothing was revoked (§15.3); `cause` `system.deleted`, `principal.left` (a member's own accounts) or `system.bot_removed` (package 5a, below) |
  | `account.refused` | USER (a token or a sign-in being connected), or the actor of the call whose refresh, or whose credential (`Accounts.refused`: SYSTEM for a conversation's tool call), the system refused | `system` for a connect, `account` for a stored account that turned `RECONNECT` | `systemId`, `principalId` or `credential` (`RefusalAudit`), `reason` (the system's, at most 500 characters, never a token) |
  | `credential.changed` | USER (OWNER, ADMIN) | `account` (the credential) | `systemId`, `name`, `changed` (`name`, `enabled`), `enabled`; a credential renamed, disabled or enabled ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md), `Credentials.update`) |
  | `credential.lent` | USER: the admin who lent it, or who set a chat system's bot | `account` (the credential) | `accountId`, `systemId`, `serviceAccountId`, `access`, and `replaced` (the credential the service account borrowed on the system until now; its triggers follow, `trigger.accounts_changed` with `why` `lending.replaced`); a service account borrows the credential now, or its lending's access changed |
  | `credential.withdrawn` | USER, or the actor of what removed it, with `cause` | `account` (the credential) | As `credential.lent` without `replaced`, with `cause` when nobody asked for it (`principal.deleted`, and the causes of the credential's own removal: `account.disconnected`, `system.deleted`, `system.bot_removed`) |
  | `grant.created` | USER | `grant` | `accountId` (the credential), `systemId`, `groupId`, `access` |
  | `grant.changed` | USER | `grant` | As `grant.created`, with `previousAccess` |
  | `grant.deleted` | USER, or the actor of what removed it, with `cause` | `grant` | As `grant.created`, with `cause` (`account.disconnected` when its credential was removed, `system.deleted`, `group.deleted`) |

  The vault's events of their secrets are as ever: `secret.created`, `secret.updated` (a refreshed grant) and `secret.deleted` by the actor of the change, with `userId` for a member's own secret; `secret.accessed` with the purpose of each reveal: the caller's for `Accounts.reveal`, `account-revoke:{accountId}` (a grant read to revoke it), `system-app:{systemId}` (an app's client secret for one OAuth request), `system-test:{systemId}` and `system-tools:{systemId}` (the caller's own account, or a credential of the system an admin named or the only usable one, for a test or a catalogue read). A credential's secret is the company's, so its events carry no `userId`.
- **Actions of package 2** (the package 2 contract, §10 and §13; §15.4), in the company chain, with the same rules for `details`: never a LiteLLM key, an admin key or a token. A request that changes nothing is not audited.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `model_settings.changed` | USER | `model_settings` (the company's id) | A change: `changed` (the names of the changed settings: `gateway`, `adminUrl`, `adminKey` when a new admin key was stored, `defaultBudgetUsd`, `budgetPeriod`, `defaultModels`), `gateway`, `relayEnabled`. A test: `tested` (true), `ok`, `relayEnabled`, and `failure` (LiteLLM's reason) when it failed |
  | `model_key.created` | SYSTEM | `principal_model_key` (the principal's id) | `principalId`, `blocked` (created blocked, for a suspended principal) |
  | `model_key.budget_changed` | USER | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `changed` (`budgetUsd`, `models`), and `budgetUsd` and `models` after the change (null: the company's default) |
  | `model_key.blocked`, `model_key.unblocked` | SYSTEM | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`; when a sync blocked the key of a suspended principal, or unblocked a resumed one's |
  | `model_key.deleted` | The actor of the change: who removed the member, deleted the service account, or changed the gateway | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `cause` (`principal.left`, `principal.deleted`, `gateway.changed`); its secret goes with it, and LiteLLM deletes the key after the commit. Not recorded for a principal whose key was never created |
