  | `model_key.budget_spent` | SYSTEM | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `resetsAt` when known; when LiteLLM refused a relayed request with `budget_exceeded`, or a sync read the period's spend at the budget |
  | `model_key.refused` | SYSTEM | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `status` (`401` or `403`), `reason` (LiteLLM's, at most 300 characters, without what it echoes of a key); when LiteLLM refused the key on a relayed request other than for a model the key may not use, and not when a rotation replaced that key since the request took it |
  | `model_key.rotation_requested` | USER: the OWNER or ADMIN who asked | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `scope` (`principal` for one key, `company` for every key of the company, one event per key); the key rotation (§15.4, security review row 2) |
  | `model_key.rotated` | SYSTEM | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `cause` (`schedule`, `request`), `graceSeconds` (how long the previous key still serves the requests that hold it: the configured grace, 0 for a request); when the next key became the principal's, in the transaction that records it |
  | `model_key.retired` | SYSTEM | `principal_model_key` | `principalId`, `rotatedAt` (the rotation that replaced it); when LiteLLM blocked the previous key at the end of its grace, at once for a request. It is deleted in LiteLLM a minute later, unaudited, as a key LiteLLM deletes after a commit |
  | `model_key.*` of the company's own key (ADR-0065) | As for a principal's | `company_model_key` (the company's id) | `holder` `company` in place of `principalId`; `rotation_requested` with `scope` `company_key` for its own rotation, `company` with every key. Never `blocked`, `unblocked`, `refused`, or `deleted` but when the gateway changed |
  | `runner_host.registered` | USER, or the actor who added the member with `cause` `principal.joined` | `runner_host` | `name`, `scope`, and for a personal host `ownerPrincipalId` and `personalHostId` (ADR-0092: one event per company the host is present in, in that company's chain; `cause` when a membership brought it) |
  | `runner_host.removed` | USER, or the actor who removed the member with `cause` `principal.left` | `runner_host` | `name`, `scope`, `cause`, and `personalHostId` for a personal host (one event per company it was present in) |

  The vault's events of package 2's secrets: a principal's key is `secret.created` by SYSTEM (no creator) and `secret.deleted` by the actor of the change (a rotation's switch, SYSTEM, creates the next key's secret and deletes the previous one's: `secret.created` and `secret.deleted`, then the relay's next reveal is `secret.accessed` again for the run); `LITELLM_ADMIN_KEY` is `secret.created`, `secret.updated` and `secret.deleted` by the admin who changed the model settings, never through the Secrets API while the settings keep it (§9); a new key is also `model_settings.changed` with `changed` `adminKey` and `relayEnabled` false. `secret.accessed` has two new purposes: `run:{runId}`, the paying principal's key revealed by the relay for a run's token, once per token and server replica (the contract's §13, row 3), as SYSTEM; and `model-gateway`, an `EXTERNAL` gateway's admin key, revealed at most every ten minutes per company and replica by the work that needs it. Relayed requests are not audited one by one: the run's token counts them (§15.4).
- **Actions of package 3a** (the package 3 contract, §8 and §11; §15.5), in the company chain. `details` holds ids, names, handles, runtimes, modes and accesses; never an agent's description or instructions, a person's added instructions or a host's reason. A request that changes nothing is not audited. They supersede the `agent.*` rows of the archived §14.3.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `agent.created` | USER | `agent` | `name`, `handle`, `runtimes`, `turnTimeLimitMinutes` (null: the deployment's), `ownerPrincipalId`, `version` (1). What it was created with follows as `agent.tools_changed`, `agent.repositories_changed`, `agent.hosts_changed` (version 1, `widens` false) and `agent.shares_changed` |
  | `agent.changed` | USER | `agent` | As `agent.created`, after the change, with `changed` (`name`, `handle`, `description`, `instructions`, `runtimes`, `turnTimeLimitMinutes`, `memoryAllowed` (§15.18): the names of the fields, never their text) |
  | `agent.deleted` | USER | `agent` | `name`, `handle` |
  | `agent.tools_changed` | USER | `agent` | `systems`: the whole list after the change, each `systemId` with its `tools` (`tool`, `mode`); `version`, `widens` |
  | `agent.repositories_changed` | USER: the editor, or the approver of a request for good | `agent` | `repositories`: the whole list after the change (`repositoryId`, `access`); `version`, `widens`; `decisionId` for an approval for good |
  | `agent.hosts_changed` | USER: the editor, or the approver of a request for good | `agent` | `hosts`: the whole list after the change; `version`, `widens`; `decisionId` for an approval for good. A change of hosts' reasons alone is audited with the current version and makes none |
  | `agent.shares_changed` | USER, or the deleting actor with `cause` `group.deleted`, or the removing actor with `cause` `principal.left` | `agent` | `groupIds` (the whole list after the change), `added`, `removed`; `principalIds` (the members, the whole list after the change), `principalsAdded`, `principalsRemoved`; `cause` |
  | `agent.paused` | USER | `agent` | `name`, `stopRunning`, `runsCancelled` (the run ids); not for an agent already paused |
  | `agent.resumed` | USER | `agent` | `name`; not for an active agent |
  | `agent.passed_to_admins` | The actor who removed the member | `agent` | `name`, `formerOwnerPrincipalId`, `cause` `principal.left` |
  | `agent.widened` | The actor of the change, after its own action | `agent` | `version`, and what it allows beyond the version before: `systems` (ids), `tools` (`systemId`, `tool`, `mode`), `repositories` (`repositoryId`, `access`), `hosts` |
  | `agent.accepted` | USER | `agent` | `principalId`, `version`, `previousVersion`; not for a version at or below the one accepted |
  | `settings.changed` | USER | `agent_settings` (the settings' id) | `agentId`, `principalId`, `changed` (`runtime`, `model`, `effort`, `modelAccess`, `instructions`, `defaultRepositoryIds`, `defaultProjects`, `systems`, `tools`, `skillsOff` (§15.19), `memory` (§15.18), `autoRetry` (§15.22)), `systemsOff`, `toolsAsk` and `toolsOff` (`systemId`, `tool`: the agent's tools switched off or to `ASK`), `ownServers` (the own servers added), `skillsOff` (the agent's skills switched off, by id); for a save and for a return to the defaults |
  | `memory.saved` | AGENT | `agent_settings` (the memory's row) | `agentId`, `principalId` or `triggerId`, `conversationId`, `runId`, `number`, `state` (`ACTIVE`, or `PROPOSED` in a trigger's memory), `about`, `replaces`, `characters`; never the note (§15.18) |
  | `memory.refused` | AGENT | `agent_settings` | The memory's ids, `conversationId`, `runId`, the vault secrets' `names` and the credential `formats` the scan found in a note, which was not saved; never the note |
  | `memory.deleted` | AGENT, or USER (its owner, a trigger's audience) | `agent_settings` | The memory's ids, `number`, `state`, `by` (`AGENT`, `OWNER`); for the agent also `conversationId`, `runId` |
  | `memory.cleared` | USER | `agent_settings` | The memory's ids, `count`; only when any note went |
  | `memory.accepted` | USER: a trigger's audience | `agent_settings` | The memory's ids, `number`, `replaced` (the note it replaced, or null) |
  | `memory.compacted` | SYSTEM: the daily sweep | `agent_settings` | The memory's ids, `before`, `after`, `duplicates`, `merged`, `dropped`, and for a merge on the company's own key `model`, `inputTokens`, `outputTokens`; when anything changed or a model answered |
  | `memory_settings.changed` | USER: the OWNER or ADMIN | `memory_settings` (the company's id) | `changed` (`compactionModel`), `compactionModel` (null for none); a request that changes nothing is not audited |

  A person's settings that go with them or with their agent, and an agent's shares that go with it, are not audited one by one: `principal.left` and `agent.deleted` stand for them.
- **Actions of package 3b** (the package 3 contract, §8; §15.6), in the company chain. `details` holds ids, numbers, statuses and codes; never a prompt, a summary, a message, a result or a token. They supersede the `run.*` rows of the archived §14.3.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `run.started` | The actor of the transaction that started the turn (`Turns.start`); SYSTEM for an automatic retry | `run` | `conversationId`, `agentId`, `turn`, `turnReason`, `payerPrincipalId`, `modelAccess` (`BUDGET` or `SUBSCRIPTION`); an automatic retry's `automatic` (true), `attempt` and `retryAt` (§15.22) |
  | `run.assigned` | SYSTEM | `run` | `conversationId`, `runnerHostId`, `hostKind` (`COMPANY`, `PERSONAL`), `epoch`, `payerPrincipalId`, `modelAccess`: the model access that paid and the host that ran it. Once per assignment, in the transaction before its frame is sent; not for one taken back while it was prepared |
  | `run.finished` | SYSTEM (the host's `run.finished`, the reaper, the dispatcher, a reconnecting host), or the actor whose cancel ended a queued run | `run` | `conversationId`, `agentId`, `turn`, `status`, `failureClass`, `lostCause`, `pushedRepositoryIds`, `commits`, `resultOutcome` |
  | `run.cancelled` | The actor of the cancel: the person or OWNER through the Runs API, who paused the agent with `stopRunning`, the caller of `Turns.cancelActive` | `run` | `conversationId`, `agentId`, `turn`, `previousStatus` (`QUEUED`, `RUNNING`); for a running run only at its first cancel; `cause` for a kill switch's cancel (§15.13) |
  | `run.resumed` | SYSTEM | `run` | `conversationId`, `runnerHostId`, `epoch`, `reason` (the frame: `hello`, `run.heartbeat`, `run.finished`), `lostAt`; recorded after the `run.finished` of its loss |
- **Actions of package 3c's part 1** (the package 3 contract, §8; §15.7), in the company chain. `details` holds ids, keys, kinds and reasons; never a message, a title, the subject's title or link, or an account's login at a system. They supersede the `work_item.*` and `job.*` rows of the archived §14.3. Part 2's `tool.*` and `decision.*` and part 3's `attachment.*` and `output.created` follow below.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `conversation.started` | USER: the person | `conversation` | `agentId`, `personPrincipalId`, `key`, `place`, `origin`, `subjectSystemId` and `subjectKey` (null without a subject), `payerPrincipalId`, `modelAccess` (`BUDGET`, `SUBSCRIPTION`), `runtime` |
  | `conversation.ended` | USER: the person or OWNER who ended it; the actor who deleted its agent or removed its person | `conversation` | `cause` (`USER`, `AGENT_DELETED`, `PERSON_LEFT`), `turns` (how many it had) |
  | `conversation.binding.bound` | The actor of the transaction that bound it: the person (a start, a message, a choice, a continue) or SYSTEM (the dispatcher's plan, a use's check, package 1's events) | `conversation_binding` | `conversationId`, `systemId`, `how` (`OWN`, `GRANTED`, `CHOSEN`; `FIXED` for a trigger's; `OPEN`, ADR-0071, with `accountId` and `holderPrincipalId` null), `accountId`, `holderPrincipalId` (null for a credential a grant lends, `V049`), `grantId` (null for the person's own account), `access` then (`READ`, `WRITE`). At the first bind, and again when a binding that waited is bound. With `followed` (the credential it was bound to until now): a trigger's binding through a service account that borrows another credential on the system now, bound to that one at its next check (`BindingService`, `V050`), which the thread is told |
  | `conversation.binding.waiting` | As `.bound` | `conversation_binding` | `conversationId`, `systemId`, `reason`; once per wait: not again while it waits for the same reason and choices |
  | `conversation.binding.gone` | As `.bound` | `conversation_binding` | `conversationId`, `systemId`, `reason` |

  A workspace's git token revealed through `ConversationBindings.use` is `secret.accessed` with the purpose `run:{runId}`, and with `userId` for a member's own account (§9). Parts 2 and 3 add their purposes below. A person's message is not audited.
- **Actions of package 3c's part 2** (the package 3 contract, §8; §15.7), in the company chain, with package R's `tool.interrupted` (§15.14). `details` holds ids, kinds, modes, outcomes, steps and codes; never a tool call's arguments or result, a question, an option, an answer, a note or a reason. They supersede the `decision.*` and `tool.called` rows of §14.3 and §14.10's `tool.denied`. A submission sent again with its idempotency key (package R) audits nothing it makes, since it makes nothing: what it replays was audited once (an OWNER's retry answer still records its `conversation.read`).

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `tool.called` | AGENT: the conversation's agent | `run` (the run whose agent called; the `decision` for an approved call without one) | `conversationId`, `runId`, `systemId`, `tool` (as its system names it), `effect` (`READ`, `WRITE`; `UNKNOWN` as `WRITE`), `mode` (`ALLOW`; `ASK` for an approved call), `accountId`, `holderPrincipalId` and `grantId` of the binding it acted with, `decisionId` for an approved call, `bindingId` for a parked one, `ok` (the system answered without an error), `code` (the system's failure for an error, e.g. `FORBIDDEN`; the system module's refusal, e.g. `RESULT_TOO_LARGE`; `RECONNECT` for a credential the system refused). For each call handed to `SystemTools.call`; not for a call refused before it |
  | `tool.denied` | AGENT | `run` | `conversationId`, `systemId`, `tool`, `step` (`AGENT`, `SETTINGS`, `BINDING`: steps 3 to 5 of §15.7). Once per run and tool (`conversation_tool_denial`, §7), so that an agent that calls a denied tool again and again is one row of the chain; each attempt still gets its refusal. `UNKNOWN_TOOL` (step 1) is not audited |
  | `decision.created` | AGENT, for the agent's request or call, a parked call's approval and a `BLOCKED` result's question; SYSTEM for an approval the server raises (a change request under `ASK`) | `decision` | `conversationId`, `runId`, `kind` (`QUESTION`, `APPROVAL`), `subject` (an approval's: `type` and its `systemId`, `tool`, `repositoryId` or `host`; never the arguments) |
  | `decision.decided` | USER: the person who decided | `decision` | `conversationId`, `kind`, `outcome` (`ANSWERED`, `APPROVED`, `DENIED`), `scope` and `access` of an approved request, `decidedByPrincipalId`, `subject`; `messageId` for a question answered by a message (`answersOpenQuestions`). Never the answerer's name at the system: a commenter's or a chat user's name stays on the decision (`decided_by_name`), where an erasure reaches it, never in the chain (`TriggerQuestionsIntegrationTest`) |
  | `decision.cancelled` | The actor of the cancel or the end: the person or OWNER, who deleted the agent, who removed the person | `decision` | `conversationId`, `kind`, `cause` (`CANCELLED`, `ENDED`, `AGENT_DELETED`, `PERSON_LEFT`), `subject` |

  | `tool.interrupted` | SYSTEM | `conversation` | `runId`, `requestId`, `systemId`, `tool` (as its system names it), `attempt`, `cause` (`TIMEOUT`: past `TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT`; `RESTART`: the replica that ran it stopped, found by a request sent again or by the journal's sweep; `ERROR`: the call ended, but its record failed). Package R (§15.14): an `UNSAFE` call of a run that Tinfirm lost track of, settled without running it again; the agent got `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`. Once per call: a request sent again answers it from the journal and records nothing. Not for a `SAFE` call (run again, or `TOOL_FAILED` past the timeout), nor for an approved or parked call a restart caught running, settled as before (§15.7) |

  A tool call's token, revealed through `ConversationBindings.use`, is `secret.accessed` with the purpose `run-tool:{runId}` (a run's call), `decision-tool:{decisionId}` (an approved call) or `binding-call:{bindingId}` (a parked call, once its binding is bound), as SYSTEM, and with `userId` for a member's own account. A credential the system refused on a call is `account.refused` (§15.3).

  Not audited: reading the inbox, and a member's dismissal of one of its notices and its undo (`PUT`, `DELETE /inbox/dismissals/{noticeKey}`, §15.7). A dismissal is the member's view of their own inbox: it grants, withdraws and decides nothing, changes nothing any other member sees, and leaves the notice's cause (a budget, a trigger, a spend alert) as it was, as a budget alert is a notice and not a choice of authority (package 6, below). A notice that asks for an act is not dismissed at all.
- **Actions of package 3c's part 3** (the package 3 contract, §8; §15.7), in the company chain. `details` holds ids, hashes, sizes, kinds and links; never a file's name or content, a note or a title. They supersede the `artifact.created` row of the archived §14.3 and the `attachment.*` events of the archived §14.7.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `attachment.uploaded` | USER: the person, who added the file or started a conversation with a copy of it; AGENT: the agent of the run whose runner host uploaded it | `attachment` | `conversationId`, `sha256`, `sizeBytes`, `contentType`, `scanned` (true), `acknowledgedCredentialFormats` when an OWNER or ADMIN stored a file that looks like a credential; `runId` for the agent's; `copiedFromAttachmentId` for a copy (`attachmentIds` on a start), which is not scanned again; `stagedFileId` for a file a client held for the start (`fileIds`, ADR-0084), scanned when it was held |
  | `attachment.deleted` | USER: the person | `attachment` | `conversationId`, `sha256` |
  | `output.created` | AGENT for what the agent saved (a link, a document); SYSTEM for a change request the server opened | `output` | `conversationId`, `runId`, `kind` (`CHANGE_REQUEST`, `DOCUMENT`, `LINK`), `attachmentId` for a document, `url` for a link and a change request, `repositoryId` and `number` for a change request |

  A refused file is not audited. The token of a conversation's binding on a code host, revealed through `ConversationBindings.use` to open a change request, to rewrite the related block of the conversation's others and to read its state, is `secret.accessed` by SYSTEM with the purpose `change-request:{runId}` (the turn whose push it is for), and with `userId` for a member's own account.
- **OWNER's reads of personal conversations** (the security review of the redesign, row 18; §15.7), in the company chain. OWNER sees every conversation in full (an instance admin who is not a member has ADMIN's view, §6); every read of a conversation of another person, or of what belongs to it, is on record. `details` holds ids and the kind of read; never a title, a message or a file's name.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `conversation.read` | USER: OWNER, who is not the conversation's person | `conversation`; `principal` (the person) for a list | `conversationId` (not for a list), `personPrincipalId`, `what`: `conversation` (`GET /conversations/{id}`, and the conversation OWNER's `…/cancel`, `…/retry` and `…/end` answer), `conversations` (`GET /conversations`: one record per person listed), `runs` (`GET /runs/{runId}`, and the run `POST …/cancel` answers), `run-events` (`GET /runs/{runId}/events`), `run-stream` (`GET /runs/{runId}/events/stream`, once, when it opens), `attachments` (`GET …/attachments`), `attachment` (`GET …/attachments/{attachmentId}`, an output's document too), `decisions` (`GET /decisions`: one record per conversation listed; `GET /decisions/{decisionId}`); from package 5a, `place` and `origin` for a conversation held elsewhere than in Tinfirm (a direct message's: `CHAT_THREAD`, `DIRECT_MESSAGE`, §15.16), whose chat messages and their chat authors, and files from the chat, the read shows |

  Not for the person's own reads, nor for OWNER's own conversations; never for a trigger's conversation (package 4, §15.15), which has no person: its trigger's audience, OWNER and ADMIN among them, reads it by right (the package 4 contract, §5.13), so no read of it, of its runs, files or decisions, and no list that shows it, is recorded; not for `GET /subjects` (a subject's issue and counts, nothing of a conversation or a person) nor the inbox (OWNER's shows only OWNER's own conversations, and the accounts' counts). No path reads messages or outputs alone: they come with `conversation`, and an output's document is downloaded as an `attachment`. **Every read is recorded**, with no throttle, as decided on 2026-10-12 (package 6, open point 9, which had proposed one record per window): two reads in a row are two records, a page that polls writes one per poll, and an event stream is one read, recorded when it opens. Recorded in a transaction of its own (`REQUIRES_NEW`; one for all the records of a request), as `admin.company.accessed` is, before the reading transaction locks anything (§10's lock order), so it is on record also when the read is read-only or what follows fails, and a record that fails fails the read. The instance admin's break-glass record says that they entered the company; this one says what they read.
- **Actions of package 6's audit and retention** (the package 6 contract, §8; §15.11), in the company chain (the instance chain for an acknowledgement of its check). `details` holds ids, kinds, counts and periods; never a hold's reason, an acknowledgement's note, a message, a title or a name.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `audit.exported` | USER | `company` | `fromSeq`, `toSeq`, `count`; `from`, `to` when asked by time. Once the export ended |
  | `audit.check_acknowledged` | USER | `audit_check` | `checkId`, `failure` |
  | `retention.changed` | USER | `retention_settings` (the company's id) | `runEventsDays`, `chatContentDays`, `contentDays`, `filesDays`, `memoryDays` (§15.18) after the change (null: kept), `changed` |
  | `content.purged` | SYSTEM: the daily sweep | `company` | `chat` and `content` (`conversations`, `messages`, `decisions`, `outputs`, `files`), `runTexts`, `files`, `runEvents`, `records` (per kind of `RetainedRecords`, and `memoryNotes`, §15.18); once per company and sweep that purged anything |
  | `hold.set`, `hold.lifted` | USER | `legal_hold` | `holdId`, `conversationId` or `principalId` |
  | `principal.erased` | USER | `principal` | `principalId`, `left`, `erased` (the counts per kind), `remains` (the kinds of what remains) |
  | `identity.erased` | USER | `system` | `systemId`, `externalId`, `erased`, `remains` |

  The daily check is not audited (it is kept in `audit_check`); reading the audit, the retention page or the list of reads is not; the chain itself is never purged (G11). Nor is a preview of retention periods or of an erasure (§15.11): it changes nothing and reads counts and the company's own holds, never a message, a title or a name, as the retention page's `due` and a departure's preview do; reading a conversation is what §8 records (`conversation.read`), and the erasure the preview prepares is audited with the same counts.
- **Actions of package 6's spend** (the package 6 contract, §8; §15.12), in the company chain:

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `spend.exported` | USER | none | `filters` (the ids and values asked for), `groupBy`, `bucket`, `from`, `to`, `zone`, `rows`: for each CSV of `GET /spend`. Reading spend in the app is not audited |
  | `model_settings.changed` | USER | `model_settings` | Package 2's; `changed` also names `budgetAlertThresholds` and `monthlyAlertUsd` |

  Not audited: a run's spend settling, a budget alert (a notice, not a choice of authority; package 2's `model_key.budget_spent` stays) and the company's monthly alert.
- **Actions of package 4's triggers** (the package 4 contract, §8; §15.15), in the company chain. `details` holds ids, enums, cron, zone and names of Tinfirm's own objects; never a trigger's instruction, a firing's request text, an issue's content or an account's login. The actor is the user who acted, or SYSTEM for what the gate and the kill switches did (with `cause`).

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `trigger.created` | USER: its author, or an admin creating it for them | `trigger` | `name`, `source`, `systemId`, `agentId`, `events`, `subject`, `scope`, `repeat`, `schedule` (cron, zone, overlap, catch-up), `teamGroupId`, `results`, `authorPrincipalId`, `payerPrincipalId`, `modelAccess` |
  | `trigger.changed` | USER: its author or an admin; SYSTEM with `cause` `group.deleted` (the team removed) or `company_settings.changed` (a firing limit lowered to the new maximum) | `trigger` | as created, `changed` (the fields), `editorPrincipalId` |
  | `trigger.accounts_changed` | USER: its author or an admin; for `lending.replaced` the admin who lent the service account another credential | `trigger` | `why` (`created`, `changed`, `reassigned`, and `lending.replaced`: the service account the row names borrows another credential on the system now, and the row follows it, `TriggerLendings`, ADR-0072), `accounts` (per system: `systemId`, `accountId`, `holderPrincipalId`, `filledFrom` (`CHOICE`, `ROW`)), `payerPrincipalId`, `modelAccess`: the whole table after the change |
  | `trigger.settings_changed` | USER | `trigger` | the settings as `settings.changed` records them, `editorPrincipalId` |
  | `trigger.accepted` | USER: its author | `trigger` | `version`, `previousVersion`, `editorPrincipalId` |
  | `trigger.open_events_acknowledged` | USER | `trigger` | `events`, `writes` (the `ALLOW` write tools), `accounts` |
  | `trigger.paused` | USER (`reason` `USER`), or SYSTEM for the gate and the kill switches | `trigger` | `reason` (`PausedReason`), `stopRunning`, `runsCancelled`; `systemId` for `SYSTEM_REMOVED` |
  | `trigger.resumed` | USER: an admin | `trigger` | `from` (the paused reason), `by`, `conversationsRebound` |
  | `trigger.waiting` | SYSTEM: the gate, once per wait | `trigger` | `reason` (`WaitingReason`) |
  | `trigger.reassigned` | USER: an admin | `trigger` | `fromAuthorPrincipalId`, `toAuthorPrincipalId`, `fromPayerPrincipalId`, `toPayerPrincipalId`; the table it keeps follows as `trigger.accounts_changed` (`why` `reassigned`) |
  | `trigger.deleted` | USER, or SYSTEM with `cause` `agent.deleted`, `system.deleted` | `trigger` | `cause`, `source`, `systemId`, `agentId`, `conversationsEnded` |
  | `trigger.run` | USER: of its team, its author or an admin, in the transaction of the firing it made (`ManualFiringsImpl`) | `trigger` | `subjectKey` (null for a schedule), `outcome`, `firingId` |
  | `trigger.fired` | SYSTEM for a poll's or a schedule's firing, USER for a run now | `trigger` | `firingId`, `subjectKey` (null for a schedule), `event`, `outcome` (`STARTED`, `CONTINUED`, `SKIPPED_CHAIN`, `SKIPPED_RATE` once an hour per trigger, and a schedule's every outcome), `conversationId`, `chainDepth`, `manual`. A firing the gate or a paused agent stopped on a tracker is not recorded |
  | `decision.approver_changed` | The actor of the trigger's change (its team changed or removed) | `decision` | `conversationId`, `fromGroupId`, `fromPrincipalId`, `toGroupId`, `toPrincipalId` |
  | `conversation.rebound` | The admin who resumed the reassigned trigger | `conversation` | `triggerId`, `fromAuthorPrincipalId`, `toAuthorPrincipalId`, `fromPayerPrincipalId`, `toPayerPrincipalId`, `fromModelAccess`, `toModelAccess`, `systems` (per system its state and the old and new holder, account and grant) |
  | `source.written` | SYSTEM | `conversation` | Recorded by part c (the outbox and write-back), which names its details |
  | `company_settings.changed` | USER: OWNER or ADMIN | none (the company's chain) | `triggerFiringsPerHourDefault`, `triggerFiringsPerHourMax`, `triggerSubscriptions` (`from`, `to`; ADR-0085, decision 7); the triggers whose limit the new maximum lowered follow as `trigger.changed` |
- **Actions of package 5a** (the package 5 contract, §8; §15.16), in the company chain. `details` holds ids, the platforms' ids (a chat user's, a channel's, a workspace's) and reasons; never a message, a token, a signing secret or a chat user's name (C16).

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `system.bot_set` | USER (OWNER, ADMIN) | `system` | `principalId` (the service account that borrows the bot), `accountId` (the bot's credential), `identity` (Slack's bot user id, Teams' app id), `replaced`, `teamId` (Slack); the lending is its own `credential.lent` |
  | `system.bot_removed` | USER | `system` | `accountId`, `principalId` (the service account that borrowed it), `identity`; the credential's own `credential.withdrawn` and `account.disconnected`, each with `cause` `system.bot_removed` |
  | `system.changed` (a chat system's secrets) | USER | `system` | `changed`: `signingSecret`, `appToken`; never a value |
  | `chat.link_offered` | SYSTEM | `system` | `systemId`, `chatUserId`: a sign-in link posted to the chat user |
  | `chat.link_refused` | USER (the confirming member) | `system` | `systemId`, `chatUserId`, `reason`: `NOT_MEMBER`, `IDENTITY_IN_USE`, `ALREADY_LINKED` (the link is consumed all the same) |
  | `chat.refused` | SYSTEM | `system` | `systemId`, `chatUserId`, `channelId`, `reason`: `GUEST`, `EXTERNAL`, `BOT`, `DELETED` (C6); once an hour per chat user, when they are told; never their message |
  | `chat.settings_changed` | USER (OWNER, ADMIN) | `company` | `messagesPerUserPerMinute`, `messagesPerUserPerDay`, `eventsPerSystemPerMinute` after the change |

  Package 5a adds to actions of earlier packages: `account.connected` of a `LINK` account names `how` (`SIGN_IN_LINK` by the confirming member, `AUTOMATIC` by SYSTEM: Teams' Entra tenant and object) and `chatUserId`; `account.disconnected` takes `cause` `admin` (an admin removed a member's chat account, open point 16) and `system.bot_removed`; `account.refused` on a bot's account its platform refused twice; `conversation.started` of a direct message names `place` `CHAT_THREAD`, `origin` `DIRECT_MESSAGE`, `systemId`, `channelId`, `threadId` and `chatUserId`; `decision.decided` from the chat names `via` (`SLACK`, `TEAMS`), `place` and `chatUserId`, the presser's linked member being the actor's principal (`decidedByPrincipalId`); `secret.accessed` names the purposes `chat-signing:{systemId}`, `chat-socket:{systemId}` and `chat-bot:{systemId}`; `attachment.uploaded` of a file that came from a chat or an issue names `via`.

  Part b reveals a trigger's source account as `secret.accessed` with the purpose `trigger-poll:{pollId}` (one reveal per pass of a poll, as SYSTEM) and `trigger-run:{triggerId}` (a run now's read of its subject, and the editor's search of subjects), with `userId` for a member's own account. A poll's `401` is `account.refused` (SYSTEM).
- **Actions of package 5b** (the package 5 contract, §8; §15.17), in the company chain. Never a message, a body, a chat user's name or a secret (C16).

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `system.webhook_secret_rotated` | USER (OWNER, ADMIN) | `system` | `previousUntil` (until when the previous secret is accepted; null when there was none) |
  | `webhook.refused` | SYSTEM | `system` | `reason` (`SIGNATURE`, `WINDOW`, `SIZE`, `RATE`, `SOURCE_RATE`), `count` (the refusals since the last); once a minute per system and reason, per replica |

  Package 5b adds to actions of earlier packages: `trigger.fired` of a mention names `systemId`, `channelId`, `threadId`, `messageId`, `chatUserId` and `memberPrincipalId` (the linked member, else null), and of a delivery `systemId` and `deliveryId`; `system.created` and `system.changed` of a webhook system name `scheme`, `mapping` (the pointers) and `maxPerMinute`, never its secret; `system.changed` with `changed` `maxPerMinute` when the company's lowered maximum lowers a system; `company_settings.changed` names `webhookDeliveriesPerMinuteDefault` and `webhookDeliveriesPerMinuteMax` (`from`, `to`); `source.written` and `system_write` `CHAT_MESSAGE` for a bot's delivered post (the outbox's `BOT` writer); `secret.accessed` names the purpose `webhook-signing:{systemId}`, once per replica in 10 minutes, and again once on a refused signature.
- **Actions of the write-back** (package 4's part c, the package 4 contract, §8; §15.15), in the company chain, by SYSTEM (the delivery). They supersede the archived §14.3's row of `source.written`.

  | Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `source.written` | SYSTEM | `outbox` (the row) | `conversationId`, `systemId`, `subjectKey`, `kind` (`COMMENT`, `TRANSITION`, `LABEL_ADD`, `LABEL_REMOVE`, `CLOSE`, `ATTACHMENT`, `REMOTE_LINK`), `cause` (`RESULT`, `FAILURE`, `QUESTION`, `ANSWER`, `ON_SUCCESS`, `ON_FAILURE`, `CHANGE_REQUEST`), `accountId` and `holderPrincipalId` (the binding's account that wrote it), `attempts`, `outcome` (`DELIVERED`, `DROPPED`), `reason` for a drop (at most 500 characters). Once per row, when it is delivered or dropped; an attempt that is tried again is not audited. Never the body |

  The token of a trigger's conversation's binding, revealed through `ConversationBindings.use` for one attempt of a row, is `secret.accessed` by SYSTEM with the purpose `write-back:{outboxId}`, and with `userId` for a member's own account. A credential the system refused on a write is `account.refused` (§15.3).
- **Actions through a bearer credential** ([ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md)). The audit log itself (`JdbcAuditLog`) adds the credential to the `details` of every event recorded while the thread holds a `BearerGrant` (`shared.security.BearerGrantHolder`): `tokenId` for a personal access token, `grantId` for an OAuth grant, the id as a string, before the canonical form is hashed. Call sites add nothing. The actor stays the person (`USER`); an event of another actor recorded in the same call carries the credential too, since the person's call caused it. A key the caller set itself is kept. Work that a call hands to another thread is the system's and carries none: the dispatcher, the write-back's delivery, the related blocks of change requests after the commit, and what an approval sets going, an approved tool call and an approved change request, which run after the deciding transaction commits (§15.7). The event of the person's act, recorded in the call, carries it (`decision.decided`).
- `details` never contains secret values or passwords.
- **Personal data stays out of the hashed part.** Erasure requests must not break the chain.
  - The canonical actor is `{type, id}` only. The API resolves `actor.display` at read time; a deleted user shows as `deleted user`.
  - **Targets are named at read time too** (`AuditEvent.targetName`): never stored, read from the live rows once per page and type (`AuditNames`: the users' and agents' names, the principals through `AuditPrincipals`, every other type through the module that keeps it, `AuditTargetNames`), and only as the reader may see the name elsewhere. A principal, and a model key (its principal's id), by its member's display name or its service account's name, `"{name} (left)"` for one who left; a user by their display name; a company, a group, a live agent, a system, a repository, a runner host and a live trigger by their names, a personal MCP server and a personal runner host only to their owner (they are `404` to everyone else); a conversation as `SpendKey.label` names it (§15.12): key and title for the reader's own and a trigger's, another person's personal conversation by key to OWNER and not at all to ADMIN (an instance admin acting in the company has ADMIN's view), so that no name is a read of it. A target that is gone (a deleted agent or trigger included), of another type, or that the reader may not see has none; a target's name at the time stays where §8 put it, in `details.name` of the events that record one. The instance's chain names its users (and a company, should an event target one).
  - **An erased person is named nowhere in the company's chain.** A member whose content the company erased after they left (`principal.erased` with `left` true, §15.11) and who has not joined again has no `targetName` as a principal, a model key or a user, and their acts show `actor.display` `erased person`; the chain, which keeps their ids only, is the record of the erasure (`audit_event_target_idx`). Their name stays on their user account at the instance, whose chain still names them, until an instance admin deletes the user (§15.11's `USER_ACCOUNT`); joining the company again names them again.
  - `details` holds IDs, roles and names of non-personal objects, never emails or display names.
  - A failed login for an unknown email records `{"reason":"unknown_user"}` with no email.
  - OIDC login events record the registration id as `provider`, and a refused OIDC login its `reason` (`not_provisioned`, `email_missing`, `email_not_verified`, `email_domain_not_allowed`, `invalid_email`, `email_in_use`, `inactive`, `oidc_error`). `user.created` for an OIDC user records the claim the email came from (`emailClaim`), never the email.

## 9. Credential vault

- **Envelope encryption.** Each secret version gets a random 256-bit data key (DEK).
  - The value is encrypted with AES-256-GCM under the DEK, with a 96-bit random nonce and AAD `companyId|secretId|version`.
  - The DEK is wrapped with AES-256-GCM under the active master key.
  - The server stores the `keyId`, the wrapped DEK, the nonces and the ciphertext.
- **Master keys** come only from `TINFIRM_VAULT_KEYS` and `TINFIRM_VAULT_ACTIVE_KEY`. In Kubernetes these env vars are filled from a Secret. HashiCorp Vault or KMS is a later extension point, behind a `MasterKeyProvider` interface.
- **Rewrap:** `POST /api/admin/vault/rewrap` rewraps every DEK that is not under the active key. It is idempotent and audited.
- **Instance keys** ([ADR-0045](adr/0045-mcp-server-for-peoples-clients.md)). Keys of the instance rather than of a company live in `instance_key`: the same envelope encryption, with the AAD `instance|name|version` in place of the company's. Today two: `oauth-signing`, the RSA-2048 key that signs the authorization server's access tokens, and `sign-in-pkce`, 256 random bits for HMAC-SHA256 (`algorithm` `HMAC`, from `V025`) from which a sign-in's PKCE verifier is derived (§15.3). Each is generated on its first use (concurrent replicas insert with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and read the one that won) and audited as `secret.created` in the instance chain, actor SYSTEM, target `instance_key`, `details` `name`, `kid`, `algorithm`, `bits`. `InstanceKeys.rsaKey(name)` and `InstanceKeys.hmacSha256(name, data)` are the internal API (each refuses a key of the other algorithm, `IllegalStateException`); an HMAC key never leaves the vault, only its MACs do; no endpoint lists or reveals a key, and the JWK set publishes only the public half of the RSA key. The rewrap covers these rows.
- **Rotating the signing key** is later work ([threat model](threat-model.md) Q23). The proposed way: add a key under a new name (`oauth-signing-2`), publish both in the JWK set, sign with the new one, set `retired_at` on the old one, and drop it from the set once its last access token expired (one hour). Refresh tokens are not signed and survive the rotation. Replacing the key at once instead invalidates every access token, and clients refresh.
- **Secrets other modules write.** `SecretWriter` (`create`, `replace`, `delete`) stores a value a member typed into another module's form, or a credential another module obtained, as a secret of the company with the usual envelope encryption and audit; the caller authorizes. Today three here, and a chat system's and a webhook system's below: a company system's OAuth client secret (`SYSTEM_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`, §15.2), the token or OAuth grant of a credential a system holds (`SYSTEM_CREDENTIAL`, §15.3, `AccountSecrets`; a credential that was a service identity's account before `V049` keeps its secret's name, `SERVICE_ACCOUNT`; a second one is `_2`, `_3`, … as the vault names it) and the admin key of a company's own LiteLLM (`LITELLM_ADMIN_KEY`, §15.4). Such a secret is listed on the Secrets page like any other. The credential's and the app's, as the chat and webhook systems' below, are **managed** (`SecretMeta.managed`, with `managedBy` naming what maintains the secret, by its name: `SYSTEM_CREDENTIAL` and `SERVICE_ACCOUNT` both "a system's credential", `ManagedSecrets`): the page offers no replace, since a pasted value would bypass the check of a token or break a grant, and the Secrets API refuses an update of the value with `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. While the credential or the app keeps it (the port `vault.SecretKeeper`, implemented by the account and system modules, and by the model module for the admin key below), the Secrets API does not delete it either: `409 SECRET_MANAGED`, whose detail names the credential and its system and says where it is removed (`` `SYSTEM_CREDENTIAL` is kept by the credential `Bot` of `Support Slack`: remove it on the system's page ``, `AccountSecretKeeper`), or the system's app, so that the credential's grants, lendings, events and audit go with it. A secret of such a name that nothing keeps is deleted as any other.
- **A chat system's own secrets** (package 5a, §15.16). A Slack system's signing secret (Events API) and app-level token (Socket Mode), written with `PUT /systems/{id}/chat/secrets` and never returned, are managed company secrets the system keeps (`SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, `SLACK_APP_TOKEN`; `external_system.chat_signing_secret_id`, `chat_app_token_secret_id`): the system module's `SystemSecretKeeper` keeps the Secrets API from replacing or deleting them (`409 SECRET_MANAGED`), and they go with the system. `ChatSystems.revealSecret` reveals one for one use in a short transaction of its own (`secret.accessed`, purpose `chat-signing:{systemId}` or `chat-socket:{systemId}`); the signing secret is kept in memory per replica for 10 minutes and revealed again on a signature that fails, at most once per system per 10 seconds. A chat system's bot is a `TOKEN` credential of the system, named `Bot` (`Bot 2` and on when the name is taken), lent `WRITE` to the service account the admin chose ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md); `SYSTEM_CREDENTIAL`, §15.3; Slack's bot token, Teams' app password), revealed with `Accounts.reveal` as that service account (purpose `chat-bot:{systemId}`), kept 10 minutes per replica, used only by the chat module to post and read the platform, never a binding, a tool's credential or sent to a runner host (C9).
- **A webhook system's secret** (package 5b, §15.17). Tinfirm generates it on the system's creation (`whsec_` and 32 random bytes in base64, what Standard Webhooks' libraries take) and answers it once; `POST /systems/{id}/webhook/rotate` makes a new one, answered once, keeps the current one as the previous for 24 hours (`webhook_previous_until`) and deletes the one before. Both are managed company secrets the system keeps (`WEBHOOK_SECRET_{HANDLE}`, the system's handle upper-cased, and `…_2` while a rotated one is kept, so that each system takes its own names and a company may have any number of webhook systems; the Secrets page matches the `WEBHOOK_SECRET` prefix; `external_system.webhook_secret_id`, `webhook_previous_secret_id`): `SystemSecretKeeper` keeps the Secrets API from replacing or deleting them, and they go with the system. `WebhookSystems.reveal` reveals both for one use in a short transaction of its own (`secret.accessed`, purpose `webhook-signing:{systemId}`); the inbound endpoint keeps them 10 minutes per replica and reveals them again once on a refused signature, so that a rotation through another replica counts at once; a kept previous secret is refused from its own `webhook_previous_until` on (the system as read for the delivery), not when the kept secrets are next revealed.
- **The admin key of a company's own LiteLLM** (package 2, §15.4). The model settings keep the master or admin key an admin enters for an `EXTERNAL` gateway as the company secret `LITELLM_ADMIN_KEY`: created with the first key (`SecretWriter.create`), replaced by a new key entered later (`replace`), deleted when the company goes back to `BUNDLED`, and never returned (`adminKeySet`). The model settings are the only way to a new key, which turns the relay off in the same transaction until the gateway passes its test again (§15.4). It is not a managed name (`managed` false: its value is one an admin types, not one Tinfirm obtains), and the Secrets page lists it; but while the settings keep it, the Secrets API neither replaces its value nor deletes it: the model module implements `vault.SecretKeeper` (`ModelSecretKeeper`), and both are `409 SECRET_MANAGED`, whose detail names the company's LiteLLM gateway and says to enter a new admin key or change the gateway in the model settings. A value pasted there would otherwise point Tinfirm's management of LiteLLM at another key (within the ten minutes a revealed admin key is kept in memory) with the relay still on and no test. A secret of that name that the settings do not keep (one made by hand before; the settings' own is then `LITELLM_ADMIN_KEY_2`) is replaced and deleted as any other. It is revealed under the purpose `model-gateway` (§8).
- **Secrets Tinfirm keeps for itself** (package 2; the package 2 contract, §3.2; §15.4). A company secret with `internal` set is one Tinfirm keeps for itself: today a principal's LiteLLM key (`MODEL_KEY_{principalId without hyphens}`, suffixed as the vault suffixes a taken name) and the company's own LiteLLM key (`MODEL_KEY_COMPANY`, [ADR-0065](adr/0065-company-model-key.md), `KeyHolder`), which only Tinfirm's own requests reveal (purpose `platform:{purpose}`, §15.4). It is never listed through the Secrets API or page, its id answers `404` there (to an update and a delete, from OWNER and ADMIN too), and no configuration can name it (`SecretCatalog.names` skips it). Only the model module writes it, with `SecretWriter.createInternal` (the actor may be SYSTEM: the key is made after the commit that created its principal) and `deleteInternal`, and reveals it with `VaultService.reveal` for the model relay (`secret.accessed`, purpose `run:{runId}`). It is never a person's (`secret_internal_ck`), and it is encrypted and rewrapped as every secret.
- **Secrets a person owns** ([ADR-0049](adr/0049-agent-kinds-groups-and-personal-accounts.md), the package 1 contract's I3). `secret.owner_user_id` names the member whose secret it is (null for the company's own secrets; deleted with the user): the token or OAuth grant of their own account on a system (`MEMBER_ACCOUNT`) and a personal MCP server's client secret (`PERSONAL_SYSTEM_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`). An owned secret is never listed, revealed, replaced or deleted through the Secrets API or page, and its id answers `404` there, to OWNER and ADMIN too; `SecretCatalog.names` skips it; it is included in the upload scan. The vault API is `SecretWriter.createOwned`, `replaceOwned`, `deleteOwned` and `deleteAllOwned` and `VaultService.revealOwned`, which names the user and fails for another; `reveal` refuses an owned secret with an `IllegalStateException`, never a value. Every `secret.*` event of an owned secret carries `userId`, `secret.accessed` included. The secret goes with what keeps it: the account's disconnect, the system's removal, and every secret the member owns in the company when they leave it (I8).
- **A value that cannot be decrypted.** A data key wrapped under a master key that is not configured any more (dropped before the rewrap), a wrong key and tampered data all fail a decryption the same way (`VaultCryptoException`, which never carries key material or a value). `reveal` and `revealOwned` write nothing before the decryption (the access mark and `secret.accessed` follow it), so such a failure leaves the caller's transaction usable (`noRollbackFor`): a caller that can do without the value catches it and goes on, as the revocation of an account's grant does when the account goes (§15.3). Any other caller fails as before.
- **No API returns a value.** The internal `VaultService.reveal(companyId, secretId, purpose)` (and `revealOwned`), which the account, model, chat and webhook modules use, is audited as `secret.accessed`, updates `lastAccessedAt`, and is not exposed over HTTP.

## 10. Server code conventions

- **Spring Modulith**: one top-level package per module under `com.flowtivo.tinfirm`: `shared`, `identity`, `company`, `audit`, `vault`, `bootstrap`, `runner`, `mcp`, and the redesign's `principal`, `system` and `account` (package 1), `model` (package 2), `agent` (package 3a), `run` (package 3b), `conversation` (package 3c), `trigger` (package 4), `governance` (package 6), `chat` (package 5a), `skill` (ADR-0064) and `diagnostics` (ADR-0070, §15.21; `ModularityTest` lists them). A module's internals are package-private or in an `internal` subpackage. The build runs `ApplicationModules.verify()`. A lower module never depends on a higher one: it publishes events or offers a port the higher one implements. The redesign's modules, lowest first: `company` (members, groups), `principal`, `system`, `account`; `runner` stands on `principal` (a personal host's owner) and `model` on `runner`, `principal`, `company` (the company's name, for its LiteLLM team) and `vault`;
 `agent` stands on `company` (groups), `principal`, `system`, `account` (the readiness's binding), `runner` (runtimes, a subscription's host) and `model` (the models allowed, who pays, which hosts), and from ADR-0064 on `skill` (an agent's pins, an editor's sight of a skill), which stands on `company` (groups), `principal`, `vault` (the scan), `audit` and `shared` (§15.19); `run` stands on `principal` (whom a run acts for), `runner` (hosts, connections, the protocol), `model` (who pays, where, the relay), `agent` (the agent and the settings in effect), `skill` (the runner host's download of a run's skills), `audit` and `shared`; `conversation` stands on `run` (turns), `agent` (the agent, the settings; from part 2 the approvals for good), `account` (binding, checking and revealing an account; from part 2 a credential refused on a call), `system` (systems and repositories; from part 2 the tools' calls; from part 3 change requests), `principal` (the person, a binding's holder), `company` (groups), `model` (who pays, which hosts), `runner` (hosts, the protocol's records, a runner host's token), `vault` (from part 3, the scan of a file at upload), `audit` and `shared`; `trigger` (package 4, §15.15) stands on `conversation` (a trigger's conversations, through `TriggerConversations`), `agent` (the agent, a trigger's settings), `account` (binding a trigger's table, `Accounts.bind(Trigger)`), `system`, `principal` (the author, the payer, a service account's users), `company` (the team, the members' roles), `model` (the payer's model access), `runner`, `audit` and `shared`, and the conversation module reaches it only through the ports it declares (`TriggerAudience`, `TriggerTurns`, and `InboxItems` for its inbox items), looked up when used; `governance` (package 6, §15.11 and §15.12) stands on `conversation` (the purge API, the inbox's ports, a run's conversation), `run` (the purge API; a run's end, facts and tokens), `agent` (agents' names), `model` (LiteLLM's spend logs and prices, the relay's count, budget alerts), `principal`, `system`, `trigger` (a principal's triggers, their names and payers: `TriggerDirectory`), `account`, `company` (its directory, switches and groups), `identity` (`UserDirectory`), `runner` (`RunnerDirectory`), `audit` (its checks) and `shared` (the ports `IdentityContent` and `RetainedRecords`), and nothing but `mcp` may depend on it (`ModularityTest`). `mcp`, at the top, stands on `identity`, `shared`, `conversation` and `agent`, through their public APIs only (package 6), and on `trigger` (a trigger's memory, its reads and a webhook's), `system` (its reads and its test) and `governance` (spend) the same way (§15.20). `chat` (package 5a, §15.16) stands on `conversation` (`PersonalConversations`, `PlaceConversations`, `PlaceDecisions`, `PlaceFiles`, `Conversations`, its events; it implements the ports `PlacePrompts` and `InboxItems`), `system` (`ChatSystems`, `ChatCalls`, `Systems`, `SystemEvents`; it implements the port `ChatSystemTests`, which the system module looks up when a chat system is tested), `account` (`Accounts`, `ChatAccounts`), `agent`, `principal`, `company` (`CompanySwitches`, the company's stop), `identity` (`UserDirectory`, `CompanyNames`), `run` (`RunStatus`), `audit` and `shared`; from package 5b (§15.17) also on `trigger`: its outbox (`PlaceOutbox`; it implements the port `PlaceWriter` for `CHAT_THREAD`, the `BOT` writer) and its channel triggers (`ChannelTriggers`; it implements the port `ChannelSources`, which the trigger module's editor and gate look up when used). Nothing depends on it. The webhook (package 5b) is a source of the `trigger` module, which implements the system module's port `WebhookLimits` (the company's webhook rates, looked up when a webhook system is registered) and the conversation module's `PlacePrompts` for `WEBHOOK`, and uses the system module's `WebhookSystems`.
  - The outbound guard (the URL and address checks and the connect-time guarded resolver, §14.4) lives in `shared.net`, used by the system module's outbound client (`system.internal.http.OutboundHttp`: every call to a system), by the identity module's fetch of OAuth client metadata documents, and by the model module's client of a company's LiteLLM and its relay.
  - The `mcp` module keeps the endpoint, its rate limit, its scope filter, the tool invocation and the tools on conversations (§15.10). It depends on `identity`, `shared`, and the public APIs of `conversation` (`ConversationReads`, `ConversationCommands`), `agent` (`AgentReads`, `PersonalMemory`), `trigger` (`TriggerMemoryReads`, `TriggerReads`, `WebhookReads`), `system` (`SystemReads`) and `governance` (`Spend`), the same the REST controllers call, with the same inputs, returning the same DTOs; a tool never reaches another module's `internal` package, a repository or a table. The identity module's port `CompanyOwners`, implemented by the company module, names a company's OWNERs for the notices. It serves the Gmail add-on too (ADR-0083, §14.11): its endpoints, Google's ID tokens, the add-on's links, cards and tables, acting as the person through the same public APIs; for that it also depends on `audit` (`AuditLog`, the links' audit) and on `system`'s `GmailCalls` (Google's keys) and `GmailMessages` (the Gmail API, implemented under `ee/`, §15.24), through the outbound client.
  - The identity module serves the instance's OIDC providers, the registrations of the environment (iterated by `GET /api/auth/providers`), and grants the membership of a user a provider creates through the port `identity.ProvisionedMemberships`, which the company module implements: it resolves the provider's company key among the declared companies ([ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md)). It publishes `UserEvents.UserSignedIn` after every successful login, on which the company module applies the declared owners (§5). The instance's providers keep Spring's defaults: the operator names them, so the outbound guard of `shared.net` does not apply. The company module finds a user by email through `UserDirectory` to add a member; no user is hidden from any company.
- **The modules' APIs, events and ports** (packages 1 to 3). Every API runs in the caller's tenant context and, but where it says otherwise, in the caller's transaction; row-level security limits it to the company of that context. Every event carries ids only and is published with `ApplicationEventPublisher` in the transaction that made the change, after its audit event: a listener reacts after the commit (`@TransactionalEventListener`) or inside the transaction (`@EventListener`).
