Refresh tokens live 30 days, authorization codes 5 minutes; neither is configurable. A deactivated user's code exchange and refresh are `invalid_grant`, and their requests are refused (`TOKEN_INVALID`); a removed member gets `NOT_FOUND` at the next call.
- **Clients.** A client is a Client ID Metadata Document or a pre-registered client; open dynamic registration is off. A document is fetched through the outbound guard (§14.4: https only, no redirects, no loopback, link-local or private addresses, the guarded resolver at connect time), with a 5-second timeout and a 16 KiB cap; its `client_id` must equal its URL, it may name at most 10 redirect URIs of at most 200 characters and only the `none` method, and it is cached by its `Cache-Control` (no header: 5 minutes; never more than an hour) in a bounded cache. Whatever it asks for, its client gets Tinfirm's defaults; only its name and redirect URIs are taken. A loopback redirect URI (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`) matches with any port, every other exactly. The pre-registered clients are a list in code written to `oauth2_registered_client` at startup, the MCP Inspector (`mcp-inspector`, `http://localhost:6274/oauth/callback` and `.../oauth/callback/debug`) and Tinfirm's Outlook add-in (`tinfirm-outlook`, "Tinfirm for Outlook", `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/outlook/callback`; ADR-0074).
- **Files for a start** ([ADR-0084](adr/0084-email-attachments-and-gmail-drafts.md)). `POST /api/companies/{companyId}/mcp/files` (`McpFilesController`), one multipart `file`, behind the endpoint's bearer chain and its `Origin` rule, spending one request of the credential's bucket (`McpRateLimiter`, the filter's), with the scope `tinfirm:write` (else `403 INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE`, a problem, not JSON-RPC) and, from the Outlook add-in's `Origin` under a licence without `OUTLOOK_ADDIN`, `403 EDITION_REQUIRED`. `StagedFiles.stage` holds it for the caller, checked and scanned as an upload is (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, `413 ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE`, `409 TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS` past a conversation's limits for the files the person holds, `422 CREDENTIAL_FOUND`, `422 ATTACHMENT_NOT_SCANNABLE`), for an hour; `201` with `id`, `fileName`, `contentType`, `sizeBytes` and `expiresAt`. Logged at INFO with the company, the grant's kind and id, the size and the outcome, never the file's name. Tests: `McpFilesIntegrationTest`, `StagedFileSweepTest`, `theOutlookAddInsUploadsAreRefused`.
- **The Outlook add-in** (ADR-0074). A client of this endpoint served by Tinfirm, as a person's AI client is: its task pane signs in through Office's dialog on `/oauth2/authorize` with PKCE, for the company endpoint's resource and `tinfirm:write`, exchanges the code itself and keeps the refresh token in its `localStorage`; it calls `list_agents`, `start_conversation`, `get_conversation` and `send_message`, and its conversations are the person's (origin `CLIENT`). With **Send its attachments too** (ADR-0084), a start first holds the email's attachments through `/files` (Office's `getAttachmentContentAsync`, Mailbox 1.8, detected; an attached email as its `.eml`; at most 20 files and 25 MB) and names them in `fileIds`; the request names them among the fenced headers, and those left out with why. `GET /api/companies/{companyId}/outlook/manifest` (tag `Outlook`, `VIEW_COMPANY`, the `mcp` module; session only, as the rest of the REST API) answers JSON (`fileName` `tinfirm-outlook-manifest.xml`, `addInId`, `taskPaneUrl`, `manifest`) with its add-in-only XML manifest for the company, which the General section of the company's settings downloads: the task pane `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/outlook/{companyId}`, `ReadItem`, one pinnable button on the read surface (Mailbox 1.5), the icons and the app domain at the public URL, and an add-in id that is `UUID.nameUUIDFromBytes("tinfirm-outlook:{publicUrl}:{companyId}")`. Tests: `OutlookManifestIntegrationTest`, `theOutlookAddInIsABuiltInClient`, the own origin in `McpEndpointIntegrationTest`, the headers in `AuthIntegrationTest`.
- **The Gmail add-on** ([ADR-0083](adr/0083-gmail-add-on.md)). A Google Workspace add-on whose HTTP endpoints the `mcp` module serves (`/api/gmail/**`, §4; a paid feature, whose code is under `ee/` but for the verification of Google's requests and a person's links, §15.24), acting as an AI client does, through the same services, without a bearer token: `GoogleIdTokens` verifies Google's two RS256 ID tokens against Google's keys (`GmailCalls.googleKeys`, kept a day; read again when stale or for an unknown `kid`, one read at a time, at most once a minute whatever asks, so that no request makes Tinfirm call Google more often; `GoogleIdTokensTest`), the issuer `https://accounts.google.com`, `exp` with five minutes' skew, the deployment's token for the endpoint's URL under `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL` with the verified email `TINFIRM_GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, the person's for `TINFIRM_GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`; its `sub` is the Google account. An unlinked account gets a card whose button opens `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/gmail/link/{companyId}#{token}` (`OVERLAY`, `RELOAD` on close); `POST /api/companies/{companyId}/gmail/links/read` and `/confirm` (tag `Gmail`, an active member, session only) read and consume it, `IDENTITY_IN_USE` (409) for another member's account, `GMAIL_LINK_EXPIRED` (410), `GMAIL_LINK_USED` (409). A linked account's request runs as its person (`ActorAuthentication`, the user's tenant context) with `BearerGrant(GMAIL_LINK, linkId, WRITE)`: `AgentReads.list` (usable), `ConversationCommands.startFromClient` (origin `CLIENT`, `TINFIRM_MCP_STARTS_PER_HOUR`, a request id of the click: the start card's own random id, the parameter `card` on each of its starts, which Google's retry repeats and a new card changes; the conversation card's **Send** carries one too), `ConversationReads.get` and `ConversationCommands.post` in the `CLIENT` view. A start reads the one open message through `GmailMessages.message` (`users.messages.get`, `format=full`, the person's OAuth token and `X-Goog-Gmail-Access-Token`) outside any transaction, and builds the request as the Outlook pane does (`GmailEmails`: the fenced headers and plain-text body, the HTML part's text when there is no plain one, at most 100,000 characters; the fence's tag derived from the click's request id, so a retry is the same request). With **Send its attachments too** (ADR-0084), it reads each attachment (`GmailMessages.attachment`, `users.messages.attachments.get`; not the parts with a `Content-ID` shown inline), holds it as the person through `StagedFiles.stage` (at most 20 files and 25 MB) and starts with their ids; the request names them, and those left out with why. **Use as reply** (`do=draft`, `conversationId`, `runId`) under an answer of a succeeded turn reads the open message's reply headers (`GmailMessages.messageHeaders`, `format=metadata`), builds the reply (`GmailReplies`: to `Reply-To`, else `From`, else for the person's own email its `To`; `Re:`, `In-Reply-To`, `References`; `multipart/alternative` of the turn's summary as text and simple HTML; RFC 2047 words) and saves it with `GmailMessages.createDraft` (`users.drafts.create` in the thread, under `gmail.addons.current.action.compose`); the card links to the draft (`https://mail.google.com/mail/?authuser={email}#drafts?compose={messageId}`). Tinfirm never sends it. Its cards (`GmailCards`) answer a trigger with `{"action":{"navigations":[{"pushCard":…}]}}` and a button with `updateCard`; their buttons call `/act` with the parameter `do` (`start`, `refresh`, `send`, `draft`, `new`, `signout`). `GET /api/companies/{companyId}/gmail/deployment` (`VIEW_COMPANY`) answers the deployment's JSON (`fileName` `tinfirm-gmail-deployment.json`, `deployment`, `configured`); `GET /api/me/gmail/links` the caller's links in every company of theirs; `DELETE /api/companies/{companyId}/gmail/links/{linkId}` one of the caller's own (`204`). The deployment's scopes are `gmail.addons.execute`, `gmail.addons.current.message.readonly`, `gmail.addons.current.action.compose` and `userinfo.email`; its links may open the public URL and `https://mail.google.com/`. Tests: `GmailAddOnIntegrationTest` (with `FakeGoogle`), `GmailEmailsTest`, `GmailRepliesTest`.
- **Consent.** Asked on every authorization, per company: a consent is never remembered for a later one, so a consent for company A never widens one for company B, and a person who allows less gets less. The consent page (`/authorize`) shows the client's name and the host of its metadata document (or that it is built in), the company, and the levels Read, Read and write, Read, write and decide. All three are offered whatever the client asked for: an authorization request that names any `tinfirm:*` scope is treated as a request for all three (`ScopeWideningRequestConverter`, which keeps the scopes the client sent as `tinfirm_requested_scope`), and any other scope stays `invalid_scope`. The requested level is preselected (`tinfirm:write` when the client asked for deciding); deciding is never preselected. `GET /api/me/oauth/consent` gives it the pending request of the logged-in user only: `requested`, the scopes the client sent, and `offered`, the three. The code and the tokens carry the scopes the person chose, and the token response's `scope` names them. The grant records the company it was consented for.
- **Connected apps.** `GET /api/me/oauth/grants` and `DELETE /api/me/oauth/grants/{grantId}` (tag `OAuth`). Revoking a grant removes its authorizations, with their tokens, and the user's stored consent of the client when no other company's grant of it remains; idempotent.
- **Personal access tokens.** `GET` and `POST /api/me/tokens`, `DELETE /api/me/tokens/{tokenId}` (tag `Tokens`), session-only like the rest of the API. A user sees and revokes only their own; another user's token is 404. A token is the user's, not a company's: it works at every company where the user is a member. Tokens are revoked, never deleted.
- **Signing key.** An instance key of the vault, `oauth-signing` (§9). The JWK set (`/oauth2/jwks`) publishes its public half.
- **Separation of duties.** `tinfirm:decide` is granted only by a person: on the consent page, or on a personal token. The operations guide tells people never to allow-list `decide`, `start_conversation`, `send_message`, `cancel_turn`, `end_conversation` or any other write tool (`test_system` among them) in their client, and to give anything unattended `tinfirm:read`.
- **Tests.**
  - The authorization server: the whole flow against the running server with a client metadata document served by a stub (PKCE, consent, code exchange, refresh with rotation, revocation), the audience across two companies, a deactivated user, the login round trip (local and OIDC), consent refused to a non-member, `/oauth2/register` 404, a metadata document at a private address refused by the outbound guard, the loopback-port rule.
  - The endpoint through the MCP Java SDK's client (`McpConversationToolsIntegrationTest`) with a personal access token and with an OAuth access token: every tool; `tools/list` (names, annotations, no `companyId`); `initialize` with the notice; a session cookie cannot reach the MCP chain and a bearer token cannot reach the REST API; a token of company A refused at company B; the body cap, the batch, `GET` and `DELETE` 405, `Origin` 403. The tools of §15.20 are `McpSetupToolsIntegrationTest`'s. The HTTP rules (`initialize` naming the server, the body cap, the batch, the 405s, `Origin`) are `McpEndpointIntegrationTest`'s, the separation of the session cookie and the bearer token `PersonalAccessTokenIntegrationTest`'s.
  - The tenant test of the `mcp` module (§10), over every read and write tool.
  - Every write tool once with a `tinfirm:write` credential; with `tinfirm:read` each write tool gets `403` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="insufficient_scope", scope="tinfirm:write"` and the metadata, and with `tinfirm:write` `decide` gets it with `scope="tinfirm:decide"`.
  - The events of a conversation started and a decision decided through a token carry `tokenId`, through a grant `grantId`, and the chain verifies.
  - The rate limit with a small limit (`McpRateLimitIntegrationTest`): 429 with `Retry-After` once the bucket is empty, another token unaffected, recovery after `Retry-After`; the filter's rules in a unit test (`McpRequestFilterTest`).

### 14.12 Companies' own identity providers

Removed by [ADR-0075](adr/0075-companies-and-sign-in-from-the-deployment.md) on 2026-10-19: a company's OWNER no longer configures an OIDC provider in the app. Companies and their sign-in are declared in the deployment's configuration: the declared companies, their owners and a provider's company, role and email domains are in §4 and §5, the company key and the migration that dropped the providers' tables, client secrets and `app_user.provisioned_by_company_id` (`V052__companies_from_the_deployment.sql`) in §7, the audit in §8 and §14.3. The guide for operators is [operations/companies-and-sign-in.md](operations/companies-and-sign-in.md). The rules of ADR-0045's "Companies' own identity providers" (the `/api/companies/{companyId}/identity-provider` API, `MANAGE_IDENTITY_PROVIDER`, the registrations `company-{companyId}`, the guarded discovery, the email domains unique across the instance and the login page's lookup) bind no code; they are in [archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md#1412-companies-own-identity-providers).

- **Tests.** `DeclaredCompaniesIntegrationTest`: a declared company created at the start with its key and its owner, and nothing changed by the next start; adopted by id and by name, keeping its OWNER; the start refused on an unknown id, an id of another key or two companies without a key of the same name; the declared owner made OWNER at their first sign-in, or promoted from MEMBER, and never twice; an OIDC user with the owner's email not made OWNER at the start, nor by a sign-in through another company's provider or a provider of none, only through the company's own. `DeclaredCompaniesPropertiesTest`: the declarations bound from the environment variables, an empty owner as unset, a key or a missing name refused. `OidcLoginIntegrationTest`: a provider bound to a company makes a new user a member with its role; an email outside its domains refused with `EMAIL_DOMAIN_NOT_ALLOWED` and no user created; a known identity not granted a membership again; a provider without a company on an instance with several declared companies grants none; the declared owner of `beta` signing in through `acme`'s provider becomes no owner of `beta`, nor through `beta`'s in a login that asserts another email, and through `beta`'s asserting theirs does. `ExternalProvisioningTest`: a known identity's login names its provider's companies only when it asserts the stored email. A provider of several companies ([ADR-0089](adr/0089-companies-from-the-role-claim.md)): `OidcLoginIntegrationTest.theProviderFillsEachCompanyItsClaimNames` (a person in two companies with two roles from one sign-in, both sign-in pages listing the provider, the departure from the company the claim drops audited with the provider, the return, a claim that names none refused with both memberships kept); `ExternalProvisioningTest.ProviderOfSeveralCompanies` and `SamlProvisioningTest` (a new user joining each named company, a known one joining and leaving, nothing ended on a refusal, an OWNER kept, `APP` once, a provider without an own company); `AuthPropertiesTest` and `ProviderRolesTest` (the settings from the environment and what stops the start). The provider's role ([ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md)): `OidcLoginIntegrationTest` gains a promotion and a demotion at later sign-ins, `409 ROLE_MANAGED` in the app, `ROLE_NOT_MAPPED` for a new user (not created) and a known one (the membership kept), an OWNER made in the app neither changed nor refused after, the seat fallback at a sign-in and the role once a seat is free, and the release of a provider that no longer decides; `MembershipRolesTest` an OWNER and SCIM's membership kept, a non-member granted nothing, the seat fallback's item once per person; `ProviderRolesTest` the highest mapped role, exact values, no OWNER; `AuthPropertiesTest` the settings bound from the environment, a provider without a claim as `APP`, and the start refused on `IDP` without a claim, a map that cannot be applied, and two `IDP` providers of one company; `ExternalProvisioningTest` the rules without an identity provider.

### 14.13 Groups, accounts and the OAuth client

*In part superseded: the groups hold (Groups, below, with the Groups API), with the parts of Web and Tests that name them. Agent kinds and the groups' rights on agents are superseded by §15.5; visibility, deciding and interactive threads by §15.7, and running a trigger now by §15.15; a person's account, the company's account of an MCP connection, shared accounts and Tinfirm's OAuth client by §15.2's systems, with their Sign-in app, §15.3's accounts and credentials and §4's Client ID Metadata Document; the tables by §7. The rules of the design of 2026-10-05 are in [archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md](archive/v2/CONVENTIONS.md#1413-groups-accounts-and-the-oauth-client).*

The design is [ADR-0049](adr/0049-agent-kinds-groups-and-personal-accounts.md). The guides are those of the redesign: members' own accounts, the systems' credentials and service accounts are [operations/accounts.md](operations/accounts.md), the registration of systems and their Sign-in app [operations/systems.md](operations/systems.md), and MCP servers [operations/mcp-servers.md](operations/mcp-servers.md). The groups' tables, `user_group` and `user_group_member`, are `V001__baseline.sql`'s, and the instance-level `oauth_authorization_request` is §7's.

- **Groups.** `user_group` (`id`, `company_id`, `name`, `description`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; the API calls it a group, and `group` is a reserved word in SQL) and `user_group_member` (`company_id`, `group_id`, `user_id`; primary key group and user; the composite foreign key to `company_membership` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so leaving the company leaves every group). They live in the `company` module (`GroupStore`, `GroupService`, `GroupsController`). A name is 1 to 100 characters, trimmed, unique per company ignoring case (`409 DUPLICATE_GROUP_NAME`); a description up to 500; a group has at most 500 members.

  | Operation | Permission | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET …/groups` (`listGroups`) | `VIEW_GROUPS` (every role) | Names, descriptions and member counts, by name; cursor and `limit` |
  | `GET …/groups/{groupId}` (`getGroup`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` (OWNER, ADMIN) | One group with its members (`userId`, `email`, `displayName`) |
  | `GET …/groups/{groupId}/usage` (`getGroupUsage`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` | What its members may use through it (`GroupUsage`): the live agents shared with it, by name; its grants as `listGrants` shows them, by system and credential name; the service accounts it may use, by name |
  | `POST …/groups` (`createGroup`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` | Creates it. `201` |
  | `PUT …/groups/{groupId}` (`replaceGroup`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` | Replaces name, description and members as a whole; an absent or blank description clears it |
  | `PUT …/groups/{groupId}/members/{userId}` (`addGroupMember`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` | Adds one member and touches no other; one already in the group changes nothing. Answers the group with every member, as a replace does |
  | `DELETE …/groups/{groupId}/members/{userId}` (`removeGroupMember`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` | Takes one member out and touches no other; a user who is not in the group, one who left the company included, changes nothing. Answers the group with every member |
  | `DELETE …/groups/{groupId}` (`deleteGroup`) | `MANAGE_GROUPS` | Deletes it, after the modules that share agents, skills, service accounts, grants or triggers with it removed them (`GroupDependents.groupDeleting`). `204` |

  A member named twice counts once; a user who is not a member of the company (one who left, or a service account's id) is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, and so is a 501st member, on a create, a replace or an add; a suspended member is still a member. The audit is `group.created`, `group.updated` (what changed; a request that changes nothing is not audited) and `group.deleted` (§14.3). A change of members is announced as `GroupEvents.GroupMembersChanged` with the users added and removed, whichever request made it.

  A group's usage (`getGroupUsage`) is told by the modules that give groups something, through the port `company.GroupUses` (`of(access, groupId)`), as `GroupDependents` is: the agent module its live agents shared with the group (`agent_share`), the account module its grants (`account_grant`, with the names `listGrants` shows: the system's, the credential's and the group's), the principal module the service accounts it may use (`service_identity_user`). Each answers in the caller's transaction and tenant context (one query for the agents and one for the service accounts, by the tables' `(company_id, group_id)` indexes; one for the grants, one each for their systems' and groups' names, and one per credential for its name), and only to an access that sees its own list in full (`MANAGE_AGENTS`, `MANAGE_GRANTS`, `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES`; each a part of OWNER's, ADMIN's and break-glass's, as `MANAGE_GROUPS` is), else nothing, so the read shows nothing those lists hide (`GroupUsageTest`). The Groups tab reads it for each group, six at a time, instead of the company's agents (each read again for its shares), grants and service accounts.

  One member at a time (`addGroupMember`, `removeGroupMember`) keeps a change another admin makes to the same group at the same moment, which reading the group and replacing it overwrites: each locks the group's row as a replace does (so a replace and they follow one another, and the cap counts the members under the lock), then inserts or deletes that member's row alone (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, `DELETE`); the group's `updated_at` moves, and the audit and the announcement are a replace's (`GroupService.recordUpdate`). Both are idempotent and answer `200` with the group, not `204`: the answer carries every member, those another admin added or took out meanwhile included, so that the page that shows the group needs no second read, and a repeated request gets the same answer. A user who leaves the company between the check and the insert is refused as a non-member (the membership's foreign key, `user_group_member_membership_fk`). The web app adds and takes out members this way (a member's groups on Members, and Save in a group's dialog, which then replaces the group only when its name or description changed, with the members as the last answer gave them).

- **Web** (§11). Groups are a tab of Members (`/c/:companyId/members?tab=groups`; `/c/:companyId/groups` redirects there), and "Accounts" (`/c/:companyId/accounts`) follows Triggers (§15.3).

- **Tests.** `GroupIntegrationTest` (owners, admins and members on the groups API, paging, one member added or taken out by a replace's rules, a group's usage), `GroupMembersTest` (one member at a time: idempotent and announced once, admins changing different members at the same moment all keep their change, a user who leaves while being added, no 501st member), `GroupUsageTest` (a group's usage: each module's part, and nothing to an access that does not see its list), `GroupRowLevelSecurityTest` (groups and their members hidden across companies), `OwnedSecretIntegrationTest`, the support class `OAuthStubs` and `TenantIsolationTest` (the groups' paths).

## 15. The redesign's domain

The design is [PLATFORM.md](PLATFORM.md) and [ADR-0058](adr/0058-authority-from-the-audience.md); each package's exact contract is its own document, and this section holds the rules the code follows, package by package: §15.1 to §15.3 are package 1 ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-1-contract.md)), §15.4 is package 2 ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-2-contract.md), with what its build settled in the contract's §13), §15.5 is package 3's first part, 3a ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-3-contract.md), with the decisions of its §11), §15.6 its second, 3b (the same contract), the tools of systems in §15.2 its part 3d (the same contract, §5.4 and open point 5), and §15.7 its part 3c, conversations (the same contract), built in three parts: part 1, the conversation core; part 2, decisions, the tools of a run, parked calls and the inbox; part 3, files, outputs and change requests. §15.8 and §15.9 are not used: the package 4 and 5 contracts named them for their rules, which are §15.15 to §15.17, and the subsections after them keep their numbers, which the code and the migrations cite. §15.10 to §15.13 are package 6 ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-6-contract.md), with the decisions of its §11): §15.10 the MCP endpoint on conversations, §15.11 the audit, retention, holds and erasure, §15.12 spend and budget alerts, §15.13 kill switches, suspension, break-glass's view and a member who leaves. §15.14 is package R, the replay safety of tool calls and idempotent submissions ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-r-contract.md)). §15.15 is package 4, triggers, polls, schedules and the write-back ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-4-contract.md)). §15.16 is package 5's first part, 5a: chat systems, linking chat users to members, and direct messages as personal conversations ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-5-contract.md), with the decisions of its §11); §15.17 its second, 5b: channel triggers, the chat outbox and the generic webhook (the same contract). §15.18 holds agent memory, §15.19 skills (ADR-0064), §15.20 the MCP endpoint on triggers, systems, webhooks and spend, §15.21 the error reports (ADR-0070), §15.22 automatic retries ([ADR-0073](adr/0073-automatic-retries.md)), §15.23 the credentials a system holds and lends to groups and to service accounts ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md), `V049`, `V050`), and §15.24 the licence: its keys, editions and seats, and the role VIEWER ([ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md), `V053`), §15.25 SCIM provisioning (ADR-0087), §15.26 SAML sign-in (ADR-0086) and §15.27 session bundles (ADR-0097). ADR-0072 also changed the accounts and the binding rule (§15.3), service accounts (§15.1), a system's test and catalogue (§15.2), a conversation's bindings (§15.7), a leaver's checklist (§15.13), a trigger's table (§15.15) and a chat system's bot (§15.16), each where it says so. A person's message to a running turn ([ADR-0060](adr/0060-steering-a-running-turn.md), steering, `V036`) is in §15.6 (the port and the frame) and §15.7 (messages). ADR-0072 calls a service identity a **service account**: this section says so in prose, and keeps `serviceIdentity`, `/service-identities` and the kind `SERVICE` where it names the API, the code or the tables. The permissions are in §6, the tables in §7, the audit actions in §8, the managed, owned and internal secrets in §9, the modules, their APIs and events in §10. The invariants I1 to I10 are package 1's §4 (ADR-0072 restates I2, I5 and I9: §15.3, §15.16), M1 to M7 package 2's §4; each has a test. Package 3's contract numbers its invariants I1 to I19 as well: §15.5 names them "the contract's I4" and so on. The reviews' ids follow their records: H1 to H4, M1 to M18, L1 to L35, I1 to I10 and D1 to D9 of [the security review of 2026-10-27](reviews/2026-10-27-security-review.md) are cited with that review named, or bare where the text names it nearby; "the owner's decision Q1" to "Q8" are always the decisions of its plan ([§1](reviews/2026-10-27-security-review-plan.md#1-decisions-taken-by-the-owner-on-2026-10-27)), never the threat model's own open items; and "the security review, row N" is the table of the [redesign record](reviews/2026-10-12-platform-redesign.md), the only review with numbered rows.

### 15.1 Principals

Whom work acts for (module `principal`): a member of the company, or a service account (a service identity in the API and the code) that admins manage. A member's own accounts hang from their principal; a service account holds none of its own and acts with the credentials systems lend it (ADR-0072, §15.23). From package 2 each principal has its model access.

- **A member's principal** lives with the membership (`company.MemberPrincipals`): created in the transaction that adds the member (`principal.created`, also for the bootstrap's owner and a provisioning login), and `LEFT` in the transaction that ends the membership, before the membership row goes (`principal.left`, I8). The member's accounts, every secret they own in the company and their personal MCP servers go in that transaction, each audited with `cause` `principal.left` (`PrincipalDependents.memberLeaving`, implemented by the account, system, model, runner, agent, trigger and skill modules; a leaver's skills pass to the admins, §15.19). A `LEFT` principal is kept, so that what it did keeps naming who acted; a member who rejoins gets a new one, and nothing of before comes back.
- **A service account** (`principal.kind` `SERVICE`) never logs in and acts only through the credentials systems lend it, at most one per system (§15.23). Its name is 1 to 100 characters, unique in the company ignoring case (`409 DUPLICATE_SERVICE_IDENTITY_NAME`), its description at most 500. It is `ACTIVE` or `SUSPENDED`; suspending is a kill switch (I7): what it borrows acts for nothing through it (`Accounts.check` stops on its holder), while the groups' grants of the same credentials go on, and setting it as a chat system's bot is `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED`, until it is resumed. While suspended, the API shows when (`suspendedAt`), and to OWNER and ADMIN the reason typed (`suspensionReason`, as the kill switches do; never in the audit chain). Deleting it is refused `409 SERVICE_IDENTITY_IN_USE` while it borrows a chat system's bot (§15.16) or live triggers use it (§15.15); otherwise it withdraws what it borrows, each lending audited `credential.withdrawn` with `cause` `principal.deleted`, and the credentials stay with their systems (`PrincipalDependents.principalDeleting`, `AccountDependents`); then who may use it goes, and `principal.deleted` records its name.

  | Endpoint (tag `ServiceIdentities`) | Permission | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /service-identities`, `GET /service-identities/{id}` | `VIEW_SERVICE_IDENTITIES` (every member) | Names, descriptions, status and `usableByMe`, by name |
  | `POST /service-identities`, `PATCH …/{id}`, `DELETE …/{id}` | `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES` (OWNER, ADMIN) | Create; rename and describe (a blank description clears it); delete |
  | `PUT …/{id}/users` | `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES` | The groups that may use it, as a whole (at most 100, each the company's: else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`) |
  | `POST …/{id}/suspend`, `…/resume` | `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES` | The kill switch; idempotent |
  | `GET …/{id}/credentials` (tag `Credentials`) | `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES` | What it borrows, by system name: each credential with its status, the access it is lent with, whether it is a chat system's bot and the live triggers that act with it through the service account. Read-only: credentials are lent on the system's page (§15.23) |

- **Who may use a service account** (name it, with the credential it borrows, in a trigger's table and, from package 2, its budget for what one sets up): OWNER every one; anyone else only through a group in `service_identity_user`, ADMIN included. Deleting a group first takes it from every service account (`service_identity.users_changed` with `cause` `group.deleted`). `Principals.mayUse` and `usableServiceIdentities` answer it for the later packages.
- **Events** (`PrincipalEvents`): `PrincipalCreated`, `PrincipalSuspended`, `PrincipalResumed`, `PrincipalLeft`, `ServiceIdentityUsersChanged`; the company module's `GroupMembersChanged`.

### 15.2 Systems

An external service registered once (module `system`), with one OAuth app (the web app's **Sign-in app**). A company system holds the company's credentials on it (ADR-0072, §15.23), which the account module keeps; it acts with none by itself. What a system offers follows from its kind (the contract's §3.3); `BUZZ` is `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`; `WEBHOOK` is registered from package 5b (§15.17); `SLACK` and `TEAMS` are registered from package 5a, with their settings and fixed base URLs (§15.16). From package 3d the module also executes the systems' tools and opens change requests, with the credential of a conversation's account (Tools, below).

- **Company systems and personal MCP servers.** OWNER and ADMIN register, change and remove company systems (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`). Any member registers an `MCP` server as their own (`personal`, I6: any other kind is `400 PERSONAL_SYSTEM`), which only they see, change and use: to everyone else, admins included, it is `404`. The company switches personal servers off with `PUT /system-settings` (on by default): existing ones stay but bind for nobody, and registering one is `409 PERSONAL_SYSTEMS_OFF`. A member's personal servers go when they leave (I8).
- **Names, handles, base URLs.** A name is 1 to 100 characters, unique ignoring case (`409 DUPLICATE_SYSTEM_NAME`). The handle prefixes the system's tools in a run (`{handle}_{tool}`), a lower-case letter and at most 19 lower-case letters, digits and underscores, unique in the company. Nobody chooses it: the registration makes it from the name, for every kind alike (accents dropped, lower case, `_` for every run of other characters, `s_` in front of one that does not start with a letter; cut to 17 characters when longer than 20, then `_2`, `_3`… while taken, the cut shortened by a longer suffix's extra characters from `_100` on, so that every handle fits 20 characters). Registrations of a company pick their handles one at a time, under the advisory lock `system_handle:{companyId}` (`SystemStore.lockHandles`, the lock order of §10), so that two never pick one and `external_system_handle_uq` stays only as the last guard. The handle never changes: it names the system's tools in conversations already open and in agents' instructions. A `handle` in a registration or a change is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, never ignored, so that no client believes it chose or renamed one; systems registered before keep theirs, typed or not. Every kind but `GIT` and `WEBHOOK` has a base URL that passes the outbound guard (`400 INVALID_BASE_URL`; Jira Cloud's is the site, without a path). The kind and the base URL never change: a token is sent only where it was connected. The one exception is an MCP server's endpoint ([ADR-0079](adr/0079-an-mcp-servers-endpoint-changes.md)): a change takes every account and credential on the server with it (`account.disconnected` with the cause `system.moved`, their grants and lendings, the pending sign-ins), its app and its last test, and the server is `NEW` again; a server without accounts is reached at the new endpoint first; the catalogue, the agents' tools and the triggers stay. Another kind's `baseUrl` is `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`.
- **The outbound client** (`OutboundHttp`): every call to a system goes through it and its guard (§14.4): https unless the operator allows http, no redirects, only to the origin of the base URL a call names. A personal server's calls never reach loopback, link-local or private addresses, whatever `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOW_PRIVATE_ADDRESSES` says (the security review, row 12). No call runs inside a database transaction (§10).
- **The app.** One OAuth client per system, `PUT /systems/{id}/app` (OWNER, ADMIN; a personal server's owner): a tracker's endpoints are a fixed profile per kind, an MCP server's are discovered (RFC 9728, RFC 8414) between the transaction that authorises the caller and the one that stores the app. Without a client id an MCP server's app uses Tinfirm's Client ID Metadata Document where the server takes one, else Tinfirm registers itself (RFC 7591, audited `registered`); a server that needs no token, or whose discovery fails, is `409 OAUTH_NOT_AVAILABLE`. Registering an MCP server sets its app up the same way, after the transaction that registered it (audited `automatic`), so that nobody sets the app before the first sign-in; a server that needs no token, whose discovery or registration fails, or that needs a client registered by hand is registered without an app, and `PUT` without a client id discovers again and says why. The callback to register is `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/api/oauth/callback`, shown to who manages the system as the system's `oauthCallbackUrl` for every kind whose accounts may sign in with OAuth, before its app is set (the admin registers the app at the system with it first) as after, and as the app's `callbackUrl` once it has one. The client secret is write-only: a managed company secret (`SYSTEM_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`), or one the owner owns for a personal server (§9), revealed only for one OAuth request (`system-app:{systemId}`); absent, it is kept while the client id stays, and dropped for a new client id.

  | Kind | Authorization endpoint | Token endpoint | Revocation | Default scopes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `JIRA_DC` | `{base}/rest/oauth2/latest/authorize` | `{base}/rest/oauth2/latest/token` | none | `WRITE` |
  | `JIRA_CLOUD` | `https://auth.atlassian.com/authorize` (`audience=api.atlassian.com`, `prompt=consent`) | `https://auth.atlassian.com/oauth/token` | none | `read:jira-work read:jira-user write:jira-work offline_access read:me` |
  | `GITHUB` | `{web}/login/oauth/authorize` | `{web}/login/oauth/access_token` | none | `repo` |
  | `GITLAB` | `{base}/oauth/authorize` | `{base}/oauth/token` | `{base}/oauth/revoke` | `api` |
  | `MCP` | discovered | discovered | discovered, if any | the app's, else the server's `scopes_supported`; the endpoint as `resource` (RFC 8707) |

- **Removing the app.** `DELETE /systems/{id}/app` (OWNER, ADMIN; a personal server's owner; `404` when it has none) clears the client, its scopes and endpoints and deletes its client secret, audited as `system.app_changed` with `removed`. Accounts on the system stay as they are: none signs in with OAuth any more, and an OAuth account whose grant expires cannot be refreshed and turns `RECONNECT` then (§15.3).
- **Test.** `POST /systems/{id}/test` (as for changing it) reaches the system with an account, its "who am I", else without a credential (`SystemService.reach`, ADR-0072 decision 10): the credential named by the optional query parameter `credentialId` (one of the system's credentials, OWNER and ADMIN; another id is `404`); without it, the caller's own `OK` account, else the system's only enabled `OK` credential (`SystemAccounts.onlyUsableCredential`, a chat system's bot left out), else none. Each is revealed with the purpose `system-test:{systemId}`. A credential named that is disabled or not `OK` is not revealed and nothing is sent: the answer says so (`ok` false, with its `credentialId` and `credentialName`), and the system's status and last test stay. The answer says what reached the system (`withAccount`, `credentialId` and `credentialName` for a credential, null for the caller's own account, `login`) and whether the system refused the credential (`credentialRequired`). The outcome becomes the system's `status` (`OK`, `ERROR` with `lastError`). The calls run between the transaction that authorises the caller and the one that stores the status. That transaction also keeps the test as the system's last test (V043, `ExternalSystem.lastTest`; the next test replaces it, a catalogue's refresh does not): when it ended, whether it passed and why not (`error`, at most 500 characters), whether it reached the system with an account (`withAccount`: the tester's own, a credential; a chat system's bot), who ran it (`byPrincipalId`, the caller's member principal; none for an instance admin who is not a member) and the account it used (`account_id`: the one `SystemAccounts.ownCredential` or `revealCredential` revealed with its credential, so an account replaced while the test runs is not named, and only while it still exists; a chat system's bot is never named). Every member who sees the system reads when and how its last test ended, as they read its status; who ran it (`byPrincipalId`, `byName`) and the system's credential it used (`credentialId`, `credentialName` as it is named now; never the tester's own account, which nobody else is shown) only whoever manages it, as for its app (§6). The name is never stored: it is read when shown (`Principals.displayNames`, all of a page's at once), so a deleted user reads as one; a principal or an account that goes leaves the test without it (`ON DELETE SET NULL`), and the erasure of a member who left forgets them as the tester (`Systems.forgetTester`, §15.11). A test is not audited.
- **No account** ([ADR-0071](adr/0071-mcp-servers-without-accounts.md), `V048`). `accountsRequired` (`external_system.accounts_required`), true unless whoever manages an `MCP` server set it false with `PATCH /systems/{id}` (as for changing it; any other kind is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`): runs then call the server without a credential, a conversation binds it without an account (`OPEN`, §15.3, §15.7) and a trigger needs no row for it (§15.15). Set false, the server is reached first without a credential, `initialize` then `tools/list`, outside any transaction, between the transaction that authorises the caller and the one that stores the change (`SystemService.update`); a server that does not answer both is `400 CREDENTIAL_REQUIRED` with its answer, and nothing changes; a paused system is `409 SYSTEM_PAUSED`, as for its test. The storing transaction, the system locked, checks again that a server it sets to need no account was reached: one another change set to need accounts since the first read is reached again before this change is stored; and that it is not paused: one an admin paused while it was reached is `409 SYSTEM_PAUSED`, and nothing changes. Set true again, nothing is checked. Audited as `system.changed` with `accountsRequired`, and announced as `SystemEvents.AccountsRequiredChanged`, on which the conversation module looks again at the bindings on the system (§15.7). The setting is explicit, never inferred from the discovery's `NONE`: a server may answer `initialize` and ask for a token on a call. An account on it stays, and binds nothing new.
- **Repositories** of `GITHUB`, `GITLAB` and `GIT` systems, added by URL by admins (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`): an https URL without credentials, query or fragment, at most 500 characters, on the system's host for GitHub (`github.com` for `api.github.com`) and GitLab, naming `owner/repo` or a project path; unique per system (`409 DUPLICATE_REPOSITORY`). Every member lists them.
- **Catalogues.** A tracker's catalogue is fixed, in code (`FixedTools`), with its effects. An MCP server's is read by `POST /systems/{id}/tools/refresh` (admins; a personal server's owner), with the account a test takes (the credential named by `credentialId`, else the caller's own `OK` account, else the system's only enabled `OK` credential; revealed with the purpose `system-tools:{systemId}`), else without a credential, between the transaction that authorises the caller and the one that stores what changed; the answer says what read it (`withAccount`, `credentialId`, `credentialName`). Every tool is `UNKNOWN` until an admin (the owner, for a personal server) confirms it `READ` or `WRITE` (`PATCH …/tools/{name}`, which also switches a tool off), and **`UNKNOWN` counts as `WRITE` everywhere**. A refresh keeps confirmed effects unless a tool's description, schema or hints changed, which drops the confirmation (the security review, row 13); the server's `readOnlyHint` and `destructiveHint` only suggest an effect. A catalogue that cannot be read keeps the stored one and sets the system's `ERROR`, but for a server that asks for a token when none was sent: it was reached, as a test without an account finds, so the system stays `OK` and the answer says `credentialRequired` with `withAccount` false, to add a credential (or connect one's own account) and refresh again (`CatalogueService.store`). A credential named that is disabled or not `OK` reads nothing and changes nothing.
- **Tools** (package 3d; the package 3 contract, §5.4 and open point 5; `SystemTools`). The module executes a tracker's fixed tools and an MCP server's own: step 6 of the contract's §5.4, the system's own answer for the bound account. Which tools a conversation may call, under which mode and with which account, is the caller's (package 3c): it reveals the account's credential (`Accounts.reveal`, §15.3) and hands it over as a `CallCredential`, the token, whether it is an OAuth access token, and `apiBaseUrl`; its `toString` hides the token. `call(companyId, systemId, credential, tool, arguments, commentLabel)` names the tool as its system does (`get`, an MCP server's own name), not as a run lists it. It runs outside any transaction (`IllegalStateException` inside one, §10): the system's row and an MCP tool's catalogue entry are read in a short transaction of their own, in the caller's tenant context; the call then runs on a virtual thread of its own, which the caller waits for at most `tinfirm.connection.tool-timeout` (`TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT`, `PT60S`, §4); past it the thread is interrupted and the call is `TOOL_FAILED`. Requests go only to the system's origin, through the outbound client and its guard. A stored token is sent as the system's scheme says (Jira Data Center's and GitHub's as a bearer token, GitLab's in `PRIVATE-TOKEN`, Jira Cloud's `email:api-token` as Basic authentication at the site, an MCP server's in its `authHeader`); an OAuth access token always as `Authorization: Bearer`. A Jira Cloud OAuth grant goes only to Atlassian's gateway for the site: its `apiBaseUrl` must be `https://api.atlassian.com/ex/jira/{cloudId}` (a cloud id of 1 to 64 letters, digits and hyphens), else the call is `Reconnect` and nothing is sent. Every other kind ignores `apiBaseUrl`. The token is taken out of every answer, its texts, the failure's message and its structured content (as `***`, for a token of at least 8 characters), and is never logged: a failure inside Tinfirm logs the exception's class only. `SystemTools` stores and audits nothing.
- **The fixed catalogues** (`FixedTools`). Tinfirm's own tools of each tracker, each with its effect, the schema of its arguments as the runtimes see it, and a description that names the key format or the query language:

  | Kind | `READ` | `WRITE` |
  |---|---|---|
  | `JIRA_DC`, `JIRA_CLOUD` | `get`, `search` | `comment`, `transition` |
  | `GITHUB`, `GITLAB` | `get`, `search` | `comment`, `label`, `close`, `open_change_request` |
  | `SLACK` (package 5b, §15.17) | `history` | none |

  `open_change_request` is in the catalogue only to carry a mode on the agent: the server opens a change request after a turn that pushed, under that tool's mode, never the agent (open point 5), and the prompt's `# Repositories` tells the agent so (§15.7). `FixedTools.calledByAgent` is false for it alone, so no run lists it, and `check` and `call` refuse it with `UNKNOWN_TOOL`. Jira's labels are not written, and a Jira issue is closed by a transition. The catalogues are code: package 3d adds no migration (§7).
- **Arguments** (`SystemTools.check`, `ToolArguments`). `check(kind, tool, arguments)` calls nothing and runs anywhere, before a call runs or waits for an approval; `call` checks again. The arguments are a JSON object of at most 64 KiB, else `INVALID_REQUEST`. An MCP tool's pass unchanged: the server validates them against its own schema. A fixed tool's schema says `additionalProperties: false`; the check keeps the schema's fields, each stripped, and leaves any other out:

  | Field | Rule |
  |---|---|
  | `key` | 1 to 255 characters without whitespace, in the system's format: any key on Jira, `owner/repo#number` on GitHub, `group/project#iid` (an issue) or `group/project!iid` (a merge request) on GitLab |
  | `query` | 1 to 4000 characters |
  | `limit` | A whole number, clamped to 1 to 50; 20 without one |
  | `body` | 1 to 30 000 characters of Markdown |
  | `transition` | 1 to 255 characters |
  | `add`, `remove` (`label`) | At most 20 labels per list, each 1 to 100 characters and counted once; none both added and removed; at least one label |

  A tool the kind has no fixed one of, and `open_change_request`, is `UNKNOWN_TOOL`; arguments that do not fit are `INVALID_REQUEST`, with a message for the agent. `ToolCheck.Valid` carries the normalized arguments, which the call runs with now or once a person approved it, and the call in words (`comment on SHOP-241`), which an approval shows; an MCP tool's has none.
- **Results** (`ToolCallResult`: an MCP `tools/call` result, as the runner protocol's `run.request.ack` carries it). A fixed tool answers Markdown, and the same as `structuredContent`. Texts the system keeps in its markup are read as Markdown: wiki markup on Jira Data Center (`JiraMarkup`), the document format on Jira Cloud (`Adf`); GitHub's and GitLab's Markdown stays as it is.

  | Tool | Does and answers |
  |---|---|
  | `get` | The subject (key, title, status or state, type, labels, assignees, author, times, link, description) with its newest 20 comments, oldest first, each a quote under its author and time |
  | `search` | At most `limit` subjects, one line each (key, title, status or state, labels, assignees), without descriptions or comments: JQL on Jira (Cloud's `search/jql`), GitHub's issue search syntax, GitLab's search terms over its issues and merge requests, newest change first |
  | `comment` | Writes the Markdown converted to the system's markup (wiki markup on Jira Data Center, the document format on Jira Cloud), with the label the caller chose in front, bold in that markup (`commentLabel`, e.g. `Analyst (Tinfirm agent):`; null for none); answers the comment's id and link |
  | `transition` | Applies the transition the issue's status offers whose name matches, ignoring case, else the one that leads to a status of that name; when none does, a result with `isError` that lists those it offers |
  | `label` | One add of every label (GitHub creates unknown ones), then one removal per label; answers what was added, removed, not on the subject and failed (`{key, added, removed, notOn, failed}`) |
  | `close` | Closes the issue, pull request or merge request (a GitHub issue as completed) |

  An MCP server's tool answers as the server sent it: its text blocks, any other block as a text that names its type, its structured content and its error flag. A write says what it wrote (`ToolOutcome.Written`: a comment with its id, a transition, labels, a close), for package 3c's `SystemWritten`; a read, a refused write and an MCP tool say nothing.
- **Errors** (`ToolOutcome`): values, never exceptions, never a token. A `Done` is meant for the ack `DONE`, a `Refused` for `REJECTED` with its code and message.

  | What happened | Outcome |
  |---|---|
  | A `401`; an MCP server's `403` to `initialize`; a tracker without a token; a Jira Cloud token that is not `email:api-token`, or an OAuth grant without its gateway | `Reconnect`, with the system's answer: the account must be connected again, which the caller decides. Nothing was written: a `label` call that wrote already answers a result with `isError` instead |
  | A `403` (`CallResult.Kind.FORBIDDEN`: the account may not do this; on GitHub, one with its rate-limit headers is `RATE_LIMITED`), and an MCP server's `403` to `tools/call`; a `404`; a `429` (`RATE_LIMITED`, with `retryAfter`); a `5xx`, or no answer; a `400` or `422`; an MCP server's JSON-RPC error | `Done` with a result with `isError`: the tool, the system's answer and a hint of what to do. `Done.failure` keeps the kind |
  | No such system; a tool the kind does not have, `open_change_request`, an MCP tool that is not in the catalogue or is switched off | `Refused(UNKNOWN_TOOL)` |
  | Arguments that do not fit | `Refused(INVALID_REQUEST)` |
  | A result of more than 800 KiB as JSON, also after a write | `Refused(RESULT_TOO_LARGE)`, asking to narrow the query |
  | The timeout; an error inside Tinfirm | `Refused(TOOL_FAILED)` |
- **Change requests** (`SystemTools.openChangeRequest`, `changeRequestState`, `ChangeRequests`; open point 5). Outside any transaction, and within the tool timeout, as a call; the repository and its system are read in a short transaction of their own. `openChangeRequest(companyId, repositoryId, credential, New(title, head, base, body))` opens a pull request on GitHub, or on GitLab a merge request that keeps its source branch, into the request's `base`, else the repository's `defaultBranch`, else the default branch the code host names. When the code host answers that one is open for the head branch already, it finds that one and answers it with `created` false. A repository of a `GIT` system has none (`INVALID`); an unknown repository is `NOT_FOUND`, a refused credential `UNAUTHORIZED`, a code host that does not let the account open one `FORBIDDEN`, and the timeout `UNAVAILABLE`. `changeRequestState(…, number)` reads a change request by its number (a pull request's, a merge request's iid): its state, `OPEN`, `DRAFT`, `MERGED` or `CLOSED`, and its head commit (`ChangeRequests.Read`; GitHub's `head.sha`, GitLab's `sha`; null when it is not a commit's hex name), which for a merged one is the commit it was merged at. From package 3c's part 3, `changeRequestBody(…, number)` reads its body (GitHub's pull request `body`, GitLab's merge request `description`; empty without one) and `updateChangeRequestBody(…, number, body)` replaces it (`PATCH` on GitHub, `PUT` on GitLab), for the block of related change requests the conversation module keeps up to date (§15.7); outside any transaction and within the tool timeout as well, an unknown repository `NOT_FOUND`, the timeout `UNAVAILABLE`. `Systems.repository(companyId, repositoryId)` finds a repository of any code host of the company by its id.
- **Not built in package 3d**, left to package 3c and built there (§15.7): which tools a run lists, with which account and under which mode, and the audit of a call (`tool.called`, for which `Done.failure` gives `ok` and `code`), by its part 2. Its part 3 built composing a change request's body (the module sends the body it is given), rewriting the block of related change requests, and opening a change request after a turn that pushed (§15.7).
- **Events** (`SystemEvents`): `SystemDeleted`, `SystemToolsChanged`, `RepositoryRemoved`, `PersonalSystemsSwitched`; from package 6 `SystemPaused` and `SystemResumed` (§15.13), from package 5a `ChatTransportChanged` (§15.16), from ADR-0071 `AccountsRequiredChanged`. A removed system first disconnects every account and credential on it, with their grants and lendings (`SystemAccounts.systemDeleting`, `cause` `system.deleted`), then deletes its app's secret, its repositories and tools.
- **Tests.** `SystemConstraintTest` (I6, the kind's base URL, a handle's form, a tool's confirmation and another company's rows, against PostgreSQL), `SystemIntegrationTest` (systems over HTTP: roles, personal MCP servers, the kind and the base URL, the app's secret and the callback to register it with before it is set, a tracker's `pollIntervalSeconds` (30 to 3600, 60 unless set, a tracker's only), the last test kept with who ran it for whoever manages the system and without for a member, the system's credential it used when named by `credentialId`, gone once that credential is removed, and never the tester's own account; an admin without an account reading a token-secured catalogue: `credentialRequired` and the status `OK` without a credential, then the system's only usable credential, one named, and a disabled one sent nowhere; audit and events), `SystemHandleTest` (handles made from names with their suffixes, the chat and webhook kinds alike; a `handle` in a registration or a change refused; a rename keeping the handle; eight registrations at once of names that make one handle, each with its own), `FreeHandleTest` (every suffix within 20 characters), `OutsideTransactionsTest` (§10); from package 3d `FixedToolsTest` (the catalogues, their effects and schemas, `calledByAgent`), `ToolArgumentsTest` (the checks and the call in words), `JiraToolsTest`, `GitHubToolsTest`, `GitLabToolsTest` and `McpToolsTest` (each system's tools against a stub: requests, results, the credential's scheme, the system's refusals, and GitHub's and GitLab's change requests), `SystemToolsLimitsTest` (no call inside a transaction, the timeout, `RESULT_TOO_LARGE`, the token out of the text and the structured content), `AdfTest` and `JiraMarkupTest` (Jira Cloud's document format and Jira's wiki markup to Markdown and back); from package 3c's part 3 `GitLabToolsTest` also reads and replaces a merge request's description, while a pull request's body is read and rewritten in `ChangeRequestIntegrationTest` (§15.7); and `TenantIsolationTest` over every path.

### 15.3 Accounts and grants

A credential on a system and who it is there (module `account`): a member's own **account** ("your account on Jira"), connected on the Accounts page, or one of the company's **credentials**, which a company system holds and lends to groups (grants, below) and to service accounts ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md), `V049`; §15.23 holds the credentials' API, their lending to service accounts and what follows it). Both are `account` rows, so that grants, trigger rows, bindings, refresh and revocation keep one table: a member's names their principal (`principal_kind` `MEMBER`), a credential none (`principal_id` null, `principal_kind` `SYSTEM`) and has a name (`account_holder_ck`). A member holds one account per system (I1). **I2, as ADR-0072 decision 3 states it:** one identity per system belongs to members' own accounts or to the company's credentials, never both; among members' own accounts one per identity (`account_identity_uq`), while two credentials of one system may share one (two tokens of one bot user, of different scope). The database keeps members and credentials apart (`account_ck`, under a transaction-scoped advisory lock per identity). A refusal is `409 IDENTITY_IN_USE` and says who holds the identity, so that a member whose own user is a shared credential learns whom to ask: `details.holder` `CREDENTIAL` with `details.credentialName`, which every member may see; or `MEMBER`, never named (`AccountService.identityInUse`). A member's sign-in as the user of one of the system's credentials comes back as `identity_shared` instead of `identity_in_use`. A connect that loses the race to the database says neither.

- **Who manages an account** (I4). A member connects, lists and disconnects their own (`/me/accounts`). Only OWNER and ADMIN manage a company system's credentials, on the system's page (`/systems/{systemId}/credentials…`, `MANAGE_SYSTEMS`), and lend them to groups (`/grants`, `MANAGE_GRANTS`) and to service accounts (`…/credentials/{credentialId}/service-accounts/{serviceIdentityId}`, §15.23); a service account holds no account of its own, and `GET /service-identities/{id}/credentials` lists what it borrows (§15.1). Others see whether a member holds an account on each company system and its status, never more: `GET /members/{userId}/accounts` (OWNER, ADMIN; `MANAGE_MEMBERS`) answers the system's id, name and kind and the status, nothing of a personal MCP server and nothing of who the account is there; a user who is not a member is `404`.

  | Endpoint | Does |
  |---|---|
  | `GET /me/accounts` | Per system the member sees, but a webhook system, which holds no one's account: their own account and its status, every usable grant to their groups (`GrantChoice`: the grant, the credential it lends by id and name, its group and access; the one a conversation takes first leading), what a conversation would bind now (`binding`: `OWN`, `GRANTED`, `CHOOSE`, `CONNECT`, `RECONNECT_YOURS`, `RECONNECT_COMPANY`, `RECONNECT_OR_GRANT`, `UNAVAILABLE`, `NOT_NEEDED`), and whether OAuth is offered |
  | `PUT …/{systemId}/token` | Stores a token, after the system's "who am I" accepted it (Jira's `myself`, of REST API v3 with `email:api-token` as Basic for Jira Cloud; GitHub's and GitLab's `user`, an MCP server's `initialize` with the token in its `authHeader`; a `GIT` token is taken as it is). A refusal is `400 ACCOUNT_REFUSED` with the system's reason, never the token, and audited as `account.refused` in a transaction of its own. The reason names the system by its name, never its kind's enum (`Support Jira answered 401 when asked whose the token is`; a kind is named by `SystemKind.label`, `Jira Data Center`, wherever a problem's words name one). One's own token is answered as the `Account` with `binding`, what a conversation of the caller binds now as `GET /me/accounts` says it (`OWN` once it works): the answer stays an `Account`, which the web app already reads, rather than a `MyAccount` |
  | `POST …/{systemId}/oauth` | Starts a sign-in with the system's app (`409 OAUTH_NOT_AVAILABLE` without one), with `returnTo`; answers the authorization URL |
  | `DELETE …/{systemId}` | Disconnects (`404` when none): an OAuth grant is revoked at the system where it offers revocation (RFC 7009: the refresh token, else the access token), then the account and its secret go |

  A credential is added, stored again, signed in again and removed the same way, through `AccountService`, on the system's page (§15.23). An account cannot be connected while its member is suspended (`409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED`), on a paused system (`409 SYSTEM_PAUSED`), on a personal server while the company switched them off (`409 PERSONAL_SYSTEMS_OFF`), as a credential on a personal server (`400 PERSONAL_SYSTEM`, I6: a personal server holds its owner's own account only), or on a chat or webhook system (`400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`, package 5), but a sign-in on Slack for its `history` tool (§15.17); a chat system's bot changes only with the bot (`409 BOT_ACCOUNT_IN_USE`, §15.16). Connecting again replaces the credential (`account.reconnected`, `OK` again). An account's audit names a member's principal (`principalId`, and `userId`), a credential's name (`credential`), never a token.
- **Where the credential is** (I3). A member's account keeps its token or OAuth grant (`OAuthTokens` as JSON) in a secret the member owns (`MEMBER_ACCOUNT`), never listed, revealed or changed through the Secrets API, revealed only for a call that acts as that member; a credential in a managed company secret (`SYSTEM_CREDENTIAL`; one that was a service identity's account before V049 keeps `SERVICE_ACCOUNT`), which the Secrets API neither replaces nor, while the credential keeps it, deletes (`409 SECRET_MANAGED`, naming "the credential `{name}` of `{system}`: remove it on the system's page", where its grants and lendings go with their audit and events; `AccountSecretKeeper`). The database checks both (`account_ck_trg`).
- **The sign-in and the one callback.** A sign-in is a `state` of 32 random bytes kept only as its SHA-256, with a PKCE `S256` challenge, bound to the person's session, for 10 minutes (`oauth_authorization_request`, §7): for a member's own account (`principal_id`), or for a credential, a new one by its name (`credential_name`) or one to sign in again (`credential_id`), the admin signing in as the user the credential should be. Its verifier is never stored: it is derived from the state, base64url without padding of the HMAC-SHA256 of the state under the instance key `sign-in-pkce` (§9; `SignInVerifiers`), 43 characters, once for the challenge and again at the callback, which the state comes back to. Only the person's browser holds the state, the database keeps its hash, and the key stays in the vault, so nothing stored yields a verifier. `returnTo` is a path of this site (a leading `/`, no scheme, host, backslash, fragment, whitespace or control character, at most 1000 characters; else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, never a redirect away). `GET /api/oauth/callback` serves every system: it consumes the request of the state once, in a transaction of its own, only for the caller and their session (another session's or another person's stays pending), and only within its 10 minutes; then, outside any transaction, it exchanges the code (with the RFC 8707 `resource` for an MCP server) and reads who the account is (for Jira Cloud the grant must include the system's site, whose gateway the account calls), and stores the account in a transaction that checks the principal, the system and a credential again. It answers `303` to `returnTo` with `account=connected&system={systemId}` or `account={reason}` (`denied`, `expired`, `exchange_failed`, `refused`, `site_not_granted`, `identity_in_use`, `identity_shared`, `already_linked` on Slack, §15.17, `name_taken` for a new credential's name that another credential of the system took meanwhile, `forbidden`), or to `/` with `account=invalid_state` for a state it does not know.
- **Grants** (`/grants`, `MANAGE_GRANTS`): an admin lends a system's credential to a group, `READ` or `WRITE`, changes the access, withdraws it. **I5, as ADR-0072 restates it:** a grant lends only a company credential (`400 GRANT_NOT_SERVICE` for a member's own account, `Grants.create`; and the foreign key `account_grant_credential_fk` on the lent account's principal kind `SYSTEM`), never a chat system's bot (`400 BOT_ACCOUNT`, §15.16), once per group (`409 DUPLICATE_GRANT`). A grant goes with its credential and with its group, each audited with the cause. What a service account borrows is a lending, not a grant (§15.23).
- **The binding rule** (the contract's §5), `Accounts.bind(context, systemId)`, stores nothing:

  | Context | Answer |
  |---|---|
  | A personal conversation of member M (`Personal`) | M's own account when it is `OK` (`Own`; a chat link, `LINK`, is no account to act with). Else, when M holds none: the grants to M's groups whose credential is `OK` and enabled, `WRITE` before `READ`, then the oldest (a credential lent to two of M's groups is one choice, through its first grant); one is `Granted`, several `Choose`. Else `ConnectRequired`: `CHOOSE` (M's own is broken and a usable grant exists), `RECONNECT_YOURS` (M's own is broken), `RECONNECT_COMPANY` (a granted credential is broken or disabled), `NONE` |
  | A trigger of author A, its row naming whom it acts through and the account (`Trigger(companyId, authorPrincipalId, holderPrincipalId, accountId)`) | `Fixed(account, holder)` when the account is A's own and acts through A, or a credential that the service account named borrows on the system and A may use, and it is `OK` (a credential also enabled); `ConnectRequired` when it is not (`RECONNECT_YOURS` for A's own, `RECONNECT_COMPANY` for a credential); else `NotEntitled` (`ACCOUNT_NOT_USABLE`, `BOT_ACCOUNT`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE` for A or the service account not `ACTIVE`) |

  On an MCP server that needs no account (ADR-0071, §15.2) a personal conversation of M binds `Open`, after M is found `ACTIVE` and a personal server M's own while the company allows them, whatever account M holds there; `GET /me/accounts` and an agent's readiness answer `NOT_NEEDED`. `Accounts.open(companyId, systemId, actingPrincipalId)` replaces `Accounts.check` for a binding without an account, and is what a trigger's conversation asks before it binds one: `Open` when the system is there and not paused, a personal server's company switch on, the acting principal an `ACTIVE` member and a personal server's owner, and the system needs no account; else `Stopped` with `SYSTEM_GONE`, `PERSONAL_SYSTEMS_OFF`, `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE` or `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED`.

  A personal server binds only for its owner, and for nobody while the company switched them off; a member or holder who is not `ACTIVE` binds nothing (I7); nothing falls back from a broken account (I10). What binds is stored as `AccountBinding.Stored`: who acts, whom it acts through (`holderPrincipalId`: the member for their own account, a trigger's author for theirs, the service account that borrows a credential; null through a grant, since the credential is the company's, V049), the account, the grant and the identity bound. `Accounts.check(binding)` checks a stored binding at every use (the system still there and not paused, the acting member and the holder `ACTIVE`, the account still there with the bound identity and `OK`, a credential also enabled and no chat system's bot, the grant still there with the member in its group and its access read now, a trigger's author still allowed to use the service account and the service account still borrowing the credential, its lending's access read now) and answers `Usable` or `Stopped` with the reason (`AccountBinding.StopReason`): `RECONNECT_YOURS`, `RECONNECT_COMPANY` (a credential gone, broken or disabled), `GRANT_GONE`, `NOT_IN_GROUP`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE`, `IDENTITY_CHANGED`, `USE_RIGHT_GONE` (the author lost the right to the service account, or it no longer borrows the credential: withdrawn, or replaced by another), `SYSTEM_GONE`, `PERSONAL_SYSTEMS_OFF`, `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (package 6, §15.13: the binding keeps its state), and, from `Accounts.open` only, `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED` (ADR-0071). It never re-binds: a trigger's conversation follows its service account to a replaced credential itself (§15.7).
- **A credential for one call.** `Accounts.reveal(companyId, accountId, actingPrincipalId, purpose)` runs outside any transaction (§10): it reads the account and reveals its secret in a short transaction of its own (`secret.accessed` with the purpose), a member's only for a call that acts as that member (else `IllegalArgumentException`), a credential for anyone the caller checked, through a service account only while it is active and borrows it, and with no acting principal for a poll the credential's triggers share (empty for an account gone, not `OK`, a disabled credential or a `LINK`), and writes the account's last use in another, after it (the lock order of §10). An OAuth access token that expires within a minute is refreshed through the system's app outside any transaction, and the refreshed grant (a rotated refresh token included) is stored in a transaction after it, unless the account was connected again meanwhile. Refreshes of one account run one at a time in the process: a striped lock by the account's id (`AccountCredentials`), taken outside any transaction, so it takes no part in the lock order of §10. A system that rotates refresh tokens spends the one read on its first use, and a second refresh with it would be refused and turn a working account `RECONNECT`; so the call that waited reads the account's row again under the lock and, when another call refreshed it or it changed otherwise, reads the account again and takes the grant stored (its `secret.accessed` then appears twice). Across replicas, which the lock does not cover, the refresh that succeeds makes an account that turned `RECONNECT` meanwhile with the same grant (the same `connectedAt` and secret) `OK` again, since it has just proven the grant: audited as `account.reconnected` with `refreshed` true and announced as `AccountReconnected`. A refusal of the refresh turns the account `RECONNECT` (`account.refused`, `AccountNeedsReconnect`), unless the account changed since it was read, when it is read again once; a grant that expired and cannot be refreshed (no refresh token, or the system's app removed) turns it `RECONNECT` as well; a transient failure leaves it as it is and answers the current token while it is valid. A `CLIENT` credential's token is minted again with its client's credentials instead of refreshed, under the same lock and with the same outcomes (§15.23). A system that refuses the credential on the call itself (a `401`) is told with `Accounts.refused(companyId, accountId, reason)`, in the caller's transaction after the call (package 3c's part 2, §15.7): the account turns `RECONNECT` under its row's lock, audited as `account.refused` with the reason (at most 500 characters, never a token) and announced as `AccountNeedsReconnect`, as a refused refresh is; an account that is gone or not `OK` any more is left as it is.
- **Revocation.** A disconnect asked for revokes the OAuth grant between the transaction that reads it and the one that removes the account, and audits whether the system confirmed it (`revoked`). An account that goes with its member, who leaves, goes in that change's transaction; its grant, read there, is revoked after the commit on a thread of its own, as SYSTEM in the company, best effort and logged (`revocation` `after_commit`). A removed system's accounts are not revoked: the app goes with the system. A grant whose secret the vault cannot decrypt (the master key its data key is wrapped under was dropped before the rewrap, or the data was tampered with, §9) is nothing to revoke with: the failure is logged as a warning, without key material or a value, and the account goes all the same, disconnected, removed or with its member, audited with `revoked` false and `reason` `SECRET_UNREADABLE`; a grant nobody can read is no use to keep, and it would otherwise block the disconnect, a credential's removal and a member's removal. A credential that goes takes its grants (`grant.deleted`) and its lendings to service accounts (`credential.withdrawn`, with the `cause`) first.
- **Events** (`AccountEvents`, ids only; a credential's carry no `principalId`): `AccountConnected`, `AccountReconnected`, `AccountDisconnected`, `AccountNeedsReconnect`; from ADR-0072 `CredentialChanged` (a credential disabled or enabled, or lent to a service account, its lending's access changed or withdrawn: what uses it is checked again; `servicePrincipalId` for a lending) and `LendingReplaced` (a service account borrows another credential on a system in place of the one it borrowed, published in the lending's transaction, on which its triggers follow it, §15.15); `GrantCreated`, `GrantChanged`, `GrantDeleted`. The port `CredentialUses` (`liveTriggers`, the trigger module's) counts what acts with a credential through each service account, for the system's page.
- **Tests.** `AccountConstraintTest` (I1, I2 between members and credentials and two credentials of one user, I3, I5 and the one lending per service account and system, I6 with no credential on a personal server, I9's bot a credential, against PostgreSQL), `AccountBindingTest` (the binding rule and its check as tables, with the redesign's V5 and V6), `AccountIntegrationTest` (roles, I4 with the Members API's view; credentials added on a system and lent to groups and service accounts, the managed secret, I2 naming the credential, two credentials of one user, a disabled credential, a withdrawn and a deleted group's grant, a deleted service account's lendings withdrawn and the credential kept, a removed credential, audit and events; a refusal that names the system, the stored token's `binding`), `SignInIntegrationTest` (the state's reuse, another session, expiry, a foreign `returnTo`, no verifier stored with a pending sign-in and the exchange's verifier the challenge's, revocation now and after the commit; a member told that the user they signed in as is a shared credential; an admin signing a system's credential in on an MCP server; a grant whose secret cannot be decrypted, which neither the disconnect, nor a credential's removal, nor a member's removal waits on), `CredentialMigrationTest` (V049 over V048's data: a service identity's account becomes a credential named after it and lent to it `WRITE`, its grants, a chat system's bot and a trigger's row keep naming it, a binding through a grant and a credential's poll lose their holder, members' accounts stay), `CredentialRevealTest` (refresh, its refusal, the removed app, no reveal inside a transaction; from package 3c's part 2 a credential refused on a call, `RECONNECT` once; two concurrent reveals of a grant whose refresh token rotates, refreshed once and `OK`; a refresh that makes an account `RECONNECT` meanwhile `OK` again; a reveal while a transaction holds the account's row and then the audit chain head, as a refusal does, without a deadlock; a credential revealed through a service account only while it is active and borrows it), `MemberLeavesTest` (I8), `OutsideTransactionsTest` (§10), and `TenantIsolationTest` over every path.

### 15.4 Model access and runner hosts

Who pays for a run, how Tinfirm manages LiteLLM, where a run may execute, and what reaches a runner host (module `model`, on the runner module's company and personal hosts; [ADR-0059](adr/0059-litellm-budgets-and-the-model-proxy.md), the operator's side in [operations/litellm.md](operations/litellm.md)). Package 3b's run module asks the two rules below before it dispatches a run, and sends the relay's token in `run.assign` (§15.6). M1 to M7 are the contract's §4.

- **The gateway.** A company configures one LiteLLM in its model settings (`model_settings`, §7): `BUNDLED`, the deployment's (`TINFIRM_LITELLM_ADMIN_URL` with `TINFIRM_LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`, §4; `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` on a deployment that bundles none, and for an admin URL or key sent with it), or `EXTERNAL`, the company's own: an admin URL held to the outbound guard of §14.4 with the operator's choices for company systems (https unless `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOW_HTTP`, no loopback, link-local or private address unless `TINFIRM_CONNECTION_ALLOW_PRIVATE_ADDRESSES`; checked when entered and before every request, guarded at connect time, no redirects; else `400 INVALID_BASE_URL`) and a master or admin key, entered again with every new URL so that a key is sent only where it was entered (`LITELLM_ADMIN_KEY`, §9). Each company is the LiteLLM team `tinfirm-{companyId}`, and Tinfirm manages only the keys whose alias starts with `tinfirm-{companyId}-`. Without a gateway that passed its test (`relay_enabled`) no budget pays for anything.

  | Endpoint (tags `ModelSettings`, `ModelAccess`) | Permission | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /model-settings`, `PUT /model-settings` | `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS` (OWNER, ADMIN) | The gateway, its admin URL and key (never returned: `adminKeySet`), and the defaults of every key: `defaultBudgetUsd` (0 to 1000000 USD, kept to four decimals), `budgetPeriod` (LiteLLM's `budget_duration`: a number and `h`, `d`, `w` or `mo`; `30d` at first) and `defaultModels` (at most 100 names of 1 to 200 characters without whitespace; empty allows every model the gateway serves). `relayBaseUrl` shows the relay's root. Without settings, `configured` is false |
  | `POST /model-settings/test` | `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS` | Outside any transaction: makes sure of the company's team and lists the gateway's models. A pass turns the relay on, and every principal without a key gets one (the reconciliation, after the commit); a failure turns it off, `502 GATEWAY_UNREACHABLE` with LiteLLM's reason. `409 GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` without settings, or when they changed during the test |
  | `GET /model-settings/models` | `VIEW_MODEL_ACCESS` (every member) | The models the gateway serves, read at most once a minute; `409 GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` until the gateway passed its test, `502 GATEWAY_UNREACHABLE` when it does not answer |
  | `GET /me/model-access` | `VIEW_MODEL_ACCESS` | The caller's budget, period, spend, reset time, models, key status and last error (each marked as their own override or the default), and the subscriptions on their personal hosts (`OFFLINE` while a host is not connected); `404` without a principal in the company.
