The spend is LiteLLM's as of `syncedAt`, the last sync that read the key, which every reconciliation does (`tinfirm.model.reconcile-interval`, 5 minutes, on a `worker`), so the page says how old it is; a member cannot ask for a sync of their own, which would only call LiteLLM sooner than the next pass |
  | `GET /principals/{id}/model-access`, `PATCH …` | `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS` | A member's or a service account's, without subscriptions; set or clear (`clearBudget`, `clearModels`) its budget and models, bounded as the defaults (a value and its clear in one request: `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). `404` for an unknown principal or one that left |
  | `POST /principals/{id}/model-access/rotate-key` | `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS` | Rotates the principal's key now, on suspicion, the old key blocked before the answer (the key rotation, below): `200` with the model access, `202` while another replica holds the key, `502 GATEWAY_UNREACHABLE` when LiteLLM failed (the request stays); `409 GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` before a passed test; `404` as above |
  | `POST /model-settings/rotate-keys` | `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS` | Rotates every key of the company at once, after a suspected leak of the vault or of LiteLLM: `202` with how many keys (`ModelKeyRotationRequest`), rotated after the commit; `409 GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` before a passed test |

  A change of the gateway, its URL or its admin key turns the relay off in the same transaction until the next test passes, as before a first test: runs paid by a budget are refused (`GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`), the relay answers `503`, the models are `409`, and no sync or reconciliation reaches LiteLLM. It clears `lastError`, so that the settings show a gateway changed since its last test (`relayEnabled` false, `testedAt` set, no `lastError`). No replica serves the former admin key once the change committed: a revealed key is kept in memory for the settings' `updatedAt`, which the change moves, and the test reveals the new one. A change of the gateway or its URL also forgets every key (each `model_key.deleted` with `cause` `gateway.changed`; the rows keep their overrides, the secrets go), and the keys are deleted at the former gateway after the commit; a new admin key alone keeps the keys. The model settings are the only way to a new admin key: the Secrets API neither replaces nor deletes the `LITELLM_ADMIN_KEY` they keep (`409 SECRET_MANAGED`, §9). A change of the defaults reaches every key without an override after the commit.
- **One key per principal** (M1, M6; `principal_model_key`, §7). Every principal that has not left, member or service account, has its own LiteLLM key, never shared, granted or reused: alias `tinfirm-{companyId}-{principalId}` (`…-r{n}` after its n-th rotation, below), the company's team, `key_type` `llm_api` (the model routes only), `max_budget` and `budget_duration` from the override or the default, `models` likewise, metadata `tinfirm_company`, `tinfirm_company_name`, `tinfirm_principal`, `tinfirm_principal_name` (the display name, as it was when the key was made: LiteLLM's admin shows the ids alone otherwise) and `tinfirm_kind`. The team `tinfirm-{companyId}` carries the company's name as its alias, which LiteLLM's admin shows beside every key of the team; a sync gives a renamed company's team the new name. It is created after the commit that creates its principal (`PrincipalCreated`: a member joins, an admin creates a service account), and for the principals that existed before when the gateway passes its test; blocked after the commit that suspends its principal, unblocked after the one that resumes it; and deleted, with its row and its secret, in the transaction where the member leaves or the service account is deleted (`PrincipalDependents`), and in LiteLLM after the commit. `BLOCKED` is for a suspended principal only: a left one has no key. The key is an internal secret (§9) and never leaves the server (M7).
- **The company's own key** (ADR-0065; `company_model_key`, §7). The company has one more key, no principal's, which pays for Tinfirm's own requests (`PlatformModel`: a memory's compaction, §15.18) and never for a run: alias `tinfirm-{companyId}-company` (`…-r{n}` after rotations), metadata `tinfirm_company`, `tinfirm_company_name` and `tinfirm_kind` `COMPANY`, its secret `MODEL_KEY_COMPANY`.
 The work below takes a `KeyHolder` (a principal, or the company) and treats it as a principal's key that is never blocked, raises no budget alert (§15.12) and announces no status change: created by the first sync once the gateway passed its test, synced, rotated (also by `rotate-keys`, and alone by `POST /model-access/company-key/rotate-key`), kept by the reconciliation's sweep and forgotten when the gateway changes. OWNER and ADMIN read and set its budget and models (`GET`, `PATCH /model-access/company-key`, `ModelAccessView` without `principalId`), a row of Members' budgets in the web app. A request on it reveals the key (`secret.accessed`, purpose `platform:{purpose}`), is tagged `tinfirm-task:{purpose}`, and syncs the key after it.
- **The sync** (`KeySync`). One idempotent sync brings a key to what its principal and the settings ask: it creates the key (again when LiteLLM lost it), changes its budget and models, sends the period only when it changed (a new period restarts LiteLLM's), blocks or unblocks it, and reads its spend and reset time. It plans in one transaction, taking the row's lease (`sync_lease_until`, 2 minutes) so that one replica at a time talks to LiteLLM about a key, calls LiteLLM outside any transaction (§10), and records in another, as SYSTEM, with `model_key.created`, `blocked`, `unblocked` or `budget_spent`. What it learned from a gateway the settings left meanwhile is dropped, and a key it created but cannot record is deleted again. A status the relay recorded while the sync talked to LiteLLM (a spent budget or a refused key, from LiteLLM's answer to one of the key's requests) is newer than the sync's read and stays; the sync the relay asks for reads LiteLLM again. Recording the older read put a spent budget's key back to `OK` until then, and the dispatcher started the payer's waiting runs on it. The status: `BLOCKED` while the principal is suspended, `BUDGET_SPENT` once the period's spend reached the budget, else `OK`; a failure leaves `ERROR` with LiteLLM's reason until the next sync. A change of status is announced as `ModelEvents.ModelKeyStatusChanged`, on which package 3 resumes a principal's waiting turns.
- **The reconciliation** (`KeyReconciliation`, M6), on `worker` replicas, `tinfirm.model.reconcile-interval` apart and first `reconcile-initial-delay` after startup (§4): for each company whose gateway passed its test (`model_settings_companies()`), it syncs every principal that has not left (which repeats every failed create, block, unblock or change, and reads every key's spend) and runs what is due of its rotation (below: a key that is due is rotated, an old key past its grace blocked or past its settling time deleted, a rotation a crash stopped finished), deletes the keys of the company's team that carry Tinfirm's alias but that no row holds as its current or its previous key (a principal's key that is being synced or rotated is left alone), and sweeps the relay's tokens revoked more than a day ago. Keys of another alias are never touched.
- **Key rotation** (security review row 2 of the redesign: keys rotate on a schedule and at once on suspicion; `KeyRotation`, `ModelKeyRotationService`, the rotation's columns of `principal_model_key`, §7). LiteLLM v1.105.1 regenerates a key (`/key/regenerate`, with its grace period) only for an enterprise licence, and keeps an alias unique among all keys, so Tinfirm rotates a key with a new one:
  - *When.* Every key `TINFIRM_MODEL_KEY_ROTATION_INTERVAL` (30 days; at least a day) after its creation, which the reconciliation finds; and at once when an OWNER or ADMIN asks for one principal's key (`rotate-key`) or for every key of the company (`rotate-keys`), for a suspected leak of a key, of the vault or of LiteLLM. A key the sync made again because LiteLLM lost it is a new key as well: its age starts again, and it answers a pending request. A suspended principal's key rotates too, its next key created blocked.
  - *Budget and spend.* In LiteLLM a key's budget (`max_budget`, `budget_duration`) and its spend are the key's own, not its team's or a user's, and its reset time follows the period and the calendar (`get_budget_reset_time`: `30d` and `1mo` on the 1st of the month, `7d` on Mondays, `1d` at midnight, hours on their boundaries; never settable), not the key's creation. So the next key is created with the next alias, the company's team, the same budget, period, models and metadata, and `spend` the current key's: it goes on in the same period with the same spend, and resets when the old key would have. A period of another number of days (`14d`) counts from midnight of the day a key is made, so a rotation starts it again with the spend carried, stricter for that period, never looser. What the old key spends after the next key was made (requests under way) goes over to the current key when the old key is deleted. The overrides and the status stay on the row.
  - *Steps.* (1) Under the row's lease and outside any transaction, the current key's spend is read and the next key created. (2) One transaction keeps the next key in a new internal secret and makes it the principal's, deletes the old key's secret, names the old key as retiring until the end of the grace (`TINFIRM_MODEL_KEY_ROTATION_GRACE`, 10 minutes, at most an hour; none for a request, also one made while the step ran), answers the request, and records `model_key.rotated`: from then on the relay reveals the next key for every request, and a stream that took the old key goes on with it. (3) When the grace ends LiteLLM blocks the old key (`model_key.retired`), which it refuses from then on. (4) A minute later (`tinfirm.model.key-rotation-settle`, §4), once LiteLLM wrote the spend of its last requests (it writes every 10 to 15 seconds), the old key's late spend goes over to the current key (`/key/update` with `spend`), LiteLLM deletes the old key, and the row forgets it. A rotation that is due while an old key still retires does not wait: that key is blocked and deleted first. A request's old key is blocked before `rotate-key` answers; a sync at the end of a grace or a settling time wakes on the replica that started it, and the reconciliation on any. A run that finds the row held by another schedules no wake: the holder wakes its own, and a dead run's overdue step is the reconciliation's once its lease expired.
  - *Crash safety.* Each step is recorded before the next one runs, and each run reads the row again and does what it says is due, under the row's lease (renewed after each step), so a run may stop anywhere and run again: the next sync or reconciliation finishes it. Until the switch commits, the principal's key is the old one, and a next key LiteLLM made but Tinfirm did not record is deleted by its alias when the rotation asks for it again (or by the reconciliation's sweep: its alias is Tinfirm's and no row holds it); from the commit on, the principal's key is the next one. So the principal never lacks a working key, and an old key never lives on beyond its grace and the next reconciliation. A failure of LiteLLM ends the run with `rotation_error` and changes nothing else; the current key keeps working, and the next run tries again. One step is not idempotent: a crash after the late spend went over and before the old key's deletion counts that spend twice, never not at all.
  - *Status.* The model access shows `keyCreatedAt`, `keyAgeDays`, `keyRotatedAt`, `nextKeyRotationAt` (the creation plus the interval, or a pending request's time), `keyRotationRequestedAt`, `keyRotationError` and `previousKeyValidUntil`; the model settings show `keyRotationInterval` and `keyRotationGrace`. Their admin UI is later work.
  - *The actions.* `rotate-key` commits the request (`model_key.rotation_requested`, `scope` `principal`) and then rotates on the caller's thread, outside any transaction: `200` once the next key is in place and the old one blocked, `202` while another replica holds the row (it rotates in a moment, retried), `502 GATEWAY_UNREACHABLE` with LiteLLM's reason when a step failed, the rest left to the reconciliation. A principal without a key gets its first. `rotate-keys` records one request per key (`scope` `company`) in one transaction, which also ends the grace of every old key still retiring, and rotates the keys after the commit, one after the other. A principal that leaves or a gateway that changes during a rotation takes its keys along, the old one included; a next key Tinfirm could not record then is deleted again.
- **LiteLLM's admin API** (`LiteLlm`, as the pinned LiteLLM serves it): `/key/generate` (with `spend` for a rotation's next key), `/key/update` (with `spend` to move an old key's late spend), `/key/block`, `/key/unblock`, `/key/delete`, `/key/info`, `/key/list`, `/team/info`, `/team/new` and `/v1/models`, and from package 6 `/model/info` and `/spend/logs` (§15.12), each within `tinfirm.model.admin-timeout`. Its errors are passed on in one line of at most 300 characters, without what they echo of a key (`sk-…` and 64-digit hex hashes are masked). Keys are never logged. An answer is read within a limit (`shared.net.BoundedBodies`, §14.4), since a company's own gateway is an address an admin entered: 64 MiB for the admin API (`LiteLlm.MAX_ADMIN_ANSWER_BYTES`: the largest answer is a key's spend logs, whole UTC days of one key's requests that v1.105.1 does not page, a few KiB a request, some 15,000 requests; a page of keys is under 1 MiB, the model prices of a thousand deployments some 5 MiB; the relay's limit of a request is the same) and 1 MiB for a chat completion on the company's own key (`MAX_CHAT_ANSWER_BYTES`: its requests ask a few thousand tokens, an answer under 100 KiB). A body declared longer is refused unread, one that grows past the limit is cut off there; either fails the call as LiteLLM's errors do (`LiteLlmException` with the answer's status, "LiteLLM's answer (200) is larger than the 64 MiB Tinfirm reads"), which its callers handle already: a key's sync or test reports it, the spend sync retries it with its reason (§15.12), the compaction leaves the memory due (§15.18). A gateway that keeps prompts in its spend logs (`STORE_PROMPTS_IN_SPEND_LOGS`) answers a busy key's logs past the limit; the bundled one does not.
- **Who pays: `ModelAccess.forRun(RunModelContext)`** (the contract's §5.1; M2, M3). Package 3 builds a `RunModelContext` (the company, the paying principal, the `ModelAccessChoice` it named and authorised: `BUDGET` or `Subscription(runnerHostId, label)`, the runtime, and the principals whose accounts the run's workspaces use) and asks in its dispatching transaction; nothing outside the server is called. Tinfirm never switches payer: a run waits or is refused with the reason.

  | Named | Answer |
  |---|---|
  | Anything, by a paying principal that is suspended, left or unknown | `Refuse(PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE)` |
  | The principal's budget | `Refuse(GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED)` without a gateway that passed its test. Else, by the key's status: `OK`: `Budget(principalId, models)`; `BUDGET_SPENT`: `Wait(BUDGET_SPENT, resetsAt)`, or `Budget` once the reset time passed (the key is synced again); `ERROR`, or no key yet: `Wait(KEY_ERROR, reason)`; `BLOCKED` while the principal is active again: `Wait(KEY_ERROR)` (being unblocked). A missing or blocked key is synced after the commit |
  | A subscription `(host, label)` | `Refuse(PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY)` on a company host (M5); `Refuse(SUBSCRIPTION_GONE)` when the host is gone, is not the paying member's personal host, or no longer reports the label; `Wait(HOST_OFFLINE)` while the host is not connected; else `Subscription(host, label)` |

  `models` are the models the key may call (empty: every model the gateway serves); the key itself is never in the answer. `Refuse.problem()` is the API's answer: `409 GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` for `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE`, `409 SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`, `409 CANNOT_RUN_ANYWHERE`, `400 PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY`, `400 MODEL_REQUIRED`, `400 MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`. The last two are `Budget.refusal(model)`'s, which the callers ask of a `Budget` answer: a run paid by a budget names its model, since the gateway serves only the models it lists (`MODEL_REQUIRED`), and the key allows it (`MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `Budget.allows`). A waiting run's queue reason is its `WaitReason`: `BUDGET_SPENT`, `KEY_ERROR`, `HOST_OFFLINE`. `ModelAccess.refresh(companyId, principalId)` reads a key's spend again after the commit: after a run ends, and when a run waits for a budget that should have reset.
- **Where a run may execute: `Hosts.eligible(RunModelContext)`** (the contract's §5.2; M4, M5). A personal host runs only its owner's work, so that what its owner can see of a run (its prompt, workspace, transcript and model traffic) is their own:

  | Run | Answer |
  |---|---|
  | Budget of P, with a workspace account that is not P's own (a system's credential, through a grant or a trigger's service account) | `Eligible`: the company hosts that serve the runtime |
  | Budget of P, with every workspace account P's own, or no workspace | `Eligible`: the company hosts and P's personal hosts that serve the runtime |
  | Either budget row | Only hosts of the minimum runner version (below); with none left, `Eligible` with no host and `whyNone`, the queue reason for people (no host serves the runtime, or none that does has the version) |
  | Subscription on P's own personal host, every workspace account P's own | `Eligible`: that host; with no host and `whyNone` when it does not serve the runtime |
  | Subscription with a workspace account that is not P's, or on a host that is not P's personal host | `Nowhere(Refuse(CANNOT_RUN_ANYWHERE))`, to show before the conversation starts and in the trigger's editor |

  `eligible` reads the hosts as stored, connected or not; among the eligible ones the run module's dispatcher keeps its own choice (§15.6: connected, protocol v2, free slots, capacity settings, draining; [ADR-0016](adr/0016-runner-capacity-settings.md)).
- **The minimum runner version** (the contract's §7): a run paid by a budget goes only to a host whose latest `hello.runnerVersion` is `RunnerVersions.MODEL_PROXY_ALWAYS_ON`, **0.2.0**, or later, the first runner whose model proxy is always on and keeps the run's token out of the sandbox; the protocol does not say whether a host proxies, so the version decides, host by host (`RunnerHost.gatewayRuns`, `RunnerHostInfo.gatewayRuns()`). Versions compare as semantic versions: a pre-release (`0.2.0-rc.1`) is below its release, a version that is not `major.minor.patch` counts as too old, and a host that never connected has none. A subscription's run has no minimum. From `RunnerVersions.HOST_TOKEN_TO_RELAY`, **0.3.0**, the model proxy sends the host's own token to the relay, which then requires it from that host (the relay's runner host row below).
- **Runner hosts by scope** (the runner module; `/runner-hosts`, rights in §6). A `COMPANY` host, registered by OWNER and ADMIN, runs any run paid by a budget and holds no credentials. A `PERSONAL` host, the default scope of a registration, is a member's own: it runs only its owner's work (M4), and it is `404` to anyone else, OWNER and ADMIN included. The scope and the owner are set at registration and never change (§7). `RunnerHost` shows `scope`, `ownerPrincipalId` and `ownerDisplay` (null for a company host), `gatewayRuns`, and a personal host's `credentials`.
- **A personal host in every company of its owner** (ADR-0092). A personal host is a machine of its owner's (`personal_runner_host`, §7) with a presence (a `runner_host` row) in each company the owner is a member of. A registration in one company writes the machine and a presence in each of the owner's companies (`member_principals`), each in its company's tenant context and audited in its chain; the answer is the presence in the company of the request; a name is a duplicate (`409 DUPLICATE_RUNNER_HOST_NAME`) when the owner has a machine of that name, whichever company it was registered in. The capacity (`PATCH`) is the machine's, set in any company, shown in all, audited where it was set, with one `host.config` to the host. `DELETE` on any presence removes the machine with every presence and credential, audited `runner_host.removed` in each company, and closes the connection; `409 RUNNER_HOST_HAS_ACTIVE_RUNS` while a run of any company is queued for it or runs on it (`RunnerActivity.hasActiveRunsOnMachine`). A member who joins a company gets a presence of each of their machines there, in the joining transaction (`PrincipalDependents.memberJoined`; `runner_host.registered` with `cause` `principal.joined`), served by the machine's current connection from the next dispatch on; a member who leaves takes only their presences there along (`memberLeft`; `runner_host.removed` with `cause` `principal.left`), and the machine, its token and its connection stay for their other companies. A member of no company keeps their machine, which connects and takes no runs. `RunnerHostInfo.personalHostId` names the machine of a presence (null for a company host).
- **Credentials on personal hosts only** (M5; `personal_runner_credential`, §7). A personal host's `hello` stores the credentials it reports, on its machine, so every presence shows them, by label (kind, runtimes, pool): a new label is added, a known one takes the reported values and is `AVAILABLE` again, and one the `hello` no longer lists is kept with `reported` false (a run that names it is `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`); a `credential.status` updates its status, cooldown and detail. The API shows a credential `OFFLINE` while its host is not connected. A company host's credentials are not stored: after `host.config` the server sends the protocol `error` `PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY` and the connection goes on, and a `credential.status` for one of them is `UNKNOWN_CREDENTIAL`. A credential of a kind the server does not know (a host of another version, ADR-0085) is dropped from the hello and named in the `error` `UNKNOWN_CREDENTIAL_KIND`; the rest of the hello serves. No endpoint takes a credential, so `PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY` is a protocol error and a refusal of `forRun` (a subscription named on a company host, whose problem is `400`), never the answer to a credential sent to the REST API.
- **Ready sandboxes** ([ADR-0099](adr/0099-pool-settings.md); the runner module: `RunnerHostService.updateHost` and the interface `RunnerPools`, which `RunnerPoolsImpl` implements under `ee/` with `PoolSchedule` and `RunnerPoolStore`, ADR-0100). Pro's and Enterprise's (`ELASTIC_RUNNERS`): without it, `pool` or `clearPool` is `403 EDITION_REQUIRED` after the access check, the tick sends nothing, and without `ee/` a host shows no `pool`; what a host reports (`hello.pool`, `host.config.applied.pool`, `host.pool.status`) is stored in every edition. A `COMPANY` host whose `hello` lists `sandbox.pool` (§14.10) has pool settings: `readyDefault` (the count when no window applies) and up to 14 weekly windows (`days` `MON` to `SUN`, `from` and `to` as `HH:mm` from 00:00 to 23:59, a window whose `to` is before its `from` ending the next day, `ready`, and an optional `zone`), in the pool's IANA `zone` or the window's (an offset such as `+02:00` is not one). The highest count of the windows that apply wins, else `readyDefault`; a local time that the change to summer time skips moves forward by the gap, and a repeated one takes its first occurrence (`ZonedDateTime.of`). Without settings no sandbox waits (D5). `PATCH /runner-hosts/{id}` takes `pool` or `clearPool` (both: `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), with the desired slots or alone, for OWNER and ADMIN; on a personal host or a host without the feature `409 RUNNER_HOST_POOL_UNSUPPORTED`; settings that are no schedule (an unknown zone, a window without a zone where the pool has none, no days, `from` equal to `to`, a count above the pool size, which is the desired slots else the ceiling, more than 14 windows) `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` with the reason. The server evaluates the schedule (`RunnerPools.refresh`) at the host's `hello`, after a change of its settings, and every `tinfirm.runner.pool-tick` on every replica for its connected pool hosts; it stores the count now and its next change (`pool_ready_target`, `pool_next_target`, `pool_next_at`) and moves `config_version` on when they change, and a connected host gets its `host.config`. A company host's `pool` shows the settings, `maxReadyCeiling`, the count now (before the ceiling), `next`, `week` (the count now and every change of the next 7 days) and the host's last `status`. Columns of `runner_host` (`V067__runner_host_pool.sql`): `pool_max_ready_ceiling` (NULL for a host without the feature), `pool_ready_default`, `pool_zone`, `pool_windows` (jsonb), `pool_ready_target`, `pool_next_target`, `pool_next_at`, `pool_status`, `pool_status_at`, with checks; the slots' checks go to 1000. Tests: `PoolScheduleTest` and `RunnerPoolIntegrationTest` (under `ee/`), `RunnerCapacityIntegrationTest`, and the refusals of `EditionGatesIntegrationTest`.
- **The model relay** (the contract's §6.1; M7). The server is the runner hosts' model endpoint, so that a principal's LiteLLM key never leaves it and runner hosts never reach LiteLLM. The host's model proxy names the server's https origin as its gateway (`--model-proxy-local-gateway` on the server's own machine; `--egress-allow-private` when it reaches the server at a private address).

  | Part | Rule |
  |---|---|
  | Endpoints | `POST /api/runner/v1/model/anthropic/v1/messages`, `POST …/anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens`, `GET …/anthropic/v1/models`; `POST /api/runner/v1/model/openai/v1/responses`, `POST …/openai/v1/chat/completions`, `GET …/openai/v1/models`. Nothing else (§4). Not in `api/openapi.yaml` |
  | `modelAccess` | `ModelRelay.open(RelayedRun, format)` at the run's assignment, in a short transaction of its own, between the turn's content and the recording of the assignment (§15.6): `baseUrl` the relay's root for the format, `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/api/runner/v1/model/anthropic` or `…/model/openai/v1`, and `apiKey` a new one-run token; the run's tokens of that epoch and of earlier ones are revoked (a reassignment), never a later epoch's: an assignment that went stale while it was prepared (its run went back to the queue and was assigned again) may open its token after the later assignment opened its own, and must not revoke the token the run uses. The recording of a stale assignment revokes the token it opened, that epoch's only (`ModelRelay.revoke(companyId, runId, epoch)`, §15.6). The formats `anthropic` and `openai` only. The protocol does not change |
  | Token (`run_model_token`) | `tfm_` and 32 random bytes in base64url (47 characters), sent as `Authorization: Bearer`; only `hex(sha256(token))` is kept, bound to the run, its epoch, its host, the paying principal and the format. Accepted only unrevoked, at its own format's root, while the port `RunModelTokens.active(companyId, runId, epoch, runnerHostId)` says the run is `RUNNING` on that host with that epoch, and with that host's own token (below); anything else is `401`, without saying why. The run module implements the port (§15.6). `ModelRelay.revoke` at the run's end (for a run lost by lease expiry, once its resume window is over), and a stale assignment's own token as above; revoked tokens are swept a day later |
  | The runner host (the contract's open point 6) | The host's own token (`tfr_…`, bare) in `X-Tinfirm-Runner-Token`, which the model proxy of runner host 0.3.0 and later sends with every request to its server's origin, and to no other gateway. When the header is there, the relay authenticates it as the runner's other endpoints do (`RunnerAuthentication.authenticateToken`: looked up by its SHA-256 hash, never compared as text) and requires its host to be of the token's company and to be the run token's host; a malformed, unknown or repeated header, or another host's token, is `401`. Without it: `401` from a host whose latest `hello.runnerVersion` is `RunnerVersions.HOST_TOKEN_TO_RELAY` (**0.3.0**) or later, and from every host while `TINFIRM_MODEL_RELAY_REQUIRE_HOST_TOKEN` is on (§4); else accepted as before, for 0.2.x hosts, with a `WARN` once a day per host and replica naming the host and its version (`ModelRelayService.hostCheck`). A copied run token alone is therefore worthless away from its host; whoever also holds the host's token acts as the host anyway. Refusals of another host's token are logged with the hosts' ids, never a token. The header never reaches LiteLLM |
  | Before LiteLLM | `503` while the company's gateway has not passed its test or the paying principal's key does not exist (it is synced); `403` while the paying principal is not `ACTIVE` (suspended, or gone), read from the principal module on every request, so that a block LiteLLM failed (the key `ERROR` until the reconciliation) leaves no run relaying on the key, and while that key is `BLOCKED`; `429` with `Retry-After: 1` beyond `tinfirm.model.relay-run-concurrency` requests of the run at a time, or `relay-host-concurrency-per-slot` per slot of the host's ceiling; `413` over `relay-max-request-bytes` (64 MiB); `429` with `Retry-After: 1` when the body does not fit in what the replica may hold of request bodies at once, `relay-max-inflight-bytes` (256 MiB, `TINFIRM_MODEL_RELAY_MAX_INFLIGHT_BYTES`, §4; `InflightBytes`): a body counts twice while it is read and written anew, then once until its answer ends (the forwarded request holds it), so that the server's memory does not grow with the number of runs; a declared length is checked before the body is read, a streamed body as it comes |
  | Body | One JSON object without a repeated top-level name and with a string `model` (else `400`), written anew without LiteLLM's own top-level fields: the contract's list (`api_base`, `base_url`, `api_key`, `api_version`, `custom_llm_provider`, `organization`, `metadata`, `tags`, `user`, `mock_response`, `litellm_*`, `user_config`, `extra_headers`, `extra_body`), `guardrail_config`, `policies` and `include_guardrail_response` (which v1.106.6's proxy takes from the body for the request's guardrails) and the rest of v1.106.6's `all_litellm_params` (routing, retries, fallbacks, caching, cloud credentials, callbacks, prices, canned answers), by name, by prefix and by the prices' shapes (`RelayBody`, with the shared filter `LiteLlmRequests` of `libs/litellm`, which the model port uses too); everything else passes as it came, numbers exactly, and the relay adds `"drop_params": true` last, so that LiteLLM drops a parameter the model's provider does not take (a runtime's `reasoning_effort` for a model LiteLLM does not know to reason, such as Kimi K3 through OpenRouter) instead of refusing the request and failing the turn: LiteLLM v1.105.1 does so by itself only for Claude Code and Codex, by their `User-Agent`, not for pi or OpenCode. The model must be one the paying key allows (its models or the company's defaults, matched by `RunModelAccess.allows`, §15.5), else `403` before LiteLLM. A body that asks the model's provider to run a tool itself is `400`, naming the field or the tool's type, always, with no setting of the company or the deployment that lets one through ([ADR-0108](adr/0108-no-provider-run-tools.md); the security review of 2026-10-27, M1 and D8): `mcp_servers`, `container`, `web_search_options` or `search_parameters` not `null`, a stored `prompt` object, a tool whose `type` is not one the runtime runs (`LiteLlmRequests.CLIENT_TOOL_TYPES`: every web search and fetch, code execution, `mcp_toolset` and OpenAI's `mcp` refused), one that names one of Gemini's built-in tools by a field, and one whose `allowed_callers` names anything but `direct`, wherever the body defines tools. Refused, not removed: no pinned runtime sends them. Every web search and every MCP call of a run goes through Tinfirm's server-executed tools (ADR-0039, ADR-0040). `V081` dropped the former company setting, `model_settings.provider_tools` (V076), which never comes back as a column or a field |
  | Forwarding | Outside any transaction, to the same path at the gateway's model URL (the model port, `TINFIRM_LITELLM_MODEL_URL`, for the bundled gateway; else its admin URL), without the query. Request headers: only `Accept`, `anthropic-version`, `anthropic-beta`, `OpenAI-Beta` and `User-Agent` (LiteLLM treats Claude Code and Codex apart by it), each printable and at most 4096 characters, and with a body the server's own `Content-Type: application/json`. The paying principal's key as the bearer, revealed once per token and server replica (`secret.accessed`, `run:{runId}`) and kept in memory while the run uses it (at most 30 idle minutes); once a rotation replaced it (another secret), the next request reveals the next key, and a request already under way keeps the old one, which LiteLLM honours for the grace. The run, its conversation and its agent as LiteLLM request tags, `x-litellm-tags: run:…,conversation:…,agent:…` (key tags are an enterprise feature of LiteLLM) |
  | Answer | Streamed back part by part with backpressure, each part flushed; no total timeout, `relay-idle-timeout` (10 minutes) until the headers (else `504`) and between two parts (then the answer is cut off). Only `Content-Type`, `Retry-After`, `request-id`, `x-request-id`, `anthropic-ratelimit-*` and `x-ratelimit-*` pass, never LiteLLM's `x-litellm-*`. A gateway that cannot be reached is `502`. The relay's own refusals have the format's error shape, so that the runtime shows them as the provider's |
  | LiteLLM's refusals | `429` `budget_exceeded` (the bundled settings' `budget_exceeded_status_code`: LiteLLM's own default is `422` from v1.105.1): the key becomes `BUDGET_SPENT` (`model_key.budget_spent`); `401` or `403` other than for a model the key may not use (`*_model_access_denied`): `ERROR` (`model_key.refused`), unless a rotation replaced the refused key since the request took it (the old key blocked at once on a request), when the current key is left alone; both are synced again. Their text goes back with LiteLLM's status, without what it echoes of the key |
  | Usage | Not audited per request: each token counts the requests the relay passed and the bytes of both bodies, and `ModelRelay.usage(companyId, runId)` sums them for the run |

- **Problem codes** (the contract's §9 and §13): `GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` (409), `GATEWAY_UNREACHABLE` (502), `CANNOT_RUN_ANYWHERE` (409), `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE` (409), `PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY` (400 as a problem; on the protocol an `error` frame); an inactive paying principal is `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED`, and an `EXTERNAL` gateway's refused admin URL `400 INVALID_BASE_URL`.
- **Events** (`ModelEvents`): `ModelKeyStatusChanged`, `ModelSettingsChanged`.
- **Not built in package 2** (the contract's §13): the opt-in test against a real LiteLLM; Tinfirm writing the gateway's provider keys and model list (the operator does it in LiteLLM); a run's cost from LiteLLM (the relay's tags carry the run for it). Open: gateway runs on a development instance over plain http (the relay's `baseUrl` follows `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL`, and the protocol's `baseUrl` is https). Binding a token to its host, which needed a runner change, is built (runner host 0.3.0, the contract's open point 6).
- **Tests.** `ModelKeyLifecycleTest` (against `FakeLiteLlm`, an embedded fake of LiteLLM's admin API: the team and every key on a passed test, a new principal's key, an override and the defaults, suspend and resume, a leaving member and a deleted service account, the reconciliation after failures, the key's secret out of the Secrets API's reach), `ModelKeyLockOrderTest` (a key made again against a change of a service account's users, and a budget's crossed threshold against a deletion, each held where it waits for the other: the principal's row first, §10), `ModelSettingsIntegrationTest` (roles, the bundled and a company's own gateway, the keys moving), `ModelAccessRulesTest` (`forRun` and `eligible` as tables, M3 to M5, `RunModelAccess.allows`), `ModelRelayTest` (the token in place of a key and its refusals, a stale assignment's token that never revokes a later assignment's, a sync whose read of LiteLLM was older than the relay's spent budget keeping that budget spent, the fields, headers and query removed and the tags set, a model the key does not allow, an answer streamed part by part, the size and concurrency limits, a spent budget, a lost key and a suspended principal, also when LiteLLM's block failed; the host's own token: through with its own host's, refused with another host's, another company's, a malformed or a repeated one, refused without it from a 0.3.0 host and accepted from a 0.2.0 host with one warning, refused once that host says 0.3.0, and no token in the logs; a new admin key of a company's own gateway, which the Secrets API refuses and the settings take, turning the relay off, runs refused and the relay `503` until the test passes with the new key, never the old one; the runs are rows that `TestRuns` writes, which the run module's `RunModelTokens` answers for), `ModelKeyRotationTest` (against `FakeLiteLlm`, which takes a key's `spend` and computes LiteLLM's reset times: a rotation on schedule with its budget, period, metadata and spend, the old key served for its grace, then refused, then deleted with its late spend moved; `rotate-key` blocking the old key before its answer while a relayed stream that holds it finishes and the next request goes with the new key; `rotate-keys` over every key, a suspended principal's and an old key still in its grace included; a failure of LiteLLM kept as a request and carried out by the reconciliation; a crash after each step, `KeyRotation.crashAfter`, finished by the reconciliation with one live key, the spend carried and each step audited once; no key in the audit), `KeyRotationRulesTest` (the interval's and grace's bounds, the aliases, when a key is due, the late spend), `ModelRelaySettingsTest` (the relay under an operator's settings, in a context of its own: `TINFIRM_MODEL_RELAY_REQUIRE_HOST_TOKEN` on, which refuses a 0.2.0 host and one that never said its version without their token; a small `relay-max-inflight-bytes`, whose bodies beyond it are `429`, declared or streamed, until the others end), `ModelRelayServiceTest` (`InflightBytes`, the settings' bound, `guardrail_config`, and a request's plan, whose `toString` shows neither the key nor the token's hash), `ModelOutsideTransactionsTest` (§10), `shared/net/BoundedBodiesTest` (a body within the limit read whole, declared or chunked; one past it null, declared or chunked; one declared longer refused before a byte of it is read; an endless one cut off at the limit and its connection closed), `RunnerHostIntegrationTest` (visibility and rights per scope, credentials stored and refused, a leaving member's hosts, M4 and M5 in the database), `ModelRelayHostCheckTest` (the relay's runner host check as a table: the header, the version and the flag), `RunnerVersionsTest` (0.2.0 and 0.3.0), and `TenantIsolationTest` over every path.

### 15.5 Agents and settings

A specialist's definition, private to its owner until shared with groups or with members on their own, and each person's narrowing of it (module `agent`; package 3a: [the package 3 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-3-contract.md), §3.1 to §3.7, §5.1, §5.2, §6 and §11). An agent holds no credentials and names no principal it acts as (the contract's I1): whom a conversation acts for, who pays and where it runs come from its person and their settings. Its turns run as package 3b's runs (§15.6); the conversations that use what this section defines are package 3c (§15.7; the last bullets say what of this section it uses).

- **Who sees, uses and edits an agent** (`Agents.access`; the contract's §5.1 and open point 11):

  | Caller | Sees | Uses | Edits, shares, pauses, resumes, deletes |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Its owner | yes | yes | yes |
  | A member of a group it is shared with | yes | yes | no: `403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE` |
  | A member it is shared with on their own, in a group or none | yes | yes | no: `403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE` |
  | OWNER, ADMIN, an instance admin acting in the company (`MANAGE_AGENTS`) | yes | only as its owner or through a share, else `403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE` | yes |
  | Anyone else | no: `404` on every path | no | no |

  Every member creates agents (`VIEW_AGENTS`) and owns those they create; a caller without a principal in the company gets `404` for a create and uses no agent. A deleted or unknown agent is `404` to everyone. `GET /agents` lists the live agents the caller sees by name (keyset on name and id), each with `usableByMe`, `editableByMe`, `wideningPending`, `shareGroupIds` (the groups it is shared with, as the agent's page shows them to whoever sees it, so that a group's page finds its agents without reading each) and `sharePrincipalIds` (the members it is shared with, as `Agent.memberShares` shows them); `?usable=true` keeps those they use (their own, those of their groups and those shared with them). For other modules, `Agents.access(companyId, agentId, userId)` answers the same for a member of the company, and `AgentAccess.NONE` for anyone else.

  | Endpoint (tags `Agents`, `Settings`) | Who | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /agents`, `GET /agents/{agentId}` | Who sees it | The current definition: its systems with their tools (`offered` false for a row whose tool its system no longer serves or switched off), repositories, hosts and shares; for a caller who uses it, `acceptedVersion`, `wideningPending` and `widening` |
  | `POST /agents` | Every member | Create: version 1, `ACTIVE`, owned by the caller; optionally with `systems`, `repositories`, `hosts`, `shareGroupIds` and `sharePrincipalIds`, as the `PUT`s below take them |
  | `PATCH /agents/{agentId}` | Who edits it | Name, handle, description, instructions, runtimes, the time limit of a turn (`turnTimeLimitMinutes`, as `POST` takes it too: 5 to 1440 minutes, `0` back to the deployment's default, `defaultTurnTimeLimitMinutes` from `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_WALL_CLOCK`; else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`) and `memoryAllowed` (§15.18); absent fields stay |
  | `PUT …/systems`, `…/repositories`, `…/hosts` | Who edits it | The ceiling, each part replaced as a whole (below) |
  | `PUT …/shares` | Who edits it | The groups (`groupIds`) and the members (`principalIds`) that may use it: at most 50 groups, each the company's, and at most 100 members, each a current member's principal (`SUSPENDED` included; not `LEFT`, not a service account), else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. The limits count the entries of the request (their schema's `maxItems`, as for every list of this section); within them, one named twice counts once. `principalIds` absent or null keeps the members it is shared with. `Agent.memberShares` names them (`principalId`, `userId`, `name`). Not a version |
  | `POST …/pause` (`stopRunning`), `POST …/resume` | Who edits it | The kill switch (below) |
  | `DELETE /agents/{agentId}` | Who edits it | The soft delete (below) |
  | `POST …/accept` (`version`) | Who uses it | Accepts the agent's widening up to a version (below) |
  | `GET`, `PUT`, `DELETE …/settings/me` | Who uses it | The caller's own settings: read (the defaults while none are stored), save as a whole, return to the defaults |
  | `GET …/readiness` | Who uses it | Before a conversation, storing nothing (below) |

- **Names and handles** (ADR-0034's grammar, `Handles`). A name has 1 to 100 characters, trimmed, unique among the company's live agents ignoring case (`409 DUPLICATE_AGENT_NAME`). A handle has 1 to 50 lower-case ASCII letters, digits and hyphens, neither starting nor ending with a hyphen, unique among the live agents (`409 DUPLICATE_AGENT_HANDLE`); a request's handle is lower-cased (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED` outside the rule). Without one (on a create, or blank on an update) it is made from the name: Unicode NFD without its combining marks, lower case, each run of other characters one hyphen, trimmed and cut to 50, `agent` when nothing is left, then `-2`, `-3`… while taken. Renaming keeps the handle; a deleted agent frees both. The description has at most 1000 characters (blank: none), the instructions at most 100 000; the runtimes are one or more of `CLAUDE_CODE`, `CODEX`, `PI`, `OPENCODE` without duplicates (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), the first the default.
- **The ceiling and the default modes** (the contract's §3.2 to §3.5, I2, I3). `PUT …/systems` names at most 100 systems, each once. Each is a company system (another member's personal server is unknown to the caller, `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`; one's own is `400 PERSONAL_SYSTEM`: it is added in one's settings) of a kind with tools or repositories, `JIRA_DC`, `JIRA_CLOUD`, `GITHUB`, `GITLAB`, `GIT`, `MCP` or, from package 5b, `SLACK` for its `history` (else `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`: `TEAMS`, `WEBHOOK`). A system without `tools` keeps its rows when the agent lists it already, and gets every tool its system serves and has switched on when it is new to the agent: **reads `ALLOW`, writes and unconfirmed tools `ASK`**; `tools: []` lists none (a code host for its repositories only). Named tools, at most 200 per system and each once, are `ALLOW` or `ASK`; there is no `DENY`, and a tool the agent does not list is not available (I3). A tool named with the mode its row has keeps the row, even when its system no longer serves it or switched it off (`offered` false: it lists nothing); any other must be one its system serves switched on (`400 UNKNOWN_TOOL`). Each row records the effect its mode was chosen for (`chosen_for_effect`; an unconfirmed tool's is `WRITE`), and an `ALLOW` chosen for a read counts as `ASK` once the tool writes or is unconfirmed (the contract's I19, `ToolEntry.modeFor`). **Choosing a mode anew**: a tool named with `chosenForEffect`, the effect the editor showed when the owner chose its mode, is chosen for the narrower of that and the tool's effect now (`ToolEntry.chosenFor`), changed or not, while it is served: so an "Allowed" whose tool became a write, shown as "Needs approval", is confirmed for writes by one save of `ALLOW` with `WRITE` (a widening, rank 1 to 2), and a choice made while it read never covers a write. Without `chosenForEffect` an unchanged mode keeps its row, so that a save of another tool or system, which sends every mode again, never widens it; a changed or new mode is chosen for the tool's effect now. The contract does not say what a second save of the same mode means; this reading widens only on the owner's explicit choice of that tool. One's own servers' tools in settings follow the same rule. A removed system takes its tools and repositories off the agent, and every person's switches of it (a system off, a tool's `ASK` or `OFF`) out of their settings. `PUT …/repositories` names at most 100, each once, each a repository of a code host the agent lists (`400 SYSTEM_NOT_ON_AGENT`), `READ` or `WRITE`. `PUT …/hosts` names network hosts (below), at most 100 (its schema's `maxItems`: more is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`; `409 NETWORK_HOST_LIMIT` answers only an approval's 101st host, below), each with a reason of at most 500 characters; a host named twice counts once, with its first reason. A system or repository removed elsewhere leaves every agent by the database's cascades (§7), without a version or an audit event of the agent module: it narrows, and everyone's next turn is without it.
- **Versions and what widens** (the contract's §11; the security review's row 11; `AgentVersions`, `AgentDefinition`). Every change of an agent's definition makes a version: version 1 at the create, then each `PATCH` that changes a field, each change of the ceiling and each approval for good (below). A version (`agent_version`) holds the name, handle, runtimes and ceiling as of it, and whether it **widens** the version before it (`AgentDefinition.wideningOver`): it adds a system; adds a tool, or raises one along `ASK`, `ALLOW` chosen for a read, `ALLOW` chosen for a write; adds a repository, or raises one from `READ` to `WRITE`; or adds a host that no host of the version before covers (`registry.npmjs.org` under `*.npmjs.org` widens nothing). Removing or lowering anything widens nothing, nor does a change of the name, handle, description, instructions, runtimes, the time limit of a turn or `memoryAllowed`. Shares, pausing, resuming, deleting and a change of hosts' reasons alone make no version; a request that changes nothing makes none and is not audited. A widening version is audited as `agent.widened` with what widened, and announced as `AgentWidened`.
- **The agent of a person's turns** (`Agents.effective`, `EffectiveAgent`). A person's settings record the version they accepted (`accepted_version`), and their turns use the current version narrowed by it (`AgentDefinition.narrowedBy`): a system, tool or repository only when both list it, a tool's mode and a repository's access the narrower of the two, a host of either that a host of the other covers. So narrowing reaches everyone's next turn at once, and a widening only the turns of who accepted it; the name, handle, description, instructions and runtimes always follow the current version. `EffectiveAgent.pending` is what the current version adds over the accepted one: the API's `widening` (the systems; the tools with what the accepted version had, `was` null, `ASK` or `ALLOW_FOR_READS`; the repositories with their `was`; the hosts) on `Agent` and `AgentSettings`, and `wideningPending` on `Agent` and `AgentSummary`. A person without settings uses the current version, with nothing pending. Their row is made at first use, with the defaults and the current version accepted: their first conversation (`Settings.firstUse`), first save or first acceptance.
- **Accepting** (`POST …/accept`, who uses the agent). The app names the version it showed, so that a later widening is never accepted unseen: 1 to the current version (else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). A version above the accepted one is recorded (`agent.accepted` with `previousVersion`; `AgentAccepted`) and applies from the person's next turn; one at or below it changes nothing. **The editor's own settings follow their change**: a member who changes the agent, or approves a request for good, and had accepted the version before the change accepts the new one with it, unaudited (`SettingsStore.advanceAccepted`); one who was behind stays behind, and an editor without settings, or without a principal, has nothing to follow. **`DELETE …/settings/me` keeps `accepted_version`**: every other field returns to its default, and returning to the defaults accepts no widening.
- **Settings and their checks** (the contract's §3.7, §5.2, I4, I5; `SettingsService`). One row per person per agent; only who uses the agent reads, saves and resets them, and only their own. A `PUT` is the whole settings, absent fields taking their defaults, checked against the agent's current definition:

  | Field | Rule | Else |
  |---|---|---|
  | `runtime` | One of the agent's runtimes; null: its first | `400 RUNTIME_NOT_LISTED` |
  | `model` | Null (the runtime's default), or a name of the stored pattern that the models of `ModelAccess.models(companyId, principalId)` allow (the principal's override, else the company's default), matched by `RunModelAccess.allows` (below). A person's null saves; a run paid by a budget needs a model, so their readiness and their start answer `MODEL_REQUIRED` (§15.4). A trigger's settings paid by a budget must name one at the save (`SettingsService.Checks.model`) | `400 MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, naming the models allowed; `400 MODEL_REQUIRED` for a trigger's |
  | `effort` | `DEFAULT` (the default), `LOW`, `MEDIUM`, `HIGH`, `XHIGH`, `MAX` | `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` |
  | `modelAccess`, `subscription` | `BUDGET` (the default) without a subscription, or `SUBSCRIPTION` with one (below) | `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for a subscription with `BUDGET` or none with `SUBSCRIPTION`; `400 SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_SERVING` |
  | `instructions` | Added after the agent's, at most 20 000 characters; blank: none | `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` |
  | `defaultRepositoryIds` | At most 100, each a repository of the agent; empty: all of them | `400 SETTINGS_WIDEN` |
  | `defaultProjects` | At most 20 `{systemId, key}` on systems the agent lists, the key 1 to 200 characters; a repeated one counts once | `400 SYSTEM_NOT_ON_AGENT`; `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for the key |
  | `systemsOff` | Systems the agent lists | `400 SYSTEM_NOT_ON_AGENT` |
  | `toolModes` | `ASK` or `OFF` for a tool of a system the agent lists, each once; the request schema takes no `ALLOW` | `400 SYSTEM_NOT_ON_AGENT` for the system, `400 UNKNOWN_TOOL` for a tool the agent does not list, `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for `ALLOW` |
  | `ownServers` | At most 20 of the caller's own `MCP` servers, each once; without `tools`, every tool it serves switched on, reads `ALLOW`, writes and unconfirmed tools `ASK`; with `tools`, `ALLOW` or `ASK` for tools it serves switched on (the contract's open point 6) | `400 SETTINGS_WIDEN` for a server that is not the caller's own `MCP` server; `400 UNKNOWN_TOOL` |
  | `autoRetry` | `true` or `false`; absent: `false` (§15.22, ADR-0073). Not a widening: it reaches nothing a person's retry could not | — |

  So settings narrow and never widen (the contract's I4): on the agent's systems only `OFF` and `ASK`, `ALLOW` and added servers only on the person's own MCP servers; the database checks it again (§7). When the agent changes between the save's checks and its write (a system leaves the agent while its person switches it off), the database's `agent_settings_widen_ck` refuses the write, and the save answers `400 SETTINGS_WIDEN` and stores nothing. A trigger's check is named only in the server's error, not in its message, so the PostgreSQL driver is a compile dependency: `SettingsStore.widens` reads `ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint()`. A tool row whose mode stays keeps the effect it was chosen for. A save that changes anything is audited as `settings.changed` and announced as `SettingsChanged`, a save that changes nothing neither. A change applies from each conversation's next turn, except the runtime and the model access, which a conversation fixes at its start. The model is matched by `RunModelAccess.allows`, the model module's one matcher of a key's models, which the relay (§15.4) and the dispatcher (§15.6) ask too: an empty list, `all-proxy-models` or `all-team-models` allow every model; otherwise the name itself, or a pattern ending in `*` whose prefix the name has (a lone `*` allows every model).
- **The settings in effect: `Settings.effective` and `EffectiveSettings.modeOf`** (the contract's §5.2 and §5.4, steps 3 and 4). `Settings.effective(companyId, agentId, owner)` answers the stored row, or the defaults (the agent's first runtime, no model, `DEFAULT` effort, the budget, nothing added or switched off), over the agent of the person's turns (`EffectiveAgent`). A runtime the agent no longer lists gives way to its first (`chosenRuntime` keeps the choice); default repositories and projects, switches and tool modes that the agent of their turns no longer lists are ignored. A trigger's settings (`SettingsOwner.Trigger`, package 4) are the trigger's own row (`agent_settings.trigger_id`, §15.15). The run module calls it for each turn, once per dispatch, with `SettingsOwner.Person` of the run's person, and hands that one reading to both calls of `TurnSource` (§15.6). `Settings.firstUse(companyId, agentId, principalId)` makes the row of a current member's principal when there is none, then answers the same. `EffectiveSettings.modeOf(systemId, tool, effect)` takes the tool's effect now (an MCP tool's confirmed effect, `UNKNOWN` counting as `WRITE`):

  | The tool | `ToolDecision` |
  |---|---|
  | On one of the person's own servers | Its row's mode, with I19 (`ALLOW`, `ASK`); without a row `NOT_ON_AGENT` |
  | Without a row of the agent of the person's turns | `NOT_ON_AGENT` (a call will be `TOOL_DENIED`) |
  | Its system switched off, or the tool `OFF`, in the settings | `SWITCHED_OFF` (`TOOL_DENIED`) |
  | `ASK` in the settings | `ASK` |
  | Otherwise | The agent's mode, with I19 |

  `systems()`, `tools(systemId)`, `repositories()` (of the systems not switched off), `startingRepositories()` (the defaults among them, or all of them when none is chosen or left; the contract's §5.6) and `hosts()` give the rest of a turn's view.
- **Subscriptions as host and label** (`ModelAccessSetting`). Package 2 names a subscription by the personal runner host (its presence in the company, ADR-0092) and the label the host reports (`personal_runner_credential`, §15.4), so settings keep `subscription_host_id` and `subscription_label`, not an id of their own. A save takes one only on the caller's own personal host (`RunnerHostInfo.personalOf`) that still reports the label with the runtime in effect (the chosen one, else the agent's first) among its runtimes, else `400 SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_SERVING`. The host's removal leaves the label without a host (`ModelAccessSetting.SubscriptionGone`, shown with `runnerHostId` null): no `ModelAccessChoice` pays until the person chooses again, and Tinfirm never switches to the budget by itself (M3).
- **Readiness** (`GET …/readiness`, who uses the agent; `ReadinessService`). Before a conversation, storing nothing (no settings row is made) and reading only Tinfirm's rows: `settings`, the settings in effect; `payer`, what package 2's `ModelAccess.forRun` answers for the settings' model access and runtime and the principals whose git accounts the starting workspace would carry as its code hosts bind now: `READY`, `WAITING` (`reason` `BUDGET_SPENT`, `KEY_ERROR` or `HOST_OFFLINE`, `until` a budget's reset) or `REFUSED` (`GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE`, `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`, `PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY`, and for a budget `MODEL_REQUIRED` or `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `Budget.refusal` of the settings' model; a subscription whose host was removed is `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE` without asking package 2), with `models`, the models the key may use; `systems`, per system of the agent of the person's turns that their settings do not switch off, and per own server, how a conversation would bind it now (`Accounts.bind(Personal)`, in the words of `MyAccount.binding`: `OWN` with `WRITE`, `GRANTED` with the grant and its access, `CHOOSE`, `CONNECT`, `RECONNECT_YOURS`, `RECONNECT_COMPANY`, `RECONNECT_OR_GRANT`, `UNAVAILABLE`, and `NOT_NEEDED` for an MCP server that needs no account, ADR-0071); `hosts`, whether package 2's `Hosts.eligible` leaves a runner host for that run (`runnable`, `eligible` the count, `reason` why none).
- **Tool names over 64 characters** (the contract's §11; `ToolNames.of`). A run lists a tool as `{handle}_{tool}`, with its system's handle (at most 20 characters) and the tool as its system names it (at most 64). The runtimes accept 64 characters, so a longer name becomes `{handle}_{the tool cut to fit}_{4 hex}`, exactly 64 characters, the 4 hex being the first four hex digits of the SHA-256 of the tool's name in UTF-8, so that two tools cut to the same prefix still differ. A pure function: the same handle and tool give the same name in every turn, in the agent's editor as in a run. The name is in lower case, as the runner protocol's `ToolSpec.name` wants it (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,99}$`): an MCP server's `getIssue` is listed as `crm_getissue`; the hash of a long name is taken of the tool as its system spells it, so two long names that differ in case only still differ. A conversation (package 3c's part 2, `ToolNameStore`) lists a tool as `ToolNames.of` names it. A name another tool of the conversation already holds (two tools that differ in case only, a reused handle), or a platform tool's, gets `{handle}_{the tool cut to fit}_{hex}` instead, the hex taken from the SHA-256 of the system's id and the tool. The names are kept per conversation (`conversation_tool_name`, §7), so that a call is mapped back by the name it was listed by (§15.7).
- **Network hosts** (the contract's §3.5, ADR-0044; `NetworkHosts`, `PublicSuffixes`). A host is `HOST[:PORT]`: a DNS name of labels of letters, digits and hyphens (each at most 63, at most 253 in all; `xn--` for international names), or `*.example.com` with the wildcard only as the first label and at least two labels after it; a port 1 to 65535. No scheme, path, user name, query, fragment, whitespace or IP literal (a last label of digits or `0x…`, or a second colon, is refused). Stored in lower case, without a trailing dot and without `:443`. A wildcard never covers a public suffix or shared hosting: no top-level domain, no rule of the Public Suffix List bundled with the server (`server/src/main/resources/psl/public_suffix_list.dat`, the copy of 2025-12-28 with its private section; a `*.` rule makes its parent and each name directly under it a suffix, `!` exceptions hold), and none of the shared hosts the code lists beside it (`github.io`, `pages.dev`, `vercel.app`, anything under `amazonaws.com` and others). A refused host is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` naming the rule. `NetworkHosts.covers(allowed, requested)`: the same text, or a wildcard whose suffix and port the requested host, wildcard or not, has (`*.example.com` covers `api.example.com` and `*.api.example.com`, not `example.com` or `api.example.com:8443`). `wildcardOverSuffix` tells a stored wildcard that the list now counts as over a suffix, which `run.assign.allowedHosts` leaves out (§15.6). The server reads only the bundled list; the `TINFIRM_PUBLIC_SUFFIX_*` variables of §4 are not read. From package 3c's part 3 the run module pushes the same list to the runner hosts of protocol v2 (`psl.update` after `hello`, through `PublicSuffixList`; §15.7), so that a host refuses the same wildcards as the server.
- **Pausing and deleting** (the contract's §5.1, I18, open point 12). `POST …/pause` sets `PAUSED` (`paused_at`, `paused_by`), under which the agent's queued turns wait (`AGENT_PAUSED`, §15.6) and its conversations run no approved call (§15.7: approving is `409 AGENT_PAUSED`, and an approved call that has not run yet does not run); with `stopRunning` it also cancels their queued and running runs through the port `AgentRuns.cancelRunning`, which the run module implements. `…/resume` sets `ACTIVE`. Both are idempotent, audited (`agent.paused` with the runs cancelled, `agent.resumed`), announced (`AgentPaused`, `AgentResumed`), and make no version. `DELETE /agents/{agentId}` is refused with `409 AGENT_HAS_ACTIVE_RUNS` while `AgentRuns.hasActiveRuns` (a run of the agent `QUEUED` or `RUNNING`); it sets `deleted_at` (the row, its ceiling and versions stay for its conversations' history; its name and handle are free again), deletes its shares and every person's settings, is audited as `agent.deleted` and publishes `AgentDeleted` in its transaction, in which the agent's open conversations end (`AGENT_DELETED`, §15.7).
- **Approvals for good** (the contract's §5.8, scope `AGENT`; `Agents.addRepository`, `Agents.addHost`), for the decisions of package 3c's part 2 (§15.7), whose caller checks that the approver may edit the agent. A repository joins with its access, and its code host without tools when the agent does not list it; one listed with that access or more changes nothing; more than 100 is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. A host joins with its reason and the decision (`agent_host.decision_id`) unless a host of the agent covers it already; a 101st is `409 NETWORK_HOST_LIMIT`. Each addition is a version that widens, audited with `decisionId` (`agent.repositories_changed`, `agent.hosts_changed`), and the approver's own settings follow it as an editor's do. Both answer the current version.
- **A leaving owner or member and a deleted group** (the contract's §5.7; `AgentDependents`). When a member leaves (`PrincipalDependents.memberLeaving`, in the leaving transaction), every settings row of theirs goes, and each agent they own passes to the admins: `owner_principal_id` becomes `NULL` (`created_by` keeps naming the creator), audited as `agent.passed_to_admins` by the actor who removed them, with `formerOwnerPrincipalId` and `cause` `principal.left`, and announced as `AgentPassedToAdmins`; admins edit it from then on, and its groups and members use it. Each live agent shared with the leaver on their own is no longer (`agent_member_share`, locked as a deleted group's agents are), audited as `agent.shares_changed` with `principalsRemoved` and `cause` `principal.left` and announced as `AgentShared`; a share saved while the member leaves, which the leaving cannot see, names a `LEFT` principal, and `AgentStore.shares` ignores it. One who rejoins gets a new principal and is not shared with again. A service account owns no agent and has no settings (both foreign keys admit members' principals only), so `principalDeleting` does nothing. A group's deletion (`GroupDependents.groupDeleting`, before the group goes) takes it from every agent shared with it, each audited as `agent.shares_changed` with `cause` `group.deleted` and announced as `AgentShared`; the foreign key's cascade is the safety net.
- **Events** (`AgentEvents`, ids only, published in the changing transaction after its audit): `AgentCreated`; `AgentChanged` (`version`, `widens`) for every version after the first, followed by `AgentWidened` for one that widens; `AgentShared` (`addedGroupIds`, `removedGroupIds`, `addedPrincipalIds`, `removedPrincipalIds`; `narrows()` when a group or a member was taken, on which the trigger module checks the agent's triggers again, §15.15); `AgentPaused` (`stopRunning`), `AgentResumed`; `AgentDeleted`; `AgentPassedToAdmins` (`formerOwnerPrincipalId`); `AgentAccepted` (`principalId`, `version`); `SettingsChanged` (`principalId`), also for a return to the defaults. The module listens to no event: what it hangs from reaches it through the ports above (§10) and the database's cascades.
- **Not built in package 3a**: conversations with their bindings, tools, workspaces, decisions and turns (package 3c, §15.7). Its part 1 calls `Settings.firstUse` at a conversation's start, ends conversations on `AgentDeleted`, and brings `TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES` and the conversations' own codes; its part 2 approves requests for good and brings `AGENT_PAUSED` (an approval decided while the agent is paused); its part 3 reads the mode of `open_change_request` (`EffectiveSettings.modeOf`) after a turn that pushed. Built in package 3b (§15.6): `AgentRuns`, the queue reason `AGENT_PAUSED`, and the call of `Settings.effective` at each turn.
- **Tests.** `AgentRulesTest` (what a version adds over another and narrowing, I19, steps 3 and 4 of a tool's mode, tool names, network hosts, handles), `AgentConstraintTest` (the contract's I1 to I5 and another company's rows, against PostgreSQL), `AgentIntegrationTest` (who sees, uses and edits an agent on every path; names, handles, the ceiling with its default modes and its errors; shares with groups and with members, a member in no group included; pause, resume, delete; a leaving owner, a leaving member, a deleted group; audit and events; an "Allowed" tool that became a write, confirmed for writes by one save that sends its effect and by no other; `UNKNOWN_TOOL` and `SYSTEM_NOT_ON_AGENT` with their `details`), `AgentVersionsTest` (an `ALLOW` write tool reaches a person only once they accept it, a removed tool goes at once; raised modes and accesses; additions for good from approvals), `AgentSettingsTest` (every check of a save, a model under each form `RunModelAccess.allows` reads, the database's refusal of a widening as `SETTINGS_WIDEN`, own MCP servers, `Settings.effective`, subscriptions, readiness), and `TenantIsolationTest`, which calls every agent path on an agent of company B as a member of company A.

### 15.6 Runs and turns

A turn of a conversation is one run, executed in a sandbox on a runner host (module `run`; package 3b: [the package 3 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-3-contract.md), §3.11, §5.7, §6 and §9). The module depends on `model`, `runner`, `principal`, `agent`, `audit` and `shared`, from package 6 on `company` and `system` (§10), and from ADR-0064 on `skill` (`RunnerSkillsController`, a host's download of a run's skills, §15.19). It never starts a run by itself: the conversation module (package 3c, §15.7) starts each turn through `Turns` and supplies what the turn needs through four ports (below), of which its part 1 implements `TurnSource`, `TurnEnded` and `RunVisibility`, and its part 2 `RunRequests`, takes a conversation's lock through a fifth, `ConversationLock` (§10's lock order), and from ADR-0060 hears a host's answer to a person's message sent to a running turn through a sixth, `TurnSteered` (steering). The tables are in §7, the audit actions in §8, the variables in §4.

- **Turns** (`Turns`; every method needs an open transaction, the caller's, and runs in its tenant context). `Turns.start(NewTurn)` creates the turn's run `QUEUED` with the branch `agent/{key}`, audits `run.started` and nudges the dispatcher after the commit. It answers the run's id, or empty while the conversation has a `QUEUED` or `RUNNING` run: the insert is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` against `run_one_active_per_conversation_uq` (I11), so a race never aborts the caller's transaction, and the turn waits. `NewTurn` names the conversation's id and key (1 to 93 characters, so that the branch fits 100), the agent, the runtime (fixed at the conversation's start, open point 3), the reason (`FIRST`, `MESSAGE`, `DECIDED`, `CONNECTED`, `RETRY`; from package 4 `EVENT` and `REVIEW_RETURNED`, §15.15), the turn's number, the acting and the paying principal, the model access (`ModelAccessChoice`) and the user whose act started it. `Turns.cancelActive(companyId, conversationId)` cancels the conversation's active run and answers its id: a `QUEUED` run ends `CANCELLED` at once (one already assigned also gets `run.cancel` after the commit); a `RUNNING` run gets a durable cancel command (`run_command`, sent again whenever its host reconnects) and `run.cancel`, and ends when its host reports `run.finished`. Audited as `run.cancelled`. `Turns.steer(companyId, conversationId, steerId, text)` (ADR-0060) gives a person's message to the conversation's running turn: under the conversation's lock, when its active run is `RUNNING` on a connected host whose `hello.features` lists `run.steer` (`RunnerMessages.FEATURE_RUN_STEER`), the host gets `run.steer` after the commit (the run, its epoch, `steerId`, the message's id, and the text, 1 to 110 000 characters, `RunnerMessages.MAX_STEER_TEXT`, else `IllegalArgumentException`), and the answer is the run's id; else it is empty and nothing is sent. A steer is not sent again after a reconnect: one the host never answers waits for the next turn (`TurnStarter`). The rest of the API: `RunReads` (`find`, `activeOf`, `ofConversation`, `views` (the Runs API's `Run` of a conversation's runs, for its page), `activeOnHost`, `lastPush` (the run and head commit of the conversation's latest push to a repository, from `run_repository`, but for a run excepted)), `NewTurn`, `RunInfo`, `ActiveRun`, `RunStatus`, `TurnReason`, `QueueReasonCode`, and the events of `RunEvents` (`RunAccepted`, `RunQueueReasonChanged` when a queued run's code changes, `RunResumed`; from package 6 `RunEnded`), published in the changing transaction for the conversation's turn status. A run's end goes to `TurnEnded` (below).
- **The dispatcher** (`RunDispatcher`), on `worker` replicas: every 2 s (`tinfirm.run.dispatch-interval`) and, after the commit, when a run is queued or a slot is freed, a host confirms its capacity (`host.config.applied`), a principal's key is `OK` again (`ModelKeyStatusChanged`), a principal is resumed (`PrincipalResumed`), an agent is resumed (`AgentResumed`), the company's runs are resumed (`RunsResumed`) or a system is resumed (`SystemResumed`; the dispatcher's step 0 is §15.13's). A pass first takes back the assignments whose `run.accepted` is overdue, then walks the whole queue oldest first, in pages of 100 from `run_dispatch_queue`, so that runs waiting for an offline host never starve younger ones. Each run is locked `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` in its company's tenant context, after its conversation's row (the lock order, §10), and checked in the contract's order; the first check that fails gives its queue reason, which is written only when it changes:

  | Step | Asks | Else |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1. The agent | `Agents.find`: live, `ACTIVE`, still listing the conversation's runtime | Deleted: the run ends `CANCELLED`. Paused: it waits `AGENT_PAUSED` (I18), until `AgentResumed` starts a pass. A runtime the agent no longer lists: the run ends `FAILED`, `RUNTIME_REMOVED` (open point 3) |
  | | A `TurnSource` | It waits `OTHER` |
  | | The run's model access | A subscription whose host was removed: it waits `MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE`, `reason` `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE` |
  | 2. The settings | `Settings.effective(companyId, agentId, SettingsOwner.Person(acting principal))`, once per dispatch: that one reading goes to both calls of `TurnSource` and into `run.assign` | |
  | 3. The conversation | `TurnSource.plan` | `AccountRequired`: it waits `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED`, with the port's message. `Cancel`: the run ends `CANCELLED` |
  | 4. Who pays | `ModelAccess.forRun` with the principals whose accounts the workspace's git tokens are (§15.4) | `Wait`: `BUDGET_SPENT` (`retryAt` the reset), `KEY_ERROR`, `HOST_OFFLINE`. `Refuse`: `MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE`, with package 2's refusal as `reason`. A budget's settings that name no model, or one its key does not allow (`RunModelAccess.Budget.refusal`): the run ends `FAILED`, `MODEL_REQUIRED` or `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED` |
  | 5. Where | `Hosts.eligible` (M4, I13) | `Nowhere`: `NO_HOST_FOR_CREDENTIALS`. No eligible host: `NO_HOST_FOR_CREDENTIALS` when the company has hosts for the runtime but none may carry the run's credentials, else `NO_RUNNER_HOST` with package 2's `whyNone` |
  | 6. The slots | A subscription's label still reported by its host; then the eligible hosts, locked in the order of their ids so that concurrent dispatchers, on every replica, count one at a time, and under those locks a free slot of the subscription's pool (two conversations on a pool of one never both start) and the hosts' free slots, a budget's run only on hosts of the minimum runner version (§15.4) | `MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE` (`SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`), `CREDENTIAL_POOL_FULL`, `NO_RUNNER_HOST` |
  | 7. The host | `HostSelection`: connected and configured on its current connection, of protocol v2, not draining, with a free slot under its dispatch limit; the conversation's previous host first (open point 13), else the most free slots, then the name | Of one eligible host: `RUNNER_HOST_OFFLINE`, `RUNNER_HOST_DRAINING` or `RUNNER_HOST_FULL`; of several: `NO_RUNNER_HOST`, or `RUNNER_HOST_FULL` with the slots summed. Connected hosts of protocol v1 only: `OTHER` |
  | 8. Assign | A new epoch (below) | |

- **Queue reasons and failure classes.** A queued run's `queueReason` has its code, `since` (which moves only with the code), a text in English, and the facts its code names. The text never holds a time: `retryAt` and `lastSeenAt` do, for the reader's clock and locale (`BUDGET_SPENT`: "The budget that pays for this conversation is spent; the turn goes on once it resets.", the reset in `retryAt`). `MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE` carries package 2's refusal as `reason` (`GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE`, `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`, …). `HOST_OFFLINE` is a subscription's personal host that is not connected (package 2's `Wait`); `RUNNER_HOST_OFFLINE` is the one eligible host when it is not connected and configured. `ASSIGNED`: sent, awaiting `run.accepted`. A host's `run.rejected` sends the run back to the queue until `retryAt`, `TINFIRM_RUN_REJECT_BACKOFF` later: `PREVIOUS_RUN_ACTIVE` for the host's `TASK_RUN_ACTIVE` (the conversation's previous turn still ends there), else `RUNNER_HOST_REJECTED` with the host's code and reason. `RETRY_SCHEDULED` (§15.22): an automatic retry, inserted `QUEUED` with `dispatch_after` at its time and the reason's `retryAt`, `used` (its number) and `limit` (how many in a row at most), which the dispatcher does not look at before. `OTHER` covers a missing `TurnSource`, connected hosts that speak only protocol v1, and a preparation of the assignment that failed (below). A run ends `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELLED` or `LOST`; a failure class is the runner's, `LOST` (with the cause `LEASE_EXPIRED`, `HOST_RESTARTED` or `STALE_ON_RECONNECT`), or the server's `RUNTIME_REMOVED`, `MODEL_REQUIRED`, `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, and `INFRA` for a turn whose content failed as it would again (below). A run that fails with `USAGE_LIMIT` gets `retryAt` in its `TurnEnd`: the reset of the payer's spent budget as `forRun` answers it, else the latest reset of the run's `RATE_LIMIT` events; the turn is not retried by itself (open point 8). The turn's `SYSTEM` note, stored text read in any locale, writes that reset in one fixed form in UTC ("The budget resets on 12 Nov at 00:00 UTC; retry the turn then."), and leaves it out when it is not known.
- **The assignment**, in three phases, so that no transaction waits on git tokens or the relay:
  1. The dispatching transaction gives the run a new epoch, its host and the host's kind, `ASSIGNED`, and a preparation lease (`tinfirm.run.prepare-timeout`, 2 minutes), during which the accept timeout leaves the run alone.
  2. Outside any transaction, on a virtual thread: the turn's content from `TurnSource.content` (`NotReady` sends the run back to the queue with `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED`, or ends it `CANCELLED`; `Later`, a passing failure nobody needs to act on, sends it back with `OTHER` and its message after `TINFIRM_RUN_REJECT_BACKOFF`, so that it does not spin); then, for a budget, the relay's one-run token from `ModelRelay.open`, in a short transaction of its own, in the runtime's format (`openai` for Codex, `anthropic` for the others), and for a subscription its label. A failure of the relay, and a failure of the content that may pass (the database or its connections, a lock, a timeout, a read: `RunDelivery.transientFailure`), sends the run back to the queue, `OTHER`, after `TINFIRM_RUN_REJECT_BACKOFF`. Any other failure of the content (a bug, data the code cannot handle) would fail the same way on every pass, so it ends the run `FAILED` (`INFRA`, "Tinfirm could not prepare the turn: …", which names the exception's class and nothing of its message), and the conversation shows it as for any failed turn (a `SYSTEM` message). Then `run.assign` of protocol v2 is built.
  3. A short transaction checks that the run is still that assignment (`QUEUED`, the epoch, the host), writes its `run_repository` rows in place of an earlier assignment's, starts the accept timeout from now, and audits `run.assigned`; the frame is sent after the commit. An assignment that is no longer current sends nothing and revokes the token it opened, that epoch's only (`ModelRelay.revoke(companyId, runId, epoch)`): its run ended meanwhile, or went back to the queue (a `hello` that did not list it while it was prepared, or the accept timeout after a preparation longer than its lease) and may already run under a later assignment, whose token stays, as does the token of a run lost by lease expiry, which its host may bring back.
