A host that reconnected with another protocol version, and a frame that cannot be sent, are left to the accept timeout.

  `run.assign` carries `workItem.id` and `job.id`, both the conversation's id (its own session home on a host, I12), `workItem.key` and the content's title; in every repository the branch `agent/{key}` (the key lower-cased, each run of characters other than `a-z0-9._-` one hyphen, cut to fit 100 characters and trimmed of hyphens and dots; `agent/work` when nothing is left) and a directory under `/workspace` made of its name (lower-case letters, digits and hyphens, at most 40, `repo` when nothing is left, `-2`, `-3`… within the run); `resumeSessionId` of the conversation's latest earlier run that reported a session, with the content's resume prompt; `agent` with the agent's name, the runtime, and the settings' model and effort (left out at `DEFAULT`); the limits (the agent's time limit of a turn, else `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_WALL_CLOCK`; `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_TURNS`); the content's workspace with its git tokens, requestable repositories, tools, platform tools and files; `allowedHosts`: the settings' hosts (`EffectiveSettings.hosts`, the agent as the person accepted it) and the content's granted hosts, without those `NetworkHosts.wildcardOverSuffix` refuses, distinct and sorted, cut at 200 with a WARN; and `modelAccess` (the relay's root and token) or `credentialLabel`. It carries no other secret (I14).
- **The runner protocol** (`RunnerSessionListener`). Runs go only to hosts of protocol v2. The runner module still accepts v1 hosts (§14.10), whose `run.ask`, `run.repository_request` and `run.task_create` are answered `REJECTED` / `RUN_NOT_ACTIVE`. Every run frame but a platform request (below) is handled in one transaction in the host's company: a run the server does not know, or another host's, gets `error` `UNKNOWN_RUN`; a frame of another epoch gets `STALE_EPOCH` and is ignored (with `run.cancel` for `run.accepted` and `run.heartbeat`). `run.accepted` makes the run `RUNNING` with a lease (`RunAccepted`); `run.rejected` sends it back as above; `run.event` of an active run is stored once per sequence number, at most `TINFIRM_RUN_MAX_EVENTS_PER_RUN` per run (at the cap another event deletes the oldest `LOG` event, and a `LOG` event, or any event once none is left, is dropped), and a `USAGE` event's cumulative totals become the run's usage; an event of a run that ended (a host that lags behind the run's end, or sends its last frames again after a reconnect) is dropped, counted in `tinfirm_run_late_events_total` and logged at DEBUG; `run.finished` ends the run with the host's outcome, its git results (a read-only repository is never counted as pushed) and its `/output` files, and counts `tinfirm_runs_finished_total`. `run.steer.ack` (ADR-0060; protocol v2's optional feature `run.steer`, `$defs/RunSteer` and `$defs/RunSteerAck` of `protocol/runner-v2.schema.json`, no new version) answers each `run.steer` once, before the run's `run.finished`: `DELIVERED`, the agent has the text in the turn it runs, or `REFUSED` with a code (`NOT_SUPPORTED`, `NOT_RUNNING`, `FAILED`); for a run still `RUNNING` it goes to `TurnSteered.steered`, and an answer about a run that is no longer running changes nothing (`RunnerSessionHandler`). On `hello` the server reconciles the host's runs: a running run the host reports with its epoch keeps a fresh lease; one it no longer reports is `LOST` (`HOST_RESTARTED`); one it reports with another epoch, or after its lease and the resume window are over, is `LOST` (`STALE_ON_RECONNECT`) and cancelled; an assignment it never accepted goes back to the queue, unless the host reports it running, which makes it `RUNNING`; a run the server does not know, or cannot resume, is cancelled; pending cancel commands are sent again. The protocol has no ack of `run.finished`, so a runner host keeps a finished run, lists it in `hello` and writes its events' resend window and its end again after every reconnect, until that end was written on a connection that then stayed up for 3 minutes (`TINFIRM_RUNNER_FINISHED_RETENTION` on the host; time without a connection does not count): longer than the reconnect grace and the host's 45-s silence limit, so that an end lost with a dying connection, or with a server that went down before it read it, is not lost. The server cannot tell such an end from the first: the run it still holds `RUNNING` with that epoch (kept by the reconnect grace and the `hello`, or resumed within the resume window after a loss by lease expiry) ends with the host's outcome, and the end of a run that already ended is ignored. From package 3c's part 3, a v2 host whose `hello.publicSuffixList` is not the server's list, or that holds none, first gets `psl.update` with it (`PublicSuffixPush`, §15.7).
- **Leases, epochs and the reaper.** The epoch is a run's fencing token: every assignment and every return to the queue moves it on, frames of an earlier one are stale, and the relay's token answers only for its own. A `RUNNING` run holds a lease of `TINFIRM_RUNNER_LEASE_TTL` (30 s), renewed by its heartbeats, events and requests and by a repeated `run.accepted`; any frame of a host renews all its running runs, at most once per sixth of the lease. An assigned run without `run.accepted` goes back to the queue `TINFIRM_RUN_ACCEPT_TIMEOUT` (10 s) after it was sent, never while it is prepared or in a reconnect grace. **The reconnect grace** (`TINFIRM_RUNNER_RECONNECT_GRACE`, 2 minutes): when a host's last connection closes, and at the server's start (`ReconnectGrace`, on every replica, before the reaper, the dispatcher and the web server begin), every run that holds a slot keeps a lease, or an assigned run an accept deadline, of at least that long, never a shorter one. **The reaper** (`RunReaper`, on `worker` replicas every 5 s, `tinfirm.run.reaper-interval`) marks a `RUNNING` run whose lease expired `LOST` (`LEASE_EXPIRED`), unless its host's connection sent a frame within the lease time, which renews the lease instead. A run lost by lease expiry is **resumed** when its host shows, by `hello`, `run.heartbeat` or `run.finished` with the run's epoch, that it still runs it, within `TINFIRM_RUNNER_LOST_RESUME_WINDOW` (10 minutes; `PT0S` turns resuming off), while the run is its conversation's latest and `TurnEnded.turnResumed` takes the turn back, both decided under the conversation's lock (§10; `RunResumption` takes it again at no cost, after the frame's handler took it before the run's): a turn the person starts at that moment, which locks the conversation too (`Turns.start`), is either the latest already or starts after the resumption and waits for its run, so that the two never meet in `run_one_active_per_conversation_uq` and abort the `hello`: it is `RUNNING` again with what its loss wrote undone and its commands pending again, `run.resumed` follows its `run.finished` in the audit log, and `RunResumed` is published; otherwise its host is told to cancel it. The candidates across companies come from the lookups of §7, each checked again under its row lock.
- **The end of a run** (`RunCompletion`), in one transaction with all that follows: the row becomes final (status, summary and `finish` result, usage, the counts of tool calls, messages and thoughts, what the relay passed); each repository gets its git result; pending commands are done; the relay's token is revoked, but for a run lost by lease expiry; `ModelAccess.refresh` reads the payer's spend again after the commit, after every run's end; `TurnEnded.turnEnded` is told; `run.finished` is audited; a run lost for another cause than its lease is cancelled on its host after the commit; and the freed slot starts a dispatch pass. **The relay's token** (§15.4, M7): `ModelRelay.open` revokes the run's tokens of earlier epochs at each assignment, never a later one's, a stale assignment revokes its own, and `RunModelTokens.active` answers for a token only while its run is `RUNNING` on its host with its epoch. A run lost by lease expiry keeps its token unrevoked, since its host may still bring it back: the token answers nothing while the run is `LOST`, answers again once the run is resumed, and the reaper revokes it after the resume window (`run_lost_unrevoked`).
- **The ports for package 3c.** The conversation module depends on this one, so the run module looks its ports up when used (`ObjectProvider`, `RunPorts`) and falls back to a stand-in for each port no bean implements. The conversation module implements them all (§15.7: its part 1 `TurnSource`, `TurnEnded` and `RunVisibility`, its part 2 `RunRequests`, and from ADR-0060 `TurnSteered`, in `TurnService`), so the stand-ins answer only in a context without the conversation module:

  | Port | Called | Answers | Stand-in, without a bean |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `TurnSource.plan(turn, settings)` | In the dispatching transaction, in the run's company, as SYSTEM; it reads, calls nothing outside the server and reveals nothing. The conversation's `plan` deviates in one point: it resolves a binding that a workspace repository's system still lacks, as the turn's request did when it created the run, which writes the binding row with its audit event, its message and its event (§15.7) | `Ready` with the principals whose accounts the workspace's git tokens are (for `forRun` and `Hosts.eligible`, open point 7), `AccountRequired(message)` or `Cancel(message)` | No `TurnSource`: a run waits `OTHER` |
  | `TurnSource.content(turn, placement, settings)` | Outside any transaction, as SYSTEM, once the run is assigned; `Placement` names the host, its kind, the new epoch and the previous run with a session | `Content`: the title, the prompt and the resume prompt; the workspace (each repository's binding, `readOnly` with its cause `AGENT`, `GRANT` or `REQUEST`, the git token, the commits' author, a `defaultBranch` that is never null); the requestable repositories; the tools (named with `ToolNames.of`); the platform tools (null: all); the conversation's granted hosts; the files. Within `run.assign`'s limits: 20 repositories, 200 tools, 100 granted hosts, 20 platform tools, 1000 files. Or `NotReady` with `AccountRequired` or `Cancel`, or `Later` with a message (a workspace token that could not be got while its binding holds) | |
  | `RunRequests.request(run, kind, requestId, payload)` | `ASK`, `ACCESS`, `ARTIFACT` and `NETWORK`: inside the transaction that checked the run and holds its row lock, as SYSTEM; the ack is sent after the commit | The `run.request.ack` | `REJECTED` / `REQUEST_UNAVAILABLE` |
  | `RunRequests.call(run, requestId, payload)` | `TOOL`: outside any transaction, on a virtual thread, as SYSTEM. Every request is handed over, a request id sent again (after a reconnect, to another replica) too: the run module keeps nothing of it, and the implementation's call journal in the database (package R, §15.14) never runs a call twice. The implementation answers within `TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT` itself, always (the host sends a request again only after a reconnect); the run module bounds it 2 seconds longer, within the host's wait of the timeout and 5 seconds (past it, and for an implementation that fails or answers null, `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`, since the call may have run; the thread is interrupted). The answer goes to the host's current connection | The `run.request.ack` | `REJECTED` / `UNKNOWN_TOOL`: a run then lists no tools |
  | `TurnEnded.turnEnded(TurnEnd)` | In the transaction that ended the run, its row final and locked, as the actor of the end; the conversation may start its next turn in it | `TurnEnd`: the status, failure class and message, summary, result, `retryAt`, the workspace with each repository's git result, the `/output` files, the lost cause | Nobody is told |
  | `TurnEnded.turnResumed(run)` | Before a run lost by lease expiry is resumed | Whether the conversation takes the turn back | Every lost run is taken back |
  | `TurnSteered.steered(run, steerId, delivered, code)` (ADR-0060) | In the transaction that handles `run.steer.ack` for a run still `RUNNING`, which locked the conversation's row before the run's | Nothing: the conversation makes the message the run's (`DELIVERED`) or lets it wait for the next turn (§15.7) | Nobody hears it; nothing sends a steer without the conversation module either |
  | `RunVisibility.maySee`, `read`, `mayCancel` | Every call of the Runs API, in its transaction (`read` for each read that shows the run: `RUN`, `EVENTS`, and `STREAM` once, when the stream opens, before the transaction locks anything; a stream's poll checks `maySee` again, `RunService.stillVisible`, recording nothing) | Whether the caller may see the run, and cancel it; `read` also audits OWNER's read of another person's run (`conversation.read`, §15.7) | The run's person (`acting_principal_id`), OWNER sees and cancels it; ADMIN, every other member and an instance admin who is not a member do not (§6, I17); `read` is `maySee`, unaudited |

  Before `RunRequests` the run module checks what is its own, in this order: the kind is one of the protocol's, else `INVALID_REQUEST`; the run is `RUNNING` on this host with this epoch, else `RUN_NOT_ACTIVE` (the run's lease is renewed); the payload is a JSON object, else `INVALID_REQUEST`. Every request gets exactly one ack with its id. The codes of `run.request.ack` that package 3b answers itself are `REQUEST_UNAVAILABLE`, `UNKNOWN_TOOL`, `INVALID_REQUEST` and `RUN_NOT_ACTIVE` (a call it cuts off is a `DONE` result with `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`, above); they are protocol codes, not problem codes of the REST API, and the protocol schema lists them only as examples. Package 3c's part 2 answers the rest (§6, §15.7).
- **The ports of other modules it implements.** `model.RunModelTokens` (`active`, one indexed read per relayed request); `runner.RunnerActivity` (`activeRunsByHost`: runs running or assigned and awaiting `run.accepted`, a host's `activeRuns`; `hasActiveRuns`: a `QUEUED` or `RUNNING` run assigned to the host or waiting for it as a subscription's host, which keeps the host from being removed, `409 RUNNER_HOST_HAS_ACTIVE_RUNS`); `runner.RunnerSessionListener` (above); and `agent.AgentRuns`, in the agent's transaction after its row is locked: `hasActiveRuns` is true while a run of the agent is `QUEUED` or `RUNNING`, so `DELETE /agents/{agentId}` answers `409 AGENT_HAS_ACTIVE_RUNS`; `cancelRunning` (a pause with `stopRunning`) cancels each such run, oldest first, through `TurnStarter.cancel` as a person's cancel does, each audited as `run.cancelled`, and answers their ids, which `agent.paused` records as `runsCancelled`.
- **The Runs API** (tag `Runs`). `GET /runs/{runId}`; `POST /runs/{runId}/cancel` (idempotent, `200` with the run); `GET /runs/{runId}/events?afterSeq=&types=&limit=` (oldest first; `types` only events of those types, their sequence numbers keep the gaps where the others are, so that a conversation reads what a folded turn said, `AGENT_MESSAGE` and `USAGE`, without its tool calls; `limit` 1 to 1000, 500 by default; `complete` once the run ended and no later event exists); `GET /runs/{runId}/events/stream`, Server-Sent Events: a `run-status` with the run first, then `run-event` frames, the only ones with an `id`, replayed after the later of `afterSeq` and `Last-Event-ID` and then live, another `run-status` when the status or a queued run's reason changes, a keep-alive comment every `tinfirm.run.stream-keep-alive` (15 s), a final `run-status` when the run ends, closed after `TINFIRM_RUN_STREAM_TIMEOUT`, when the client reconnects with `Last-Event-ID`. Every path needs `VIEW_COMPANY`, then `RunVisibility.maySee`: to anyone else the run is `404`, as another company's is. There is no permission of its own (§6). A cancel `mayCancel` refuses is `403`. A run names its person and payer with their names, the model access and a subscription's label, its host and the host's kind, its queue reason, failure, summary and result, branch and repositories, usage, the relay's counters, the counts and the resume outcome. There is no list of runs and no manual start: runs are turns, and `RunReads.views` gives a conversation's page its runs.
- **One replica.** The run stream's signals (`RunEventHub`) and the conversation stream's (`ConversationSignals`) are in process: a stream hears only the changes its own replica commits, an accepted limit while the server runs as one replica (several would need PostgreSQL's `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` for both).
- **The conversation stream** (`ConversationStreams`). `GET /conversations/{id}/stream`, Server-Sent Events, signals only, never content: a `conversation-status` first (`ConversationStatus`: state, ended reason, turn status, what it waits on and why and since when, the active run, the turn count), then after each committed change a `conversation-changed` (`ConversationChanged`) with its `parts` (`STATUS`, `MESSAGES`, `TURNS`, `DECISIONS`, `BINDINGS`, `OUTPUTS`, `ATTACHMENTS`, `WORKSPACE`; one event per transaction, none for one rolled back), and `conversation-status` again when that summary changed, so the client follows the active run's own stream; a keep-alive comment every `tinfirm.run.stream-keep-alive`; a final `conversation-status` once the conversation ended (the stream of an ended conversation is that one event), closed after `TINFIRM_RUN_STREAM_TIMEOUT`. No event has an `id`: on a signal, and on a reconnect, the client reads the conversation again (`GET /conversations/{id}`). It is opened as that read answers (404 to whoever does not see the conversation in the app; `403 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` to a suspended member), but records no `conversation.read`: the client's reads do, as with polling. Every signal and every keep-alive checks again, as the caller, `CompanyAccessGuard.recheck` and the conversation's visibility; a caller removed, suspended or lowered gets no event more and the stream closes. The signals (`ConversationSignals.changed`, after the commit) come from the module's writes (`ConversationStore`: a rename, an end, a turn started, ended, resumed or failed, what it waits on, its workspace and hosts, its messages; the outputs; the attachments; a decision's result and approver) and from the events it publishes or hears (bindings bound, waiting or gone, a rebinding; decisions raised, decided, closed, reviewed; the run module's `RunAccepted`, `RunQueueReasonChanged`, `RunResumed`).
- **Not built in package 3b**, left to package 3c and built there (§15.7). Its part 1 built the conversation module with three of the four ports, the foreign keys of `run.conversation_id` and `run_repository.binding_id` (`V005`, §7), and a workspace repository's `defaultBranch`, which falls back to `main` where `system_repository.default_branch` is null (§15.7). Its part 2 built the fourth, `RunRequests`: a run's platform requests and its tool calls. Its part 3 built the runner host's file endpoints (the download of a conversation's files and the upload of the agent's, on `RunReads.find` and `activeOnHost`), change requests after a push (from the git results and `/output` files that `TurnEnd` carries), and the push of the Public Suffix List (above), all in §15.7.
- **Tests.** `RunConstraintTest` (I10, I11, the agent of the run's company, the model access, a read-only repository's cause, a running run's lease, against PostgreSQL; another company's runs, events and commands invisible; the runtime role deletes no run), `HostSelectionTest` (the host as a table), `BranchesTest` (the branch and the directories), `RunAssignmentTest` (a snapshot of `run.assign`: I12, I14, the relay's format per runtime, the protocol's limits, which failures to prepare the content go back to the queue), `RunDispatchIntegrationTest` (a turn on a company host with the relay's token, accepted while the run is `RUNNING` and refused after; the person's settings and the hosts in `run.assign`; the dispatcher's order; conversations in parallel, one run at a time each; the previous host and session; a rejection with a new epoch and token; a delivery that went stale while it was prepared, the run going on under a later assignment whose token still answers; a subscription on its owner's host, and two dispatchers that count a pool of one after the other; a spent budget's `retryAt` and the payer's waiting turns; a content that fails as it would again ends the run `INFRA`), `RunLeaseIntegrationTest` (a lost run resumed and its token answering again, a turn not taken back and its token revoked after the window, a resumption that meets the person's next turn, a run's end sent again after the server's restart, the reconnect grace at a disconnect and at the server's start, a restarted host, stale and foreign frames), `RunCancelIntegrationTest` (who sees and cancels a run; a queued, a running and an assigned run cancelled), `RunRequestIntegrationTest` (one ack per request, the run module's checks, `TOOL` outside any transaction, every request handed to the port, `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` for a port's null, failure or overrun of the timeout and its grace), `RunEventIntegrationTest` (events stored, paged and streamed; an event of a run that ended dropped), `RunAgentIntegrationTest` (`AgentRuns`: pause, `stopRunning`, delete, a deleted agent's turn), `RunPortsTest` (the stand-ins, the queue reason's stored form), from package 3c's part 3 `PublicSuffixPushIntegrationTest` (§15.7), from ADR-0060 `ConversationSteeringIntegrationTest` (§15.7, `run.steer` and its ack end to end) and `RunnerProtocolSchemaTest` (the frames against the v2 schema), and `TenantIsolationTest`, which calls the Runs paths with `{runId}` of a run of company B as a member of company A. `TestTurns`, a component in every integration test's context, is the switch between the run module's tests and the conversation module's: it is the `@Primary` bean of `TurnSource`, `TurnEnded` and `RunRequests` there, but answers only for the conversations it scripts (`TestTurns.conversation`, which `RunTestBench` calls for every turn it starts as the conversation module would), and hands every other conversation to the real beans, so that the conversation module's tests run on its own ports; a scripted conversation's requests get the stand-ins' answers unless the test scripts them. Every run row needs its conversation since `V005`: `TestConversations.bare` writes a bare one as the runtime role (a member's Tinfirm thread paid by their budget, without a subject, a workspace or a turn) for `RunTestBench`, `RunConstraintTest`, `TestRuns` and `TenantIsolationTest`. `TestRuns` writes run rows directly as the runtime role (a run `RUNNING` on a host with an epoch, or ended), each with a bare conversation of its own, for the model relay's tests and the tenant isolation test; `TestRuns.agent` makes the bare agent row such a run names. From package 3c's part 3, `TestRuns.running` and `ended` write the turns of an existing conversation, and `TestConversations` its subject, a `BOUND` binding, a gone one and its end, for part 3's tests of the hooks (§15.7); from its part 2, `TestRuns.ofConversation` a turn of an existing conversation `RUNNING` without a host, or ended, and `TestConversations.question` an open question to its person.

### 15.7 Conversations

One agent's work for one person in one place (module `conversation`; package 3c: [the package 3 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-3-contract.md), §3.8 to §3.13, §5.3, §5.6, §5.7, §5.9, §5.10 and §6 to §9). A conversation replaces the restart's work item and its threads (§14.7): one agent, one place (a Tinfirm thread in package 3), one person it acts for, who pays for it and who sees it with OWNER, and its runtime, model access and accounts fixed for its life. Each turn is one of package 3b's runs (§15.6). Package 3c is built in three parts: **part 1**, the conversation core (conversations, messages, turns, bindings, the workspace, the prompt, visibility); **part 2**, decisions, the tools of a run, the platform requests (`RunRequests`), parked calls and the inbox; and **part 3**, files, outputs and change requests (with the runner host's file endpoints, and the push of the Public Suffix List to hosts in the run module). The parts meet at the seams below. The module depends on `run`, `agent`, `account`, `system`, `principal`, `company`, `model`, `runner`, `vault`, `audit` and `shared` (§10); its variables are part 3's, the limits of files and the watch of change requests, and package R's, the call journal's retention and the submission keys' lifetime (§4). The permissions and codes are in §6, the tables in §7, the audit actions in §8, the API, events and ports in §10.

- **Who sees and acts** (`Conversations.maySee`, `isPerson`; the contract's §5.10, I15, I17). Every path needs `VIEW_COMPANY` and nothing more (but a file acknowledged as a credential, which needs `MANAGE_SECRETS`, below):

  | Caller | Sees it, with its messages, runs, bindings, decisions, outputs and files | Messages, renames, chooses and continues a binding, decides, adds and deletes files | Cancels, retries, ends |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Its person | yes | yes | yes |
  | OWNER (not its person) | yes, in full (`seenByOwner`) | no: `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND` | yes |
  | ADMIN, every other member, and an instance admin who is not a member (ADMIN's view, §6) | no: `404` on every path | no | no |

  A run follows its conversation: the module implements `RunVisibility`, whose `maySee` and `mayCancel` are this table's "Sees it" column, so who sees a conversation sees and cancels its runs (§6). `GET /conversations` and `GET /subjects` list the caller's own conversations, and OWNER's every conversation; `GET /conversations?personId=` narrows OWNER's list to one person.

  **OWNER's reads are audited** (`OwnerReads`; the security review of the redesign, row 18). A read by OWNER of a conversation of another person, or of what belongs to it, is `conversation.read` (§8) with `conversationId`, `personPrincipalId` and `what`, on every read path: the conversation (`ConversationService.get`, and the conversation OWNER's cancel, retry and end answer), the list, a run, its events and its stream (`RunVisibility.read`, which the Runs API calls for each read, the stream once, when it opens), the files and a file's content, and the decisions. Not recorded: the person's own reads, OWNER's own conversations, `GET /subjects` (counts per subject) and the inbox (only the caller's own conversations, §6). A page of `GET /conversations` is recorded **once per person it lists**, naming the person and no conversation: the list shows summaries (title, subject, state), what the person learns is that OWNER looked at their conversations, and one record per conversation would multiply a page view by its size; the reads of a conversation's content are each recorded on their own. A page of `GET /decisions` is recorded once per conversation it lists, since a decision carries content (its question, answer, note, result). **Every read is recorded, with no throttle** (package 6, open point 9): two reads in a row are two records, and a polling page writes one per poll (§8). The records of a request run in one transaction of their own, before the reading transaction locks anything (§10). The app shows the person a standing notice (`seenByOwner`, the API's description) on the web app's conversation page (`app-conversation-reads-button`).
- **Starting** (`POST /conversations`; the contract's §3.8, §5.6, §5.7). The caller needs a principal in the company (else `404`) and the use of a live agent (`404` when they do not see it, `403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE` when they see but do not use it, §15.5). They are its person and its payer (I10).
  - **The message** has 1 to 100 000 characters once trimmed (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). It is the conversation's first `PERSON` message and the request of its first turn.
  - **The subject**, optional, is a reference, not a copy (open point 4): a company system of kind `JIRA_DC`, `JIRA_CLOUD`, `GITHUB` or `GITLAB`, a key of 1 to 200 characters, an http(s) link without user info of at most 2000 characters, and a title of at most 500; anything else is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, a personal MCP server included.
  - **What it fixes.** `Settings.firstUse` makes the person's settings row of the agent when there is none (§15.5); the conversation takes their runtime and model access then (a budget, or a subscription by host and label) and keeps both for its life (open point 3, I10), whatever the settings say later.
  - **The key** is `{base}-{n}`, unique in the company and never reused (I12): `base` is the subject's key as a slug and the agent's handle (`shop-123-dev`), or the handle alone without a subject or when the slug is empty, cut to 80 characters and trimmed of hyphens; `n` counts from 1 per base (`conversation_key_counter`, §7). The slug is the key in NFKD without combining marks, lower case, each run of characters other than `a-z0-9` one hyphen, trimmed of hyphens. The branch is `agent/{key}`.
  - **The title** is the first non-empty line of the message, trimmed, without its leading `#`s and the space after them, cut to 200 characters; `Conversation` when nothing is left. The person renames it (`PATCH`, 1 to 200 characters).
  - **The workspace** (the contract's §5.6): `repositoryIds`, each one of the repositories of the agent of the person's turns (else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), at most 20, and an empty list for none; without `repositoryIds`, the settings' default repositories, else every repository of the agent (`EffectiveSettings.startingRepositories`). More than 20 without `repositoryIds` is `409 TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES`. Each entry is `AGENT`, read-only when the agent's access is `READ`.
  - **Files** (`attachmentIds`, part 3; the contract's §6), at most 50 in the request: files of the caller's own conversations, ended ones included, by `Attachment.id`. Each is copied into the new conversation as a file its person added, with its name, content type, note and content, and is not scanned again (it was at its upload). A file of none of the caller's conversations (another person's, which OWNER only sees, included) is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`; two of one name `409 DUPLICATE_ATTACHMENT_NAME`; more files or bytes than a conversation holds `409 TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS`. Each copy is audited as `attachment.uploaded` with `copiedFromAttachmentId`.
  - **Held files** (`fileIds` of a client's start, `ConversationCommands.startFromClient`; ADR-0084): files the caller holds (`StagedFiles`, §14.11's `/files`), unexpired. After the files of `attachmentIds`, each moves into the conversation as a file its person added and leaves `staged_file`; it is not scanned again (it was when it was held). A file the caller does not hold, or that expired, is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`; two of one name `409 DUPLICATE_ATTACHMENT_NAME`; past a conversation's limits `409 TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS`. Each is audited as `attachment.uploaded` with `stagedFileId`. A start replayed by its idempotency key moves none.
  - **What can never run is refused**, and nothing is stored. A subscription whose host was removed, and every `Refuse` of `ModelAccess.forRun` for the person, the model access, the runtime and the holders of the accounts that the workspace's code hosts would bind now (`Accounts.bind`, storing nothing; a grant's credential stands for itself, no host's owner, M4), and a budget's settings that name no model or one its key does not allow (`Budget.refusal`, §15.4), are `409 MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE`, with `details.reason` the refusal (`SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`, `GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE`, `PERSONAL_HOST_CREDENTIAL_ONLY`, `MODEL_REQUIRED`, `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`). A `Wait` (a spent budget, a key's error, an offline host) lets the conversation start, and its first turn waits. Then `409 NO_HOST_FOR_CONVERSATION` applies package 2's host scope rule (§15.4) to the registered hosts, connected or not, and ignores the runner version: for a subscription, `Hosts.eligible` (`CANNOT_RUN_ANYWHERE` for `Nowhere`, `NO_RUNNER_HOST` with its `whyNone` when no host is left); for a budget, a host serving the runtime that is a company host, or the person's personal host when every workspace account is their own: `NO_RUNNER_HOST` when no host serves the runtime, `NO_HOST_FOR_CREDENTIALS` when hosts serve it but none may carry the accounts.
  - **Then**, in one transaction: the conversation (`OPEN`, `TINFIRM_THREAD`, `APP`), `conversation.started`, the files carried into it, the message, the `FIRST` turn (below), which binds the workspace's systems, and `ConversationStarted`. A refusal of the files rolls it all back. The answer is `201` with the conversation and its first turn.
- **Messages** (`message`, §7; the contract's §3.10). `PERSON` (the person's), `AGENT` (a turn's summary) and `SYSTEM` (Tinfirm's notes), each 1 to 100 000 characters. A `PERSON` message without a run waits for the next turn (`waiting` in the API); one that waited when its turn was cancelled or its conversation ended is `unanswered`, and no turn takes it. Every message moves the conversation's `updated_at`, the order of `GET /conversations`. A message through the API (`POST …/messages`, the person, on an open conversation) is a turn, goes to the running turn, or waits:
  - With `answersOpenQuestions`, part 2's `QuestionAnswers` first answers every open question with the message's text (ADR-0033; Decisions, below); approvals stay open.
  - When no decision is open afterwards, a turn is requested: `DECIDED` when it answered any, else `MESSAGE`. While any decision is open, a plain message starts no turn, and during an active run no turn starts (I11): the message waits, or goes to the running turn (below).
  - **Steering** ([ADR-0060](adr/0060-steering-a-running-turn.md), `V036`). A message that answered no question goes to the running turn (`TurnService.steer`, through `Turns.steer`, §15.6): when the conversation's active run is `RUNNING` on a host that takes steers, the message is `SENDING` (`message.steering`; it still waits, `run_id` null) and the agent gets the text `PromptRenderer.steerText` renders: "# Message from {person} while you work", the words as a quote, cut as a request's are, and to take them into account from its next step on, calling `finish` again if it reported its result already. The host's answer (`TurnSteered.steered`) makes it the run's, `DELIVERED` with `run_id` that run, while the run is still the conversation's active one and the message still on its way; a refusal, and every message still `SENDING` when the turn ends, wait for the next turn as before (`steering` null). The messages written while the turn was queued go to it once its run is accepted (`RunAccepted`). A message that answered open questions never does: the next turn's `# Answers` tells it. What the turn took is the turn's: the next turn's resume prompt leaves it out, since the agent's session holds it, and a retry takes it as part of the retried turn's request (`ConversationStore.retake`, `steering` cleared). A host that does not list `run.steer`, or an agent that cannot take it (the host refuses), leaves the message to the next turn. The database keeps `SENDING` to a waiting `PERSON` message and `DELIVERED` to one with its run (`message_steering_ck`); the API shows it as `ConversationMessage.steering`.
- **Turns** (`ConversationTurns.request`; the contract's §5.7). One run at a time per conversation (I11): a turn is requested only for an open conversation without an active run, and what waits is taken by the next one.
  - **The reasons**: `FIRST` (the start), `MESSAGE` (a message, or messages that waited for the end of a turn), `DECIDED` (a decision decided, or a message that answered questions; part 2), `CONNECTED` (a binding with parked calls bound, or gone; part 2), `RETRY`. A `DECIDED` or `CONNECTED` turn does not start while a decision of the conversation is open or a waiting binding has parked calls.
  - **Before its run**: the agent is live, else nothing starts; the agent still lists the conversation's runtime, else the turn fails at once with `RUNTIME_REMOVED`; a subscription whose host was removed fails it with `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`. **A turn that fails before a run** gets no run row and no turn number: the conversation's `turn_status` is `FAILED` with that `failure_class` and its cause, and a `SYSTEM` message tells the person to start a new conversation. Then the settings in effect (`Settings.effective`) give the workspace, whose systems are resolved (Bindings, below), and `Turns.start` creates the run.
  - **The run takes every waiting message**, oldest first, those still on their way to the turn before it included: they become its `# Request`. The turn's number, `active_run_id` and `last_run_id` move, and the previous result and failure are cleared. A retry's run also takes the retried run's `PERSON` messages, the ones that run took while it worked included, which are re-pointed to it.
  - **Cancel** (`…/cancel`: the person or OWNER; `409 CONVERSATION_ENDED` once ended): the active run through `Turns.cancelActive` (a queued one ends at once, a running one through its host), the open decisions and the parked calls; the waiting messages become `unanswered`; the conversation stays open and its turn `CANCELLED` (a `FAILED` turn without an active run stays `FAILED`). A run cancelled through the Runs API ends its turn `CANCELLED` as well, and its waiting messages become `unanswered` at the run's end.
  - **Retry** (`…/retry`: the person or OWNER) runs the last turn's request again when it `FAILED` or was `CANCELLED` and nothing is active: `409 TURN_ACTIVE`, `NOTHING_TO_RETRY`, `CONVERSATION_ENDED`. The `RETRY` turn checks the bindings and the model access again, as every turn does.
  - **End** (`…/end`: the person or OWNER), idempotent: `ENDED` for good (`USER`, `ended_by`); the waiting messages become `unanswered`; the open decisions and the parked calls are cancelled; a running turn finishes and its result is recorded, and a queued one is cancelled by the dispatcher's `plan`. Audited as `conversation.ended`, announced as `ConversationEnded`. The waiting bindings stay `WAITING`. An ended conversation takes no message, choice, continue, cancel or retry (`409 CONVERSATION_ENDED`).
  - **Ends that follow other modules**, in their transaction: `AgentDeleted` ends the agent's open conversations (`AGENT_DELETED`); `PrincipalLeft` ends the open conversations of the person who left (`PERSON_LEFT`), each after cancelling its active run, and they stay readable to OWNER.
  - **The open decisions** of a cancel or an end, whatever its cause, go through `OpenDecisions.cancelAll` with the cause (`CANCELLED`, `ENDED`, `AGENT_DELETED`, `PERSON_LEFT`), which part 2 implements (`ConversationClosing`): each is cancelled and audited as `decision.cancelled`, and the calls parked on the conversation's bindings are cancelled; the next turn's `# Answers` tells both. Without part 2's bean, part 1 sets them `CANCELLED` with the cause, unaudited, and clears the parked calls.
- **The end of a turn** (`TurnEnded.turnEnded`, in the transaction that ended the run, which locked the conversation before the run, §10). Only the conversation's active or last run counts; another's end is ignored.
  - `SUCCEEDED` records the result and posts the `AGENT` message: the summary, then **Next steps** and **Open questions** as lists (`Done.` or `Blocked.` for an empty summary), or the run's summary without a result. **The rule of ADR-0036**: only a turn that ends on its own open questions posts no summary, that is a run without a result that asked a question still open; the agent's question cards say what it asks. A turn that raised only an approval (a request, an `ASK` tool's call) posts its summary, and a `BLOCKED` result posts its message, which lists its open questions (part 2 makes them questions, below).
  - `FAILED` and `LOST` record `FAILED` with the failure class (`LOST` for a lost run) and message, and post a `SYSTEM` note with them and what to do, with the budget's reset time for `USAGE_LIMIT` (open point 8).
  - `CANCELLED` posts "The turn was cancelled." and makes the waiting messages `unanswered`.
  - Then every `TurnEndHook` (parts 2 and 3), then the next turn when the conversation is open and no decision is open. After a `SUCCEEDED` turn: `DECIDED` when a decision was decided and no turn told it (`decision.reported_run_id` null; an approved call that has not run yet waits for its result, which requests the turn itself), else `CONNECTED` when a call that waited for a binding ran or was refused and no turn told it. These were decided or settled while the run was active: their own requests found the run active (I11) and started nothing, and the agent ended its turn to wait for them. Else `MESSAGE` when messages wait. A cancelled turn starts nothing; a failed or lost turn only `MESSAGE` (what was decided waits for the person's retry, which tells it, so that a turn that fails before its run is told never starts itself again). One turn, never two: the conversation's lock orders the end against a decision's or a call's commit, and `request` and `Turns.start` start nothing while a run is active. Then what the conversation waits on.
  - **`TurnEnded.turnResumed(run)`** answers whether the conversation takes back a run lost by lease expiry that its host still runs (§15.6): true only while the conversation is open, has no active run and the run is its last. Then the run is its active run again, the failure is cleared, and a `SYSTEM` message says the turn goes on. Otherwise false, and the run module cancels the run on its host.
- **What a conversation waits on** (`ConversationTurns.refreshWaiting`, today's resolver, ADR-0028), the first row that applies:

  | The conversation | `waiting_on` | `waiting_reason` |
  |---|---|---|
  | Ended | `NONE` | |
  | An open decision | `PERSON` | The oldest's kind: `QUESTION`, `APPROVAL`; `REVIEW` for a result's review |
  | A waiting binding with parked calls, or with the active run queued `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED` | `PERSON` | `ACCOUNT` |
  | A running run | `AGENT` | `RUNNING` |
  | A queued run | `RUNNER` | Its queue reason, `QUEUED` before the dispatcher looked |
  | The latest turn `FAILED` | `PERSON` | `FAILED` |
  | Otherwise | `NONE` | |

  `turn_status` is `QUEUED` or `RUNNING` while a run is active, and `WAITING` after a succeeded turn while a decision is open or a call parked. `waiting_since` moves only when the kind or the reason changes. It is worked out again in every transaction that changes an input, and on the run module's `RunAccepted`, `RunQueueReasonChanged` and `RunResumed`, in their transaction. The API's `Conversation` adds, read when it is shown: `waitingGroupId` and `waitingGroupName`, the group that holds the oldest open decision while it waits on `PERSON` (a trigger's team's approval or review: "Waits for Analysts · Review"); and the caller's `mayPost` and `mayAnswer`, by the rules `POST …/messages` checks (its people: its person, a trigger's author or a member of its team whatever their role, while it is open; a plain message only where its place is Tinfirm, an answer to the agent's open questions anywhere while one is open). A decision's `mayDecide` already follows the decider, never the role.
- **Bindings** (`ConversationBindings`; the contract's §5.3, I6 to I8). The account a conversation uses on a system is resolved once, at the system's first use, stored (`conversation_binding`, §7), checked at every use, and never re-bound (I7), with one exception since `V050`: a trigger's binding through a service account follows the credential the service account borrows now (ADR-0072, open point 3; `check`, below).
  - **`resolve(conversation, systemId)`** runs at the first turn whose workspace holds one of the system's repositories (in `request`, and in the dispatcher's `plan`, §15.6), and in part 2 at the first tool call on it. It stores what `Accounts.bind(Personal(person), systemId)` answers (§15.3); a binding that exists is answered as it is:

    | `Accounts.bind` | The binding |
    |---|---|
    | `Own` | `BOUND`, `OWN`; access `WRITE` |
    | `Granted` | `BOUND`, `GRANTED`; the grant's access; no holder (the credential is the company's) |
    | `Choose` | `WAITING`, `PICK_GRANT`, the grants as `choices` |
    | `ConnectRequired` | `WAITING` with its reason: `NONE`, `RECONNECT_YOURS`, `RECONNECT_COMPANY`, or `CHOOSE` with its `choices` |
    | `NotEntitled` | `WAITING`: `SYSTEM_OFF` while the company switched personal servers off; `SUSPENDED` when the person is not active, which an open conversation never meets (their leaving ends it); else `NONE` (another member's personal server) |
    | `Open` (ADR-0071) | `BOUND`, `OPEN`: no account, holder, identity or grant; access `WRITE` |

    A trigger's conversation binds such a server too, at its first use, though its table has no row for it: `BOUND`, `OPEN`, when `Accounts.open` answers `Open` for its author (§15.3); else it binds nothing there (§15.15).

  - **`check(binding)`** runs `Accounts.check` on the stored binding and records a failure; `use` runs it first. A usable check of a waiting binding binds it again, and a new account row of the holder with the same identity (a reconnect) replaces the bound account (I6):

    | `Accounts.check` stops for | The binding |
    |---|---|
    | The holder suspended | Waits: `SUSPENDED` |
    | The holder left | `GONE`: `HOLDER_LEFT` |
    | The account missing or not `OK`, or a credential disabled | Waits: `RECONNECT_YOURS` (the person's own), `RECONNECT_COMPANY` (a credential, through a grant or a trigger's service account) |
    | The account another identity now | `GONE`: `IDENTITY_CHANGED` |
    | The grant withdrawn, or the use right gone | `GONE`: `GRANT_REMOVED`; a trigger's binding that lost its use right (`USE_RIGHT_GONE`) first follows its service account (below), else waits `NOT_ENTITLED` until the right comes back or the trigger is changed |
    | The system paused (§15.13) | Kept as it is: nothing waits on it, and nothing uses it |
    | The person out of the grant's group | `GONE`: `LEFT_GROUP` |
    | The system removed | `GONE`: `SYSTEM_REMOVED` |
    | Personal servers switched off | Waits: `SYSTEM_OFF` |

    So `SUSPENDED` waits for a suspended service account (a trigger's), or for a member suspended by package 6's switch (§15.13): the conversation's person, also through a grant, whose binding has no holder, or a trigger's author. A holder who left makes the binding `GONE`, and the person's own leaving ends the conversation.

    **A trigger follows its service account** (ADR-0072, open point 3 as decided again; `V050`; `BindingService.followed`). When the check of a trigger's `FIXED` binding through a service account stops for `USE_RIGHT_GONE` and the service account now borrows another credential on the system (an admin lent it one in place of the one bound, which the trigger's row already names, §15.15), the binding is bound to that credential with its identity, the holder and the binding's kind unchanged, audited as `conversation.binding.bound` with the credential it `followed`, said in the thread ("Jira: svc-dev's Second now, in place of First: an admin lent it to svc-dev."), announced as `BindingBound` and checked again with the new credential, which may itself be disabled or out of the author's reach. A chat system's bot is never followed (C9). A credential withdrawn, with nothing lent in its place, leaves the binding waiting `NOT_ENTITLED`; one disabled, `RECONNECT_COMPANY`.

    An `OPEN` binding is checked by `Accounts.open` for whom the conversation acts instead (ADR-0071): it holds, or holds again after it waited; a paused system keeps it as it is; once the system needs accounts again (`ACCOUNT_REQUIRED`), a personal conversation's waits `NONE` (keeping `OPEN`, so that the person's account or a grant binds when they continue, as for one never bound) and a trigger's goes `ACCOUNT_REMOVED`; the system removed, `SYSTEM_REMOVED`; personal servers switched off, `SYSTEM_OFF`; the acting person not active, `SUSPENDED`.
  - **`use(binding, purpose)`** is the only path to a token (I7). It runs outside any transaction (`IllegalStateException` inside one, §10): the check in a transaction of its own, then `Accounts.reveal` (`secret.accessed` with the purpose, §8). It answers `Token`, with the access now (`WRITE` for the person's own account, the grant's access at this use, open point 9), `Open` for an `OPEN` binding that holds (nothing revealed, no `secret.accessed`: the call goes without a credential, ADR-0071), or `Unavailable` when the binding waits or is gone. When the reveal finds nothing after the check, the binding is checked again: it waits for its reconnect (the account went, or a refused refresh turned it `RECONNECT`) or is gone, and the answer is `Unavailable`; or it still holds, and the answer is `Failed`: a passing failure, such as a token endpoint that answered 5xx or 429 or could not be reached when an OAuth grant had to be refreshed. Nothing changed then, and a later use may work. Each caller tells `Failed` apart from a gone binding: a tool call answers `TOOL_FAILED`, a parked or approved call runs again later, a turn goes back to the queue after the back-off (below).
  - **The person's choices.** `…/bindings/{systemId}/choose`, on `PICK_GRANT` or `CHOOSE` only (else `409 BINDING_NOT_WAITING`): `grantId`, one of `choices` and usable now (else `400 GRANT_NOT_OFFERED`), binds `CHOSEN` with `bound_by`; `reconnect` turns `CHOOSE` into `RECONNECT_YOURS` and `PICK_GRANT` into `NONE`; neither or both is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. `…/continue` resolves a waiting binding again (`resolveAgain`); a bound one is answered as it is, `200`, changing nothing, since the same connect's event or another continue settled it a moment earlier (the web's race); a gone one is `409 BINDING_NOT_WAITING`.
  - **The binding in the API.** `text` is written for the conversation's person ("GitHub: your own account"); another reader words it from `state`, `how`, `reason`, `holderPrincipalId`, `holderName` (the person's own too) and `holderKind` (`MEMBER`, `SERVICE`; none through a grant), the credential it acts with (`credentialName`, for `GRANTED`, `CHOSEN` and a service account's `FIXED`; `credentialKind`, `TOKEN` or `OAUTH`, so that an admin knows whether to store a new token or sign it in again; `credentialEnabled`, false for a disabled one that waits `RECONNECT_COMPANY`), the group, `access` (a grant's now, or a trigger's service account's lending's) and the conversation's `personName`. A `PICK_GRANT` or `CHOOSE` binding's `choices` name each grant's credential (`credentialName`). `runId` and `turnNumber` name the run that first asked for it, where the page places its card. A system the conversation has not used is `404`.
  - **`resolveAgain(binding)`** answers a binding that does not wait, or whose conversation ended, as it is. A waiting binding that was never bound is resolved by `Accounts.bind` again, by the same rule, never by a fallback; one bound before is checked instead, never bound to another account (I6, I7) but by a trigger's following of its service account (above).
  - **Package 1's events.** After their commit, on a virtual thread of its own and as SYSTEM in the company, every binding of an open conversation that an event may concern is looked at again, each in a transaction of its own: a waiting one is resolved again, a bound one checked. The events: an account connected, reconnected, disconnected or needing a reconnect; a credential disabled, enabled, lent to a service account or withdrawn from it (`CredentialChanged`, ADR-0072); a grant created, changed or deleted; the members of a group changed; a principal suspended or resumed; a system removed; personal servers switched on or off; an MCP server set to need no account, or to need one again (`AccountsRequiredChanged`, ADR-0071). Which bindings: on the system, of the account, through the grant, of the holder, or of the persons' conversations. An ended conversation's bindings are not looked at: its waiting bindings stay `WAITING`.
  - **What the thread says.** A binding through a grant (`GRANTED` or `CHOSEN`) that becomes `BOUND`, first or after a wait, posts a `SYSTEM` message with its header, "GitHub: via Analysts, read-only."; the person's own account posts none ("GitHub: your own account" is the binding's `text` in the API), nor does an `OPEN` binding ("Logs: no account needed"). Each wait posts its card (its reason's text, e.g. "GitHub is not connected for this conversation yet: connect your account to go on."), once per reason. A gone binding posts "GitHub can no longer be used in this conversation: {why}. Start a new conversation to use GitHub."
  - Every change is audited (`conversation.binding.*`, §8), announced (`BindingBound`, `BindingWaiting`, `BindingGone`), handed to part 2's `BindingHook`s when `BOUND` or `GONE`, and followed by what the conversation waits on.
- **The workspace** (`ConversationWorkspace.of`; the contract's §5.6), at each turn, in order: the start's repositories, then the approved requests (part 2). An `AGENT` entry leaves when the agent of the person's turns no longer lists the repository or its system, or the settings switch the system off; a repository that was removed, that is on a system the agent no longer lists, or whose system's binding is `GONE`, is left out. Each comes with its binding as resolved so far (null before) and whether it is read-only and why: the agent's access `READ` (`AGENT`), the approval's `READ` (`REQUEST`), or the binding's access `READ` now (`GRANT`). It resolves and reveals nothing. At most 20 repositories (`ConversationInfo.MAX_WORKSPACE`): `TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES` at the start, and `addRequested` adds nothing to a full workspace. A repository without a default branch (`system_repository.default_branch` null) is based on `main`. In `run.assign` every repository carries its git token from `use(binding, "run:{runId}")`, with the git user name `x-access-token` on GitHub, `oauth2` on GitLab and `git` on the others, and the commits' author `{Person} via Tinfirm agent {agent}`, email `agent+{agentId}@tinfirm.invalid`; a binding's `READ` access at that use makes the repository read-only (`GRANT`).
- **A turn's content** (`TurnSource`, §15.6). `plan` cancels the run of an ended conversation ("The conversation ended."); resolves the bindings a workspace repository's system lacks (a write, which §15.6 notes); keeps the run queued with `AccountRequired` and the bindings' cards while a workspace binding waits; else is `Ready` with the bound holders, for package 2's host rules (open point 7). `content`, outside any transaction, reads the conversation and what the prompt needs in short transactions of its own, asks parts 2 and 3 for the tools, the prompt's sections and the files, then uses each workspace binding; a binding that is not `BOUND`, or a use that is `Unavailable`, sends the run back as `NotReady` with the binding's text, and a use that `Failed` as `Later` ("Tinfirm could not get a token for GitHub right now; the turn is tried again shortly."), which the run module requeues as `OTHER` after its back-off, not as `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED`: nobody has an account to connect, and a token endpoint that is down is not asked again at once. It answers the title, the prompt, the resume prompt when the run resumes a session, the workspace, the requestable repositories (the contract's §5.5: the agent's repositories the workspace lacks, then the other repositories of its systems, at most 200; `pendingRequest` while an open request of the conversation asks for one, part 2), the tools (at most 200, part 2's), every platform tool, the conversation's granted hosts (at most 100) and the files.
- **The prompt** (`PromptRenderer`; the contract's §5.9), in this order; a part's section without a body is left out:

  | Section | Holds | Filled by |
  |---|---|---|
  | The agent's instructions | As written | Part 1 |
  | `## Added by {person}` | The person's added instructions (their settings) | Part 1 |
  | `---`, `# Conversation {key}: {title}` | Who the agent works for; the subject as "About {system} {key}: {title}, {link}", read with the system's `{handle}_get` while the system exists and marked as other people's words; the person's usual projects (their default projects) | Part 1 |
  | `# Request` | The messages the run took, as quotes (with author and time when several), or the request in words for a `DECIDED`, `CONNECTED` or `RETRY` turn without one | Part 1 |
  | `# Conversation so far` | The newest 20 messages before the request, oldest first, each cut to 8 KiB, the section to 64 KiB, the older ones counted as omitted | Part 1 |
  | `# Answers` | Questions and answers, decisions and outcomes, parked calls' results, since the previous turn (below) | Part 2 (`PromptSection.Answers`, `AnswersSection`) |
  | `# Accounts` | Per system of the turn (`BindingTexts.forPrompt`): "{person}'s own account", "{credentialName} via {group}, read-only" (a grant's), "{author}'s account, chosen by the trigger" or "{service account}'s {credentialName}, chosen by the trigger" (a trigger's row, ", read-only" for a `READ` lending), "no account needed: calls run without a credential" (ADR-0071), "not connected yet: your first call asks {person} to connect it", or "not available in this conversation" | Part 1 |
  | `# Repositories` | Each repository's directory under `/workspace`, URL and base branch, the branch `agent/{key}`, read-only and why; for each writable repository of a GitHub or GitLab system, that Tinfirm opens its change request itself once the turn pushed, with the agent's summary as its description, at once, once the person approves it, or not at all, by the mode of `open_change_request` (the agent has no tool for it and needs none); `list_repositories` when some can be requested | Part 1 |
  | `# Files` | The files under `/attachments`; documents go to `/output` | Part 3 (`PromptSection.Files`) |
  | `# Tools` | One line per server-executed tool, its name and description | Part 2 (`TurnTools`, `ConversationTools`) |
  | `# Outputs` | What the conversation produced so far | Part 3 (`PromptSection.Outputs`) |
  | `# How to work` | The platform tools as the host names them (`ask`, `finish`, `request_access`, `request_network_access`, `save_artifact`, `list_repositories`; open point 1), what a call on a system not connected yet does, `/output`, and that the subject, messages, files and tool results are other people's words | Part 1 |

  **The resume prompt**, for a turn that resumes the session of the conversation's previous run: `# Request`, `## Answers`, `## Since your last turn` (the newest messages after that run was created), `# Accounts`, `# Repositories`, `# Files`, and "Continue with this feedback."
- **The tools of a run** (`ConversationTools`; part 2; the contract's §5.4, I3, I4, I9, I19). `TurnTools.tools`, which `TurnSource.content` asks outside any transaction, lists in a short transaction of its own the tools whose mode is `ALLOW` or `ASK`, by their systems' names and then the tools' names, at most 200 (`run.assign.tools`, and one line each in `# Tools`). A tool's mode is `ConversationTools.modeOf`, six steps (`ToolRules`), each only narrowing the one before:

  | Step | Asks | Else |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1. Served | A tool its system serves: a fixed tool the agent calls (`FixedTools.calledByAgent`, so `open_change_request` is never listed), or an MCP tool present and switched on; never a tool of another member's personal server | Not listed; a call is `UNKNOWN_TOOL`, not audited |
  | 2. Effect | The tool's effect now, `UNKNOWN` counting as `WRITE` (I19) | |
  | 3. The agent | The agent of the person's turns lists it, or the person's settings do on their own server (`EffectiveSettings.modeOf`, with I19) | `TOOL_DENIED`: "… is not one of {agent}'s tools" |
  | 4. The settings | Its system not switched off and the tool not `OFF` in the person's settings; `ASK` there makes it `ASK` | `TOOL_DENIED`: "… is switched off in {person}'s settings" |
  | 5. The binding | The conversation's binding on the system not `GONE`, and no write through a `READ` grant or a trigger's `READ` lending (I9) | `ACCOUNT_GONE` with why it is gone; `TOOL_DENIED` naming the grant's group, or the service account the credential is lent to read-only |
  | 6. The system | The system's own answer for the bound account, which only the call learns (`SystemTools.call`, §15.2) | The call's result with `isError` |

  A system not bound yet lists its tools (the first call binds it), and so does a waiting binding (a call parks, below); listing reads the binding as it is, with a grant's access now (open point 9), and checks nothing. Each refusal is a message for the agent: why, and to tell the person in its summary when its work needs the tool. Steps 3 to 5 are audited as `tool.denied` once per run and tool (§8). A tool's description is its system's (else its title), then "Each call waits for {person}'s approval." under `ASK`, then whom it runs as: "Runs as {person}'s own account.", "Runs through {credentialName} via {group}." (", read-only." for a `READ` grant), "Runs as {holder}'s account, chosen by the trigger." (a trigger's row, ", read-only." for a `READ` lending), "{system} needs no account: calls run without a credential." (ADR-0071, bound or not yet), or, before its system is bound, that the first call uses the person's account or asks them to connect one; the system's part is cut to keep the whole within the protocol's 4000 characters.
- **Tool names** (`ToolNameStore`; the contract's §11). A tool is listed by the name the conversation holds for it, else a new one, stored in the listing's transaction (`conversation_tool_name`, §7): `ToolNames.of(handle, tool)` in lower case (§15.5), since the protocol's `ToolSpec.name` takes lower-case letters, digits, `_` and `-` only. A name another tool of the conversation already holds (two tools that differ in case only, a handle reused), or a platform tool's (`ask`, `finish`, `request_access`, `request_network_access`, `save_artifact`, `list_repositories`), is replaced by `{handle}_{the tool cut to fit}_{hex}`, at most 64 characters, the hex the first 6, 8, … digits of the SHA-256 of the system's id and the tool. The conversation keeps each name for its life, also after its system's handle changed (a new conversation takes the new handle). A call is mapped back by that name before anything else; a name the conversation never listed (one the agent crafts) is matched against the first names the tools of the systems the person sees would get, so that a tool the agent does not list is refused for what it is (`TOOL_DENIED`), not as unknown.
- **A tool call** (`ToolCalls`; part 2; `RunRequests.call`, outside any transaction, as SYSTEM, within `TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT`, §15.6; the contract's §5.3, §5.4):
  1. The tool named, and its arguments a JSON object of at most 64 KiB (else `INVALID_REQUEST`). Then, in a short transaction under the conversation's row lock, taken before the conversation is read (so that an end that commits meanwhile is seen, and an ended conversation raises no approval and parks no call; `PlatformRequests` reads it after its lock too): the conversation open (else `CONVERSATION_ENDED`); a request id sent again answers what it raised before, its approval or the binding it is parked on (`PENDING`), never a second one; the name mapped back (else `UNKNOWN_TOOL`); steps 1 to 4; the arguments checked (`SystemTools.check`, `INVALID_REQUEST`); the conversation's binding on the system, resolved at its first use (`ConversationBindings.resolve`, §15.3) and checked when bound. A waiting binding parks the call (below). Then step 5; under `ASK` the checked call becomes an approval and the answer is `PENDING` with its `decisionId` (`TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` at 10 open approvals).
  2. Under `ALLOW`, outside any transaction (`ToolRunner`): `ConversationBindings.use` with the purpose `run-tool:{runId}`, the only path to the token, then `SystemTools.call` with the comment's label, `{agent} (Tinfirm agent):`, or `{agent} (Tinfirm agent) for {person}:` through a grant. An `OPEN` binding's call goes with `CallCredential.none()` (ADR-0071), and a `401` to it is the agent's error (`DONE` with `isError`, `UNAUTHORIZED`): there is no account to sign in again, and `tool.called` names none. A use that gives no token parks the call when its binding waits, and answers `ACCOUNT_GONE` when it is gone; a use that `Failed` while the binding holds answers `REJECTED` with `TOOL_FAILED` ("Tinfirm could not get a token for Jira right now; try again shortly."), and the agent may call again.
  3. In another transaction, under the conversation's lock: a `401` (`ToolOutcome.Reconnect`) turns the account `RECONNECT` (`Accounts.refused`, §15.3), the binding is checked and waits for its card, and the call is parked on it; then the answer, `DONE` with the system's result (an error it answered included), or `REJECTED` with the system module's refusal (§15.2), into the call's journal row; then `tool.called` (§8) and a `SystemWritten` per write (§10), last.

  From package R (§15.14) a call that passed step 1 is claimed in the call journal before step 2 (`ToolJournal`), and a request id the journal holds is answered from it before step 1: the stored answer, a wait for the call that runs elsewhere, or for a call its replica lost a `SAFE` call run again and an `UNSAFE` one `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`. Step 2 is bounded by the tool timeout, past which an `UNSAFE` call is `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` and a `SAFE` one `TOOL_FAILED`.

  A `PENDING` answer tells the agent to end its turn, without a summary when it has nothing else to do.
- **Parked calls** (`ParkedCalls`, `ParkedCallStore`; part 2; the contract's §3.9, §5.3). A call that meets a waiting binding is parked on it (`conversation_binding.waiting_calls`): its run, request id, names, checked arguments, the call in words, a status (`PARKED`, `ALLOWED`, `RUNNING`, `DONE`, `REFUSED`, `CANCELLED`) and its result. At most 10 per binding (`TOO_MANY_REQUESTS`; calls a turn told already make room); a request id parked before is not parked again. It is answered `PENDING` with `payload.decisionId` set to the binding's id, and the turn ends `WAITING` (`ACCOUNT`).
  - When the binding is `BOUND` (part 1's `BindingHook.bound`, in its transaction), each parked call passes steps 1 to 5 again, with the access now: a refusal is its result; `ASK` raises its approval, with the call's request id and run, while the conversation holds fewer than 10 open approvals, counted once for the bind (past them the call is `REFUSED`: "The call did not run (TOO_MANY_REQUESTS): this conversation had 10 approvals open already, the most it may have. Call it again once they are decided."; raising one while the agent is paused is fine, since approving it is refused then); `ALLOW` turns it `ALLOWED` and runs it after the commit (Work after the commit, below) with the purpose `binding-call:{bindingId}`, `tool.called` with `bindingId`, and keeps its result (a `401` parks it again; a use that `Failed` leaves it `ALLOWED`, and the recovery runs it later). I18, the kill switch: while the agent is paused, an `ALLOW` call is `REFUSED` ("The call did not run: {agent} is paused.") at the bind, and so is an `ALLOWED` one when it is taken to run, as an approved call is (Decisions, below); the next turn tells the agent, which calls again once resumed if it still needs to. A call is taken to run under the binding's lock (`ALLOWED` to `RUNNING`, with `claimedAt`), so that no other thread or replica runs it too. Once no call is pending, a `CONNECTED` turn is requested; part 1 does not start it while a decision of the conversation is open (an approval the bind raised included), and the turn that follows the decision tells the results then.
  - When the binding is `GONE` (`BindingHook.gone`), its parked calls are answered with the reason ("The call did not run: {system} can no longer be used in this conversation (the system was removed)."), and a `CONNECTED` turn follows, so that the agent goes on without the system; a later call on it is `ACCOUNT_GONE`.
  - A cancel or an end of the conversation cancels them (`OpenDecisions.cancelAll`).

  The next turn's `# Answers` tells each settled call once (the call's `reportedRunId`), and a later turn drops what an earlier one told from the binding. Only pending calls count as parked for part 1 (What part 2 changed, below).
- **The other platform requests** (`PlatformRequests`; part 2; `RunRequests.request`, in the run module's transaction that checked the run, as SYSTEM; the contract's §5.5, §5.8). A decision a request raises is audited by the agent (`decision.created`) and posted as its card, an `AGENT` message with its `decisionId`; its answer is `PENDING` with the `decisionId`, telling the agent to end its turn without a summary once it has nothing else to do. A request id sent again answers the decision it raised, also once the conversation ended; otherwise an ended conversation raises nothing (`CONVERSATION_ENDED`). A `TOOL` sent here is `INVALID_REQUEST`.

  | Kind | Raises | Refusals |
  |---|---|---|
  | `ASK` | A question of 1 to 2000 characters with at most 10 options of 1 to 100, trimmed, a repeat ignoring case dropped; its card is the question with its options as a list | `INVALID_REQUEST`; `TOO_MANY_QUESTIONS` at 10 open questions |
  | `ACCESS` | An approval of a repository of a code host the agent of the person's turns lists, named by its id, name or URL, then its name ignoring case, with the agent's `reason` | `TOOL_DENIED` for a `tool` (tools are the agent's definition's, which whoever edits it changes); `INVALID_REQUEST` without a repository; `UNKNOWN_REPOSITORY`; `ALREADY_IN_WORKSPACE`; `ALREADY_PENDING` (an open request of it); `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` |
  | `NETWORK` | An approval of a host in `NetworkHosts`' grammar (§15.5), with the agent's `reason`. A request of a host whose request is open already answers `PENDING` with that decision | `INVALID_REQUEST`; `ALREADY_ALLOWED` (a host of the agent as the person accepted it, or one the conversation was granted, covers it); `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` |
  | `ARTIFACT` | Part 3's: handed to `ArtifactRequests` before anything else is checked (Outputs, below) | `REQUEST_UNAVAILABLE` without part 3's bean |

  `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` answers a request or an `ASK` tool's call while the conversation holds 10 open approvals. One approval is never refused for that limit, though it counts among the ten while open: a change request's under `ASK` (Change requests, below), which the server raises, so that its card is a `SYSTEM` message and its actor SYSTEM. A parked `ASK` call's, raised once its binding is bound, keeps the limit: past it the call is `REFUSED` with `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` (Parked calls, above).
- **Decisions** (`DecisionService`, `DecisionOps`, `DecisionStore`; part 2; the contract's §3.12, §5.8; I15, I16, I18). Questions and approvals of a conversation, each with its approver, the conversation's person (`approver_principal_id`). `mayDecide` (the API's `mayDecide`) is true for the approver alone, while the decision is open: OWNER, who sees every conversation, is not its person, and no agent decides. A decision is decided or cancelled once (I16, `409 DECISION_DECIDED`), under the conversation's row lock, while the conversation is open (`409 CONVERSATION_ENDED`). Raising one posts its card and works out what the conversation waits on; deciding one requests the `DECIDED` turn (for an approved tool call, once the call ran), which part 1 starts only when no decision is open and no waiting binding holds parked calls, so that the last one settled starts it. Each is audited (`decision.created`, `.decided`, `.cancelled`, §8).
  - **A question** is answered with an option (one of its own, matched exactly, then ignoring case, else `400`), a text, or both (`**{option}**`, a blank line, the text); an outcome, note, scope or access is `400`. The answer is also the person's message, quoting the question's first line (at most 200 characters). A message with `answersOpenQuestions` answers every open question with its text (`QuestionAnswers`, ADR-0033), audited with `messageId`.
  - **An approval** takes `APPROVED` or `DENIED` and a note (at most 20 000 characters); an answer or an option is `400`. A denial does nothing more. Approving needs the agent active: a paused agent's approvals wait (`409 AGENT_PAUSED`, I18), while an answer and a denial are taken. `scope`, `CONVERSATION` by default or `AGENT` for good, applies to an approved repository or host only (else `400`), and `AGENT` needs the right to edit the agent (`Agents.access`, else `403 AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE`, the decision staying open); `access` applies to an approved repository only.
  - **What an approval does**, in the deciding transaction. A repository joins the workspace (`ConversationWorkspace.addRequested`, as `REQUESTED`; `409 TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES` when it is full) with its access (open point 10): the agent's when the agent lists the repository, which the approver may lower to `READ`, else `READ` unless the approver picks `WRITE`; a `READ` grant still keeps it read-only (`GRANT`). Scope `AGENT` also adds it to the agent (`Agents.addRepository`, §15.5), and it joins the workspace as well. A host joins the conversation's granted hosts (`409 NETWORK_HOST_LIMIT` at 100), or with scope `AGENT` the agent's hosts (`Agents.addHost`). A host a granted one covers already (`NetworkHosts.covers`: itself, or a wildcard over it, such as `api.example.com` under `*.example.com`) is approved and adds nothing (`ConversationWorkspace.grantHost` answers `ADDED`, `COVERED` or `FULL`), whatever the number of hosts. A tool call and a change request call a system, so they follow after the commit.
  - **An approved tool call** runs right after the deciding transaction commits, not inside it, since no system is called inside a transaction (§10; the contract ran it in the deciding transaction): on a thread of its own (Work after the commit, below) it passes steps 1 to 5 again, and runs with the conversation's binding and the purpose `decision-tool:{decisionId}`, audited as `tool.called` with `mode` `ASK` and `decisionId`. An ended conversation, a deleted or paused agent (I18), a removed system, a binding that waits or is gone, or a step's refusal run nothing and say why. It is taken to run under the conversation's lock and then the decision's (`decision.call_started_at`, §7), so that no other thread or replica runs it too. Its result, or why it did not run, is kept on the decision (`result`, at most 50 000 characters), and only then is the `DECIDED` turn requested, so that its `# Answers` tells it. A use that `Failed` (its token could not be got right now) calls nothing and keeps no result: the call is given back (`call_started_at` null again), and the recovery runs it later (Work after the commit, below).
  - **An approved change request** is opened by part 3 after the commit (`ChangeRequestOpening.approved`, Change requests, below); its `DECIDED` turn is requested in the deciding transaction.
  - **A `BLOCKED` result's open questions** become questions of the person (`TurnQuestions`, a `TurnEndHook`), each cut to 2000 characters, as long as the conversation holds fewer than 10 open questions; they get no card of their own, since the turn's message lists them, and they hold the next turn as any question does.
  - **Told once.** `decision.reported_run_id` (§7) records the turn whose prompt told a decided or cancelled decision, so that `# Answers` reports each once; the same run's content asked again (a reassignment) reports the same. An approved call whose result is not there yet is reported as running, and is not marked, so that a later turn reports its result.
- **`# Answers`** (`AnswersSection`; part 2; the contract's §5.9), in the full prompt and, as `## Answers`, in the resume prompt: what was decided and what ran since the previous turn, marked as other people's words and systems' data, under three sub-headings, each left out when it has nothing: `**Questions**` (each `**Q:**` with its `**A:**`, or why it was not answered); `**Decisions**` (each approval with its outcome and who decided it, the person's note, and what follows: an approved call's result as a quote, a repository in the workspace from this turn, a host for this conversation or for the agent, a change request that Tinfirm opens; or why it was cancelled); and `**Calls that waited for an account**` (each parked call's result, or why it did not run).
- **The inbox** (`InboxService`; part 2; the contract's §5.10): what needs the caller now, the longest waiting first, at most 100 items of each kind, with a count of each kind. Only open conversations count. Five kinds of item of part 2 (the later packages add theirs, §15.11 to §15.17):

  | Kind | What |
  |---|---|
  | `DECISION` | An open decision the caller decides, with its `conversation` when the caller sees it; a tool call's with the account it runs as in the binding's words (`subject.accountText`), a request's for repositories and hosts with whether the caller edits the agent (`agentEditableByMe`), which approving it for good needs |
  | `BINDING` | A waiting binding of the caller's conversation that they act on themselves: `NONE`, `RECONNECT_YOURS`, `CHOOSE`, `PICK_GRANT`, with the grants offered (by id, and as `grantChoices` with their group, the credential each lends by name and their access, as the conversation's binding shows them), the system's kind and whether its app lets the caller sign in (`oauthAvailable`): the Connect card needs no second call |
  | `FAILED` | The caller's conversation whose latest turn failed, with its failure |
  | `WAITING` | The caller's conversation that waits on a runner, with its queue reason, the reason in words as its run's `queueReason.text` says it (`waitingText`, `RunReads.queueReasonText`) and, for a spent budget, the reset time |
  | `ACCOUNT` | For OWNER, ADMIN and an instance admin acting in the company: a system's credential to fix, or a suspended service account, that keeps conversations waiting (`RECONNECT_COMPANY`, `SUSPENDED`), grouped by system, credential, holder and reason, with the number of conversations and nothing of them; it names the credential as it is now (`credential`: its name, kind, status and whether it is enabled, so a token is stored again, a sign-in signed in again, a disabled one enabled; none for a suspended service account's), whether it is a chat system's bot (`bot`: fixed with the bot, §15.16), and the service account (`holderPrincipalId`, `holderName`) |

  The first four are the caller's own: OWNER's inbox shows no other member's conversation.
- **Notices and their dismissal** (`InboxService`, `InboxDismissalStore`; V041). The items of the higher modules (the port `InboxItems`: `BUDGET`, `COMPANY_SPEND`, `SPEND_SYNC`, `TRIGGER`, `DEPARTURE`, `CHAT_LINKED`, `CHAT_SUBSCRIPTION_SILENT`, counted in `counts.notices`) are notices. Each carries its `noticeKey`, computed by the module that raises it (`InboxItems.key`: the kind, what it is about and the condition that raised it, joined by `:`, an instant as epoch milliseconds, at most 200 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]`), and `dismissible`; the inbox shows one item per key. A key stays while its condition holds and changes with it:

  | Kind | Key | Dismissible |
  |---|---|---|
  | `BUDGET` | `BUDGET:{principalId}:{threshold}:{crossedAt}`: the alert, one threshold in one period, so the next threshold and the next period come back; more spend under the same threshold does not | yes |
  | `COMPANY_SPEND` | `COMPANY_SPEND:{yyyy-MM}`: the month | yes |
  | `SPEND_SYNC` | `SPEND_SYNC:{since}`: since the logs failed, else since the day began | yes |
