  | `TRIGGER` | `TRIGGER:{triggerId}:{reason}:` and its state: a pause's or a wait's reason and since when, the agent's version (`WIDENING`), the latest write-back dropped or firing skipped, the admin's change, the poll and since when it waits | but `WIDENING`, `TO_REASSIGN`, `PAUSED` `ACCOUNT_BROKEN` and `WAITING` `RECONNECT_YOURS` |
  | `DEPARTURE` | `DEPARTURE:{principalId}:{leftAt}` | no: the admins close the checklist |
  | `CHAT_LINKED` | `CHAT_LINKED:{noticeId}`: the link | yes: "Not you? Unlink it" is for a link the member does not recognise |
  | `CHAT_SUBSCRIPTION_SILENT` | `CHAT_SUBSCRIPTION_SILENT:{noticeId}` | no: an admin re-enables the events at Slack |

  A notice the reader is the one to act on (the web's orange) is not dismissible: it leaves once handled (`409 NOTICE_NOT_DISMISSIBLE`). `PUT /inbox/dismissals/{noticeKey}` dismisses a notice of the caller's inbox now, for the caller alone, while its key stands: it leaves their `items` and `counts.notices`, never another member's (OWNER's and ADMIN's included); a key that is none of their notices is `404`, and so is every key for a caller without a principal in the company (an instance admin who is not a member, whose notices are not dismissible); a second dismissal changes nothing. A dismissal whose notice left the inbox stays until the daily sweep: the inbox reads at most 100 notices of each kind, so one missing from them may still stand. `DELETE /inbox/dismissals/{noticeKey}` is its undo: the notice shows again while it stands; a key not dismissed changes nothing. A member's dismissals go when they leave (`PrincipalLeft`, in the leaving transaction), with their erasure (`ConversationPurge.eraseDismissals`, counted as `inboxDismissals`, §15.11), and 400 days after they were made (`RetainedRecords` `inboxDismissals`), when a notice whose condition held that long shows again. Neither is audited (§8). The MCP endpoint's `get_inbox` reads the same inbox, without the dismissed notices.
- **Work after the commit** (`ConversationWorkers`; part 2). What an approval or a bind sets going and that calls a system, an approved tool call, the parked calls of a binding just bound and an approved change request's opening, runs once the transaction that set it going committed (`AfterCommit`), on a virtual thread of its own named `conversation-work-*`, as SYSTEM in the company. A failure is logged, and what it should have recorded is not; the recovery picks it up.
  - **The recovery** (`CallRecovery`) of what a restart left behind, since the threads die with the server (`shutdownNow`): an approved call whose transaction committed but whose result was never recorded, which `# Answers` would tell as running for good, and parked calls left `ALLOWED` or `RUNNING`, which hold their binding's slots; neither turn that should tell them starts. On `worker` replicas, `tinfirm.conversation.call-recovery.initial-delay` (`PT20S`) after the start and then every `tinfirm.conversation.call-recovery.interval` (`PT2M`), a pass looks them up in every company (`conversation_calls_due`, §7: ids and company ids only) and handles each in its company, as SYSTEM, at most 100 of each kind a pass:
    - An approved `TOOL_CALL` decision without a result that nobody took to run (`call_started_at` null), approved longer than the tool timeout ago (`TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT`), runs now, as after its approval; so does one given back after a use that `Failed`. A parked call `ALLOWED` longer ago than the tool timeout (`claimedAt`) runs now, as after a bind; so does one left `ALLOWED` after a use that `Failed`. A call left `PARKED` on a bound binding passes the tools' rule again, as at the bind.
    - A call taken to run longer ago than the tool timeout and two minutes (any call ends by then, its token's refresh included), and still without its record, was running when the server stopped. From package R (§15.14) its tool's replay class decides: a `SAFE` one runs again, an approved one given back (`call_started_at` null) and run as after its approval, a parked one `ALLOWED` again and run as after a bind; an `UNSAFE` one may have written, so it is never run again: an approved one keeps as its result, and a parked one is `REFUSED` with, the words of `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` ("The call of `jira_comment` on Jira was interrupted (TOOL_INTERRUPTED): Tinfirm restarted during the call, so it may or may not have taken effect. Check Jira's state, with a read tool where you have one, before you call it again."). A tool no longer served counts as `UNSAFE`. Neither is audited as `tool.interrupted`: they are settled as before.
    - Then the turn that tells them is requested as their own settling requests it: `DECIDED` for an approved call, `CONNECTED` once no call of the binding is pending (the end of a turn's rule, above).

    Each is claimed under its rows' locks, the conversation's first (the decision's `call_started_at`, the parked call's status), so passes on several replicas at once, and a pass beside the thread its commit set going, run a call once. The test profile sets both delays to `PT1H`: the tests run the pass on demand.
- **What part 2 changed in part 1.** `BindingStore` counts as a binding's parked calls only the pending ones (`PARKED`, `ALLOWED`, `RUNNING`): a call that ran keeps its result on the binding until a turn told it, and holds nothing up. `ConversationViews` fills the detail's `decisions` (`DecisionPresenter`, oldest first, each with `mayDecide`). `TurnSourceImpl` sets a requestable repository's `pendingRequest` while an open request of the conversation asks for it.
- **Files** (`AttachmentService`; part 3; the contract's §3.13, §5.10, §6; ADR-0014, ADR-0021). A conversation's files are those its person adds and the documents its agent saves (Outputs, below); its turns mount them all read-only. A file has at most `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_FILE_SIZE` (25 MB), and a conversation holds at most `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_FILES_PER_CONVERSATION` (50) files and `TINFIRM_ATTACHMENT_MAX_CONVERSATION_SIZE` (250 MB), its outputs' documents included (§4). An upload is checked, then scanned, then locks the conversation's row and checks its state, name and limits again, so that the limits hold under concurrent uploads and no scan holds up the conversation's other requests.
  - **Who.** Whoever sees the conversation lists its files (`GET …/attachments`) and downloads them (`GET …/attachments/{attachmentId}`). Only its person adds (`POST`) and deletes (`DELETE`) them: OWNER gets `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`, ADMIN, every other member and an instance admin who is not a member `404`, as for the conversation.
  - **A person's file.** Its name is the last segment of the name the browser sent, in NFC, trimmed, of 1 to 255 characters without control or formatting characters, not `.` or `..` (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), and unique in the conversation (`409 DUPLICATE_ATTACHMENT_NAME`). A note has at most 2000 characters. The content type is kept as `type/subtype`, else `application/octet-stream`. A file too large is `413 ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE`; more files or bytes than the conversation holds `409 TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS`. An ended conversation takes no file (`409 CONVERSATION_ENDED`); its person still downloads and deletes its files.
  - **The scan**, once, at upload, before anything is stored (`VaultService.scan`). The value of a vault secret of the company is `422 CREDENTIAL_FOUND`, never overridable. Content that cannot be scanned completely is `422 ATTACHMENT_NOT_SCANNABLE`: an encrypted or damaged archive, archives nested more than 3 levels deep, more than 64 MiB unpacked from the archives of one file in all, or a scan that takes longer than 10 s (a backstop). The scan streams: the vault values in one pass that carries over from one read to the next, the formats in windows of 1 MiB of which the next repeats the last 64 KiB (a format match of up to 64 KiB is found wherever it is), each format tried only where one of its anchors is (`sk-`, `eyJ`, `ghp_`…) and written to take linear time (a left guard or bounded repetitions); no member of an archive is held whole. A known credential format is `422 CREDENTIAL_FOUND` unless the person acknowledges it (`acknowledgeCredentialMatch`), which needs `MANAGE_SECRETS` (OWNER, ADMIN; else `403`) and is recorded in the audit event (`acknowledgedCredentialFormats`). The problem names each match (`credentialMatches`), never the value.
  - **The download** is the file as stored, as an attachment (`Content-Disposition: attachment` with the last segment of its name), of its content type, with `nosniff` and `no-store`; never inline.
  - **The delete** of an output's document is `409 ATTACHMENT_IS_OUTPUT` (the database's `output_attachment_fk` too). Any other file goes, audited as `attachment.deleted`.
  - **The agent's files** (`storeForAgent`, from the runner host's upload, below) follow the same rules, but the answer is written for the agent instead of thrown: `REJECTED` with `INVALID_REQUEST` (a name, a path or a note that does not fit; the conversation or its agent gone), `CONVERSATION_ENDED`, `ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE`, `DUPLICATE_ATTACHMENT_NAME`, `TOO_MANY_ATTACHMENTS`, `CREDENTIAL_FOUND` (a vault value or any known format: nothing acknowledges one for an agent) or `ATTACHMENT_NOT_SCANNABLE`, each with what to do, and nothing stored. A document from `/output` keeps its path relative to the folder as its name (`reports/q3.md`: NFC, at most 1024 characters, as runner protocol v2's `ArtifactResult.name`, segments of 1 to 255 characters without spaces around them, none `.` or `..`, no `\` and no leading `/`; §7). Characters are counted as code points, as the protocol and the database count them. Audited as `attachment.uploaded` by the agent, with `runId`.
- **The runner host's files** (`RunnerFilesController`; runner protocol v2's `run.assign.attachments`, `save_artifact` and `run.finished.artifacts`; ADR-0014, ADR-0021). Two endpoints under `/api/runner/v1/` take the host's bearer token (§4) and read in the host's company:
  - `GET …/runs/{runId}/attachments/{attachmentId}?epoch=`: a file of the run's conversation, while the run is assigned to this host with this epoch and `QUEUED` awaiting `run.accepted` (the host downloads before its sandbox starts) or `RUNNING`. Anything else is `404`: another epoch, host, company or conversation, an unknown file, an ended run.
  - `POST …/runs/{runId}/attachments?epoch=&artifact=&source=`: only for a run `RUNNING` on this host with this epoch (`RunReads.activeOnHost`), else `404`. The file becomes the agent's file of the conversation (above); with `artifact=true` its name may be a path. Unless `artifact=false`, it is an output: at once for `source=save_artifact` or no source, when its turn ends for `source=output`. `artifact=false` stores the file alone. The answer is `200` with `status` `STORED` (`attachmentId`, `fileName`, and `artifactId` when it became an output at once) or `REJECTED` (`code`), and a `message` for the agent. A file over the multipart limit is `413 ATTACHMENT_TOO_LARGE`.
- **Outputs** (`OutputService`; part 3; the contract's §3.13, §5.5): what a conversation produced, oldest first in its detail (`outputs`), never deleted. Each is audited as `output.created`, by the agent for what it saved and by SYSTEM for a change request.
  - **`LINK`**: `save_artifact` with a link, the run request `ARTIFACT`, which part 2's `RunRequests` hands to `ArtifactRequests`. The link is http(s) with a host, without user info or whitespace, at most 2000 characters (else `REJECTED` / `INVALID_REQUEST`, and nothing is saved), in an open conversation (else `CONVERSATION_ENDED`). One output per request id: the same id again answers the same output, `DONE {artifactId}`. Its title is the one given, trimmed, else the link itself.
  - **`DOCUMENT`**: a file the agent saved with `save_artifact` is one from its upload. A file it left in `/output` becomes one when its turn ends, from `run.finished.artifacts` (`TurnEnd`'s `/output` files), when the entry names the agent's file of that run in the conversation and no output's document yet; any other entry is logged and skipped. A turn that ends without the host's `run.finished` (a lost run) lists no files, so its uploads stay plain files. A document's title is its name; a path longer than 200 characters keeps its end, after `…`.
  - **`CHANGE_REQUEST`**: the server's (below), titled `{repository} #{number}` on GitHub and `{repository} !{number}` on GitLab, with its link, repository, number and the state as last read.
  - A title has at most 200 characters: a longer one is cut and ends with `…`.
- **What a turn gets** (`OutputPrompt`, as part 1's seams `TurnAttachments`, `PromptSection.Files` and `PromptSection.Outputs`; each reads in a short transaction of its own):
  - `run.assign.attachments`: every file of the conversation, oldest first, with its id, size and SHA-256, which the host mounts read-only under `/attachments`. A name that is a path, and a name of more than 255 bytes in UTF-8 (the most a runner host takes: a longer one fails its check of the whole `run.assign`), is mounted flat: `/` becomes `_` (`reports/q3.md` is `reports_q3.md`), cut from the front to 255 bytes of UTF-8, with `-2`, `-3`… before the extension when another file of the conversation has that name (at the end of the name, cut as a whole, when the extension leaves no room for the suffix: `b.` and 253 bytes more); names mounted as they are are taken first.
  - `# Files` lists each file as mounted, with its size, content type and whether the person or an agent added it, as data and not as instructions ("This conversation has no files." without any). It then says that each file put into `/output` becomes an output when the turn ends, under its path, which no file of the conversation may have yet; that a file holding a credential is not saved; and to call `save_artifact` for a link or a file elsewhere. It is in the full prompt and the resume prompt.
  - `# Outputs`, in the full prompt only, has one line per output: a change request with its link and state, a link, a document with where it is mounted. It is left out while the conversation produced nothing.
- **Change requests** (`OutputTurnEnd`, `ChangeRequestOpener`; part 3; the contract's §5.4, open point 5, I9). At the end of a turn (`TurnEndHook`), for each repository the turn pushed:
  - A repository where the conversation's latest change request is not `MERGED`: the push goes to it, and its state is due to be read again, while the conversation is open and the change request is watched; one no longer watched (its conversation ended, its binding gone) stays so, a push the turn's end reports after the conversation ended included. One last read `CLOSED` gets nothing, and a `SYSTEM` message says so ("…is closed, so the push is on none that is open. Reopen #12 to have it reviewed.") after a turn that would be due one (below) and pushed a fresh head.
  - A repository where it is `MERGED` (V038): a push of the commit it was merged at, or an abbreviation of it, opens nothing (a squash or rebase merge leaves the branch ahead of the default branch with no new work); a push of another commit is due the next one, as below. One whose commit Tinfirm never read (merged before V038: its `head_sha` is `NULL`) is read once more instead (`watched` again, due now), and the watch decides.
  - Otherwise one is due after a `SUCCEEDED` or `CANCELLED` turn of an open conversation, in a repository that is not read-only (I9: a read-only repository gets none) with new commits (`commits` > 0), on a `GITHUB` or `GITLAB` system. The mode of `open_change_request` on the agent for the person decides (`EffectiveSettings.modeOf`, §15.5). `ALLOW` opens it after the commit of the turn's end, on a thread of its own. `ASK` asks part 2 for the person's approval (`ChangeRequestApprovals.raise`, in the turn's transaction): an approval of the subject `CHANGE_REQUEST` with the repository, raised by SYSTEM with a `SYSTEM` card ("Open a pull request of `agent/{key}` in {repository} on {system}.", a merge request on GitLab, then that it opens once approved), and kept with the run that pushed (`decision.run_id`); a later push to that repository while it is open answers the same approval. A denial opens nothing. (Without part 2's bean, a `SYSTEM` message says the branch stays pushed without one.) Without the tool, or with it switched off, the branch stays pushed, and a `SYSTEM` message says why ("Tinfirm did not open a pull request for `agent/{key}` in `{repository}`: {agent} does not have `open_change_request` on {system}, so the branch stays pushed without one.") when the push is fresh: its head is another than the conversation's previous push there (`RunReads.lastPush`), so a turn that pushes the same commit again says nothing more. `ChangeRequestPolicy` decides, for the turn's end and for the watch.
  - **Opening**, outside any transaction and safe to repeat. A short transaction plans it: the conversation still open and without a change request in the repository that is not `MERGED`, the repository on a GitHub or GitLab system, the conversation's binding there, the title and the body. Then the binding's token comes from `ConversationBindings.use` (purpose `change-request:{runId}`) and must have `WRITE` access (I9: a read-only grant opens none), and `SystemTools.openChangeRequest` opens `agent/{key}` into the repository's default branch; one the code host has open for the branch already is taken as it is. A transaction under the conversation's lock records the `CHANGE_REQUEST` output and its `run_change_request` row, unless one that is not `MERGED` was recorded meanwhile. When none can be opened (no binding, one that cannot be used now, a read-only grant, the code host's refusal, a removed repository), a `SYSTEM` message says why. An attempt lost between the commit and the call (the server stopped) is not retried by itself: the conversation's next push opens it.
  - **The title** is `{subject key}: {title}`, or the conversation's title without a subject, at most 255 characters (then `…`).
  - **The body** holds, in order: the turn's summary ("The agent left no summary of its work." without one; for a turn that ended on its own open questions, a note that the agent asked in the conversation, then its last message); the conversation's page, `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/c/{companyId}/conversations/{id}`; for a conversation about a GitHub issue, `Closes #57` in the issue's own repository, `Refs owner/repo#57` in another GitHub repository, or `Issue: {link}` in a GitLab merge request; the block of the conversation's other change requests between `<!-- tinfirm:related -->` and `<!-- /tinfirm:related -->` ("Related change requests:", then `- {repository}: {url}` each); and "Opened by {Person} via Tinfirm agent {agent}." (`CommitAuthor`, as the commits name them). Only the server writes the block's markers: every HTML comment opener in what it does not write itself (the summary, the issue's line, the related lines, the names) is broken as `<!- -`.
  - **The related block** of the others: once a change request is created in a conversation that has others, in other repositories or merged ones in its own, each other's body is read and, when its block (the last pair of markers: a body opened before the openers were broken may hold one in its summary) differs, rewritten (`SystemTools.changeRequestBody`, `updateChangeRequestBody`), each with the binding it was opened with, which must have `WRITE` access now. A failure is logged and not retried.
  - **Under `ASK`**, once the person approved and the approval committed, part 2 calls `ChangeRequestOpening.approved` outside any transaction, on a thread of its own (Work after the commit, above). It opens the change request as above, for the turn the approval was raised for (`decision.run_id`), else the conversation's latest turn.
- **The watch** of change requests (`ChangeRequestWatch`; ADR-0022), on `worker` replicas every `tinfirm.conversation.change-requests.state-tick` (`PT15S`, §4). It reads the watched change requests never read, made due by a push, or last read more than `TINFIRM_CHANGE_REQUEST_STATE_INTERVAL` (`PT5M`) ago: at most 100 a pass, from `run_change_request_state_due` (§7). Each is claimed in its company, its read time stamped so that no other replica reads it. Then, outside any transaction, each binding's token is used once (purpose `change-request:{runId}`) to read each state and head commit (`SystemTools.changeRequestState`), which are recorded on the row and the state on the output. A change request read `MERGED` that is its conversation's latest in the repository, in an open conversation, is followed by the next one when the branch's latest push (`RunReads.lastPush`), by a turn that succeeded or was cancelled, has another head than the commit it was merged at: `ChangeRequestPolicy` decides as at a turn's end, the push fresh. That covers a push between a merge and its read, and one merged before V038. A change request is read until it is `MERGED`, its conversation ends (`ConversationEnded`) or its binding is gone (`BindingGone`, or a use that finds it gone). A binding that waits skips the pass; a failed read keeps the last state and is read again after the interval.
- **The Public Suffix List for runner hosts** (part 3, in the run module: `PublicSuffixPush`; ADR-0044). After `hello`, among the frames `RunnerSessionListener.hostConnected` answers (after `welcome` and `host.config`), a v2 host whose `hello.publicSuffixList.sha256` differs from the server's list, or that holds none, gets `psl.update`: the bundled list `NetworkHosts` reads (`agent.PublicSuffixList`, §15.5), gzipped in base64, with its SHA-256, its rule count and, as `fetchedAt`, the server's start. The frame is built once. A host that holds that list, and a v1 host, get nothing. The server reads no other list, so nothing pushes one again while it runs.
- **The seams for parts 2 and 3**, interfaces of `conversation.internal` (the parts are one module). Each part implements its own as beans:

  | Seam | Kind | Part | Without a bean |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `TurnTools` | An optional provider: at most one bean, looked up when used (`ObjectProvider`); called in `TurnSource.content`, outside any transaction | 2 | No tools |
  | `PromptSection.Answers` | As above | 2 | No `# Answers` |
  | `PromptSection.Files`, `PromptSection.Outputs` | As above | 3 | No `# Files`, `# Outputs` |
  | `TurnAttachments` | As above | 3 | No files |
  | `OpenDecisions` (`cancelAll`) | An optional provider; called in the transaction of a cancel, an end, a deleted agent or a person who left | 2 | Part 1 cancels the open decisions, unaudited, and clears the parked calls |
  | `QuestionAnswers` (`answerAll`) | An optional provider; called in the transaction of a message with `answersOpenQuestions` | 2 | Answers nothing |
  | `TurnEndHook` | A hook, injected as a `List`: every bean, in order, in `TurnEnded.turnEnded` after part 1 recorded the turn | 2 (a `BLOCKED` result's questions), 3 (documents, change requests after a push) | |
  | `BindingHook` (`bound`, `gone`) | A hook, injected as a `List`: every bean, after the change's audit | 2 (parked calls, the `CONNECTED` turn) | |
  | `ConversationOutputs` (`outputs`, `outputViews`, `attachments`) | A reader part 1 needs for a conversation's detail: a required bean, in the caller's transaction | 3 | |
  | `ArtifactRequests` (`request`) | Between parts 2 and 3: part 2's `RunRequests` hands it an `ARTIFACT` request, in the transaction and tenant context it was given | 3 | `REJECTED` / `REQUEST_UNAVAILABLE` |
  | `ChangeRequestApprovals` (`raise`) | Between parts 2 and 3: an optional provider (`getIfUnique`), called by part 3 in the transaction of a turn's end for a change request under `ASK`; answers the approval's decision, the same one while an approval for that repository is open | 2 | A `SYSTEM` message: the branch stays pushed without a change request |
  | `ChangeRequestOpening` (`approved`) | Between parts 2 and 3: part 2 calls it once the person's approval committed, outside any transaction | 3 | |

  What part 1 offers them, in the caller's transaction: `ConversationTurns` (`request`, `refreshWaiting`), `ConversationMessages` (`post`), `ConversationWorkspace` (`of`, `requestable`, `addRequested`, `grantHost`), and the module's API (`Conversations`, `ConversationBindings`). Part 2 reaches them through `ConversationCore`, which looks them up when used: part 1 calls part 2's hooks from inside its own beans, so a constructor dependency both ways would be a cycle. Part 2 implements its seams with `ConversationTools` (`TurnTools`), `AnswersSection` (`PromptSection.Answers`), `ConversationClosing` (`OpenDecisions`), `DecisionOps` (`QuestionAnswers`, and `ChangeRequestApprovals` between parts 2 and 3), `TurnQuestions` (a `TurnEndHook`) and `ParkedCalls` (a `BindingHook`); its `PlatformRequests` routes an `ARTIFACT` request to part 3's `ArtifactRequests`, and its `ApprovedCalls` calls part 3's `ChangeRequestOpening.approved` once an approval committed. Part 3 implements its seams with `OutputPrompt` (`TurnAttachments`, and `PromptSection.Files` and `.Outputs` as two small beans), `OutputTurnEnd` (a `TurnEndHook`), `OutputService` (`ArtifactRequests`, `ConversationOutputs`) and `ChangeRequestOpener` (`ChangeRequestOpening`). `CommitAuthor` (`name`, `email`) names a conversation's work in one place: the commits' author for part 1, `{Person} via Tinfirm agent {agent}` at `agent+{agentId}@tinfirm.invalid`, and the last line of a change request's body for part 3.
- **The Conversations API** (tag `Conversations`; who may call each is in the first bullet and §6):

  | Endpoint | Who | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /conversations` | Every member | The conversations the caller sees, newest activity first (keyset on `updated_at` and id); filters `agentId`, `subjectSystemId`, `subjectKey`, `state`, `waitingOn`, and for OWNER `personId` |
  | `POST /conversations` | Who uses the agent | Start (above): `{agentId, message, subject?, repositoryIds?, attachmentIds?}`; `201` |
  | `GET /subjects` | Every member | The subjects of the conversations the caller sees, by key and system, with the number of conversations, the open ones and the last activity; `?systemId=` keeps those on one system |
  | `GET /conversations/{conversationId}` | Its person, OWNER | The conversation: its key, branch, subject, runtime, payer, state, turn status and wait, result or failure, workspace, granted hosts and network hosts (the agent's as the person accepted it, and the granted ones), bindings, messages and turns (its runs, `RunReads.views`), outputs and files (`ConversationOutputs`), and its decisions, oldest first, each with `mayDecide` (part 2) |
  | `PATCH /conversations/{conversationId}` | Its person | The title |
  | `POST …/messages` | Its person | `{body, answersOpenQuestions?}`; `201` with the conversation |
  | `POST …/cancel`, `…/retry`, `…/end` | Its person, OWNER | Above |
  | `POST …/bindings/{systemId}/choose`, `…/continue` | Its person | Above; the binding as it is now |
  | `GET …/attachments`, `GET …/attachments/{attachmentId}` | Its person, OWNER | Its files, oldest first, each with `outputId` when it is an output's document; one file's content (Files, above) |
  | `POST …/attachments` | Its person | `multipart/form-data`: `file`, `note?`, `acknowledgeCredentialMatch?`; `201` with the file |
  | `DELETE …/attachments/{attachmentId}` | Its person | `204`; never an output's document |
- **The Decisions API and the inbox** (part 2; tags `Decisions` and `Inbox`; who may call each is in §6):

  | Endpoint | Who | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /decisions` | Every member | The decisions of the conversations the caller sees, newest first (keyset on `created_at` and id): a person's own conversations', OWNER's every conversation's (an instance admin who is not a member: none, §15.13). `?open=true` keeps the open ones, `?mine=true` those the caller decides (none for an instance admin who is not a member) |
  | `GET /decisions/{decisionId}` | Who sees its conversation | One decision, with its conversation's key and title, its agent, the names of its approver and of who decided it, a subject's system and repository by name, and `mayDecide`; `404` to anyone who does not see the conversation |
  | `POST /decisions/{decisionId}/decide` | Its approver, the conversation's person | Above: `{answer?, option?}` for a question, `{outcome, note?, scope?, access?}` for an approval; `200` with the decision. Anyone else who sees the conversation (OWNER, an instance admin) gets `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND`, anyone else `404` |
  | `GET /inbox` | Every member | Above: `items`, the longest waiting first, and `counts` per kind (`decisions`, `bindings`, `failed`, `waiting`, `accounts`; `notices` without the dismissed ones) |
  | `PUT /inbox/dismissals/{noticeKey}`, `DELETE …` | Every member with a principal in the company, for themselves | Dismiss a notice of one's inbox, and undo it (Notices and their dismissal, above); `204` |

- **Events** (`ConversationEvents`, ids only, published in the changing transaction after its audit): `ConversationStarted`, `ConversationEnded` (with the reason), `BindingBound`, `BindingWaiting` and `BindingGone` (with the reason); from part 2 `SystemWritten`, once per write of a tool call, with `what` (`"{WriteKind} {key}"`, e.g. `COMMENT SHOP-241`) and the system's `externalId` of what was written (§10).
- **Not tested in part 1.** Implemented but not tested: a binding that waits `SYSTEM_OFF`, and one gone `HOLDER_LEFT`; `TurnEnded.turnResumed`; `TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES`; `NO_HOST_FOR_CONVERSATION` with `NO_HOST_FOR_CREDENTIALS` or `CANNOT_RUN_ANYWHERE`; a conversation paid by a subscription, and a turn that fails `SUBSCRIPTION_GONE`.
- **Not tested in part 2.** Implemented but not tested: a decision refused for a full workspace (`TOO_MANY_REPOSITORIES`) or the conversation's 101st host (`NETWORK_HOST_LIMIT`); an approved call that does not run (its agent paused or deleted after the approval, its binding waiting or gone, a step's refusal), and one whose token could not be got (given back for the recovery); the inbox's `SUSPENDED` account; `REQUEST_UNAVAILABLE`, which only a context without part 3 answers.
- **The conversation's page.** A change request's body links to the web app's route `/c/:companyId/conversations/:conversationId` (the SPA serves the path, §4), the web app's conversation page (`web/README.md`).
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `ConversationRulesTest` | A key's base (the slug) and a title, a subject's link, the prompt's sections in their order and without parts 2 and 3, the resume prompt's last line, the git user names, the directories as the run module makes them |
  | `ConversationConstraintTest` | As the runtime role against PostgreSQL: I12 (a key unique and of the branch's characters), I10 (the payer is the person; the model access's shape), what a conversation fixes, an ended conversation stays ended, none deleted, `run_conversation_fk`; I6 (one binding per system, fixed once bound, a reconnect of the same identity replaces the account and another identity does not), I7 (`GONE` is final), I8 (never another member's account, a credential only through the grant that lends it, which names no holder); one decision per request id, decided once, each kind's shape; another company's rows invisible |
  | `ConversationIntegrationTest` | Through the API and a fake runner host: a start with a subject (key, title, branch, payer, runtime, workspace, the person's own binding); a snapshot of `run.assign` (ids, key and branch, the relay's token, the git token only from the binding and only in `workspaces[].git`, no LiteLLM key, the prompt's sections); a message during a run and the next turn that takes it; subjects; what can never run (`MODEL_ACCESS_UNAVAILABLE`, `NO_HOST_FOR_CONVERSATION` with `NO_RUNNER_HOST`; a registered host never connected suffices); who sees a conversation and its runs (I17) and who speaks (I15); cancel and retry by the person, cancel and end by OWNER; a runtime the agent dropped; a deleted agent and a leaving person; V1, Alice and Bob in parallel; an open question that holds a plain message, a turn ending on it without a summary, and a cancel that cancels it |
  | `OwnerReadAuditTest` | Through the API and a fake runner host: OWNER's reads of the member's conversation on every path (the conversation, the list by person and the whole list, a run, its events, its stream, the files and a file, a decision and the decision list, OWNER's end), one `conversation.read` each with its `what`, the list once per person and the decision list once per conversation; none for the person's own reads and none for OWNER's own conversation; the same reads again recorded again, with no throttle (two reads in a row, two records; a stream one when it opens); an instance admin's read recorded as OWNER's |
  | `OwnerReadsTest` | `OwnerReads` as a unit: every read recorded, two in a row two records; the person and a caller who does not see every conversation never recorded, nor OWNER's own conversation; a list once per other person and a decision page once per other person's conversation, per call, in one `REQUIRES_NEW` transaction; a record that failed failing the read |
  | `ConversationBindingTest` | Each outcome of `Accounts.bind` as `resolve` stores it, and `use` after each change: a grant's credential unaffected by a suspended service account that borrows it, then disabled (waiting `RECONNECT_COMPANY` with `credentialEnabled` false and its kind, the text naming it) and bound again once enabled, the person out of the group, a grant removed, a grant's access read at every use, `PICK_GRANT` and `choose` (`GRANT_NOT_OFFERED`, `CONVERSATION_BOUND`, `BINDING_NOT_WAITING`), a continue of a bound binding answered as it is and of a gone one refused, the own binding's holder in the API, `NONE`, `RECONNECT_YOURS`, `CHOOSE` with `reconnect`, `RECONNECT_COMPANY` bound again when an admin reconnects, an account to reconnect, the same identity connected again, another identity, a system removed; V6, a workspace account to connect, naming the turn's run, keeping the turn queued `ACCOUNT_REQUIRED` (after `AGENT_PAUSED` in the dispatcher's order) until the person connects it; V5, a `READ` grant making the workspace read-only (`GRANT`) and said in the thread, and the person's own account bound by a new conversation |
  | `ConversationTestBench` | The fixture: a company with OWNER, ADMIN and MEMBER, the bundled gateway with their keys, a company host of protocol v2 at runner 0.2.0, `GIT` code hosts whose tokens are taken as they are, agents made through the API |
  | `ConversationOutputsEndToEndTest` (part 3) | Through started conversations and a fake runner host: a start with another conversation's file (`attachmentIds`; a file of none of the person's conversations refused), the copy in `run.assign.attachments` and `# Files`, its download while the run awaits `run.accepted`, a `save_artifact` document and an `/output` one as outputs of the detail, OWNER seeing them and ADMIN not, the next turn's mounts (a path flat) and `# Outputs`; a turn that pushed to a GitHub repository and its pull request, opened with the person's own account (`change-request:{runId}`) |
  | `OutputRulesTest` (part 3) | Part 3's rules as tables: the issue line, the related block replaced or appended (the last pair of markers), the body with every comment opener the server does not write broken, the title, the links `save_artifact` takes, the names of files and of `/output` documents, the names a path is mounted under (an extension that leaves no room for the suffix included) |
  | `AttachmentIntegrationTest` (part 3) | Through the API: the person adds, lists, downloads and deletes; OWNER reads and gets `CONVERSATION_BOUND`; ADMIN and another member get `404`; the limits per file, count and size (the test profile's, §4); the scan (a vault value even when acknowledged, a format a MEMBER may not acknowledge and OWNER does, an encrypted archive); an ended conversation takes no file and keeps its own |
  | `RunnerFilesIntegrationTest` (part 3) | The runner host's endpoints: a download only for its active run, epoch and conversation (another host, epoch, company, file; no or a wrong token; an ended run); a `save_artifact` file a document at once, an `/output` file kept with its path and a document at its turn's end, entries that are not the run's own skipped, refusals answered to the agent (a credential, a path out of the folder, a name taken), `ATTACHMENT_IS_OUTPUT`, the audit and its chain |
  | `OutputIntegrationTest` (part 3) | A link once per request id, links refused and an ended conversation, through the seam `ArtifactRequests`; `run.assign.attachments`, a path mounted flat beside a taken name, names of 400 bytes mounted within 255, `# Files` and `# Outputs` |
  | `ChangeRequestIntegrationTest` (part 3) | On the GitHub stub, under `ALLOW`: one change request with the person's token, its title and body, the output, `output.created` by SYSTEM and `change-request:{runId}`; a later push to the same one; a second one that lists the first, whose body is rewritten; the watch until `MERGED`, and an ended conversation's no longer read, a push reported after the end included; a summary with the block's marker, whose link and issue line a rewrite keeps; none for a read-only repository, no new commits, a read-only grant (with a message), the code host's refusal (with a message) or an agent without the tool; under `ASK` part 2's approval to the person, with its `SYSTEM` card and nothing opened, then, once the person approved it through the Decisions API, the pull request opened with their account for the turn that pushed (`change-request:{runId}`) |
  | `ChangeRequestApprovalIntegrationTest` (part 3) | `ASK` through the seams between parts 2 and 3, on part 2's approvals: raised in the turn's transaction (the seam refuses to run outside one), one per repository while it is open, kept with the run that pushed (`decision.run_id`), a later push answering the same one and another repository getting its own; a denial opening nothing; an approval opening it once after the commit, for that run; `ChangeRequestOpening.approved` again opening nothing more |

  | `ConversationCallsIntegrationTest` (part 2) | A token that cannot be got right now (the token endpoint `503` while the grant must be refreshed): the call `TOOL_FAILED`, the binding still `BOUND`, nothing denied, and the same call running once the endpoint answers; a tool call and a platform request that read the conversation open and waited for its row while the person ended it: `CONVERSATION_ENDED`, nothing raised; the lock order (§10): two calls in each of two conversations raced while their account breaks (Jira `401`), all parked, no `40P01` |

  | `TurnTokenIntegrationTest` (part 2) | A workspace's GitHub account connected with OAuth whose grant must be refreshed while GitHub's token endpoint is down: `TurnSource.content` answers `Later`, the binding still `BOUND`; `RunDispatchIntegrationTest` shows the run module requeueing `Later` as `OTHER` after the back-off |
  | `DecisionIntegrationTest` (part 2) | Through the Decisions API: the person sees and decides, OWNER sees and gets `CONVERSATION_BOUND`, ADMIN and another member `404`, `?open` and `?mine`; what a question is not decided with (`400`); the answer through its card as the person's message quoting the question's first line, `decision.decided` by the person without the answer; `DECISION_DECIDED` (I16); the `DECIDED` turn and its `# Answers`; a repository approved `WRITE` into the workspace, a host for the conversation and one for the agent (`agent_host.decision_id`), a denial with a note, each in `# Answers`; a repository approved `WRITE` that a `READ` grant keeps read-only (V5's workspace half); scope `AGENT` without the edit right (`AGENT_NOT_EDITABLE`, the decision open), `AGENT_PAUSED` while an answer and a denial are taken (I18); an approved comment written once after the commit with the person's account and the agent's label, its result on the decision, `tool.called` `ASK` with `decisionId`, `decision-tool:{decisionId}`, `SystemWritten` `COMMENT SHOP-7` (V2's conversation half); a host under a wildcard the conversation was granted already, approved as covered and adding nothing |

  | `SpendNoticeKeysTest`, `TriggerNoticeKeysTest` | The keys: a budget's the same under more spend and another at the next threshold and period; the company's spend's by month; the sync's by since when; a trigger's by its pause, the latest drop or skip, its agent's version; which trigger notices are not dismissible; a key's characters and length |
  | `ConversationStreamTest` | The conversation stream: a change of each part signals it, after its commit and never for one rolled back; ADMIN and another company get `404`; OWNER streams with no `conversation.read`; a suspended person is cut off at the next check and opens none (`403`), OWNER's stream going on; the final status when the conversation ends |
  | `TurnFailureMessageTest` | A failed turn's note: a spent budget's reset in one fixed form in UTC, none when it is not known |
  | `ConversationDecisionsEndToEndTest` (part 2) | Through the API, a fake runner host and the Jira stub: `run.assign.tools` and `# Tools`; a read at once with the person's own account; an `ASK` tool's call and a question `PENDING`; the turn ending on its own question without its summary (ADR-0036); the inbox with both for the person and nothing for OWNER; a message answering the question while the approval holds the turn; the approval writing the comment with the agent's label, then the `DECIDED` turn's `# Answers` with the answer and the call's result; a `BLOCKED` result's question, cancelled when the conversation ends. And `save_artifact` with a link through `run.request` to part 3: an output, once per request id |
  | `ConversationBench` (part 2) | The fixture: OWNER, ADMIN and MEMBER, a Jira Data Center stub, the person's agent with Jira's `get` and `search` `ALLOW` and `comment` `ASK`, conversations as rows with a running turn (`TestConversations.bare`, `TestRuns.ofConversation`), whose requests go to the module's `RunRequests` as the run module sends them, and the next turn the conversation requested |
  | `ConversationLockOrderTest` | The lock order (§10), raced round after round through the API and a fake runner host: a running turn's `run.finished`, the person's cancel and the host's `save_artifact` upload, which deadlocked under the earlier order; and an agent paused with `stopRunning` while a run of it ends and starts the next turn; a run's end while its person leaves. No transaction fails (no `40P01`, no `500`, no `INTERNAL_ERROR` frame), the run ends once, the turn's status is consistent. And a person's leaving and an agent's deletion while a transaction holds their conversation and then takes the audit chain head: the leaving and the deletion wait for the conversation without the head (their events are appended at the commit), and all commit; they deadlocked while each record locked the head. From package R: a tool call's record whose system refused the credential waits for the account's row without its journal row, and records `account.refused` before `tool.called` |

  `TenantIsolationTest` calls every Conversations path, the files' included (from part 3, with a file of that conversation), and every Decisions path and the inbox (from part 2, with an open question of that conversation, `TestConversations.question`), with a bare conversation of company B (`TestConversations.bare`, §15.6) as a member of company A; B's OWNER still finds the question open, on the conversation and through `GET /decisions/{decisionId}`; and the dismissals' paths with the key of a notice B's OWNER dismissed, which stays dismissed (V041). The test classes are under `server/src/test/java/com/flowtivo/tinfirm/conversation/` (the rules tests, `OwnerReadsTest`, and the integration tests and benches of parts 2 and 3 in its `internal` package, their end-to-end tests beside part 1's), `PublicSuffixPushIntegrationTest` under `run/internal/`, and `Accounts.refused`'s in `CredentialRevealTest` (§15.3).

### 15.10 Package 6: the MCP endpoint on conversations

The design is the [package 6 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-6-contract.md), §5.6 and §5.7, with its §11 (decided on review), and the design record's Part 1 (an AI client is a personal space acting as the person), Part 8 and security review row 18. The endpoint itself, its credentials, scopes, filter and rate limit stay as §14.11 says.

- **One path for the app and the client** (open point 20). The conversation module's public API is `ConversationReads` (list, subjects, get, get by key, files and a file's download, decisions, a decision, the inbox) and `ConversationCommands` (start from the app, start from a client, rename, post, cancel, retry, end, choose and continue a binding, upload and delete a file, decide, and dismiss and restore an inbox notice, `dismissNotice` and `restoreNotice`, §15.7), over the built services, each call as the current user and with a `ConversationView`: `APP` for the REST controllers, which go through it (`ConversationsController`, `DecisionsController`, `InboxController`), `CLIENT` for the MCP endpoint. Every read of a conversation, its files and decisions passes there, so that the Owner's reads are recorded at one place (`conversation.read`). The agent module's public `AgentReads` (list, get, `readiness`, a handle's id) serves `GET /agents`, `GET /agents/{agentId}` and its readiness to the controller and to the endpoint.
- **The `CLIENT` view.** The caller sees only the conversations they are the person of, OWNER included (open point 11); another's is `404` (`NOT_FOUND`), never `CONVERSATION_BOUND`. Decisions and their lists follow the conversations. A trigger's conversations (package 4, §15.15; open point 15) come in it for the members who act in them as their people: the trigger's author and the members of its team (T12), who read them, list them (`ConversationService.scope` with the `CLIENT` view: the triggers they act on, never the author it started with once the trigger is reassigned), answer their questions, speak where the place takes it, and cancel, retry and end them, as in the app. OWNER and ADMIN, of every trigger's audience in the app, see through a client only the triggers' conversations they act on; another is `404`.
- **Starts.** `startFromClient` starts as the app does, with origin `CLIENT`, the member its person and payer. At most `TINFIRM_MCP_STARTS_PER_HOUR` (30) such starts per member and hour in a company (open point 14): counted from `conversation` (`origin = 'CLIENT'`, `created_at` within the hour, on `conversation_person_idx`) after a transaction-scoped advisory lock on the member's principal (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(0x4D435053, hashtext(principal))`, taken before any row lock of the start), so the limit holds across replicas. Over it, `429 RATE_LIMITED` with `retryAfter`, until the oldest of those starts leaves the hour; the app's starts are not counted.
- **A team's approval** (open point 15). Through a client, `decide` on an approval of a trigger's conversation (origin `TRIGGER`: an approval whose approver is its team, or its author when it has none, a result's review among them, package 4) is `409 DECIDE_IN_APP` with `conversationId` and `decisionId`, before anything else is checked; the tool error adds the conversation's link in the app. Its questions are answered through the client. A decision decided through a client is audited `decision.decided` with `via: CLIENT` (and the credential's `tokenId` or `grantId`, §8).
- **No break-glass through a client** (G9). The authorization server refuses an instance admin who is not a member of the `resource`'s company with `access_denied` before the access guard, so nothing is recorded; the endpoint answers every tool of such a caller `NOT_FOUND` before the tool runs. An instance admin who is a member acts by their role.
- **The Owner's notice** (row 18, G10). "Notice: conversations you start through this AI client are personal: agents act with your own accounts and budget. The Owners of {company} ({names}) can read them in full; each read is recorded and shown to you." `GET /api/me/oauth/consent` carries `ownerNotice: {companyId, companyName, owners}`, which the consent page shows above the levels; `POST /api/me/tokens` answers `ownerNotice: {companies: [...]}` for every company of the user, which `/me` shows when a token is created (a personal token sees no consent page); the `initialize` instructions carry it without names, and every answer of `start_conversation` with the company's and its OWNERs' names. The names come from the identity port `CompanyOwners` (display names only), which the company module implements.
- **Not built here.** `get_turn` (a run's transcript) of the contract's §5.7: the run module offers no public read of run events yet. Its `get_my_spend` came as `get_spend` (§15.20): the spend the caller sees, not their budget and its reset, which the Spend API does not answer. `conversation.client_name` (the contract's V021) is not added: the audit names the credential.
- **Tests.** `McpConversationToolsIntegrationTest`: each tool with a personal access token and with an OAuth grant; a start as the member, `CLIENT`, paid by them, with the notice; OWNER's own-only view; `DECIDE_IN_APP` with its link, the approval left open; the hourly limit, the app and another member unaffected; an instance admin who is not a member `NOT_FOUND` with no `admin.company.accessed`; a member of company A at company B's endpoint `NOT_FOUND` from every tool; the scopes. `OAuthAuthorizationServerIntegrationTest` (the consent's `ownerNotice`; `access_denied` for an instance admin who is not a member) and `PersonalAccessTokenIntegrationTest` (a token's `ownerNotice` per company).

### 15.11 Governance: the audit, retention, holds and erasure (package 6)

The part of package 6 that keeps the company's records ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-6-contract.md), §3.6, §3.7, §5.6, §5.8, §5.9, §5.11 and the decisions of §11; open points 10, 16, 17, 22 and 25): the audit's filters, export and daily check (module `audit`), the person's list of who read their conversation (`conversation`), and retention, legal holds and erasure (the new module `governance`). The spend views, the kill switches, departures, the MCP tools, break-glass and the person's export (open point 23, later by §11) are other parts of the package. The tables are in §7, the actions in §8, the permissions and codes in §6.

- **The audit's filters** (`GET /audit-events`): `action` (exact, or a prefix ending in `.`), `actorType`, `actorId`, `targetType`, `targetId`, `from` (included), `to` (not), `conversationId` in the details, `conversationKey` and `principalId`; combined, newest first, keyset on `seq`. `actorType` outside `USER`, `SYSTEM`, `AGENT`, `SCIM`, or `from` not before `to`, is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. In detail:
  - `conversationKey` is the key people know a conversation by (§15.7, `shop-123-dev-1`; trimmed, any case), resolved to the company's conversation's id (`AuditConversationKeys`, any conversation, as `conversationId` takes any) and matched as `conversationId`; an unknown key, or one whose conversation is not the `conversationId` also given, answers an empty page. A new parameter rather than `conversationId` taking either: that one stays a `uuid` for every client, and an id from an event's details and a key typed by a person stay two clear filters.
  - `principalId` finds **the events about the principal**: those that name it in `details.principalId` (a model key's, a hold's, an account's), those whose target is it (`principal`) or its member's user (`user`: added, changed, removed), and those its member's user acted in (`USER`). A member's user is the same each time they join, so either of a rejoined member's principals finds all that concerns their user; a service account, which never acts, is found by the first two. Each is a branch on its own index of V023 (`audit_event_principal_idx`, `audit_event_target_idx` twice, `audit_event_actor_idx`), read backwards and cut at the page's size before a `UNION` (`AuditEventStore.newestFirst`), so a page reads at most four pages of rows however long the history.
  - Each event names its target (`targetName`, §8), the instance's list too (`GET /api/admin/audit-events`).
- **The export** (`GET /audit-events/export`, `VIEW_AUDIT`): a contiguous range of `seq`, by `fromSeq` and `toSeq` (both included; from 1 and up to the head by default; beyond the head is the head) or by `from` and `to`, which resolve to the range from the lowest `seq` whose `occurredAt` is at or after `from` to the highest before `to` (`occurredAt` follows the calls and `seq` the commits, §8, so the range may hold an event from outside the times at either end); the two ways do not mix (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), nor may `fromSeq` follow `toSeq`. At most 1 000 000 events (`TINFIRM_AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX_EVENTS`, `400 AUDIT_EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`). `application/x-ndjson`: a first line `{"kind":"export", companyId, fromSeq, toSeq, count, prevHash, from, to}`, one line per event in its canonical form (ADR-0010) with `prevHash` and `hash` added, keys sorted, oldest first, and a last line `{"kind":"head", seq, hash}`, the head when the export began. `AuditExport.plan` authorises and resolves the range in a short transaction; the lines are read 1000 a transaction and streamed outside any, on a virtual thread of their own through a `ResponseBodyEmitter` (the generated interface declares the body a string, as for event streams), and `audit.exported` is recorded once the last line is written. Anyone holding an export verifies it offline: drop `prevHash` and `hash`, sort the keys, `hash = hex(sha256(prevHash + "\n" + canonical))`, the first event chaining from the first line's `prevHash` (`AuditExportTest` does it with a verifier of its own).
- **The daily check** (`AuditCheckService`, on `worker` replicas, every `TINFIRM_AUDIT_CHECK_TICK`, an hour): each chain not checked for `TINFIRM_AUDIT_CHECK_INTERVAL` (a day; `audit_check_due`), the instance's included, is checked in its own transaction under an advisory lock (two workers check it once), from the anchor: the latest check that passed or whose failure was acknowledged (the chain's start before any). It fails `SHORTER` when the event at the anchor is gone or the head row is behind it (the newest events removed: a valid, shorter chain that the full verification does not notice, ADR-0010), `HEAD_CHANGED` when the event at the anchor has another hash, and `BROKEN` when an event after it does not verify (a changed event, a broken link, a gap, a head row past the last event or at another hash), with `first_invalid_seq`. Each check records the chain's last event as found (`head_seq`, `head_hash`); the last 400 per chain are kept, and always the anchor. A failure is logged at `ERROR` at every check until one passes or it is acknowledged, and is an `AUDIT_CHECK` item of the inbox of OWNER and ADMIN meanwhile. `GET /audit-events/checks` lists them (`VIEW_AUDIT`), `POST /audit-events/checks/{checkId}/acknowledge` `{note}` (`MANAGE_RETENTION`: OWNER) acknowledges the chain's latest check, if it failed (else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`; again: unchanged), so that the next check starts from the head the failed one found; audited, the note on the row only. The instance chain's: `GET /api/admin/audit-events/checks` and `POST …/{checkId}/acknowledge`, instance admins. The check itself is not audited.
- **Who read a conversation** (open point 10): `GET /conversations/{id}/reads`, for its person and whoever sees it (OWNER), `404` to anyone else, one entry per reader from the company's `conversation.read` events (`AuditReads.conversationReads`): `userId` and `name` (resolved now), `firstReadAt`, `lastReadAt`, `reads`, `kinds` (each event's `what`); at most 100, the latest reader first. §11 records every read by an OWNER who is not the person, in a transaction of its own, so the chain is the record; reading the list is not a read. The reads are recorded by `OwnerReads` (§15.7, §15.10); this part reads what it records.
- **Retention** (`GET`, `PUT /retention`; admins read with `VIEW_AUDIT`, the OWNER changes with `MANAGE_RETENTION` and `RETENTION_SETTINGS`, ADR-0077): the periods in force are `RetentionPolicy`'s (§15.24: under Community everything is kept, after a licence expired the periods are frozen; `mode` says which); the four periods of §7, `null` keeping content or files for good, and from §15.18 `memoryDays` (agents' memory notes not used for that long, 730 by default; `0` keeps them; absent in a change keeps the period), `400 RETENTION_INVALID` out of range or with chats kept longer than content; a change is audited `retention.changed` (a change that changes nothing is not), and the answer says what the next sweep purges under the periods if it ran now, outside what an open hold covers: `nextSweep` (`RetentionSweepCounts`, `RetentionService.forecast` of the stored periods, the very count `POST /retention/preview` answers as `current`, each row once) and `due` from it (`conversations` whose content goes, each once even when a chat's is due under both its periods; `runs` whose events go, only in ended conversations no hold covers; `files` whose content goes) and how many open conversations are older than their period (`openOlder`). `GET /retention/open-conversations` (`MANAGE_RETENTION`) lists those by key, place and age, never their title; a conversation is ended on its own page. `POST /retention/preview` (`MANAGE_RETENTION`, the periods' editor before a change is saved) takes the body of the change, checked as the change checks it (`400 RETENTION_INVALID`), and answers what the next sweep would purge if it ran now under the proposed periods (`proposed`) and under the stored ones (`current`), and what the open holds keep from it under the proposed ones (`held`: the same count without the holds less the one with them, since a hold only ever keeps rows), as `RetentionSweepCounts`: `conversations` (whose content goes), `messages`, `decisions`, `outputs`, `files`, `runs` (texts), `runEvents` and `memoryNotes` (the agents' notes past `memoryDays` and a trigger's proposals not reviewed in 30 days, by the sweep's own selection: `AgentMemory.countPurge`, sharing `MemoryStore`'s SQL with the purge, and the sweep's `heldTriggers` and `proposalsBefore`), each row once. `RetentionService.forecast` counts with the sweep's own selection through the purge APIs' counts (`ConversationPurge.countSweep`, `dueSelection`, `endedUnheldSelection`; `RunPurge.countTextsIn`, `countEventsKeptIn`), each sharing its SQL with the purge it counts (`dueWhere`, `ENDED_UNHELD` and the rows' predicates in `ConversationPurgeImpl`, `TEXTS_KEPT` and `EVENTS_KEPT` in `RunPurgeImpl`); a module writes SQL over its own tables only, and a count of runs reads the conversations it is handed as `RunPurge.ConversationSelection`, the conversation module's SQL. Nothing is stored or audited (§8).
- **The daily sweep** (`RetentionSweep`, on `worker` replicas every `TINFIRM_RETENTION_SWEEP_TICK`, an hour; each company once per `TINFIRM_RETENTION_SWEEP_INTERVAL`, a day, `retention_due`), as SYSTEM in the company's context, through the content modules' purge APIs: `CHAT` (a `CHAT_THREAD`'s or `WEBHOOK`'s messages and decisions' texts, `chat_content_days` after the conversation's end), `CONTENT` (every message, the title, decisions' texts, outputs' titles, the latest result and the subject's title, and the runs' texts through `RunPurge.purgeTextsOf`, `content_days` after), `FILES` (content and note, `files_days` after), run events (`run_events_days` after the run's end, only in ended conversations), then each `RetainedRecords` (lifted holds 400 days after their lift; the seam beside them: package 5's `chat_event`, a day after it arrived, and `chat_user`, 90 days unseen, and a webhook's deliveries, 7 days, their bodies 24 hours; a member's dismissals of inbox notices, 400 days after they were made, §15.7), then agents' memory notes (`memoryDays` after their last use, and a trigger's proposals after 30 days, but those a hold keeps) and the compaction of the memories that changed (§15.18). Open conversations are never purged (G12): they are counted, and the OWNER's inbox shows `RETENTION_OPEN` with how many while any is older than its period. One conversation, or up to 500 runs, a transaction: each purge locks its conversations first (the lock order, §10), then takes the company's hold lock shared (an advisory lock that setting and lifting a hold take exclusively) and reads the open holds again, so that a hold set meanwhile wins (G16). What stays is the tombstone: the conversation's key, agent, person, place, subject key, dates, turn count and ended reason; runs' status, hosts, payer, usage and counters; decisions' kinds and outcomes; outputs' kinds and links; files' names, sizes, types and hashes; spend and the audit. The API shows what went: a message's body is the marker `[This message was removed.]` with `purgedAt`, a decision's texts are null with `purgedAt`, an output's title is empty with `purgedAt`, a file has `contentPurgedAt` and its download is `410 CONTENT_PURGED`, a run has `textsPurgedAt` and `eventsPurgedAt` and no events, a conversation shows its key as its title with `contentPurgedAt` or `erasedAt`. A turn's prompt in an open conversation shows the marker in a purged message's place. Each sweep that purged anything is audited once, `content.purged` with counts.
- **Legal holds** (`GET`, `POST /holds`, `DELETE /holds/{holdId}`, and `GET /holds/conversations/{key}`, a conversation to hold by its key, answering its id and key alone, so not a read of it; `MANAGE_RETENTION`; setting one needs `LEGAL_HOLDS`, Enterprise, ADR-0077, while the open ones stay, are listed and lifted in every edition): on a conversation (all of it, its runs' events included) or a member's principal (their personal conversations, the conversations of the triggers they author, which act for them and have no person (package 4, §15.15; `ConversationPurge` reaches them through `author_principal_id`), and their messages, files and decided decisions in others'; a service account's is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), with a reason of 1 to 500 characters that stays on the hold; audited `hold.set` and `hold.lifted` with the ids; lifting a lifted hold changes nothing. The person is not told; a hold brings back nothing purged before it. `GET /holds?lifted=true` lists the lifted ones too until their deletion.
- **Erasure** (`MANAGE_RETENTION`, one transaction: for a member who left, the systems whose last test names them first; the conversations then, locked in the order of their ids, then the hold lock shared and the holds read, then the purge, the audit last): `POST /principals/{principalId}/erase` erases, for a member who left, every personal conversation of theirs and their messages, files and decisions' texts in others' (`ConversationPurge.eraseAuthored`; a trigger's conversations among them, which are nobody's personal conversations and are never erased whole by a person's erasure), and forgets them as the tester of the systems' last tests, with the account each of those tests used (`Systems.forgetTester`, before the conversations are locked, since a system's row comes before its conversations as a pause takes them; counted in `erased.other` as `systemTests`), so that no system's page names them; the other "by" columns (a decision's `decided_by_principal_id`, a switch's `byUserId`) keep the id, which names nobody once the instance admin deletes the user (`USER_ACCOUNT` below); for a member still in the company, their ended personal conversations, or those named in `conversationIds` (an open one named: `409 ERASURE_NOT_ALLOWED`; open ones not named stay and are listed in the answer). Every kind at once, runs' texts and events included, and `erased_at` on each conversation; the person's dismissals of inbox notices go too (`ConversationPurge.eraseDismissals`, counted in `erased.other` as `inboxDismissals`; a member who left took theirs along, §15.7). `POST /systems/{systemId}/identities/{externalId}/erase` erases an outside identity's words through the modules that hold them (`shared.retention.IdentityContent`, which packages 4 and 5 implement: package 4's `TriggerIdentityContent` in the conversation module, for the `EXTERNAL` comments of a trigger's conversations outside a chat and the questions they answered (§15.15); package 5's `ChatThreadIdentityContent` in the conversation module, for the conversations held in the chat system's threads (`place` `CHAT_THREAD`, a channel trigger's included): the chat user's `EXTERNAL` messages (a mention, and the answer a press stores) lose body and author as `TriggerIdentityContent`'s do, the questions they answered their texts and `decided_by_name`, a decision they pressed `decided_by_external_id` and `decided_by_name`, a linked member's `PERSON` message the chat's author (its body is the member's, for the person's erasure), and a conversation their message started its title; and the chat module's `ChatIdentityContent`, which deletes their `chat_user` row, the `chat_event` rows they sent and their `chat_link_request` rows, after the conversations in the lock order (§10), as the retention sweep deletes them; counted in `erased.other` as `messages`, `decisions`, `messageAuthors`, `titles`, `chatUsers`, `chatEvents` and `chatLinks` (`ChannelTriggersTest`); a firing's `actor` and `request` (part b's rows) are still to be wired), in open conversations too, with the events of the runs whose prompts quoted them. Both are `409 ON_HOLD` (with `holdIds`) while an open hold covers any of it, and answer `erased` (the counts) and `remains` (what stays, where and who removes it: `AUDIT`, `TOMBSTONES`, `RUNNER_SESSIONS`, `SOURCE_SYSTEM`, and as they apply `OPEN_CONVERSATIONS`, `AGENT_TEXTS`, `USER_ACCOUNT`, the last for an instance admin deleting the user); audited `principal.erased` and `identity.erased` with counts and the kinds that remain. A member erased after they left is named nowhere in the company's audit from then on (§8: no target's name, `erased person` as the actor), though their user account keeps the name at the instance. **Each erasure's dry run**, `POST /principals/{principalId}/erase/preview` (the erasure's body) and `POST /systems/{systemId}/identities/{externalId}/erase/preview` (`MANAGE_RETENTION`, the erasure's authorization), answers `ErasurePreview`: `erases`, the counts the erasure's answer would give; `remains`, as it would answer; `holds`, the open holds (`LegalHold`, with the held person's name or the conversation's key and who set it) that would refuse it `409 ON_HOLD`, empty when none does, the counts then being what would go once they are lifted. Every other refusal is the erasure's (`404`, `409 ERASURE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `400` for an empty identity). `ErasureService` shares the erasure's selection (`personScope`, `identityScope`), its check of the holds (`covering`, on the same conversations and principals) and its remains, and counts through the purge APIs' counts in place of their purges (`ConversationPurge.countErase`, `countAuthored`, `countDismissals`; `Systems.countTester`; `AgentMemory.countLearnedIn`; `RunPurge.countTextsOf`, `countEventsOf`, `countEvents`; `IdentityContent.count`, which each content module implements with its erasure's own selection: `TriggerIdentityContent`, `ChatThreadIdentityContent`, `ChatIdentityContent`). It changes nothing and is not audited (§8).
- **Module boundaries.** `governance` reaches content only through `ConversationPurge` and `RunPurge`, public APIs of those modules, and the `shared` ports; no lower module depends on it. The purge APIs know nothing of holds but what governance hands them (`ConversationPurge.Held`). The inbox's items of package 6 come through the conversation module's port `RetentionInbox`, named for them: the spend's and package 5's ports of inbox items are separate providers the inbox reads beside it.
- **Configuration** (§4): `TINFIRM_AUDIT_CHECK_INTERVAL` (`P1D`), `TINFIRM_AUDIT_CHECK_TICK` (`PT1H`), `TINFIRM_AUDIT_CHECK_INITIAL_DELAY` (`PT10M`), `TINFIRM_AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX_EVENTS` (1 000 000), `TINFIRM_RETENTION_SWEEP_INTERVAL` (`P1D`), `TINFIRM_RETENTION_SWEEP_TICK` (`PT1H`), `TINFIRM_RETENTION_SWEEP_INITIAL_DELAY` (`PT15M`). The test profile starts neither job and limits an export to 200 events.
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `AuditQueryTest` | Each filter and their combination, a prefix, time bounds, the details' keys, pages that keep the filter, ADMIN reading and MEMBER `403`, invalid filters `400`; the events about a principal (named, its target, its member's user as the target and as the actor, never another's; with the other filters and in pages of one) and a service account's; a conversation by its key in any case, with a matching or another `conversationId`, an unknown key empty |
  | `AuditTargetNamesTest` | Each type's name for OWNER (a service account, a member, a model key, a user, the company, a group, an agent, a company and a personal MCP server, a company and a personal host, a trigger, the OWNER's own, another person's and a trigger's conversation); none for a deleted agent, an unknown target, a type without a name; ADMIN without the OWNER's personal server, host and conversation, nor another person's conversation, and no title of theirs; a member who left named "(left)", then after their erasure named nowhere in the company's chain (`erased person` as the actor, for OWNER and ADMIN) while the instance's chain names the user, then named again once they joined again (their machine's new presence registered by the actor who added them, with `cause` `principal.joined`, ADR-0092); the instance's chain naming a user by their current name |
  | `AuditNamesTest` | Against fakes: each source of names asked once per page of 100 events; a principal by its member, its own name, "(left)", none for an unknown or malformed id; an erased person and the same person joined again; the instance's chain asking no company's principals |
  | `AuditExportTest` | The whole chain and a range by `seq` and by time (its ends from the events up to the head the export saw, by `seq`), each verified offline by a verifier of the test's own from its first `prevHash` to the head; a changed line failing it; `audit.exported`; the limit (`AUDIT_EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`), a mixed or inverted range, MEMBER `403` |
  | `AuditCheckTest` | A check from the start and then from its head; the newest events removed with the head moved back (`SHORTER`) while the full verification still passes; a head rewritten (`HEAD_CHANGED`); an event changed after the head (`BROKEN`, its seq); the inbox item for OWNER and ADMIN; ADMIN's `403` on the acknowledgement, an older or a passed check refused, the note never audited, the next check anchoring anew; the instance chain; the daily pass checking each chain once |
  | `AuditAtCommitTest` (from package 4's part c) | Two transactions that record an event and lock the same row in opposite orders both commit (they deadlocked while the head was locked at each record), and the chain verifies; a transaction that records an instance event and then enters two companies for an event each appends each in its own chain, nothing in another, and all three chains verify; a rolled-back transaction appends nothing; a transaction's events keep their order and the times of their calls; one recorded after the commit is appended in a transaction of its own; a synchronization registered after the transaction's first event, whose `beforeCommit` waits for a row, waits without the head; an event a synchronization's `beforeCommit` records first is appended in the transaction, before its commit |
  | `EntitiesBeforeTheAuditHeadTest` | A JPA entity changed and not flushed, whose row is held at the commit: the commit waits for the row without the head |
  | `ConversationReadsTest` | The person's list of readers from `conversation.read` events: one per reader with reads and what was read; OWNER sees it, ADMIN `404` |
  | `RetentionSweepTest` | Each kind on time (31, 91, 366 days), open conversations untouched and reported (the inbox, the list by key without title), the tombstone kept, what the API shows (markers, `410`, a run's markers and no events), one `content.purged` per sweep that purged, the chain verifying (G11, G12); kept content and files staying |
  | `HoldTest` | A held conversation and a held person skipped and purged after the lift; holds audited without their reason; who holds; one target, a member's; lifted holds deleted after 400 days |
  | `ErasureTest` | An active member's ended conversations erased and an open one named refused, the answer's counts and what remains, `erasedAt`; a left member's words in others' conversations; a left member forgotten as who tested a system (`systemTests`), an active one not; `ON_HOLD` naming the holds, until each is lifted; a service account; an outside identity's words in an open conversation through `IdentityContent` (the test's `TestIdentityContent`), with the run events that quoted them |
  | `ErasurePreviewTest` | An active member's and a left member's preview equal to the erasure's answer that follows (counts and remains), changing nothing and recording nothing; an open conversation named and a service account refused as the erasure refuses them; the holds named (on the person, on a conversation, on a trigger's author), the erasure refusing with the same ids; an outside identity's words and quoting transcripts through `IdentityContent`; OWNER only (ADMIN and MEMBER `403`), another company's `404`. `ChannelTriggersTest` and `TriggerConversationEndToEndTest` check the chat's and the triggers' modules' counts against their erasures |
  | `RetentionPreviewTest` | Proposed periods against the stored ones, kept content and files, the open holds' share (a held conversation, a held person's words in another's conversation); nothing stored or audited; `RETENTION_INVALID`, OWNER only, another company's `404`; `RetentionService.forecast` equal to what the sweep then purges, kind by kind, at 31, 91, 366 and 367 days, held rows included |
  | `RetentionSettingsTest` | The defaults, who reads and changes, the ranges (`RETENTION_INVALID`), the audit once per change, the answer's `due`; a chat's conversation due under both its periods counted once, a held and an open conversation's runs not counted, `due` from `nextSweep` and `nextSweep` equal to the preview's `current` |
  | `RetentionConstraintTest` | As the runtime role: each text `NULL` exactly with its marker, a purged file keeping its size, a hold's one target and reason, the periods' ranges, another company's conversation never held |
  | `ModularityTest` | Nothing but `mcp` depends on `governance` |

  `TenantIsolationTest` covers every new path with company B's hold, a check of its chain (`TestAuditChecks`) and an outside identity's id.

### 15.12 Governance: spend and budget alerts (package 6)

The part of package 6 that accounts for spend ([its contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-6-contract.md), §3.1, §3.2, §5.1 to §5.3, §5.11 and the decisions of its §11; open points 1 to 4 and 18): what each run cost and who may see it, and the alerts on budgets. It shares the module `governance` with the audit and retention (§15.11); `run_spend` is its own, written only by `SpendSync` and read only by its views. The MCP endpoint's tools and the audit and retention are their own sections; so are the kill switches (§15.13). G1 to G3 are the contract's.

- **A run's cost, never the sandbox's** (G2). The model relay meters each successful answer of the routes that cost (`/v1/messages`, `/v1/responses`, `/v1/chat/completions`) as it streams it back (`RelayUsageMeter`): the `usage` as the format reports it (Anthropic's `message_start` and `message_delta`, OpenAI's final usage, the Responses API's `response.completed`, or a plain JSON answer), the last values reported, since a stream reports running totals; the model the request named (else the answer's); the key's LiteLLM id (its SHA-256, never the key). An answer without usage counts a request and no tokens. The relay publishes it after the answer ended, outside any transaction, as an internal event of the model module, which `RelayUsageStore` adds to `relay_usage` per run, model and key; `ModelRelay.usageByModel` reads it. The sandbox's `USAGE` totals stay on the run (`RunReads.tokens`) as an estimate shown until the run settles; they set no cost, and alert nothing.
- **Settling a run** (`SpendSync`, the only writer of `run_spend`; G3):
  1. A run's end publishes `RunEvents.RunEnded` in its transaction, after `TurnEnded` was told; `SpendSync` hears it there. A run paid by a budget that relayed a request (`ModelRelay.usage`) gets a `PENDING` settlement due `settle-delay` (2 minutes) later, since LiteLLM writes its logs in batches. A subscription's run that started settles at once: a `SUBSCRIPTION` row under `run.model` (else `default`) with the tokens its sandbox reported and no cost. Any other run gets nothing. A run that ends again (lost by lease expiry, then resumed) moves its pending end.
  2. On `worker` replicas every `sync-interval` (a minute), `spend_due` names at most 200 due runs; each company's are claimed in one transaction that first takes a transaction-scoped advisory lock of the company (`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`), so one worker claims all of a company's due runs or none, and leases them five minutes (`lease_until`). Two workers never read one key's logs twice.
  3. Outside any transaction, `SpendLogs.of` reads LiteLLM's `GET /spend/logs?api_key=&start_date=&end_date=&summarize=false` once per key the relay forwarded the claimed runs with, over their span (a minute before the first start to five after the last end, whole UTC days); each run keeps the entries whose `request_tags` hold `run:{runId}` and whose start lies in its span.
  4. A run with at least as many entries as the relay counted answers settles from them: a `GATEWAY` row per model (LiteLLM's model group), requests counted, tokens and LiteLLM's `spend` summed.
  5. Otherwise it is tried again 2, 4, 8… minutes later (at most 32), with LiteLLM's reason in `last_error` when the logs could not be read. Once `relay-after` (an hour) passed since its end, the relay's count settles it: a `RELAY` row per model, priced with LiteLLM's `GET /model/info` (`SpendLogs.prices`, kept an hour; a model without a price leaves the cost `NULL`). With `tinfirm.spend.source` `RELAY` every run settles from the relay's count as soon as it is due.

  Each settlement's rows are written in one transaction that locks it again under the worker's lease, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, and sets it `SETTLED`. A row copies the run's conversation, agent, payer and acting principal, `personal` (the conversation is not a trigger's), the trigger (`TriggerSpend.triggerOf`) and the UTC day of its end, so that views need no join. LiteLLM's spend log fields are read as v1.105.1 is documented to write them (open point 1: an opt-in test against a real LiteLLM is still to confirm that the tags come back; else `source` `RELAY`).
- **The spend views** (`GET /spend`, tag `Spend`; `SpendQueryService`; G1). `from` inclusive and `to` exclusive, dates in `zone` (IANA, default `UTC`), the current calendar month by default, at most 366 days; a run counts at its end.
