At most two of `groupBy` `payer`, `actor`, `agent`, `trigger`, `conversation`, `model`, `period` (bucketed `DAY`, `WEEK` from Monday or `MONTH` in the zone); filters `payerId`, `actorId`, `agentId`, `triggerId`, `conversationId`, `model`, `source`. A row's keys carry ids and labels: a principal's name, "{name} (left)" for one who left, an agent's name (a deleted one's last), a trigger's name, a conversation's key, and its title only when it is the caller's own or a trigger's; the amounts are `runs`, `requests`, the four token counts, `costUsd` (of `GATEWAY` and `RELAY`), `relayCostUsd` (the relay's share) and `subscriptionTokens`; `totals` beside. At most 10 000 rows (100 000 as CSV). The scope is part of the query's `WHERE`, before every filter and grouping:

  | Caller | Runs counted | Conversation rows |
  |---|---|---|
  | MEMBER | Those they paid, and those of the triggers whose author or team they are (`TriggerSpend.audienceOf`) | Their own and the triggers', titled |
  | ADMIN; an instance admin who is not a member | Every run | Their own and the triggers', titled; every other person's personal conversations folded into one row per person (`folded`, `personPrincipalId`, no `id`, key or title) |
  | OWNER | Every run | Every conversation by key; titled only their own and the triggers' |

  A `conversationId`, `payerId` or any other filter on something the caller may not see answers exactly as an unknown id: no rows, zero totals, never `404`. An agent's owner sees nothing of the agent's spend as such (open point 3).
- **A conversation's cost** (`Conversation.cost`, `ConversationSummary.costUsd` and `tokens`). What `getSpend` with the conversation's `conversationId` counts over all time, in the same scope (G1), so that a conversation's page never shows more of spend than the spend views do: `costUsd` (of `GATEWAY` and `RELAY`), `tokens` (`ConversationTokens`: input, cache writes, cache reads and output, of every settled row, subscriptions' included; the four add up to the total), `subscriptionTokens` and its settled turns, each once with its models together (`runId`, `costUsd`, null for a subscription's or an unpriced `RELAY` turn, and `source`), oldest first; the list carries the cost and tokens alone. A turn not settled yet is not in it, and a reader outside the scope (a trigger's author it started with, no longer in its audience) gets zero and no turns, as for an unknown conversation. The conversation module declares the port `ConversationCosts`, looked up when used; the governance module implements it (`ConversationSpend`, over `SpendQueryService`'s scope), in the read's transaction, for the request's user (nothing without one). Not audited, as the spend views are not. The web app shows the total in the side column's "Cost" (the settled spend, with what the runtime reported of the finished turns on a budget not settled yet added, its `usage.costUsd` where it gave one and its tokens either way, and the total marked "~" while such a turn remains: one whose requests the relay passed (`relay.requests`), which will settle, or without the relay's count one whose runtime reported use; a turn that relayed nothing never settles and is not waited for; under it the tokens, in, cache write, cache read and out), each turn's settled cost on its status line (else its estimate, marked "~"; a subscription's turn "subscription", without an amount), and the cost and the tokens together (the four as its title) in the list's row; the MCP endpoint's `get_conversation` says both in its header and `list_conversations` per row. `Accept: text/csv` (preferred over JSON) answers the same rows as CSV (per grouping its id and label, then the amounts; a field a spreadsheet would read as a formula is prefixed with `'`), audited `spend.exported`; reading in the app is not audited. `400 SPEND_RANGE_INVALID` for a longer range, `to` not after `from`, more than two groupings or one twice, an unknown zone.
- **Budget alerts** (`BudgetAlerts`, the model module's; open point 4). After each sync that read a key's spend (`KeySync`: after a run, in the reconciliation), each threshold of `model_settings.budget_alert_thresholds` (1 to 3 percentages, `[80, 100]` at first, `PUT /model-settings`' `budgetAlertThresholds`) that the period's spend reached is recorded once per period (`budget_alert`). An alert stands while its period has not reset and the spend is still at its threshold of the budget as it is now (a raised budget ends it). The inbox shows each recipient the highest threshold that stands, once per principal (`InboxItemKind.BUDGET`, `InboxBudget`):

  | Principal | Told at each threshold | Also told at 100 % |
  |---|---|---|
  | A member | The member (`own`) | OWNER and ADMIN (an instance admin acting in the company too), with `waitingRuns` (the member's queued turns waiting `BUDGET_SPENT`) and `waitingTriggers`: counts only |
  | A service account | OWNER and ADMIN, and the authors of the live triggers it pays (`TriggerSpend.authorsPaidBy`) | The same, with the counts |

  An admin changes the budget through package 2's `PATCH /principals/{principalId}/model-access`. Not audited.
- **The company's monthly amount** (open point 18). `PUT /model-settings`' `monthlyAlertUsd` (`clearMonthlyAlert` removes it; both in one request is `400`); after each settlement with a cost, the calendar month's (UTC) `run_spend` cost is compared with it and a crossing is recorded once (`company_spend_alert`); OWNER and ADMIN see `COMPANY_SPEND` until the month ends. Nothing is capped.
- **The sync's fallback** (`SPEND_SYNC`, OWNER and ADMIN): while more than 10 of today's (UTC) runs were settled from the relay's count, or a pending run's logs could not be read for an hour, with LiteLLM's reason; it goes once the logs are read again.
- **The inbox's port.** The conversation module declares `InboxItems` (package 5's port; `itemsFor(caller, limit)`), looked up when used, beside the retention's `RetentionInbox`; `InboxService` adds its items, longest waiting first, and counts them in `InboxCounts.notices`. The governance module implements it (`SpendInboxItems`), computing every item from the state on each read; none is stored as a notification. Each is a notice with its key and dismissible (a budget's key is its alert, the company's spend's its month, the sync's since when; §15.7): a member's dismissal is stored, the notices never are.
- **Triggers' seam.** Every rule above reads triggers through `governance.internal.TriggerSpend` (`triggerOf`, `audienceOf`, `names`, `authorsPaidBy`, `livePaidBy`), which `TriggersSpend` answers over package 4's public API (§15.15): a run's trigger is its conversation's (`RunReads.find`, `Conversations.find`; the database keeps `run.trigger_id` equal to it), and the trigger module's `TriggerDirectory` names the triggers a principal authors or whose team they are in, live or deleted (`ofPrincipal`), their names, a deleted one's last name included (`names`), and the live triggers a principal pays (`livePaidBy`). `run_spend.trigger_id` has no foreign key. A test stands triggers in with one `@Primary` bean (`SpendTriggerScopeTest`).
- **Not built here**, of the contract's spend: a principal's `periodSpend` on `GET /me/model-access` and an admin's view of a principal (§5.2), and the key rotation's dates on the principal's page (§5.3). The MCP endpoint's spend tool is `get_spend` on `Spend.report`, the Spend API's JSON report in the caller's scope (§15.20; `McpSpendTools`), not the contract's `get_my_spend`.
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `SpendSyncTest` | Against `FakeLiteLlm`'s spend logs and prices (`logSpend`, `price`): the relay's count per model and key (Anthropic's stream, OpenAI's JSON, an answer without usage); a run's tagged requests summed per model and another run's ignored; written once; logs under two keys; late logs waited for; none after an hour, then `RELAY` at LiteLLM's price; unreadable logs retried with their reason and the admins' `SPEND_SYNC` item after an hour; logs larger than the 64 MiB the server reads refused unread and retried with their reason; a subscription's run at once without a cost; a run that relayed nothing; two workers reading each key's logs once; the company's monthly amount and its `COMPANY_SPEND` item |
  | `SpendRelaySourceTest` | `tinfirm.spend.source` `RELAY`: settled from the relay's count without reading the logs |
  | `SpendScopeTest` | Every row of the scope's table: a member's own; ADMIN's folded rows; an instance admin as ADMIN; OWNER by key and titled only their own; an unseen `conversationId` answering exactly as an unknown one; filters within the scope; labels, "(left)", two groupings, sources; periods in a zone; the range's checks; the CSV and `spend.exported`; another company's member `404`; an agent's owner seeing nothing more |
  | `SpendTriggerScopeTest` | Through a stand-in `TriggerSpend`: a trigger's author and team see its spend titled, an outsider nothing (a `triggerId` filter as an unknown id), ADMIN a trigger's conversation titled and never folded; the conversation's cost to its author and ADMIN, and none to the author out of its audience; the sync copying a run's trigger |
  | `ConversationCostTest` | A conversation's cost on its page and in the list: each settled turn once with its models together, a turn not settled yet left out, another conversation's spend its own, `getSpend` over the conversation agreeing, OWNER seeing the same; a subscription's turn with its tokens and no cost; its tokens, in, cache write, cache read and out, summed over its settled rows, the same in the list and in `getSpend` |
  | `BudgetAlertTest` | Each threshold once per period; a member's own at 80 % and admins' only at 100 %, with counts; a service account's to admins at each threshold; a raised budget and a new period ending the item; the thresholds' bounds, their audit, `monthlyAlertUsd` and its clear |
  | `SpendConstraintTest` | As the runtime role: one spend row per run and model, never changed or deleted; the cost by source; the day of the end; never another company's run; every table behind row-level security; `spend_due` to the runtime role; a settlement settled exactly with its time; thresholds and alerts in range |
  | `RelayUsageMeterTest`, `SpendCsvTest` | The meter over parts of any size, the Responses stream, a plain answer, the routes not metered; the CSV's quoting and formulas, and which `Accept` asks for CSV |
  | `RunEventIntegrationTest` (changed) | `RunEnded` published once with the final status |

  `TenantIsolationTest` calls `GET /spend` as a member of company A against company B (404), and `ModularityTest` lists `governance`.

### 15.13 Package 6: kill switches, suspension, break-glass's view and a member who leaves

The [package 6 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-6-contract.md) §5.4, §5.5 and §5.10, with its §11 (open points 5, 6, 7, 12, 13, 21 and 24); migration `V021__switches.sql`. The `governance` module (§10, §15.11, §15.12) also serves the switches' page and the leaver's checklist and reaches every other module through its public API; nothing but `mcp` may depend on it (`ModularityTest`). Each switch's state stays with its data, and what a switch does above its owner is done by the higher module in a port the owner declares, in the switch's transaction:

| Switch | State (V021) | Owner's public API | Port above it (implemented by) | Event |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The company's stop | `company.runs_stopped_at`, `_by`, `_reason`, `_access_id` | `company.CompanySwitches` (`runsStopped`, `stopRuns`, `liftRuns`) | `company.CompanyRunsDependents` (`runsStopping`: run and trigger; `runsLifted`: trigger, its outbox included) | `CompanyEvents.RunsStopped`, `RunsResumed` |
| A system's pause | `external_system.paused_at`, `_by`, `_reason` (a company system only) | `system.SystemSwitches` (`pause`, `resume`, `paused`); `SystemInfo.paused` | `system.SystemSwitchDependents` (`systemPausing`: conversation and trigger; `systemResuming`: trigger) | `SystemEvents.SystemPaused`, `SystemResumed` |
| A principal's suspension | `principal.status` with `suspended_at`, `_by`, `_reason`, `_cause` (`ADMIN`, `USER_DEACTIVATED`, `SCIM`: a company's SCIM, which only it lifts, §15.25) | `principal.PrincipalSwitches` (`suspendMember`, `resumeMember`, `suspended`, `ofMember`) | `company.MemberStatuses` (principal), asked by the access guard and the last-owner checks; its `principalIds` gives the Members API each member's `principalId`, which a member's own budget and models are set by (`/principals/{principalId}/model-access`) | `PrincipalEvents.PrincipalSuspended`, `PrincipalResumed` |

- **The company's stop** (`POST /runs-stop {reason}`, `DELETE /runs-stop`; `STOP_RUNS`: OWNER, ADMIN, an instance admin under break-glass). The stop locks the company's row (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`), marks it, and the run module cancels every `RUNNING` run and every run assigned to a host (`RunCancellations.cancelRunningOfCompany`, conversations locked first in the order of their ids), each `run.cancelled` with `cause` `company.runs_stopped`; queued runs wait `COMPANY_STOPPED` at the dispatcher's step 0. While it lasts: starting a conversation (in `ConversationService.startFor`, so from the app, a direct message and the endpoint alike) and approving are `409 COMPANY_STOPPED` (answers, denials and messages are taken; their turns wait), an approved call not yet run does not run, and the model relay answers `503`. The chats' part (direct messages, a paused chat system, a suspended member) is in §15.16, Kill switches. The lift dispatches the queue (`RunsResumed`) and answers what the dependents armed and released and every other switch that stays on. Triggers (§15.15): the stop makes every active trigger wait `COMPANY_STOPPED` and holds its conversations' write-back, `TriggerGate` answers the same while the stop lasts (fires nothing; polls go on), and the lift arms every active trigger at the lift, clears the wait and releases the held rows (the stated exception to package 4's decision 9); package 5's `WebhookInbox` answers `503` with `Retry-After: 300` while `runsStopped` is present.
- **A system's pause** (`POST /systems/{id}/pause {reason, stopRunning}`, `POST …/resume {resumeTriggers}`, on by default; `MANAGE_SYSTEMS`; a personal server is `400 PERSONAL_SYSTEM`). `Accounts.check` answers `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, which leaves a conversation's binding as it is (`BindingService`: nothing waits on it); a run lists none of its tools and a call from an earlier list is `TOOL_DENIED` ("{system} is paused by an admin", `ToolRules` step 5); a queued turn whose workspace holds one of its repositories waits `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (`TurnSource.pausedWorkspaceSystem`, the dispatcher's step 0); testing, refreshing and connecting on it are `409 SYSTEM_PAUSED`. With `stopRunning` the conversation module cancels the running turns of the open conversations bound to it (`cause` `system.paused`). The trigger module pauses every active trigger whose source or table names the system (`paused_reason SYSTEM_PAUSED`; the gate pauses one the same way when `Accounts.check` answers `SYSTEM_PAUSED`), and the resume, with `resumeTriggers`, resumes each through its gate with `armed_at` moved to the resume (one the gate stops stays paused for the gate's reason); the switches' page lists them per paused system through `governance.TriggerSwitchReads`.
- **A member's suspension** (`POST /members/{userId}/suspend {reason}`, `…/resume`; `MANAGE_MEMBERS`, an OWNER's needs `MANAGE_OWNERS`): `409 CANNOT_SUSPEND_SELF`, `409 LAST_OWNER` for the last active OWNER (the membership's lock serialises it with role changes). The guard refuses the member everywhere but the company's page and the switches' page (§6), their bearer credentials and the consent answer as a non-member's, and a suspended principal decides nothing, as a conversation's person or as a trigger's approver, author or team (`DecisionPresenter.decider` gives them no principal; `DecisionService.decide` answers `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED`). After the commit of `PrincipalSuspended` the model module blocks the key (built) and the run module cancels, on its workers as SYSTEM, every active run the principal acts for or pays (`RunCancellations.cancelOfPrincipal`, `cause` `principal.suspended`), for both kinds. A service account's suspension takes an optional `reason`. Resuming wakes what waits; a suspension the user's deactivation made answers `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` to an admin's resume.
- **A user's deactivation** (`PATCH /api/admin/users/{id} {active: false}`) publishes `identity.UserEvents.UserDeactivated`; the principal module suspends the user's principal in every company they are a member of (found through the narrow `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup `member_companies(uuid)`, ids only, since the instance admin's flag reads no other user's memberships since V016), in the deactivating transaction, each audited in its company's chain with `cause` `USER_DEACTIVATED`. Reactivating (`UserReactivated`) resumes those, and only those.
- **The switches' page** (`GET /kill-switches`): admins get every switch that is on (`RUNS_STOPPED`, `SYSTEM_PAUSED` with its paused triggers, `MEMBER_SUSPENDED`, `SERVICE_IDENTITY_SUSPENDED`, `AGENT_PAUSED`, `TRIGGER_PAUSED`, `PERSONAL_MCP_SERVERS_OFF`) with who, when, why and the counts per kind; a member gets the company's stop (the banner, `runsStop`) and their own suspension (`mySuspension`). The reasons stay on their rows and never enter the audit chain. A `MEMBER_SUSPENDED` row names the member's principal (`targetId`) and their user (`userId`), which resuming them takes (`POST /members/{userId}/resume`), so that the page resumes in place. `GET /departures` (OWNER, ADMIN; `MANAGE_MEMBERS`) lists the members who left and whose checklist is not closed, newest first, at most 500, each with its principal, user, name and when they left: the admins' `DEPARTURE` items without the inbox.
- **Break-glass's view** (§6): `CompanyAccess.breakGlassAllows` (ADMIN's permissions and `MANAGE_OWNERS`); `Conversations.maySee`, the decisions' list and `RunVisibility` give OWNER, and nobody by instance-admin rights, another person's personal conversation; `MemberService` lets break-glass appoint an OWNER and neither demote nor remove one.
- **A member who leaves** (open point 21). Removing a member publishes `GroupEvents.GroupMembersChanged` (`removedUserIds` the leaver) for each group the membership's cascade takes them out of, and records those groups (`principal_departure_group`); an agent that passes to the admins keeps `former_owner_principal_id`. `GET /members/{userId}/departure` previews it (triggers that pause, open conversations that end, agents, personal hosts, accounts, groups); `GET /principals/{principalId}/departure` is the checklist, computed from the state: 1. the triggers paused `AUTHOR_LEFT` (`TriggerSwitchReads`), 2. the recorded groups that have no member now, 3. the company's credentials they connected (stored or signed in last) that nobody connected again since, to store again with a new token or sign in again (`connectedCredentials`, from `accounts.credentialsConnectedBy(companyId, userId)`, ADR-0072), 4. the agents passed to the admins (`Agents.passedFrom`), 5. the open change requests their conversations opened with their own account (`PersonConversations.openChangeRequestsOf`), 6. their ended personal conversations. `POST …/departure/close {force}` closes it once items 1 to 3 are empty (`409 DEPARTURE_OPEN`), or with `force`, audited `principal.departure_closed` (`force`, `openItems`). Admins get a `DEPARTURE` inbox item until it is closed (`conversation.InboxItems`, the generic port of higher modules' inbox items, counted with spend's in `counts.notices`); the team's `TRIGGER_ORPHANED` item is package 4's, through the same port.
- **An agent whose owner left** (open point 13): `PUT /agents/{agentId}/owner {principalId}` (`MANAGE_AGENTS`) gives any agent a new owner, an active member, audited `agent.owner_changed` (`formerOwnerPrincipalId`, null when the admins held it, `ownerPrincipalId`).

Audit actions (§8 rules: ids and kinds, never a reason or a person's name):

| Action | Actor | Target | `details` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `company.runs_stopped`, `company.runs_resumed` | USER | `company` | `runsCancelled` (ids); at the lift `triggersArmed`, `heldRows` |
| `system.paused`, `system.resumed` | USER | `system` | `stopRunning`, `runsCancelled`, `triggersPaused`; `resumeTriggers`, `triggersResumed` |
| `principal.suspended`, `principal.resumed` (changed) | USER (the admin, or the instance admin who deactivated the user) | `principal` | `kind`, `cause` (`ADMIN`, `USER_DEACTIVATED`, `SCIM`); `name` for a service account, `userId` for a member |
| `run.cancelled` (changed) | the switch's actor; SYSTEM after a suspension | `run` | `cause`: `principal.suspended`, `company.runs_stopped`, `system.paused` |
| `principal.departure_closed` | USER | `principal` | `userId`, `force`, `openItems` (counts) |
| `agent.owner_changed` | USER | `agent` | `formerOwnerPrincipalId`, `ownerPrincipalId` |

Queue reasons gain `COMPANY_STOPPED` and `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (step 0, before the agent). Tests: `KillSwitchesIntegrationTest` (a suspended member's reads, writes, decisions and running turn; the last active owner; a deactivation across companies; the stop and its lift, by an instance admin too; a pause, its 409s, the turn it holds), `DepartureIntegrationTest` (the preview, `GroupMembersChanged`, the checklist and its closing, `force`, a passed agent's new owner), `BreakGlassViewTest` (ADMIN's view and appointing an OWNER; an instance admin who is a member; no break-glass through a bearer credential or the consent, a suspended member a non-member there); `TenantIsolationTest` covers every new path.

**Triggers (package 4a), wired.** Each port is called in the switch's transaction, in the company's tenant context, as the actor of the switch, before the switch's own audit record. The trigger module's `TriggerRunSwitches` is ordered first among the dependents, so that each trigger's row is locked before the conversation and run modules lock its conversations (§10).

| Port (module) | Method | Implemented by, and what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `company.CompanyRunsDependents` | `List<UUID> runsStopping(UUID companyId)` | `trigger.internal.TriggerRunSwitches`: every active trigger waits `COMPANY_STOPPED` (`trigger.waiting`) and its open conversations' write-back is held (`outbox.status HELD`); answers no runs (the run module's `RunSwitches` cancels them) |
| `company.CompanyRunsDependents` | `Lifted runsLifted(UUID companyId)`; `Lifted(int triggersArmed, int heldRowsReleased)` | `TriggerRunSwitches`: every active trigger's `armed_at` moves to the lift (the stated exception to decision 9: what happened during the stop fires nothing), its `COMPANY_STOPPED` wait is cleared and its held rows released; both counts go into `company.runs_resumed` |
| `system.SystemSwitchDependents` | `Pausing systemPausing(UUID companyId, UUID systemId, boolean stopRunning)`; `Pausing(List<UUID> runsCancelled, int triggersPaused)` | `TriggerRunSwitches`: every active trigger whose source is the system or whose table has a row on it pauses `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (`trigger.paused`, `cause` `system.paused`; its queued turns wait `TRIGGER_PAUSED`, its write-back is held); the running turns are the conversation module's (`ConversationSwitches`) |
| `system.SystemSwitchDependents` | `Resuming systemResuming(UUID companyId, UUID systemId, boolean resumeTriggers)`; `Resuming(int triggersResumed, int triggersStillPaused)` | `TriggerRunSwitches`: first the windows of the system's polls move to the resume, less the overlap (part b, §15.15: what changed during the pause is history), with or without its triggers; with `resumeTriggers`, each trigger paused `SYSTEM_PAUSED` on the system goes through the gate's verdict: it resumes armed at the resume (`trigger.resumed`, `cause` `system.resumed`; a `Wait` verdict resumes it waiting), or stays paused for the gate's reason (`trigger.paused`, `cause` `system.resumed`), or is deleted as the gate deletes it; without, they stay `SYSTEM_PAUSED` |
| `governance.TriggerSwitchReads` | `paused`, `pausedBySystem`, `pausedAuthorLeft`, `authoredBy`; `PausedTrigger(…)` | `governance.internal.TriggerSwitchReadsImpl`, an adapter over the trigger module's public `Triggers` (`paused`, `onSystem`, `ofAuthor`, added for it) and the conversation module's `TriggerConversations.openOf` (the waiting conversations), so that `trigger` never depends on `governance`; `pausedBy` is on the audit record, not here |
| (the gate) | `TriggerGate.check`, `TriggerGateImpl.verdict` | Asks `CompanySwitches.runsStopped` after its own checks: a trigger that would fire or wait waits `COMPANY_STOPPED`, one that would pause or be deleted still is; a row whose `Accounts.check` answers `SYSTEM_PAUSED` pauses it `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, the row kept |

V021 adds `SYSTEM_PAUSED` to `trigger_paused_reason_ck` and `COMPANY_STOPPED` to `trigger_waiting_reason_ck` (and both to the API's `TriggerPausedReason` and `TriggerWaitingReason`). Part c's delivery of the outbox puts a row off by `first-retry` while the company's runs are stopped or its system is paused (`OutboxDelivery`; a row written during the stop is `PENDING`). `TriggerSwitchesIntegrationTest` proves each wiring with real triggers: the stop's wait and the gate refusing a run, a person's resume during the stop waiting again, the lift arming without a resume; a system's pause pausing its tracker trigger and not a schedule, the switches' page listing it, the author's resume refused while it lasts, a resume without and then with its triggers; a leaver's checklist listing the trigger paused `AUTHOR_LEFT`. `TriggerGateTest` adds the paused system and the stopped company to the gate's table.

### 15.14 Package R: replay safety and idempotent submissions

The design is the [package R contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-r-contract.md): three ideas of pi-durable, a durable agent harness, taken without the library, in the server's own PostgreSQL record. A tool's call is journaled before it runs, and after Tinfirm lost track of it runs again only when its tool is `SAFE`; a request sent again gets the stored result, on any replica and after a restart; and a submission sent again with its key returns what the first one made. The system module holds the replay class, the conversation module the call journal and the submission keys (both behind its existing public APIs, once for the REST API and the MCP endpoint), the run module's router keeps nothing of a tool request, and runner protocol v2 carries the class to the runtime. The tables are in §7, the action in §8, the code in §6, the variables in §4. It also covers what packages 4a, 4b, 5a and 6's switches added, and the audit appended at the commit (§8): a trigger's run's calls are journaled as a person's, and the paths they put behind the five submissions take the caller's key.

- **The replay class** (`system.ToolReplay`, beside `ToolEffect`; the contract's §3.1). Every tool of a system has one (`ToolInfo.replay`). The default follows the effect (`ToolReplay.of`): `READ` is `SAFE`, `WRITE` and `UNKNOWN` are `UNSAFE`, so an MCP server's tool takes its confirmed effect's. A system's catalogue may declare a write `SAFE` only when the system's API makes the call idempotent (the same call twice leaves the same state and writes nothing new: a field set to a value with `PUT`, a label added to a set); a comment, an issue, a change request or a transition with side effects never is. **No write is declared `SAFE`**: every fixed tool keeps its effect's default (`FixedTools`; GitHub's `label` creates a label that does not exist yet, so it stays `UNSAFE`), and each later declaration is reviewed with its test. The run's tool list carries the class (`run.assign.tools[].replay`, `SAFE` or `UNSAFE`; `ConversationTools.spec`): optional in `protocol/runner-v2.schema.json` and in the Rust crate (`ToolSpec.replay: Option<ToolReplay>`), absent from older servers, for the runtime's information only. The server decides.
- **The call journal** (`ToolJournal`, `ToolCallStore`, `tool_call`; the contract's §3.2), which replaced the run module's in-memory map of tool requests (two minutes, one replica). `ToolCalls` handles a `TOOL` request (`RunRequests.call`) so:
  1. The tool named and its arguments parsed as before; their SHA-256 in canonical form (keys sorted at every level, no whitespace), of the arguments as the run sent them, not as the system's check normalizes them (the contract's §3.2 allows either: a request sent again carries the same arguments, and the hash is known before any check).
  2. The request's row, if any, in a short transaction, before anything is checked:

     | The row | The answer |
     |---|---|
     | None | The checks of §15.7 (step 1), then the **claim** in a transaction of its own that locks nothing but the row: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (run_id, request_id) DO NOTHING`, then the row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. A new row is `RUNNING` at attempt 1, and the call runs (3); a row another request inserted first is handled as below. The claim reads the run first (`RunReads.find`): a run no longer `RUNNING` with the request's epoch claims and calls nothing (`RUN_NOT_ACTIVE`), since a check that waited for the conversation while the run's end held it would leave a row that end never reached. A call refused by its checks (a paused system, a tool switched off) has no row |
     | Other arguments (`arguments_sha256`) | `INVALID_REQUEST`, never a call |
     | `DONE` | Its stored answer (`result`). Nothing is called |
     | `INTERRUPTED`, `UNSAFE` | `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` again, from the stored answer |
     | `RUNNING`, claimed less than the tool timeout and 15 seconds ago | It runs elsewhere, on this replica or another: the request reads the row every 500 ms until it is `DONE` or `INTERRUPTED` and answers what it holds. Its wait ends `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.answer-within` (55 seconds) after the call's claim, or at the tool timeout from the request when that is sooner, within the host's wait of 65 seconds from its first send; a row still `RUNNING` then is answered `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `TIMEOUT`; a `SAFE` call `TOOL_FAILED`) and left as it is, unaudited (`ToolJournal.unanswered`): the replica that runs it records it, or the sweep settles it, and a request sent again later gets that answer. Never nothing: the host sends a request again only after a reconnect |
     | `RUNNING` and older, `INTERRUPTED` and `SAFE` (the sweep's), or `DONE` without an answer (a run resumed after its end cleared it) | Its replica stopped (`ToolJournal.retake`, under the conversation's lock and then the row's, checked again): a `SAFE` call is claimed again (attempt + 1, `started_at` now), checked again (a refusal now becomes its answer) and runs; an `UNSAFE` one running is set `INTERRUPTED`, noted and audited (cause `RESTART`) and answered `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`; one that ended is answered `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` as it stands |
  3. **The call**, outside any transaction, on a thread of its own (`ConversationWorkers.submit`), until 5 seconds before the request's tool timeout (`ToolJournal.recordReserve`, a quarter of a shorter timeout), so that the record has the rest within the run module's bound: `ConversationBindings.use`, then `SystemTools.call`, as before. A system paused after the call's check (package 6's switch) gives no token (`Accounts.check` answers `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, the binding keeps its state): the call is refused as the check refuses it (`TOOL_DENIED`, "Jira is paused by an admin. …", `tool.denied`), its row `DONE` with that refusal, and the system is never called.
  4. **The record**, in a transaction under the conversation's lock (§10): the account and the binding first (`ToolRunner.effects`: a credential refused is `Accounts.refused` and the binding's check, then the call parked on it), then the journal row `DONE` with the answer as it goes out (`ToolJournal.finished`, only while it is still that attempt running), then `tool.called` and a write's `SystemWritten` last (`ToolRunner.audited`). Every answer after a claim settles the row: a parked call's `PENDING`, `ACCOUNT_GONE`, a token not got now (`TOOL_FAILED`, try again), a refusal; an exception inside Tinfirm is `DONE` with `TOOL_FAILED` ("The tool call failed in Tinfirm."), never run again for its request. A row settled while the call ran (the sweep or a request sent again elsewhere took it, its replica's clock behind) keeps its answer: the record still takes the account and audits `tool.called`, parks nothing, and answers the row's answer, as every replica answers it. A row whose run ended while the call ran keeps no answer (the run's end cleared it; `finish` stores none once `run_ended_at` is set). A record that fails (its transaction rolled back, the call made) settles the call in a transaction of its own, the conversation first (`ToolJournal.recordFailed`): an `UNSAFE` call `INTERRUPTED`, noted and audited (cause `ERROR`), answered `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`; a `SAFE` one `DONE` with `TOOL_FAILED`; so the host never hears an outcome the journal does not hold, nor `TOOL_FAILED` for a write that may have happened.

  **Timeout** (`ToolJournal.timedOut`). Past `TINFIRM_RUN_TOOL_TIMEOUT` less the record's reserve the call's thread is interrupted, as before, and the row settled in a transaction of its own, the conversation first: an `UNSAFE` call `INTERRUPTED`, noted and audited (cause `TIMEOUT`), answered `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`; a `SAFE` call `DONE` with `TOOL_FAILED` ("The tool did not answer within 55 seconds."), so that a request sent again does not run it a second time. A call that ends later anyway is still recorded (`tool.called`, on a thread of its own), its answer gone. The run module's router keeps a bound of its own, the tool timeout and 2 seconds, within the host's wait of the timeout and 5 seconds, against a port whose record waits too long (a lock wait the thread's interrupt cannot end): past it, and for a port that fails or answers null, the router answers `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `TIMEOUT`, "Tinfirm did not answer within 62 seconds", the tool timeout and 2 seconds), never `TOOL_FAILED`, since it does not know whether the call ran and the journal may still record its outcome, which a request sent again gets; the thread is interrupted. The runner host words its own timeout of a TOOL request the same way, and waits once more from a send after a reconnect (the Rust crate's `HostRequester`): the replica that takes the request again answers within its own timeout.

  **`TOOL_INTERRUPTED`** is a tool error result (`DONE`, `isError: true`), not a rejection: its text names the tool and the system, says that the call may or may not have taken effect, and asks the agent to check the system's state, with a read tool where it has one, before calling again ("The call of `jira_comment` on Jira was interrupted (TOOL_INTERRUPTED): it did not answer within 55 seconds, so it may or may not have taken effect. Check Jira's state, with a read tool where you have one, before you call it again."); its structured content is `{code: "TOOL_INTERRUPTED", cause: "TIMEOUT" | "RESTART" | "ERROR"}`. Audited `tool.interrupted` (§8), and the open conversation shows it as a `SYSTEM` message of the run ("{agent}'s call of `jira_comment` on Jira was interrupted: … It may or may not have taken effect; {agent} was told to check Jira before calling it again."), which the person sees and the next turn's prompt quotes.

  **The sweep** (`ToolJournalSweep`, on `worker` replicas every `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.sweep-interval`, a minute; `tool_call_due`, ids only, each settled again under its locks in its company): a call `RUNNING` and claimed longer ago than the tool timeout and 15 seconds, whose host never sent its request again, is settled: an `UNSAFE` one `INTERRUPTED` and audited (cause `RESTART`), a `SAFE` one `INTERRUPTED` without an audit or an answer, claimed again if its request comes back. The rows of runs that ended longer ago than `TINFIRM_TOOL_JOURNAL_RETENTION` (seven days) are deleted, and rows claimed longer ago than that whose run's end never reached them (`run_ended_at` null: a claim that raced the end; `tool_call_unended_idx`), at most 1000 rows a transaction. **When a run ends** (`TurnEnded.turnEnded`, in its end's transaction, before anything else of the conversation) its rows' answers are cleared and `run_ended_at` set; a run resumed after its loss (`turnResumed`) clears `run_ended_at` again. The retention's purge of a conversation's content, the purge of runs' events (`ConversationPurge.purgeToolCalls` beside `RunPurge.purgeEvents`, in the sweep and in an identity's erasure) and a conversation's erasure delete its rows.
- **Agent memory's writes** (§15.18; `MemoryTools`, `V044`). `remember` and `forget` are journaled as a system tool's call, by their run and request id, never on a note: the note's own `request_id` (`V039`), which made `remember` idempotent until a later request replaced the note or a forget, the owner's delete, a compaction or retention took it, is gone. A request id the journal holds is answered from it before any check (another tool or other arguments under it are `INVALID_REQUEST`), with the answer its first request got, whatever became of the note since, and writes and audits nothing; a row without its answer (a run resumed after its end cleared it) ran once and runs no more (`This request was answered before, and Tinfirm no longer keeps its answer: nothing was saved or deleted again. …`). After the checks of §15.18 (the arguments, memory in effect; a refusal there keeps nothing) the row is claimed in the transaction that writes the memory, before its lock: the run read again (`RUN_NOT_ACTIVE`, as a claim), the row inserted `RUNNING` with no system (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), the write, then the row `DONE` with the answer as it went out, a refusal (`MEMORY_FULL`, `MEMORY_REFUSED`, …) included, all committed together; a request with the same id at the same time waits on the row's key and then answers what the first stored, as a submission key's (§10). So no row of a memory tool is ever seen `RUNNING`, and the sweep, the timeout and the retake never reach one. It is kept by the journal's rules, as long as a request may be sent again: the answer until the run ends (the run module refuses a request of a run that is not `RUNNING` with its epoch), the row `TINFIRM_TOOL_JOURNAL_RETENTION` after, and none past its conversation's purge or erasure. A row holds ids, the arguments' SHA-256 and the answer (`Saved as m3.`), never the note's words. `recall` is a read and is not journaled. The owner's and the audience's writes through the API (a note's delete, `clear`, a proposal's accept) take no key: a note's number is never reused and a proposal is accepted once, so a delete or an accept sent again finds nothing and changes nothing (`404`); `clear` sent again empties the memory again, as a `DELETE` of the collection does.
- **Trigger conversations** (package 4a). A trigger's run's calls are journaled, waited for, retaken, interrupted and swept as a person's: nothing of the journal or its recovery reads the conversation's person or payer (a trigger's conversation has none of the first, and its runs may be paid by a service account), its `FIXED` bindings are used as any binding, and `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`'s `SYSTEM` message names the agent.
- **Approved and parked calls** (the contract's §3.3; `CallRecovery`, `ApprovedCalls.recover`, `ParkedCalls.recover`). `V015`'s recovery keeps its rules and its cutoffs (the tool timeout and two minutes), and asks the tool's replay class as the conversation lists it now (an unserved tool counts as `UNSAFE`): a stale approved call (`decision.call_started_at` set long ago, no result) or a parked call left `RUNNING` whose tool is `SAFE` runs again, as one never taken; an `UNSAFE` one is settled as before, its answer to the agent the words of `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `RESTART`). No migration changes `V015`'s lookup.
- **Lock order** (the contract's §3.4; §10). The `tool_call` row is the last of a conversation's rows, after its runs, bindings and decisions (and package 4's outbox), before the audit chain head; every transaction that writes it and audits locks the conversation first. The claim's transaction and the sweep's lookup lock `tool_call` rows only. In a call's record a credential refused takes the account's row before the journal row, and `tool.called` is recorded last (`account.refused` before it). Package R closed §10's gap where `record` audited before `Accounts.refused` locked the account; since the audit log appends a transaction's events at its commit (`JdbcAuditLog`), the audit chain head comes last whatever the order, and the order of the records stays as it was. Nothing of package R reads its own audit rows inside the transaction that recorded them.
- **Idempotent submissions** (the contract's §3.5; `SubmissionKeys`, `submission_key`). The optional header `Idempotency-Key` (1 to 200 visible ASCII characters; a UUID is recommended; else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`) on five operations, and the optional `requestId` argument of the MCP tools that do the same (`start_conversation`, `send_message`, `retry_turn`, `decide`), whose descriptions tell the client to send a new one for each intent and the same one when it retries. Both reach `ConversationCommands`, which takes the key and answers `ConversationCommands.Submitted` (the DTO and `replayed`): the REST controllers set `Idempotent-Replayed: true` on a replay, and the MCP tools say so in the first line of the text.

  | Operation | MCP tool | What it makes (the key's resource) | Status | The replay answers |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `startConversation` | `start_conversation` | `conversation` | 201 | The conversation as it is now |
  | `postConversationMessage` | `send_message` | `message` | 201 | The conversation as it is now |
  | `uploadAttachment` | none | `attachment` | 201 | The file as it is now (`404` once deleted) |
  | `retryConversation` | `retry_turn` | `conversation` | 200 | The conversation as it is now |
  | `decideDecision` | `decide` | `decision` | 200 | The decision as it is now |

  The service claims the key in the transaction that makes the resource, after the caller's access check and visibility (so `404` and `409 CONVERSATION_BOUND` stay what they were) and before anything is counted, locked or made: a first use inserts it (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and writes the resource with it before the commit, so a concurrent request with the same key waits on the primary key and then finds the first one's resource; the same key for the same operation and request (the SHA-256 of `POST`, the path and the canonical body; an upload's of the file's SHA-256, its name and its note) is a replay, which makes, audits, charges and counts nothing new (no turn, no message, no start against the hourly limit of clients), and answers before the state's checks (a decided decision is no `DECISION_DECIDED`, a retried turn no `TURN_ACTIVE`); another operation or request is `422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; a request that answered an error rolled its transaction back, the key with it, so the client may retry with the same key. A key is the caller's own, by their principal in the company: the same key from another member is another key. Keys are kept `TINFIRM_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL` (a day): an older one counts as gone at the claim, and `worker` replicas delete them every sweep interval (`submission_key_expired`).

  The paths of packages 4a, 5a and 6 behind these operations take the key too. In a trigger's conversation, which has no person, the key is the caller's principal (its author or a member of its team, who message, upload, retry and decide its team's approvals in the app), never the conversation's. A start refused while the company's runs are stopped (`409 COMPANY_STOPPED`, checked after the key and before the clients' hourly limit) stores no key, as every refusal, and a replay of a start made before the stop answers that conversation; a suspended approver's decision is `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED` before any key. A chat's inbound message (package 5a) is deduplicated by its platform's event id and takes no key; a trigger's "run now" is not behind `ConversationCommands` and takes none. The request's hash is of the method, the path and the body as the service has it (the generated request type serialized with nulls, in canonical form; none of the five has a `JsonNullable` field), not of the surface: the same key through the app (`Idempotency-Key`) and then through a client (`requestId`), for the same request of the same caller, is one submission and replays; another request under it is `422`.
- **What the code differs in from the contract, and why.**
  - `tool_call` has two columns beyond the contract's: `name` (the tool as the run lists it, for `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`'s text) and `run_ended_at` (the conversation module never reads `run`, and the retention counts from the run's end).
  - A request that waits for a call running elsewhere never answers nothing (the contract's §3.2 said the host sends it again): the host sends a request again only after a reconnect, and gives up at 65 seconds from its first send. It answers `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (a `SAFE` call `TOOL_FAILED`) at 55 seconds from the call's claim (`answer-within`) or at the tool timeout from the request, whichever is first, and leaves the row as it is.
  - A call runs until 5 seconds before the tool timeout, not to it, so that its record fits within the run module's bound; the router's answer past its bound, or for a port that failed, is `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`, not `TOOL_FAILED`; a record that failed settles the call with a third cause, `ERROR`, beyond the contract's `TIMEOUT` and `RESTART`; and the runner host words its own timeout of a TOOL request as `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` and waits once more after a resend.
  - A record that failed after a write records no `system_write` row (its transaction, which publishes `SystemWritten`, rolled back), so package 4b's poll may take that one write for someone else's. A known gap; the call is `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`, and the agent checks the system first.
  - A record that finds its row settled answers the row's answer; a claim reads the run again; a row whose run ended stores no answer, and the sweep deletes the rows a run's end never reached by their claim. The sweep deletes 1000 rows a transaction.
  - The timeout is the conversation module's (it knows the class and the attempt); the run module keeps a safety bound. "Its thread is interrupted as today" holds.
  - "The conversation's view shows the call as interrupted" is a `SYSTEM` message of the run; the API has no field of its own for it.
  - A caller without a principal in the company (an instance admin who is not a member, who may only retry, and whom `TURN_ACTIVE` guards against a second turn) keeps no key: the request runs as one without.
  - An approved or parked call a restart caught running is settled as before and not audited as `tool.interrupted` (the contract says "settled as today").
  - The web app's keys: the web app sends one `Idempotency-Key` per submission, kept when the same request is sent again after a failure on the way (no answer, `408` or a `5xx`) and new for any other (`web/src/app/core/submission-key.ts`), on each of the five operations: a start (`new-conversation`), a message and a retry (`conversation-store`, the inbox's row), an upload (`conversation-files`) and a decision (`decision-card`, the inbox).
- **Configuration** (§4): `TINFIRM_TOOL_JOURNAL_RETENTION` (`P7D`), `TINFIRM_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL` (`PT24H`), `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.sweep-interval` (`PT1M`; the test profile `PT1H`, the tests sweep on demand as of a time they choose), `tinfirm.conversation.tool-journal.answer-within` (`PT55S`; the test profile `PT3S`, within its tool timeout of 4 seconds).
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `ToolCallJournalIntegrationTest` | A request sent again after the call ended returns the stored result with no second call, on this replica (nothing in memory) and on another (a second `ToolCalls` over the same database); a request sent again while the call runs waits for it; other arguments under the same id are `INVALID_REQUEST`; the canonical arguments; the result cleared when the run ends, the row kept six days and deleted after eight; a system paused between the check and the call: never called, `TOOL_DENIED`, the row `DONE` with it, the binding `BOUND`; a record that finds its row settled answers the row's `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` and still audits `tool.called`; a run that ends during its call leaves the row without an answer, and a claim after the end is `RUN_NOT_ACTIVE` with no row; the sweep deletes a row its run's end never reached after the retention from its claim, and 2001 rows of an ended run in batches; a trigger's conversation paid by a service account, without a person, journaled and interrupted as a person's |
  | `ToolInterruptedIntegrationTest` | An `UNSAFE` call a stopped replica left `RUNNING` is `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `RESTART`), audited once with its details, noted in the conversation, never called again; a `SAFE` one is called again (attempt 2); a call within the timeout and the margin is waited for until `answer-within` and answered `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (a `SAFE` one `TOOL_FAILED`), the row left `RUNNING`, unaudited, and a request sent again after its record gets the recorded answer; a call is cut off before the tool timeout so that its record fits; a record that fails settles an `UNSAFE` call `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `ERROR`, audited) and a `SAFE` one `TOOL_FAILED`; a `SAFE` call lost while its system is paused is claimed again, refused `TOOL_DENIED` and `DONE`, never called; past the timeout an `UNSAFE` call is `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `TIMEOUT`) and a `SAFE` one `TOOL_FAILED`, neither run again, both late ends still `tool.called`; the sweep settles the rows whose request never comes back, audits only the `UNSAFE` one, and a `SAFE` one runs once its request returns. Time moves on a clock the test controls (`ToolJournalBench.MovableClock`) and a replica's short tool timeout, never by sleeping |
  | `ToolReplayTest` | The defaults by effect; every fixed tool on its default, no write `SAFE`; a catalogue's `SAFE` write keeps its class; the class in `run.assign.tools`, valid against the v2 schema, and absent without one |
  | `CallRecoveryIntegrationTest` (extended) | A stale approved `SAFE` call runs again; an `UNSAFE` one is settled as before with the words of `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`; a parked `SAFE` call left `RUNNING` runs again, an `UNSAFE` one is `REFUSED` |
  | `ConversationLockOrderTest` (extended) | `record` takes the account before the journal row and audits last |
  | `IdempotentSubmissionIntegrationTest` | For each operation a replay returns the first answer with `Idempotent-Replayed: true` and makes, audits and charges nothing new (one conversation, one run, one message, one file, one decision); two requests with one key at once make one resource; another body or operation is `422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; a malformed key `400`; a failed first request stores no key; keys are per principal; the sweep deletes keys after 24 hours; a start refused `COMPANY_STOPPED` stores no key, a start made before the stop replays, and the refused key starts once the stop is lifted; in a trigger's conversation, which has no person, the key of its author's decision is the author's |
  | `McpConversationToolsIntegrationTest` (extended) | `requestId` on `start_conversation`, `send_message`, `retry_turn` and `decide`; one key through the app and then the client replays, another body under it is `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED` |
  | `RunRequestIntegrationTest` (changed) | The run module hands every tool request to the port, answers `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` for a port's null answer, failure or overrun of the timeout and its grace, never nothing nor `TOOL_FAILED` |
  | The Rust crate's `host.rs` and `mcp.rs` tests | A TOOL request sent again after a reconnect waits once more from that send, not twice; the host's own timeout of a server-executed tool is `TOOL_INTERRUPTED` (cause `TIMEOUT`) |
  | `RunnerProtocolSchemaTest` (extended), the Rust crate's `schema.rs` | `run.assign.tools[].replay` and `TOOL_INTERRUPTED`'s result against the v2 schema; the sample `run.assign.json` carries the class |
  | `AgentMemoryIntegrationTest` (extended, §15.18) | A `remember` sent again after a later request replaced its note, after the owner deleted it, after a `forget` and after a compaction dropped it answers its first answer and saves nothing; a `forget` sent again answers `Forgot m{n}.`; each write audited once; another call under the id `INVALID_REQUEST`; two at once save once; a row without its answer is not run again; a refusal of the checks keeps nothing; a trigger's proposal sent again after it was accepted proposes nothing; the rows hold no note's words |
  | `TenantIsolationTest` | Company B's journal rows and keys are invisible to company A's tenant context |

### 15.15 Package 4, part a: the trigger core

The design is the [package 4 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-4-contract.md), with its §11 (decided on review): a trigger is a company's standing rule on a tracker's issues and change requests (`TRACKER`, ADR-0043), or a schedule (`SCHEDULE`, ADR-0032), that starts conversations in which an agent does the author's instruction with the accounts of the trigger's table and the model access of its payer. Part a is the core: the tables, the module and its seams, the API, the gate, a trigger's conversations and the kill switches. Polling and firing (part b) and the outbox and write-back (part c) build on its seams. The actions are in §8, the lock order in §10, the configuration in §4.

- **Tables** (V011, V012, V013, V024): `trigger` (the rule, author, team, payer, results, firing limit, status and health, `armed_at`; from V024 `admin_changed_at`, `_by`, `_what` for the author's inbox item after an admin's edit), `trigger_account` (one account per system, a row's account `NULL` only while its trigger is paused: a deferred check; from `V049` a row names whom it acts through, `holder_principal_id`, and its author's own account or the credential that service account borrows on the system, `trigger_account_ck`), `trigger_setting` (the company's firing limits), `agent_settings.trigger_id` (a trigger's settings, its other owner); `conversation` gains `trigger_id`, `author_principal_id`, `author_kind` and `chain_depth` (and `conversation_place_ref_trigger_ck` on `place_ref`), and its `person_principal_id` is `NULL` for a trigger's (`conversation_payer_ck`); `conversation_binding.how` gains `FIXED`; `message.kind` gains `EXTERNAL` with `external_id` and `external_author`; `run.trigger_id`; `decision.approver_group_id`, `decided_by_name`, `decided_by_message_id`, subject `RESULT`; `conversation_rebind` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, the one way a waiting conversation takes a reassigned trigger's table and payer). V012 (`trigger_poll`, `trigger_subject`, `trigger_firing`, `system_write`) and V013 (`outbox`) are part a's tables for parts b and c. A conversation's agent, person, trigger, key, runtime, place, subject id, origin and chain depth are fixed (`conversation_fixed_ck`): a test fixture inserts a conversation with its origin, never changes it.
- **Who does what** (§5.1 as §11 decided, T15; `TriggerCallers`): its author sees, edits, accepts, runs, pauses, resumes and deletes it; a member of its team sees, runs and pauses it (`403 TRIGGER_NOT_EDITABLE` for an edit); OWNER and ADMIN, and an instance admin acting in the company, do all of it, an edit on the author's behalf (checked against the author's entitlements, never the admin's, `editorPrincipalId` in the audit, a `CHANGED_BY_ADMIN` item in the author's inbox), and alone reassign it. Anyone else gets `404` on every path, the trigger's firings and conversations included. No permission of its own: every path needs `VIEW_COMPANY` first.
- **A trigger's conversation** (§3.4 to §3.8, T12, T14). It has no person: its turns act as the author it started with (`ConversationInfo.actingPrincipalId`) with the trigger's settings, paid by its payer. Its bindings are copies of the trigger's table made at its start (`FIXED`), and it binds nothing else but an MCP server that needs no account, `OPEN` at its first use (ADR-0071): a tool on another system without a row is `TOOL_DENIED`, a repository of such a system is neither in its workspace nor requestable. Its audience (§5.13) is the trigger's: its author, its team, OWNER and ADMIN see it, and the author it started with keeps seeing it after a reassignment; its people, who speak, answer, cancel, retry and end it, are its trigger's author and team (`TriggerAudience.acts`), OWNER and ADMIN only as one of them. An approval goes to its team, or to its author without one; its questions name nobody and its people answer them, on a tracker issue also from a comment there (`decided_by_name`). On a tracker issue the place is the issue: a plain message in Tinfirm is `409 PLACE_IS_EXTERNAL`, an answer to an open question is not. A firing during a run waits as a message does, and the run's end starts its `EVENT` turn; a result's review (subject `RESULT`) is decided by the team, a return needs a note and starts a `REVIEW_RETURNED` turn. While its trigger is paused, its queued runs wait `TRIGGER_PAUSED` (`TurnSource.TurnPlan.TriggerPaused`) and no new run is made. OWNER's reads of it are never `conversation.read` (§8). Through a client (§15.10) its people see it and answer its questions; its approvals are `DECIDE_IN_APP`. A legal hold on its author covers it, and a person's erasure reaches what they said in it, never the conversation whole (§15.11).
- **The gate** (§5.3 with §11, point 9; `TriggerGate.check`, the trigger's row locked): before a firing, and again after the commit of a change it depends on (a reconnect, a suspension or a resume, who may use an identity or an agent, a group, a model key or settings, and from ADR-0072 a credential disabled, enabled, lent or withdrawn, `CredentialChanged`), as SYSTEM on a virtual thread. Each row goes through `Accounts.check` with the author acting (`TriggerGateImpl.row`). A broken account, a credential to sign in again or disabled (`RECONNECT_COMPANY`), or a suspended holder pauses it `ACCOUNT_BROKEN`; a payer not active or no gateway makes it wait (`trigger.waiting`, once per wait); a lost source system deletes it; another lost system pauses it `SYSTEM_REMOVED` and removes that row; an account of another identity now, a holder who left or a row without its account pauses it `ACCOUNT_REMOVED`. It pauses `NOT_ENTITLED` when the author is suspended, may no longer use the agent, a row's account (`USE_RIGHT_GONE`: the service account out of their reach, or the credential withdrawn from it; `GRANT_GONE`, `NOT_IN_GROUP`, `PERSONAL_SYSTEMS_OFF`) or the payer's budget, when the trigger's settings cannot be read or the subscription that paid is gone, when a budget's settings name no model or one its key no longer allows (`Budget.refusal`: every firing would fail the same way), and on any other refusal of `ModelAccess.forRun` but a suspended payer and no gateway, which wait (`TriggerGateImpl.entitled`).
- **Kill switches**, in the changing transaction (a row's account may be `NULL` only while its trigger is paused): an author who leaves pauses their triggers `AUTHOR_LEFT` and cancels their turns, the conversations staying open for a reassignment; a disconnected account pauses `ACCOUNT_REMOVED`; a removed system deletes the triggers on it; a deleted agent deletes its triggers before the conversation module ends their conversations (`@Order(0)`, `AGENT_DELETED`); a deleted team moves the open approvals to the author (`decision.approver_changed`); a service account a live trigger's table or payer names is not deleted (`409 SERVICE_IDENTITY_IN_USE`); package 6's switches (§15.13, `TriggerRunSwitches`): the company's stop makes active triggers wait `COMPANY_STOPPED` until its lift arms them, a system's pause pauses its triggers `SYSTEM_PAUSED` until its resume resumes them through the gate. Resuming a reassigned trigger rebinds its waiting conversations (`conversation_rebind`, `conversation.rebound`, a SYSTEM message in each).
- **Lendings** (ADR-0072, open point 3 as decided again; `V050`; `TriggerLendings`). A row through a service account acts with the credential the service account borrows on the system (§15.23), and the trigger follows the service account: when an admin lends it another credential there in place of the one it borrowed (`AccountEvents.LendingReplaced`, in the lending's transaction), each live trigger whose row acted through it with the replaced credential names the new one with its identity in that transaction (`TriggerStore.followLending`), audited as `trigger.accounts_changed` with `why` `lending.replaced`; its poll follows after the commit (`PollKeeper`); its open conversations bind the new credential at their next check (§15.7); and its author is told in their inbox as of an admin's change on their behalf (`admin_changed_*`, the `TRIGGER` item `CHANGED_BY_ADMIN`: "svc-dev now borrows the credential Second on Jira in place of First, and the trigger acts with it"), unless they lent it themselves. A credential stored or signed in again keeps its id, so rotating a token changes no trigger. The trigger keeps its row, and its gate pauses it as for a withdrawn credential, when the new one is a chat system's bot (C9) or its tracker's source row would name a credential without an identity (T2). A credential withdrawn from the service account, with nothing lent in its place, pauses the trigger `NOT_ENTITLED` until its author names another; one disabled pauses it `ACCOUNT_BROKEN` until an admin enables it and the author resumes it (the gate, above). `V050` made the rows of live triggers whose service account borrowed another credential by then name that one.
- **Caps** (§3.1): a source system holds at most 100 live triggers (`TriggerService.MAX_PER_SYSTEM`), whatever their source: a tracker's, and from package 5b a chat system's channel triggers and a webhook system's (§15.17); a company at most 50 live schedules (`MAX_SCHEDULES`). A create beyond either is `409 TOO_MANY_TRIGGERS`; a deleted trigger frees its place, and a change never moves a trigger to another system. The count and the insert are serialised per cap: the create takes the cap's advisory lock before it counts (§10), so that parallel creates one short of a cap create one and refuse the rest (`TriggerCapRaceTest`).
- **Names**: a trigger's name is unique among the company's live triggers ignoring case (`409 DUPLICATE_TRIGGER_NAME`). A create and a change read the names without locks (`TriggerStore.nameTaken`), so two saves of one name at once both pass that check; V011's `trigger_name_uq` refuses the second, which is answered `409 DUPLICATE_TRIGGER_NAME` too, never `500` (`TriggerNameRaceTest`), as a channel's indexes are (§15.17).
- **Firing limits** (§11, open point 15): each trigger's `maxFiringsPerHour`, at most the company's maximum (`400 TRIGGER_LIMIT_TOO_HIGH`); `GET`, `PUT /settings` (`triggerFiringsPerHour`, admins change it); a lower maximum lowers the triggers above it (`trigger.changed`, `cause` `company_settings.changed`). The settings carry a `version` for optimistic locking (`trigger_setting.version`, V060; 0 without a row): a change names the version it read (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED` without), and one made meanwhile is `409 SETTINGS_CHANGED` with nothing written; `PUT /chat-settings` does the same with `chat_setting.version`. The same endpoint's `triggerSubscriptions` (ADR-0085, decision 7; `trigger_setting.subscriptions`, off by default) lets a trigger pay with its author's subscription: a trigger's settings take one only while it is on and the payer is the author (`SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_SERVING` otherwise), and the gate pauses `NOT_ENTITLED` a trigger that still names one once it is off.
- **The editor's reads**, for the web app's trigger editor. `POST /triggers/preview` answers every refusal as the save would, but for the open events' acknowledgement: a draft that needs it and gives it nowhere (nor did the trigger) is answered with `openToAnyone.acknowledgementMissing` and the save's sentence in `warnings` (`TriggerEditor.check(…, preview)`, `Checked.unacknowledged`), and the save still refuses it (`400 OPEN_EVENTS_NOT_ACKNOWLEDGED`). `GET /triggers/choices?forPrincipalId=` answers that member's one choice as a save checks it for the author: OWNER and ADMIN for the author of a trigger they edit on the author's behalf or a reassignment's new author, anyone else only themselves (`403`); `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for a principal that is not a member or left, `403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE` when the member may not use the agent. Each choice's `budget.models` is its key's models (`ModelAccess.models`, as `getMyModelAccess` answers them; empty: every model), for the model field; `TriggerPayer.models` is null elsewhere. `GET /triggers?accountHolderPrincipalId=` keeps the triggers with a row through that principal (`holder_principal_id`: a member's own account, or the credential a service account borrows; a service account's page), among those the caller sees. An MCP server that needs no account (ADR-0071) takes no row: each choice answers it `NOT_NEEDED` and fills none there, and the trigger and its preview list it in `noAccountSystems`, never in `unavailableSystems`; a row on it saved before is checked and used as any other.
- **Spend** (§15.12): a trigger's runs' spend carries its `trigger_id` and is not personal; its author and its team's members see it under its name, a deleted one's too, through `TriggerDirectory` (`ofPrincipal`, `names`); a service account's budget alerts also tell the authors of the live triggers it pays, with how many at 100 % (`livePaidBy`).
- **An outside identity's erasure** (§15.11; `TriggerIdentityContent`, the conversation module's, for trigger's conversations outside a chat only; a channel trigger's, held in a chat's thread, is `ChatThreadIdentityContent`'s): the identity is its `id` at the trigger's source system (`external_author ->> 'id'` of an `EXTERNAL` comment, the conversation's subject's system). Its comments' bodies go as a purge does, and their author's id, login and name with them (`external_author` becomes `{}`); a question a comment answered loses its texts as a purge does and `decided_by_name`; in open conversations too. The runs of those conversations from the first such comment on are the runs whose events the erasure purges (`messages`, `decisions` in the answer's `other`).
- **The module and its seams** (§10): `Triggers`, `TriggerGate` and `TriggerEvents` for other modules; `FiringConversations`, `TriggerLocks`, `ManualFirings`, `SubjectSearch` inside the module for parts b and c (part b implements both; run now of either source is part b's). The conversation module's `TriggerConversations` starts, continues, posts to, cancels, ends and rebinds a trigger's conversations and raises a result's review; it reaches the trigger module through its ports `TriggerAudience` and `TriggerTurns`, looked up when used. The trigger module's inbox items (`TRIGGER`, with `InboxTrigger`'s reasons) come through the generic port `InboxItems` (`TriggerInboxImpl`) and count in `InboxCounts.notices`. `ConversationEvents.TurnRecorded`, `DecisionDecided`, `ResultReviewed` and `ConversationRebound` are for parts b and c.
- **Not built in part a.** `IdentityContent` for a firing's `actor` and `request` (part b's rows, §15.11); the agent's delete dialog listing its triggers (open point 11); the reveal purpose `trigger-run:{triggerId}` of a run's own tokens (the runs reveal with `run:{runId}`; part b uses `trigger-run:{triggerId}` for a run now's read of its subject, §8).
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `TriggerConstraintTest` | As the runtime role: a trigger acts and pays only as its author or a service account; a tracker's source identity, and a row's account `NULL` only while paused (deferred); a trigger's settings; a conversation a person's or a trigger's, keeping its author and payer, binding only fixed accounts; a run paid as its conversation is; `ONCE` per subject; a decision's approver per conversation; the outbox's once; `conversation_rebind` the one way to a reassigned trigger's table and payer; the firing limits and another company's triggers |
  | `TriggerGateTest` | The gate as a table: an author who left or is suspended, an agent no longer shared or deleted, each row checked for the author, a holder suspended or gone, a removed system (the source's trigger deleted, another's paused), a row without its account, a payer the author may no longer use, the model access waiting or pausing |
  | `TriggerEditorTest` | The editor's checks of the table and the payer as a unit: a fixed service account without the acknowledgement, the author's own account under open events needing it (`OPEN_EVENTS_NOT_ACKNOWLEDGED`), an account the author is not entitled to (`ACCOUNT_NOT_ENTITLED`), one that needs signing in, the source's (`SOURCE_ACCOUNT_REQUIRED`), a payer the author may not use (`MODEL_ACCESS_NOT_ENTITLED`), the settings' checks, the choices offered with each payer's models; a preview's missing acknowledgement answered in words, not refused |
  | `TriggerIntegrationTest` | The API end to end: a schedule seen, run, paused and deleted by its audience only; the editor's checks for the author; the firing limits as the admins'; an author who leaves, the reassignment and the rebinding of a waiting conversation (V9); a service account a live trigger names not deleted; a tracker's accounts its author is entitled to; a service account's row naming the credential it borrows, lent `READ` and going on, then following the credential lent in its place (`trigger.accounts_changed` with `why` `lending.replaced`, the author's `CHANGED_BY_ADMIN`, its open conversation bound to the new one), and paused `NOT_ENTITLED` once it is withdrawn; the preview's missing acknowledgement and the save's refusal of it, an admin's choices for the author (`forPrincipalId`), the list by an account's holder; another company's trigger `404` |
  | `LendingFollowMigrationTest` (ADR-0072) | V050 over V049's data: a live trigger's row whose service account borrows another credential now names that one with its identity; a row whose service account still borrows its credential, and a deleted trigger's, stay as they were |
  | `TriggerCapRaceTest` | Creates one short of a webhook system's 100 triggers and of the company's 50 schedules, released together while the audit chain head is held so that one past its count waits at its commit: exactly one created, the others `409 TOO_MANY_TRIGGERS`; a channel trigger refused at its chat system's 100, and created once one is deleted |
  | `TriggerNameRaceTest` | A create and a rename that take a name another save took and has not committed: `trigger_name_uq` refuses each, answered `409 DUPLICATE_TRIGGER_NAME`, not `500` |
  | `TriggerConversationEndToEndTest` | A firing's conversation through a fake runner host: its prompt, a comment during the run and its `EVENT` turn, who may write in it (`mayPost`, `mayAnswer`), a question answered from the issue, a result (its subject's `writes`, the team's `REVIEW` wait)'s review returned with a note and its `REVIEW_RETURNED` turn; OWNER's reads of it recording no `conversation.read`; the commenter's erasure by identity: their comment, its author and the answer it gave gone in the open conversation |
  | `CronTest`, `ScheduleTest` | Cron fields, zones across the changes of summer time, a cron that never fires; IANA zones only, the 15 minutes' floor, the next firings |
  | `McpConversationToolsIntegrationTest` | Through a client: a trigger's conversation listed, read and its question answered by its author, its approval `DECIDE_IN_APP`; OWNER not one of its people does not see it there |
  | `OwnerReadsTest` | A conversation without a person is never recorded |
  | `SpendTriggerAudienceTest`, `BudgetAlertTest` (added) | Through the trigger module: a trigger's author and team see its spend under its name, a member outside it nothing, a deleted trigger keeping its name; the author of a trigger a service account pays told of its budget, with the trigger counted at 100 % |
  | `ModularityTest` | The `trigger` module |

  `TenantIsolationTest` covers every new path with a trigger of company B.

#### Polling, events, firings and schedules (part b)

The [package 4 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-4-contract.md)'s §5.4 to §5.6, §5.10 and §11 (points 1, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10, 12, 13, 15). The tables are in §7, the actions and purposes in §8, the configuration in §4, the lock order and the work outside transactions in §10.

- **Polls** (`TriggerPolls`, one per system and source account, shared by the triggers whose source row names it; polling only, no webhooks). A credential's poll has no holder (`V049`: `trigger_poll.holder_principal_id` null), so it serves the triggers of every service account that borrows the credential and stays when one of them goes, and reveals the credential with no acting principal; a member's own account's poll keeps its member. Every `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_POLL_TICK` a `worker` replica takes the due polls (`trigger_poll_due`), one after the other. A pass: claims the poll (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a lease); finds its triggers (live, `ACTIVE`, the gate's verdict applied in a transaction of each; the poll's own waits `SYSTEM_UNREACHABLE` and `SCOPE_UNREADABLE` are kept for the pass to repair), none making it `IDLE` with its window moved to now; reveals the account once (`trigger-poll:{pollId}`), an account that cannot be used making it `WAITING`; lists the subjects changed since the window over the union of its triggers' scopes (`SystemPolls`: Jira's one JQL `project in (…) AND updated >= … ORDER BY updated ASC, key ASC` in the account's zone, Cloud by its cursor; GitHub one listing per repository, conditional on its ETag; GitLab a project's, else a group's, issues and merge requests; no scope: everything the account sees, `assignee = currentUser()` and the like when every trigger is unscoped and fires only on `ASSIGNED`), 50 a page, at most `max-per-pass`; skips a subject changed within the settle time (the window stops there) and one whose `updated` its snapshot has; completes the others (the newest 50 comments, GitHub's `since` the snapshot's last, a change request's head commit) and handles each in a transaction of its own (`SubjectHandling`); moves the window (`PollWindow`: the newest change handled minus the overlap, never back, never past the cutoff; an offset of keys when a cut pass would not move the bound by the listing's precision, a minute in Jira) and sets the next pass after the system's interval. Its sight is its account's (T8): every read uses its token, and assigned and mentioned mean its identity (`external_id`, `external_login`).
- **What a system answers.** A `401` runs `Accounts.refused` (the poll `WAITING`; the gate, after the commit of `AccountNeedsReconnect`, pauses every trigger on the account `ACCOUNT_BROKEN`; `AccountReconnected` wakes the poll, a person resumes the triggers). A scope that answers `403` or `404` (a Jira search over several projects is read again per project) makes its triggers wait `SCOPE_UNREADABLE`; the other scopes are read and a later pass that reads it repairs the wait. A rate limit (`429`, GitHub's `403` with its headers) backs off until `Retry-After` or the reset, at most an hour; `5xx`, a timeout or no connection back off 1, 2, 4… minutes up to 30, and after `unreachable-after` the triggers wait `SYSTEM_UNREACHABLE`. The window holds in each. Less than a tenth of the rate limit left doubles the interval until the reset; GitHub's `304` reads nothing.
- **A subject's transaction** (`SubjectHandling`): its snapshot inserted (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) or locked, so that a second handling of one change finds no edge (T10); a read older than the snapshot left alone. Tinfirm's own writes are found in `system_write`, never by identity (row 10): a comment id recorded there is not new; a status, a label, a close or a pushed head commit recorded since the snapshot's read (minus the overlap) is no edge. `EventDetector` then finds the edges: without a snapshot `CREATED` (created at or after the bound), `CHANGE_REQUEST_OPENED`, `LABEL_ADDED` per label, `ASSIGNED` when created assigned to the account; with one `UPDATED` (title, description, type), `STATUS_ENTERED`, `LABEL_ADDED`, `LABEL_REMOVED`, `ASSIGNED` (the account newly among the assignees), `CHANGE_REQUEST_UPDATED`, and `CLOSED` alone for a subject that closed; new comments of people `MENTIONED` (the account in the system's syntax) or `COMMENTED`. `TriggerEvaluator` takes the triggers in the order of their names: scope and subject kind, the first of its events that happened at or after its `armed_at` (T16) with its value, its condition groups (`authors` `TINFIRM` for a subject Tinfirm created), `ONCE`; a change older than the catch-up counts in `missed_count`. Then the triggers are locked by id, the conversations open on the subject by id, the firings fired, every new comment stored in each trigger conversation open on the subject as `EXTERNAL` (oldest first; while questions are open, the first answers them all, §11 point 4), and a closed subject's conversations that the close did not fire ended `SUBJECT_CLOSED` (those it fired end after their turn, on `TurnRecorded`).
- **A firing** (`Firings`): the gate (a pause or a stopping wait fires nothing and records nothing); the agent paused (nothing); the rate (`max_firings_per_hour` within the last hour: `SKIPPED_RATE`, counted in `rate_skipped_count` and recorded once an hour, the inbox's one item an hour); the chain (the first read of a subject Tinfirm created at depth *d* fires at *d* + 1; past 3 `SKIPPED_CHAIN`, and a `SYSTEM` message in the conversation that created it); the trigger's open conversation on the subject takes it (`CONTINUED`: the row with the conversation and no run, then `FiringConversations.continueWith`), else a new one starts (`STARTED`, with the subject's `# Subject` and the firing's `# Request`). A comment that fires is also stored as `EXTERNAL`. `ONCE` inserts with `once` (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). Each recorded firing is `trigger.fired`.
- **Run now** (`ManualFiringsImpl`, both sources): a tracker's subject read with the source account (`trigger-run:{triggerId}`; unreadable `409 SUBJECT_NOT_VISIBLE`), its scope, kind and conditions checked on the read (`409 TRIGGER_CONDITIONS_FAIL`, naming the clauses), fired as its first event, `manual_by` set, never `once`, depth 0, the gate and the rate applied (`409 TRIGGER_PAUSED` with the reason when nothing fired); a schedule fired once now with its overlap and without its catch-up, its next firing kept. `trigger.run` in the firing's transaction. The editor's search (`SubjectSearch`) reads at most 20 subjects in the trigger's scope with the same account.
