- **Schedules** (`ScheduleFirings`): every `TINFIRM_TRIGGER_SCHEDULE_TICK`, the due schedules (`trigger_schedule_due`), each under its row lock (`SKIP LOCKED`, due checked again), as SYSTEM: the gate (nothing recorded, the schedule moves on); the agent paused (`SKIPPED_AGENT_PAUSED`); overlap `SKIP` while a conversation of it is busy (`SKIPPED_OPEN`); catch-up `NONE` and due longer ago than `missed-after` (`SKIPPED_MISSED`, once for any number); the rate; else `STARTED` in a `SCHEDULE` conversation of its own, the idle previous ones ending `SUPERSEDED`. The trigger records the due time, the outcome and its next firing after now; a firing that cannot start rolls back and is recorded `FAILED` in a new transaction. A change of the trigger keeps its next firing unless the cron or the zone changes, so that a firing due but not yet claimed by the tick is not lost to a rename or another overlap; a new cron or zone computes it from now, and a paused schedule has none until its resume computes it.
- **Tinfirm's writes** (`SystemWriteRecorder`): every `ConversationEvents.SystemWritten` (§10) becomes a `system_write` row in its transaction, with the creating conversation's depth for a change request; the recorder is its one writer. `ToolOutcome.Written` carries the status a transition entered and the labels added and removed.
- **Polls' life** (`PollKeeper`): a tracker trigger created, given another table or resumed makes or wakes its source account's poll after the commit; `AccountReconnected`, `CredentialChanged` (a credential enabled again or lent anew, ADR-0072), `PrincipalResumed` and `SystemResumed` wake the polls of that account, holder or system; a poll without an active trigger turns `IDLE` at its next pass, and goes with its account.
- **Package 6's switches** (§15.13; the package 6 contract's §5.4, which decides both).
  - *The company's stop.* Polls go on ("`TriggerGate` fires nothing and records nothing, while polls go on"): a pass serves the triggers that wait `COMPANY_STOPPED`, reads, moves its window and keeps its snapshots, stores new comments in the trigger conversations open on their subjects (their turns wait) and ends a closed subject's conversations, and the gate, `Firings`' first step, fires and records nothing. The poll's own waits (`SYSTEM_UNREACHABLE`, `SCOPE_UNREADABLE`) do not replace `COMPANY_STOPPED` while it lasts. Nothing of the stop fires after the lift: the lift arms the triggers at the lift, and what the polls read during the stop is in their snapshots, so there is nothing to catch up. A schedule due during the stop records nothing and moves to its next firing; the lift brings none back, whatever its catch-up. Run now is `409 TRIGGER_PAUSED` (`reason` `COMPANY_STOPPED`) from the gate, which a tracker's run now asks before it reads the subject.
  - *A system's pause.* Its polls call nothing: a pass finds the system paused before it looks at its triggers and waits (`WAITING`, `last_error` "The system is paused by an admin"), with no failure, no backoff and its window held; `SystemPolls.open` refuses a paused system too (`OTHER`). Its triggers are paused `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (part a's wiring), so run now is `409 TRIGGER_PAUSED` (`reason` `SYSTEM_PAUSED`) before any read, and the editor's search answers nothing without a call. The resume moves the windows of the system's polls to the resume, less the overlap (`PollStore.moveWindows`, in the resume's transaction, before any trigger is locked): what changed during the pause is history (§5.4: "The polls' windows move to the resume too"). After the commit, `SystemResumed` and the resumed triggers' `TriggerResumed` wake the polls; a poll whose triggers stayed paused turns `IDLE` at its next pass.
- **Not built in part b.** Webhooks (open point 8); Jira's changelog, so that an issue assigned before a poll first read it fires no `ASSIGNED` (§5.5 as written); pull requests' review comments; `decided_by_principal_id` for an answer from the issue (no lookup of an account by its identity).
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `PollWindowTest`, `EventDetectorTest` | The window (overlap, never back, the cutoff, a cut pass's offset, several listings, a failed listing); every event per account, Tinfirm's own comment, status, labels, close and push, the account's own comment, `authors TINFIRM`, `armed_at`, the catch-up, `ONCE`, scope and kind |
  | `JiraPollingIntegrationTest` | V4 (Alice's account and budget, only her assignments, Bob `404`; the next pass after the system's `pollIntervalSeconds`); a second pass finds no edge; Alice's own comment fires and Tinfirm's does not; a `401` pauses the trigger `ACCOUNT_BROKEN`; the rate once an hour; nothing from before a resume; a rate limit's and failures' backoff with the window held and `SYSTEM_UNREACHABLE` repaired; another company's poll; run now and the editor's search; Jira Cloud's cursor and mention node; two handlings of one change fire once, a conversation per trigger, a closed subject's end |
  | `CodeHostPollingIntegrationTest` | GitHub's `304`; a `404` scope waiting while another fires; a pushed head commit of Tinfirm's is no update, a person's is; the chain of 3 through change requests; GitLab's group, a merge request's head and a mention |
  | `ScheduleFiringIntegrationTest` | A due schedule in its zone, overlap `SKIP`, catch-up `NONE` and `ONE`, `SUPERSEDED`, run now, a paused agent, the tick; a due schedule renamed and given another overlap before the tick still fires at its due time, another cron or zone moves it from now |
  | `TriggerQuestionsIntegrationTest` | V2's trigger half (svc-ba's token, prompt and payer, the team sees and Alice does not) and a question answered by a polled comment, Tinfirm's own comment answering nothing |
  | `DecisionIntegrationTest`, `ChangeRequestIntegrationTest` | A tool's comment, a change request and a push recorded in `system_write` |
  | `PollSwitchesIntegrationTest` | Package 6's switches with real triggers: during the company's stop a poll reads, stores a comment in the open conversation and fires and records nothing, run now is `409 TRIGGER_PAUSED` before any read, and after the lift the stop's subjects fire nothing while a later one does; a paused system's poll calls nothing and waits without a failure, its window held, run now and the search read nothing, a resume without its triggers idles the poll, one with them moves its window to the resume and the pause's subject is history; a schedule due during the stop records nothing, and its catch-up `ONE` does not bring it back after the lift |

#### The outbox and write-back (part c)

What a trigger's conversation writes back to its issue (the [package 4 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-4-contract.md), §3.13, §5.8, §5.9, T13, T14 and §11, points 4, 6 and 9; built in the `trigger` module beside the trigger core of part a). A `SCHEDULE` conversation writes nothing outside Tinfirm; package 5b adds the chat's place through the same outbox (its `BOT` writer, §15.17), and nothing is queued for a place no `PlaceWriter` writes to.

- **Causes, queued in the transaction that commits them** (`OutboxQueue`), for a `TRACKER_ISSUE` conversation of a live trigger, with its binding on the subject's system. Each row has a cause key, unique per conversation, so a cause queued twice queues once (T13), and the next `seq` of its conversation. A row of a paused trigger is queued `HELD`.

  | Cause | On | Rows |
  |---|---|---|
  | `RESULT`, `ON_SUCCESS` | `TurnRecorded` `SUCCEEDED` with a `COMPLETED` result (or none, while no question of the turn is open) | With `results.comment`: a `COMMENT` of the summary, the next steps, the turn's outputs (change requests with their links, documents by name, links) and the conversation's link; then the actions the system's kind has, in their order (Jira's `TRANSITION` and `ATTACH_DOCUMENTS`, one `ATTACHMENT` per document of the turn; GitHub's and GitLab's `LABEL_ADD`, `LABEL_REMOVE`, `CLOSE`); on Jira, once per conversation, a `REMOTE_LINK` to the conversation in Tinfirm (`RESULT:LINK`) |
  | `FAILURE`, `ON_FAILURE` | `TurnRecorded` `FAILED` or `LOST` | "{agent} could not finish this: {cause}. The team of {trigger} was told." with `results.comment`; the failure's actions. Never reviewed |
  | `QUESTION` | `TurnRecorded` of a turn that left questions open (an `ask`, a `BLOCKED` result's) | One `COMMENT` listing them, ending "Reply in a comment on this issue to answer." |
  | `ANSWER` | `DecisionDecided`: a question answered in Tinfirm by the trigger's team or author (no commenter's name) | One `COMMENT` per answering message (`ANSWER:{messageId}`, else `ANSWER:{decisionId}`): "**{member} answered in Tinfirm:**", every question it answered quoted, the answer |
  | `CHANGE_REQUEST` | `SystemWritten` `CHANGE_REQUEST` of the conversation (the opening of its change request) | On Jira, a `REMOTE_LINK` to it |

  A cancelled turn queues nothing. Every comment is written with the label `{agent} (Tinfirm agent):` in front.
- **The review of a result** (§5.8, T14). With `results.review`, a turn's `RESULT` and `ON_SUCCESS` rows are not queued: `TriggerConversations.raiseResultReview` raises the team's approval with the same rows in words (`writes`), and `outbox_review` keeps the rows exactly. The API's decision shows them in its `subject` (`{type: RESULT, runId, summary, writes}`), for its card, and also `writeItems`, the same writes in the same order, each `{kind, target, value, text}`: its outbox row's kind (`COMMENT`, `TRANSITION`, `LABEL_ADD`, `LABEL_REMOVE`, `CLOSE`, `ATTACHMENT`, `REMOTE_LINK`; a chat thread's `CHAT_POST`, `CHAT_FILE`), the subject it is written to (`SHOP-1`, the thread's reference), what it names (a transition's or label's name, a document's title, the link's address) and its words, so that the web labels them without reading the words. `OutboxPlans.Plan.items` and the chat outbox's review make them (`TriggerConversations.ReviewWrite`); a review stored with its words only answers its words alone (`kind` null). `ResultReviewed` `approved` queues them (`HELD` when the trigger is paused); returned, and once the conversation ended, they go. A turn of an ended conversation is not reviewed.
- **Delivery** (`OutboxDelivery`), on `worker` replicas every `tinfirm.trigger.outbox.tick` and after the commit of a transaction that queued rows or made them due, one pass at a time per replica: rounds of the due rows of every company (`outbox_due`), in their company as SYSTEM. A row is claimed in a short transaction (`claimed_until`); there a paused trigger holds it (`HELD`), and a deleted trigger or an ended conversation drops it, but a `SUBJECT_CLOSED` conversation, whose last turn's result is still written. Under package 6's switches the claim puts it off by `first-retry`, without an attempt, while the company's runs are stopped (`CompanySwitches.runsStopped`; a row queued during the stop is `PENDING`, as the stop holds only those queued before it) or the row's system is paused (`SystemInfo.paused`): the lift arms the triggers again (`TriggerResumed`), which makes their rows due at once, and a system's resume resumes its triggers, which releases their held rows. Outside any transaction its binding's token is taken (`ConversationBindings.use`, purpose `write-back:{outboxId}`): a waiting binding keeps the row waiting without an attempt, until `BindingBound` or at most `binding-wait` from the binding's `waiting_since`, then drops it; a gone one drops it. A binding that holds while its system is paused (`SYSTEM_PAUSED` changes no binding) puts the row off by `first-retry`, without an attempt; a token not revealed for a passing failure is a failed attempt. After a reassignment's resume the rebound binding holds the new table's account, which writes (§11, point 6). The place's writer (`PlaceWriter`, by the conversation's place) writes it; a row whose last attempt may have reached the system (`ambiguous`) is first looked for (`PlaceWriter.written`).
- **The tracker's writer** (`TrackerWriter`): `COMMENT`, `TRANSITION`, `LABEL_*` and `CLOSE` through `SystemTools.call` with the fixed tools, `ATTACHMENT` and `REMOTE_LINK` through `SystemWrites` (Jira; a document from `/output` named with its path's `/` as `_`; a remote link idempotent by its global id `tinfirm:{url}`). A comment on GitHub and GitLab ends with the row's marker, an HTML comment the page does not show (`<!-- tinfirm-outbox:{id} -->`); Jira's markup has none, so there its letters and digits identify it among the issue's newest comments written since the row was queued. Before a repeated attempt: a comment found by its marker or text, an attachment by its name and size, a transition by the issue's status already entered, are taken as written; labels, the close and a remote link are idempotent on the system.
- **Outcomes**, each recorded in a transaction of its own in the lock order (§10): delivered, with its `external_id`, the status, labels and close it wrote in every poll's snapshot of its subject (`trigger_subject`, so that no event follows, T9), `source.written` `DELIVERED` and `SystemWritten` with `outboxId` and the `WrittenValues` it set, from which `SystemWriteRecorder` records its `system_write` row; a retryable failure (no answer, a `5xx`, a timeout, all `ambiguous`; the rate limit) tried again after 1, 2, 4… minutes up to an hour, or the system's longer `Retry-After`, at most 10 attempts; any other failure, and the last attempt, dropped with the reason, posted as a `SYSTEM` message (the inbox counts it) and audited `DROPPED`; a `401` refuses the account (`Accounts.refused`), whose binding then waits and the row with it, without an attempt, and the gate pauses the trigger `ACCOUNT_BROKEN` (§11, point 9), which holds the rows until a person resumes it. A record its transaction could not commit is tried again; failing that, the row is marked `ambiguous`.
- **Ending and pausing.** A pause holds the trigger's pending rows (`HELD`), a resume releases them and makes the waiting ones due at once, a delete drops them (`TRIGGER_DELETED`, part a's `TriggerStates`); the company's stop holds the pending rows of every trigger's open conversations and its lift releases them (`TriggerRunSwitches`). An ended conversation's undelivered rows are dropped with the reason and audited, its reviews deleted, but a `SUBJECT_CLOSED` one's. An author who leaves ends nothing: the rows stay held until the reassignment's resume (§11, point 6).
- **Tests**: `OutboxPlansTest` (each cause's rows and words, and their items as a review shows them, the backoff, a comment's fingerprint), `OutboxJiraIntegrationTest` (every Jira write kind, the records and snapshots, a dropped transition, the change request's link, an ambiguous comment not written twice, a `503` tried again, an ended conversation's rows, tenant isolation), `OutboxCodeHostsIntegrationTest` (GitHub's and GitLab's comments, labels and close, a comment found by its marker), `OutboxReviewIntegrationTest` (the review approved and returned, V13, a failure not reviewed, a question and the team's answer), `OutboxAccountsIntegrationTest` (a `401` pausing the trigger and the reconnected account writing after the resume, the rebind after a reassignment, V2's write-back half), `OutboxSwitchesIntegrationTest` (a row put off without an attempt during the company's stop and written after its lift, a paused system's rows held and written after its resume, a live trigger on a paused system put off), `OutboxLockOrderTest` (§10).

### 15.16 Package 5a: chat systems and direct messages

The design is the [package 5 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-5-contract.md), part a, with all 21 open points of its §11 as decided (21: a bot that cannot be used makes channel triggers wait). Module `chat` (§10), on the system module's `ChatSystems` and `ChatCalls` and the conversation module's public API; tables in §7, audit actions in §8, its secrets in §9, its paths and configuration in §4.

- **Chat systems** (§3.1, §5.1). `SLACK` (`slack: {transport: EVENTS_API | SOCKET_MODE}`) and `TEAMS` (`teams: {tenantId, appId}`) are registered as other systems are, by admins; their base URLs are fixed (`ChatEndpoints`: Slack's Web API; the Bot Framework's connector, to which an activity's `serviceUrl` must belong), and a base URL given is `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`. One system per Slack workspace or Teams tenant in a company (`409 CHAT_WORKSPACE_TAKEN`). The outbound profile of each kind is its fixed hosts only: Slack's API, file host and the Socket Mode hosts `apps.connections.open` names; Microsoft's login, the Bot Framework's OpenID metadata and keys, and the connector. `GET /systems/{id}` shows a chat system's settings, whether its bot and secrets are set, and to admins where the platform sends its events.
- **The bot** (§3.2, C9). Set, replaced and removed only with `PUT` and `DELETE /systems/{id}/bot` (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`): the token is checked with the platform between the transaction that authorises the caller and the one that stores it (Slack's `auth.test`, whose workspace becomes the system's `teamId` and is fixed from then on; Teams' `client_credentials` token for the app), then stored as the chat system's `TOKEN` credential (ADR-0072 decision 9, open point 2; `ChatAccounts.storeBot`): the system's bot credential stored again, or a new one named `Bot` (`Bot 2` and on while the name is taken), a managed company secret, lent `WRITE` to the service account the request names (`credential.lent`). I9 as ADR-0072 restates it: at most one bot per chat system, a credential on that system lent to a service account (`external_system.bot_account_id`, `external_system_bot_fk`). Another workspace or bot user is `400 BOT_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`; a refused token `400 ACCOUNT_REFUSED`; a suspended service account `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED`; a bot another service account borrows `409 BOT_ACCOUNT_IN_USE` (removed first). The bot's credential is lent to no group (`400 BOT_ACCOUNT`, and the trigger `account_grant_bot_ck`), never changed, lent, withdrawn or removed among the system's credentials (`409 BOT_ACCOUNT_IN_USE`), never bound or in a trigger's table (`BOT_ACCOUNT`), and its service account is not deleted while it borrows one (`409 SERVICE_IDENTITY_IN_USE`). The system shows whoever manages it the bot's credential and the service account that borrows it, by id and name (`botCredentialId`, `botCredentialName`, `botPrincipalId`, `botPrincipalName`; the account module answers who borrows it, `SystemAccounts.holderOf`). `ChatBot` reveals the credential for one use and keeps it 10 minutes per replica; a refusal reveals it again once, and a second refusal runs `Accounts.refused` and turns the system `ERROR`. `PUT /systems/{id}/chat/secrets` stores a Slack system's signing secret or app-level token (`xapp-`; §9); `GET …/chat/manifest` answers Slack's app manifest (the bot scopes and events of §5.1, the private channels' `group_*` and `channel_unshared` among them, never `message.channels` or history as the bot's) or Teams' app package (a zip in base64: the bot in the `personal` and `team` scopes, no files). `POST /systems/{id}/test` of a chat system asks the chat module (`ChatSystemTests`): the bot's token, the transport (a Socket Mode connection opens) and the stored secrets.
- **Receiving** (§5.2, C1 to C3). Over HTTP (§4 Paths) and over Socket Mode alike, a verified event goes through `ChatReceiver`: the workspace or tenant it names must be the system's (else stored nowhere; Slack is answered `X-Slack-No-Retry`), then the company's rates (C15: every verified event counts against the system's, a message against its sender's; the excess is stored `REFUSED RATE_LIMITED` and acknowledged, the sender told once a minute; OWNER and ADMIN read and set them with `GET` and `PUT /chat-settings`, `messagesPerUserPerMinute`, `messagesPerUserPerDay` and `eventsPerSystemPerMinute`, the instance's values until set, audited `chat.settings_changed`), then one short transaction inserts the `chat_event` (`ON CONFLICT (system_id, event_id) DO NOTHING`), and the answer is `200` at once. The database failing is `503` on HTTP, no acknowledgement on Socket Mode; a Slack system answered `503` and silent for `tinfirm.chat.silence-after` since turns `ERROR` with an item for admins, which its next stored event clears (`ChatSilence`). Slack's signature: `v0:{timestamp}:{raw body}` under the signing secret, within 300 seconds either way, in constant time. Teams' token: RS256 against the keys of the Bot Framework's OpenID metadata (kept a day; read again when stale or for an unknown `kid`, one read at a time, at most once a minute whatever asks, so that no activity makes Tinfirm call the Bot Framework more often; `TeamsVerifierTest`) endorsed for `msteams`, `iss` `https://api.botframework.com`, `aud` the app id, `exp` and `nbf` with five minutes' skew, the `serviceurl` claim the activity's `serviceUrl`, on the system's origin, and the activity's tenant the system's. Teams' event id is `{conversation.id}:{activity id}`. Socket Mode (`SlackSockets`, `worker` replicas): `tinfirm.chat.sockets-per-system` slots per system, each a lease of `chat_socket`; an envelope is acknowledged once its event is stored; a `disconnect` opens a new connection before the old one goes; a closed connection comes back after 1, 2, 4… seconds, at most a minute; a refused app-level token is revealed again once, then the system turns `ERROR`. `tinfirm.chat.max-body` (`TINFIRM_CHAT_MAX_BODY`, a size, `1MB` by default; `DataSize`) bounds what is read: an HTTP request's raw body in bytes (`413` beyond), and a Socket Mode message, put together from its parts up to the same number of characters (`SlackSockets.maxChars`); a longer one is dropped as it arrives, never held whole, and not acknowledged, as the Events API answers `413`, and the connection carries on.
- **Handling** (`ChatInbox`, `worker` replicas, every `tinfirm.chat.tick`). `chat_event_due` gives each thread's oldest `RECEIVED` event in the platform's order; it is claimed for two minutes (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), and its handler runs on a virtual thread, platform calls outside any transaction. A failure is tried again after 1, 2, 4 and 8 seconds, then `FAILED`, and the sender is told. Before a handler: the system must not be paused (else `IGNORED SYSTEM_PAUSED`, never answered later; Kill switches, below); the bot must be usable (else `IGNORED NO_BOT`, never answered later, C14); the sender must be a member of the workspace (`ChatAudience`, C6: Slack's `users.info`, Teams' roster, read again after an hour; a guest, another organisation's user, a bot or a deleted user is `REFUSED`, told once an hour, audited `chat.refused`, never offered a link), and not a suspended member (`REFUSED SUSPENDED` in the same way); an unknown user the platform cannot be asked about is tried again. What is never handled is `IGNORED` at once: bots' and apps' messages, edits and deletions (`EDITED`), other subtypes, channel messages that do not mention the bot (C7), Teams group chats (`NOT_SERVED`). Held events (`AWAITING_LINK`, `AWAITING_AGENT`) end with their hold, their sender told; every row goes a day after it arrived.
- **Linking** (§5.4, rows 5 and 6). A link is the member's `LINK` account on the chat system, its identity Slack's user id or Teams' Entra object id (Teams' chat users are keyed by their `29:` id, with `aad_object_id`); never by an email or a name. Teams links automatically when the activity's object id and the system's tenant are exactly one `ACTIVE` member's `app_user.oidc_object_id` and `oidc_tenant_id` (the ID token of their last Entra v2.0 login, read from the token itself). Otherwise an unlinked member of the workspace gets a sign-in link in their direct message, at most one per 10 minutes, `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/link/{companyId}#{token}`: bound to that chat user, used once, for `tinfirm.chat.link-lifetime`; their messages wait for it (at most 5). The token travels in the fragment and in request bodies only (`POST /chat-links/read`, which consumes nothing and names the chat user by what they cannot choose; `POST /chat-links/confirm`, which consumes the request whatever the outcome: `410 CHAT_LINK_EXPIRED`, `409 CHAT_LINK_USED`, `IDENTITY_IN_USE`, `ALREADY_LINKED`). Both sides are told: the direct message ("Linked to …", with the Owner notice) and the member's inbox for 7 days with its unlink. An admin removes a member's chat account (`DELETE /members/{userId}/accounts/{systemId}`, `MANAGE_MEMBERS`, `400 NOT_A_CHAT_ACCOUNT`, open point 16); a member who leaves loses their links with their accounts.
- **Direct messages** (§5.5, §5.6, C5, C8). A direct message from a linked member is their personal conversation exactly as the app's (`PersonalConversations.start`: their agents, bindings, budget, settings and visibility), held in the chat: `place` `CHAT_THREAD`, `origin` `DIRECT_MESSAGE`, `place_ref` the thread. The conversation shows whoever sees it its place there (`Conversation.chat`, built by the conversation module from `place_ref`): the system, the channel, the thread and a link that opens it, Slack's `https://slack.com/app_redirect?team=…&channel=…` or Teams' chat with the bot (`https://teams.microsoft.com/l/chat/0/0?users=28:{appId}`). On Slack each top-level message starts a conversation, answered in its thread, and a reply in the thread continues it; Teams' personal chat has one open conversation per member and system, which "New conversation" or another agent's handle ends `SUPERSEDED`. The agent: a leading `@handle`, else the member's default (`GET`, `PUT /me/chat`: an agent the caller uses, else `404` or `403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE`), else a picker of the agents they use, which only the message's own sender presses ("Always" makes it the default), the message held an hour. Another member's message in the thread is `REFUSED NOT_YOURS`; an ended conversation's thread takes nothing (`THREAD_ENDED`, C8). The first conversation of a chat user carries the Owner notice (row 18). Files: a Slack message's files are downloaded with the bot's token outside any transaction and added under package 3's limits and scan (`PlaceFiles.add`); Teams files are not read (open point 12), the bot points to the app.
- **Answers and buttons** (§5.8 to §5.10, C10, C11). The outbox's `BOT` writer (§15.17) posts after the conversation module's events (`TurnRecorded`, `DecisionRaised`, `DecisionClosed`, `BindingWaiting`), in order per conversation, through `ChatPoster`: the answer headed by the agent's name with End (and Teams' New conversation); question and approval cards; Connect cards linking to the app; failures. Slack posts Block Kit with `parse: none`, no link names and no unfurls, the Markdown as mrkdwn with `&`, `<` and `>` escaped (`ChatMarkdown`); Teams posts Markdown with `<at>` and HTML as text, buttons as Adaptive Cards 1.5 `Action.Submit`; long answers split at blank lines, never inside a fence, at most 4 parts, then the whole as `answer.md` (Slack) or a link (Teams). A decision is decided only by its card's button (`PlaceDecisions.decide`): the press names the card the bot posted for it in that channel, and the presser's linked member must be the one who may decide it; anyone else is told so, and typed words decide nothing. `decision.decided_via` is `CHAT` with the presser's chat id.
- **Retention** (package 6's seam `RetainedRecords`, §15.11). A chat event goes a day after it arrived and a chat user 90 days after they were last seen (the contract's §3.3, §3.7): `ChatEventRetention` (`chatEvents`) and `ChatUserRetention` (`chatUsers`) delete them in the daily sweep, beside `ChatInbox`'s own pass over the events. A direct message's conversation is a chat's content, purged after `chat_content_days` from its end (`ConversationPurge`); its `chat_post` rows name it and stay.
- **Reads.** The chat's entry points into the conversation module (`PersonalConversations`, `PlaceConversations`, `PlaceDecisions`, `PlaceFiles`) act for the conversation's own person, in their direct message, and show nothing to anyone else: no read by OWNER passes through them. OWNER reads a direct message's conversation through the app's paths (`ConversationReads`), audited `conversation.read` with its place and origin (§8); a trigger's conversation has no person, and these entry points refuse it.
- **Inbox** (`conversation.InboxItems`, the port package 6 shares). The chat module serves `CHAT_LINKED` (the member's link, 7 days, with its unlink) and, to admins, `CHAT_SUBSCRIPTION_SILENT`, each with `chat: {systemId, systemName, text, unlinkSystemId}`; `CHAT_LINKED` also with `chatUserId` and `chatUserName` (the display name the link stored, else the chat user's as the platform last said it), and its text names them so: "Slack user Jane Doe (U04ABC123) of Acme".
- **Kill switches** (§5.14). Removing the bot drops events unanswered (`NO_BOT`) until one is set again; a refused bot token, a suspended service account likewise; removing the system ends its open conversations `SYSTEM_REMOVED`, after the commit, each in a transaction of its own. Package 6's switches (its contract, §5.4, G4 to G6; §15.13), each told to the chat user in their direct message (on Teams only there) and recorded with its reason (V028):
  - **The company's stop** (G4). No conversation starts while it lasts, from the app, a direct message or the endpoint alike: `ConversationService.startFor`, the one start of a personal conversation, refuses `409 COMPANY_STOPPED` (`PersonalConversations.StartRefused`). A direct message that would start one is `REFUSED COMPANY_STOPPED` before the agent's choice, held for no picker, and a picker's press during the stop ends its held message so; a message to an open conversation is taken (`HANDLED`) and its turn waits for the lift (the dispatcher's step 0). The bot says "Tinfirm is stopped in {company} by an admin; nothing was started" or "…; your message waits until the stop is lifted", at most once an hour per chat user (`chat_user.stop_told_at`). An approval's press is refused `COMPANY_STOPPED` and told why, its card staying open; answers and denials are taken. A mention fires nothing (package 5b's `ChannelMentions` checks the stop first: it stores nothing and answers nothing later, §15.17).
  - **A paused chat system** (G5; the contract decides it: "verified and dropped (`SYSTEM_PAUSED`), since answering needs the system"). Its events are verified, stored and acknowledged as always, then dropped `IGNORED SYSTEM_PAUSED` and never answered later; the lifecycle events are still kept (channels, a revoked token), since they call nothing. `ChatBot.state` answers `PAUSED` before anything else and `ChatBot.credential` reads the pause at every use, a kept credential's too, so the bot posts and reads nothing on any replica. A conversation's posts wait while its system is paused, in order, and are posted after the resume: in package 5b the outbox's rows, put off by the delivery's claim and made due again by the system's resume (§15.17). Setting its bot is `409 SYSTEM_PAUSED` (connecting an account, §5.4); removing it is not; testing it is refused by the system module. A Socket Mode connection stays open, so that its envelopes are acknowledged and dropped (the app-level token is the system's own secret, not an account). `ChatSystems.find` reads `paused_at`.
  - **A suspended member** (G6). A chat user linked to a suspended member, or on Teams the user their Entra object id names, is refused as a non-member is: `REFUSED SUSPENDED`, "Your Tinfirm account is suspended in {company}." once an hour (`refusal_told_at`), audited `chat.refused`, never offered a sign-in link, for messages and presses alike. A member resumed is served again; their links stay.

  Package 5b's seams (§15.17): `ChatBot.State` (what a channel trigger waits on), `ChannelMentions`, `ChatLifecycle.channelChanged` (the hook of `trigger_channel`'s state); the chat's posts go through the outbox.
- **Problem codes** (§6): `BOT_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` (400), `BOT_ACCOUNT` (400), `BOT_ACCOUNT_IN_USE` (409), `SERVICE_IDENTITY_IN_USE` (409), `BOT_NOT_SET` (409), `CHAT_WORKSPACE_TAKEN` (409), `CHAT_LINK_EXPIRED` (410), `CHAT_LINK_USED` (409), `ALREADY_LINKED` (409), `NOT_A_CHAT_ACCOUNT` (400); package 6's `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (409) for setting a paused system's bot.
- **Tests** (`server/src/test/java/com/flowtivo/tinfirm/chat/`), with the stubs `FakeSlack` (Slack's Web API and file host on WireMock, Events API deliveries signed as Slack signs them), `SlackSocketStub` (a minimal WebSocket server for Socket Mode) and `FakeBotFramework` (login, OpenID metadata, a test JWK set with endorsements, the connector, activities with JWTs its keys sign), whose endpoints `IntegrationTest` registers as `tinfirm.chat.endpoints.*`:

  | Test | What it shows |
  |---|---|
  | `ChatSystemsIntegrationTest` | Registering with settings and fixed base URLs; one system per tenant; the bot set only with a token the platform accepts for the system's workspace, replaced by its own service account only, removed; C9's refusals by the API and the database; the secrets stored and never answered, managed; the manifests; the company's rates |
  | `SlackReceivingTest` | The signature before anything (window, a forged one, a rotated secret), `404`, `413`; the acknowledgement within 3 seconds while `users.info` is slow, a retry acting once; another workspace's events; what is ignored; guests and other organisations refused and told once an hour; the rates |
  | `TeamsReceivingTest` | The token's every check, the `serviceurl` claim, the origin and the tenant; an activity acting once, by its conversation and id; guests; group chats |
  | `internal/SlackSocketModeTest` | Socket Mode: acknowledged once stored, a redelivery acting once, another workspace's envelope, a `disconnect`, a dropped connection, the transport switched off, a refused app-level token; the silent subscription and its clearing |
  | `ChatLinkingTest` | The sign-in link: what it names, single use, expiry, both sides told (the inbox's item naming the chat user by name and id), the held messages taken; a chat user linked to another member, a member linked twice; an admin's removal; a member who leaves; Teams' automatic link by tenant and object id |
  | `DirectMessagesTest` | End to end with the fake runner host and the member's own budget: the picker, the answer in the thread, a reply's next turn, another member's message, End, a leading handle, Slack files in, the Owner notice once and the Owner's audited read; a question decided by its card's button by the linked person only; Teams' one open conversation, New conversation, files pointed to the app |
  | `ChatKillSwitchesTest` | Package 6's switches end to end: during the company's stop a picker's press and a new direct message start nothing (`COMPANY_STOPPED`), a reply to the open conversation is taken and its turn runs after the lift, the chat user told once an hour; a suspended member refused `SUSPENDED`, told once, audited, never offered a link, served again once resumed; a paused system's event dropped `SYSTEM_PAUSED`, nothing posted or reacted, its bot not set (`409 SYSTEM_PAUSED`), the answer held meanwhile posted after the resume |
  | `ChatConstraintTest` | The database's rules of `chat_event`, `chat_link_request`, chat threads' conversations and a Slack system's secrets; `chat_event_due`'s order per thread |
  | `internal/ChatRetentionTest` | `RetainedRecords`: events after a day, chat users after 90 days, in their company only |
  | `internal/ChatMarkdownTest`, `internal/MentionsTest` | Formatting and splitting; handles outside code |

  `CrossTenantBodyTest` covers the platforms' paths across companies (§10), and `TenantIsolationTest` every chat path under `/api/companies`.

### 15.17 Package 5b: channel triggers, the chat outbox and the webhook

The design is the [package 5 contract](reviews/2026-10-12-package-5-contract.md) as decided (§11; open point 21(b)), the part 5a left: the outbox's `BOT` writer, channel triggers and the generic webhook. Modules `chat` and `trigger` (§10), on package 4's outbox and firings; tables in §7 (`V019`, `V029`, `V030`, `V031`), audit actions in §8, the webhook's secret in §9, its path and configuration in §4.

- **The chat's answers through the outbox** (§5.9, C12, open point 14; `V029`). Every `CHAT_THREAD` conversation's posts are package 4's outbox rows, queued in the transaction that commits their cause (`ChatOutbox`, in-transaction listeners of `TurnRecorded`, `DecisionRaised`, `DecisionClosed`, `BindingWaiting`, `BindingBound`, `ConversationRebound`, and the chat's own `RECEIVED` and `NOTICE`), with `writer` `BOT` and no binding, through the trigger module's port `PlaceOutbox` (a trigger's held while it is paused, its results reviewed first when the trigger asks, T14). Cause keys: `RESULT:{run}`, `FILE:{output}`, `FAILURE:{run}`, `REVIEW:{run}` (the note that a result waits for its review), `CARD:{decision}`, `DECIDED:{decision}` (a `CHAT_UPDATE` of its card, `target_outbox_id`), `ANSWER:{decision}`, `CONNECT:{binding}:{n}`, `CONNECTED:{binding}:{n}`, `RECEIVED:{message}`, `NOTICE:{key}` (`NOTICE:REBOUND:{n}`: a rebind's note, §5.14). `OutboxDelivery` delivers them in order per conversation through `ChatPlaceWriter` (the `PlaceWriter` of `CHAT_THREAD`), one attempt each: no usable bot answers `Outcome.Waiting` (tried again every minute, dropped after the binding wait's 7 days); `429` waits its `Retry-After`; `5xx` or no answer is ambiguous and tried again (it may post twice, accepted); a channel gone drops the row and makes the trigger channel `UNAVAILABLE`. A delivered row records the message's `ts` or activity id, a `system_write` `CHAT_MESSAGE` and `source.written`; a card's post is recorded in `chat_post`, so that a press counts only on it (C10). A paused system's rows and a stopped company's are put off by the delivery's claim, and the system's resume (`SystemResumed`) and the stop's lift (`RunsResumed`) make them due at once. Nothing waits in memory: a restart loses no answer.
- **Channel triggers** (§5.7, open points 7, 8, 21). `source CHANNEL` on a company `SLACK` or `TEAMS` system, its channels in `trigger_channel` (1 to 20, `channels: [{channelId, default}]` on create, preview and change, replaced as a whole). `TriggerEditor` checks, for the author whoever edits: the system has a bot (`409 BOT_NOT_SET`); each channel is one the bot is in, not archived, deleted or shared (`400 CHANNEL_UNAVAILABLE`); the author is linked on the system and a member of each channel added, of all of them for a new trigger or another author (`400 CHANNEL_NOT_JOINED`), as the platform said **outside any transaction** before the save (`ChannelSources.joined`: Slack's `users.conversations` with the author's chat user; Teams' roster at the channel's last `serviceUrl`; the platform not answering is `CHANNEL_NOT_JOINED` "try again"); one trigger per agent per channel and one default (`409 CHANNEL_TAKEN`; read without locks, so two saves that claim a channel at once both pass, and V030's `trigger_channel_agent_uq` or `trigger_channel_default_uq` refuses the second, answered `409 CHANNEL_TAKEN` too, never `500`). A channel trigger counts against its chat system's cap of 100 live triggers, as a tracker's does (§15.15, `409 TOO_MANY_TRIGGERS`). It is open to anyone: the preview names "any member of {workspace} in #support", and the acknowledgement of open writes applies. Run now is refused (a mention fires it). `GET /systems/{id}/chat/channels?query=&authorPrincipalId=` lists the channels the bot is in (Slack's read again first, at most once a minute per system and replica, else the kept list, so that members listing do not spend the bot's rate; nothing is claimed while the system has no bot, a call the bot could not make gives the minute back, and a change of the system's accounts (the bot set, replaced or removed) or its resume forgets the minute, `ChatBotWatch`): public ones, private ones to a linked member the platform says is in them; each with `kind`, `archived`, `available`, `hasDefault` (whoever's) and the live channel triggers on it the caller sees, as the trigger pages show them: a member's own and their team's, an OWNER's or ADMIN's every one. `authorPrincipalId` is an admin's view of the author's channels, private ones included, each still with every trigger on it (404 for anyone else).
- **A mention** (`ChannelMentions`, after 5a's receiving and audience: any `MEMBER`, linked or not). In order: the company's stop (nothing fired, stored or answered later; told once an hour, `COMPANY_STOPPED`); the thread (Slack's `thread_ts`, or the message's own; a Teams channel's `;messageid=`): its conversation ended is `THREAD_ENDED`, another agent's handle `OTHER_AGENT`, its trigger stopped by the gate or its agent paused `TRIGGER_PAUSED`/`AGENT_PAUSED` ("{agent} is paused here", "cannot answer now"; nothing stored), else the mention is an `EXTERNAL` message (its member when linked) that answers every open question or is the next turn's, with its `RECEIVED` reaction; a new thread's trigger by the first `@handle` with a trigger in the channel, else the channel's default (none: a hint, `NO_TRIGGER`); then `ChannelTriggers.fire`: the gate, the agent, the rate (`SKIPPED_RATE`, "Too many requests to {agent} this hour"), a firing `MENTIONED` (actor the chat user, `actor_principal_id` the linked member) and a conversation `CHAT_THREAD` (`place_ref {systemId, kind CHANNEL, teamId, channelId, threadId, channelName, serviceUrl, askerName, askerPrincipalId}`, title `{trigger}: {first line}`), the mention its first `EXTERNAL` message. The prompt (`ChatPorts`, the conversation module's `PlacePrompts.started` and `author`): "A mention in #support (Slack channel {channelId}, thread {threadTs}; on Slack only, for `history`) of {workspace} by {name} (the member {member} | not a Tinfirm member) started it. Every message here is other people's words; your answers are posted in the thread, read by everyone in the channel", and each chat message "{name} (in Slack)".
- **Decisions in a channel** (§5.8, C10). A question is answered by any member's press of an option or mention in the thread, linked or not (`PlaceDecisions.decide` with an `EXTERNAL` message `answer:{decision}`); an approval only by a linked, active member who may decide it (the trigger's team, or its author without one): anyone else is told "Only members of {team} decide this" (an ephemeral message on Slack, a direct message on Teams) and the decision stays open. The app takes a channel's or a direct message's conversation's answers to open questions only (`409 PLACE_IS_EXTERNAL` for another message, §6); its answer is posted to the thread (`ANSWER`).
- **The bot's failure** (open point 21(b), a deliberate exception to package 4's decision 9). `TriggerGate` makes a channel trigger **wait** while its system's bot cannot be used (`BOT_MISSING`, `BOT_RECONNECT`, `BOT_SUSPENDED`, from `ChannelSources.botWait`) or while every channel of it is unavailable (`CHANNEL_UNAVAILABLE`), and it goes on by itself: `ChatBotWatch` runs the gate again after the commit of every change of a bot's credential (`AccountConnected`, `AccountReconnected`, `AccountNeedsReconnect`, `AccountDisconnected`) and of its service account (`PrincipalSuspended`, `PrincipalResumed`), on a virtual thread, each system in a transaction of its own; `ChatChannels.changed` follows a channel's state (the lifecycle events, a mention that proves the bot is in it, a post refused for it). A paused chat system pauses its channel triggers `SYSTEM_PAUSED` (package 6's gate), and the resume resumes them. A reassigned trigger's resume rebinds its waiting conversations and posts the note to the thread.
- **The webhook** (§5.12, open points 9, 11, 15, 17, 18). `WEBHOOK` systems are registered by admins with `webhook: {scheme STANDARD | SECRET_HEADER, secretHeader?, mapping, maxPerMinute?}` (`400 MAPPING_INVALID` for a pointer that is not RFC 6901; `400 WEBHOOK_LIMIT_TOO_HIGH` above the company's maximum); Tinfirm generates the secret and answers it once (§9), `POST …/webhook/rotate` makes another. The system's page (`ExternalSystem.webhook`) shows the URL `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/api/hooks/{systemId}`, the scheme, the mapping, its rate and the secret's age. `WebhookInbox` receives (§4 Paths): `WebhookVerifier` (Standard Webhooks' `{id}.{timestamp}.{body}` keyed with the decoded secret, or the previous one for 24 hours, within 300 seconds either way; or the header equal to the secret, in constant time), `WebhookLimiter` (in memory per replica: refusals per client address, checked first, keyed by the path's system id before the system is looked up, then verified deliveries per system; `webhook.refused` once a minute per system and reason), the body parsed (`400` for one too deep, too large, or with `\u0000`, which `jsonb` refuses: before it counts or is stored, never a `503`), then `WebhookReceiver` in one transaction of the company: `webhook_delivery` inserted `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (the id: `webhook-id`, else the mapped `deliveryId`, else the body's SHA-256), mapped (`WebhookMapping`: JSON Pointers only, a string, number or boolean read as text and cut, anything else null; a pointer that cannot be read or an unprintable mapped id is `MAPPING_FAILED`), and fired: each live `WEBHOOK` trigger of the system, in the order of their ids, whose events (`DELIVERED` with the mapped event among their values, or one without a value for any) and conditions (`webhookConditions`, at most 5 `{pointer, op: EQUALS | IN | PRESENT | ABSENT, values}` on the raw body, all of which hold) match. `WebhookFirings` as package 4's firings: the gate (its `COMPANY_STOPPED`, or `SYSTEM_PAUSED` with the source system paused, a stop or a pause committed after the inbox's checks, throws `WebhookFirings.Held`: the delivery's transaction rolls back and the inbox answers `503` with `Retry-After: 300` or `200 {"paused": true}`, so the delivery is not lost as `NO_TRIGGER`), the agent, the rate; **echoes**: a delivery about a subject key that a conversation of the trigger wrote to (`system_write`) within `tinfirm.trigger.settle` fires one deeper than its deepest firing, beyond 3 `SKIPPED_CHAIN` with a `SYSTEM` message in the writing conversation; the conversation: with a key the trigger's open one on it (`CONTINUED`, an `EVENT` turn), else a new one; without a key one per delivery, the previous idle one ending `SUPERSEDED`. Place `WEBHOOK`, `place_ref {systemId, deliveryId, subjectKey?}`, title `{trigger}: {key} {title}`; the firing's actor is the sender's (`{id, login: null, name}`), never a member; the delivery's text is an `EXTERNAL` message without an author that answers nothing (C13); the first turn's `# Event` (the request's system, time, event, subject, actor "as the sender says, unverified", text and the body cut to 16 KiB, fenced) and the prompt's lines from the trigger module's `PlacePrompts` (`WebhookPrompts`). Results stay in Tinfirm; the team and the author write in the conversation (a `MESSAGE` turn), as in a `SCHEDULE` one. A webhook system holds at most 100 live triggers, as a tracker does (§15.15, `409 TOO_MANY_TRIGGERS`). The preview warns of echoes (`warnings`), and asks for `subject.key` when the system's mapping has none. Admins try a body against the mapping and the triggers (`POST …/webhook/map`, nothing stored; from §15.20 each trigger with its `status`, `pausedReason`, `waitingReason` and, when it does not match, its `mismatch`: the first of its events or conditions the body fails, with what the body holds there, by `WebhookMapping.mismatch`, the receiver's own rule) and list the deliveries (`GET …/webhook/deliveries`, newest first, the body for 24 hours); the first page also counts the last 7 days (`lastSevenDays`: `deliveries` received, of them `failed`, `MAPPING_FAILED`, on `webhook_delivery_list_idx`; `refused`, the sum of the system's `webhook.refused` counts through `AuditReads.webhookRefusals`, on V043's `audit_event_webhook_refused_idx`, without the refusals a replica has not recorded yet in the last minute). Rows are kept for exactly that week, so the counts are whole. Rows go after 7 days and bodies after 24 hours (`WebhookRetention`, `webhookDeliveries`, the daily sweep). The company's rates: `PUT /settings` `webhookDeliveriesPerMinute: {default, max}` (1 to 6000), seeded from `TINFIRM_WEBHOOK_DELIVERIES_PER_MINUTE_*`; lowering the maximum lowers the systems above it (`WebhookSystems.lowerPerMinute`). Run now is refused.
- **Kill switches** (§5.14). The company's stop: a mention fires nothing and is never answered later (told once an hour); a delivery is `503` with `Retry-After: 300`, nothing stored. A paused chat system pauses its channel triggers `SYSTEM_PAUSED`; a paused webhook system answers `200 {"paused": true}`, nothing stored, and its triggers pause too. A paused channel or webhook trigger fires nothing, then or later; its conversations' turns and outbox rows wait as package 4's do. The bot's failure: above.
- **Problem codes** (§6): `CHANNEL_UNAVAILABLE`, `CHANNEL_NOT_JOINED` (400), `CHANNEL_TAKEN` (409), `MAPPING_INVALID` (400), `WEBHOOK_LIMIT_TOO_HIGH` (400); 5a's `BOT_NOT_SET` (409) now from the editor.
- **Slack's `history` tool** (§2.2, §5.4, open point 13). `SLACK` has a fixed catalogue (`SystemKind.hasFixedTools`; `tracker()` tells the trackers' apart): `history` (`READ`, `ToolReplay.SAFE`), `{channel, thread?, limit?, cursor?}` (`channel` a `C…` or `G…` id, `thread` a message's `ts`, `limit` 1 to 100, 20 without one, `cursor` a previous result's): `conversations.history` of the channel or `conversations.replies` of the thread (`SlackApi.history`), shown oldest first with each message's time, `<@user>`, `ts`, replies and text (cut at 4000 characters) and the next page's cursor; `missing_scope` and a refused token are `Reconnect`, a channel the account's user may not read is an error result. It reads any channel that account's user may read, as Slack decides (Part 6: the account bounds what the system permits), with the conversation's own account on Slack, never the bot's token (C9); in a channel trigger's conversation it reads only the thread the agent was asked in, and any other read is `TOOL_DENIED` (the contract's open point 13 as amended 2026-10-14; `ToolRules.outsideThread`). An agent may list a `SLACK` system (`AgentService.KINDS`, and V031's `agent_system_ck()`); `TEAMS` and `WEBHOOK` stay refused. **The accounts**: `PUT /systems/{id}/app` on `SLACK` stores the Slack app's client id and secret (the secret required, `scopes` refused: they are fixed); `SystemKind.oauth()` includes `SLACK`, whose profile (`OAuthProfile.slack`) is Slack's OAuth v2 at the base URL's web origin (`/oauth/v2/authorize` with `user_scope=channels:history,groups:history`, never a bot scope), `oauth.v2.access` (`client_secret_post`; the user token is `authed_user`'s, `OAuthClient.slackAnswer`, Slack's errors taken as RFC 6749's nearest) and `auth.revoke`; `whoAmI` is `auth.test` with the user token, its `team_id` the system's `teamId` (none yet, a bot's token or another workspace's user: refused). `AccountService.requireConnectable` lets a sign-in through on `SLACK` for a member's own account or a system's credential, never the bot's (`409 BOT_ACCOUNT_IN_USE`); a pasted token stays `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`, as every account on `TEAMS` and `WEBHOOK`. A member's `OAUTH` account is also their link: it replaces their `LINK` of the same Slack user (the same row, `account.reconnected`), and a sign-in as another Slack user than their link or account is the callback's `already_linked`; a new one (no link before) tells their inbox and releases the direct messages that waited for the link (`ChatLinks.accountConnected`). **The binding** (`AccountsImpl`): a `LINK` is no account to act with (a member's own counts as none, so a conversation's Connect card asks for the sign-in; a trigger's row naming one is `400 ACCOUNT_NOT_ENTITLED`, `ACCOUNT_NOT_USABLE`; a stored binding's check stops on one), and a chat system's bot is never bound (`AccountBinding.Refusal.BOT_ACCOUNT`, `400 BOT_ACCOUNT` from the trigger editor). A channel trigger may have a row on its chat system, for `history`.
- **Tests**:

  | Test | What it shows |
  |---|---|
  | `chat/ChannelTriggersTest` | A reviewed answer (T14): its review's writes, the post and the document, as `CHAT_POST` and `CHAT_FILE` items on the thread's reference, nothing posted before the team approves, and a purge of the decided review removing its summary, words and items; the editor's checks for the author (not joined, unavailable, taken twice), the channel list (private channels to their members, the author's view for admins only; a member sees the triggers they author or are in the team of, an OWNER or ADMIN every one, in their own view and in an author's); a mention by a member who is not linked fires the trigger, its conversation in the thread, the prompt's lines, the reaction and the answer in the thread through the outbox (`writer` `BOT`, `system_write` `CHAT_MESSAGE`); `PLACE_IS_EXTERNAL` in the app; a mention in the thread continues it; paused, a new and a threaded mention are told and store nothing; the company's stop; no default, a hint. An approval pressed by an unlinked asker (told, still open) and by the team's linked member; a question answered by the unlinked asker's press. The bot's service account suspended and resumed, the bot removed and set again: the trigger waits and goes on, active all along; the bot out of its only channel and back; the channel list reads the bot's channels from Slack at most once a minute, and at once after the bot is set (none claimed without one), set again, or the system resumes. On Teams: the author linked by their Entra object id and in the channel by its roster, a mention answered in the channel's thread (`{channel};messageid={root}`), a reply continuing it |
  | `trigger/WebhookTriggersTest` | V18: registration with its secret once, `MAPPING_INVALID`; a signed delivery fires the trigger (events and a condition), the conversation in the webhook's place, `# Event`, the actor no member, `message_place_ck`; the same delivery again, a forged and a stale one; a delivery the condition leaves out; a comment on the subject continues the conversation; the team writes in it and starts a turn; the deliveries list and the mapping's try. The secret header, a rotation (the previous accepted), the system's rate and the company's limit, the company's stop (`503`), the system's pause (`200`), a stranger's flood. An echo beyond the chain: `SKIPPED_CHAIN` and the writing conversation told. From the package 5b review: a verified body with `\u0000` is `400`, stored nowhere, the next one unaffected; a kept previous secret refused from its own `previousUntil`; a flooded address `429` before the system is looked up (still `429` once the system is deleted); 60 webhook systems of one company rotated twice each, every secret under its system's `WEBHOOK_SECRET_{HANDLE}` and `_2` |
  | `trigger/internal/WebhookStopRaceTest` | A stop and a system's pause committed after the inbox's checks: the receiver holds the delivery (`WebhookFirings.Held`), nothing stored; resumed, the same delivery fires |
  | `trigger/internal/ChannelStateLockOrderTest` | §10's lock order: a channel's state change (the trigger, then its rows, then the gate) against the editor's change of the trigger's channels (the trigger, then its rows), the editor holding the trigger first: no `40P01` |
  | `trigger/internal/ChannelTakenRaceTest` | A save that claims a channel another save took and has not committed: V030's unique index refuses it, answered `409 CHANNEL_TAKEN`, not `500` |
  | `chat/SlackHistoryTest` | The Slack app on the system (its secret required, its scopes fixed; none on Teams); a pasted token, a Teams sign-in and a sign-in of the bot's credential refused; a member's LINK binds nothing, their sign-in asks for the user scopes only and replaces the LINK of the same Slack user (the same row), another member's sign-in as a third Slack user `already_linked` and as a taken one `identity_in_use`; a new link told in the owner's inbox; a Slack credential of the system, which links nobody; a refused code. The agent lists Slack (`history`, a read; Teams refused): its call waits on the member's LINK and runs on their sign-in with their user token, never the bot's; a channel's page oldest first with its cursor, a thread, arguments out of shape, a refused token (`RECONNECT`). A channel trigger's table: the bot's account `BOT_ACCOUNT`, a LINK `ACCOUNT_NOT_ENTITLED`, the author's own Slack account taken; the prompt names the channel and thread, which `history` reads with the row's account |
  | `trigger/internal/WebhookVerifierTest` | Standard Webhooks' own vector, the decoded key, the previous secret, the window either way, the secret header; the mapping's pointers and limits, the conditions; bodies too deep or too large, or with `\u0000` in a name or a string; a cut never splits a surrogate pair; a kept previous secret expires at its own `previousUntil` |
  | `chat/ChatConstraintTest`, `trigger/TriggerConstraintTest` | V018's and V030's rules on chat threads and triggers, with the outbox's `BOT` rows |

### 15.18 Agent memory

Notes an agent keeps for the owner of its settings, a person or a trigger ([ADR-0063](adr/0063-agent-memory.md)): the agent module keeps them (`AgentMemory`, `MemoryService`, `MemoryStore`, `MemoryCompaction`), the conversation module gives a run its tools and its notes (`MemoryTools`, `MemoryPrompt`, `SystemNotes`), the trigger module serves a trigger's notes and their review (`TriggerMemory`, the inbox), the model module the company's own key (`PlatformModel`, ADR-0065, §15.4), the mcp module people's clients' tools (§14.11), and the governance module retention, holds and erasure. `V039` and `V045` hold the tables (§7), the audit actions are `memory.*` and `memory_settings.changed` (§8), the variable `TINFIRM_MEMORY_MAX_CHARACTERS` (§4).

- **Whose.** A memory is one settings row (`agent_settings`, §15.5): a person's of one agent, or a trigger's. Its notes are read and written only in that owner's conversations (`ConversationInfo.settingsOwner`); a person's and a trigger's never meet, nor two people's. Who reads and changes a memory through the API is who may use the agent, for their own (`GET`, `DELETE …/agents/{agentId}/settings/me/memory`, `DELETE …/memory/{noteNumber}`; `403 AGENT_NOT_USABLE`, `404` as for the agent), and a trigger's audience (its author, its team, OWNER, ADMIN; §15.15) for the trigger's (`GET`, `DELETE …/triggers/{triggerId}/memory`, `DELETE …/memory/{noteNumber}`, `POST …/memory/{noteNumber}/accept`; `404` for anyone else). Through their AI client (§14.11), a person lists and deletes their own notes (`PersonalMemory`: `list_memory_notes`, `delete_memory_note`) and a trigger's audience lists its notes (`TriggerMemoryReads`: `list_trigger_memory_notes`, with the link to the trigger's memory in Tinfirm); a client accepts or rejects no proposal. Reads are not audited.
- **Opt-in.** `agent.memory_allowed` (`Agent.memoryAllowed`, true by default; whoever edits the agent changes it, not a widening, audited in `agent.changed`'s `changed`) and `agent_settings.memory` (`AgentSettings.memory`, false by default; the person's save, or a trigger's settings' save, audited in `settings.changed` or `trigger.settings_changed`). `EffectiveSettings.memory()` is true when both are. Off keeps the notes, unread and unwritten; returning one's settings to the defaults turns memory off and keeps the notes.
- **A note.** One line: every run of whitespace, control and format characters is one space, then trimmed (`MemoryService.normalized`); 1 to 300 characters (`INVALID_REQUEST`); about the owner in general, a system the settings in effect list (`EffectiveSettings.systems`, own servers included) or one of their repositories (`INVALID_REQUEST` for anything else); scanned by the vault before it is written (`VaultService.scan`: `MEMORY_REFUSED` and `memory.refused` with the names and formats found). A memory holds at most 200 active notes and `TINFIRM_MEMORY_MAX_CHARACTERS` characters (`MEMORY_FULL`, naming how full it is), and a trigger's at most 20 proposals (`MEMORY_REVIEW_FULL`). Every write takes the settings row's lock first (`MemoryStore.lock`); a note's number comes from `memory_next_number` and is never reused in its row.
- **The tools** (`MemoryTools`), server-executed, listed first in `run.assign.tools` while memory is in effect (the systems' tools after them, 200 in all), and answered apart from the systems' tools, each in one short transaction that reads the settings in effect again (`UNKNOWN_TOOL` once memory is off); `remember` and `forget` are journaled in the call journal (§15.14), by their run and request id, never on a note:

  | Tool | Arguments | Answers |
  |---|---|---|
  | `remember` | `note`; `about` (a system's handle or name, a repository's name or URL, `general`); `replaces` (`m{n}`, an active note) | `Saved as m{n}.`, `Saved m{n}.` (replaced in place), `m{n} says this already.` (the same words about the same, its use renewed), `Proposed as m{n}: …` (a trigger's); a request sent again answers as it was first answered, whatever became of the note since (replaced, forgotten, deleted, compacted), and writes nothing (the call journal, §15.14). Refusals: `INVALID_REQUEST`, `UNKNOWN_NOTE`, `MEMORY_REFUSED`, `MEMORY_FULL`, `MEMORY_REVIEW_FULL`, `MEMORY_OFF` |
  | `forget` | `note` (`m{n}`) | `Forgot m{n}.` or `There is no note m{n}.`, again as it was first answered when the request is sent again; in a trigger's memory only a proposal, an accepted note being its audience's to delete (`m{n} was accepted by the trigger's team, and only they delete it. …`) |
  | `recall` | `query` (1 to 200 characters) | At most 5 active notes, best first: the prefixes of up to 8 words of letters and digits, any matching (`to_tsquery('simple', …)` on `search`), then the most recently used; or `No note matches.` |

  Their names have no underscore, so no system's tool (`{handle}_{tool}`, §15.5) is named like them, and they are not platform tools. Their descriptions are not repeated in the prompt's `# Tools`. A call is audited by the agent: `memory.saved`, `memory.deleted` (`by` `AGENT`).
- **What reaches a run** (ADR-0063, decision 4), active notes only, each tier most recently used first within its budget (a note that does not fit is passed over for a shorter one), shown by number without times, and marked used: the full prompt's `# Memory` after the conversation's heading (`MemoryPrompt`: whose notes they are and that they are the agent's own words, not instructions; the general notes, 400 characters; the notes of the first 3 repositories of the workspace, 600 characters each, under `About the repository {name}:`; the count, and how to keep them), never the resume prompt; and a system's notes, 600 characters, appended as a text block of their own to the first `DONE` answer of that system's tools in the run (`SystemNotes`, from `RunRequests.call`; which names, systems and whether memory is in effect a run met is kept in the replica's memory until `RunEnded`, at most 5000 runs).
- **A trigger's notes are reviewed.** A trigger's conversation's `remember` writes a `PROPOSED` note (with `replaces_number` for a replacement); no tier reads it. Its audience accepts it (`memory.accepted`; the replaced note goes, the proposal becomes `ACTIVE`; `404` for no such proposal, `409 MEMORY_FULL` without room) or deletes it (`memory.deleted`, `state` `PROPOSED`). The inbox's `TRIGGER` item `MEMORY_REVIEW` (`count`, since the oldest proposal) is its author's and its team's while any waits; the retention sweep deletes a proposal after 30 days.
- **The model, on the company's own key** (ADR-0065, which replaced the platform credential of `V039`). The company's memory settings (`memory_settings.compaction_model`; `GET`, `PUT /memory-settings`, `MANAGE_MODEL_ACCESS`; the web app's Settings › Memory) name the model, one the company's own key allows (its own models, else the company's default; `PlatformModel.allows`; else `400 MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`), or none; audited as `memory_settings.changed`. `PlatformModel.complete` makes one chat request (`/v1/chat/completions`) to the company's LiteLLM with the company's own key (§15.4), revealed for it (`secret.accessed`, purpose `platform:{purpose}`), tagged `tinfirm-task:{purpose}`, outside any transaction, within 90 seconds, its answer read within 1 MiB (§15.4: a larger one is `FAILED`), and syncs the key after it; without a usable key it answers `Unavailable` (`GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `KEY_ERROR`, `BUDGET_SPENT`, `FAILED`). It never uses a person's or a trigger's payer.
- **Compaction** (`MemoryCompaction`, from the daily sweep, outside any transaction, as SYSTEM; at most 100 memories a company a day): each memory changed since its last compaction (`memory_changed_at`, `memory_compacted_at`), at most once in 30 days. First, under the row's lock, active notes about the same with the same words ignoring case, spaces and punctuation are one, the most recently used, with the latest use, the first save and every source's `learned_in`. Then, per `about` with 5 notes or more, one request on the company's own key to the memory settings' model (purpose `memory-compaction`): the notes as data, its answer JSON (`notes` with `from`, `dropped` with `by`), applied under the lock only when those notes did not change meanwhile and every given note is in exactly one `from` or dropped by a newer note that stays, there are no more notes and no more characters, and each note passes a write's checks (one line, 300 characters, the vault's scan); a new note takes the latest use and first save of its sources and where they were learned. Without a model, or with one the key no longer allows, only the first step runs and the memory counts as compacted; a key that does not answer leaves it due and ends the company's compaction for the day (its other memories would only wait for it too). A company's compaction starts no request 15 minutes after it starts (`MemoryCompaction.BUDGET`; each waits at most 90 seconds); the memories it did not reach stay due. The sweep queues each company it purged (`RetentionSweep.sweepDue`), and one virtual thread compacts them one at a time in that order (`MemoryCompactions`), never the scheduler's thread, which runs every fixed-delay job: a slow model holds up neither a purge, nor the next pass, nor the dispatcher, the heartbeats or the polls. A company waiting or being compacted is not queued again; after a restart its next sweep queues it. Audited as `memory.compacted`.
- **Retention, holds and erasure** (§15.11). `retention_settings.memory_days` (`Retention.memoryDays`, 730 by default, 30 to 3650; `0` in the API keeps notes; absent in a `PUT` keeps the period): the sweep deletes the notes not used (saved, shown or recalled) since then, and proposals after 30 days, but those of a held person, of a trigger a held person authors or is in the team of, or learned in a held conversation (`conversation_id` or `learned_in`); counted as `records.memoryNotes` in `content.purged`. An erasure of a person's conversations erases the notes learned in them, merged notes included (`erased.other.memoryNotes`), and its dry run counts them (`AgentMemory.countLearnedIn`, the same selection). A member who leaves, a deleted trigger and a deleted agent take their settings rows, and the notes with them.
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `conversation/internal/AgentMemoryIntegrationTest` | Opt-in and an agent that forbids memory; the tools listed first and their answers; once per request and per note, a request sent again answered from the call journal whatever became of its note (§15.14); `about` a system and nothing else; one line, 300 characters; replace, recall, forget; the person's page and its deletes; another person's notes never read; a credential and a company secret's value refused and audited without the note; a system's notes with the first result of its tools, once a run; the prompt's tiers within their budgets; a full memory; a trigger's notes proposed, read by no conversation, reviewed by its audience, `MEMORY_REVIEW` in the inbox |
  | `governance/internal/AgentMemoryGovernanceTest` | The memory settings' model: only one the company's own key allows, its audit, not allowed once the key's models narrow; the sweep's compaction on the company's own key (the request tagged and no principal's, the duplicate gone without a model, merges, a drop, where notes were learned, not again within 30 days); a model's answer that fails its checks changing nothing; an answer larger than the 1 MiB the server reads leaving the memory due, and the next sweep compacting it; a key that does not answer ending the company's compaction for the day, and no request started past its 15 minutes; `memoryDays`, kept when a change leaves it out; notes past it purged, a held person's kept; an erasure reaching a merged note |
  | `agent/internal/MemoryRulesTest`, `conversation/internal/MemoryPromptTest` | A note on one line; a recall's words, never `to_tsquery`'s operators; the compaction's fold; the section the same bytes for the same notes, the repositories in the workspace's order, a trigger's, and only in the full prompt |
  | `TenantIsolationTest` | The memory paths with another company's agent and trigger (`{noteNumber}`) |
  | `model/CompanyModelKeyTest`, and `ModelKeyLifecycleTest` and `ModelKeyRotationTest` (the company key among the keys) | The company's own key created with the gateway, its metadata, its own budget and models and back to the default, its spent budget, its audit without a principal; the sweep keeping it and deleting a stray key of its alias; its rotation on request and with every key |
  | `mcp/McpConversationToolsIntegrationTest` | Through a client: the person's notes as data, one deleted (and not twice), another person's never listed; a trigger's notes and proposals with the link to review them in Tinfirm, `NOT_FOUND` for an unknown trigger and at another company's endpoint; the tools' scopes and hints |
  | Web: `shared/memory-notes.spec`, `settings/memory-settings.spec`, `model-access/model-access.spec` (the company key's row and its dialog), and the specs of the settings form, the agent's draft, the settings, trigger and retention pages and the inbox's trigger item | The notes grouped by what they are about, in the agent's names; a note and every note deleted after asking; memory off, or forbidden by the agent's editor; a trigger's proposals accepted (409 `MEMORY_FULL` said) and rejected; the switches in the settings and in the agent's editor, which never widens; the memory settings' model among the company key's models, a model it no longer allows and its spent budget said, and none; `memoryDays` kept as 0; `MEMORY_REVIEW` opening the trigger's memory |

### 15.19 Skills (ADR-0064)

A company's library of skills in the Agent Skills format, pinned by agents and delivered to runs ([ADR-0064](adr/0064-skills-library.md); the exact rules are [the skills contract](reviews/2026-10-15-skills-contract.md)).

- **The module `skill`** stands on `company` (groups), `principal`, `vault` (the scan), `audit` and `shared`; it implements `principal.PrincipalDependents` (a leaving member's skills pass to the admins, their member shares go) and `company.GroupDependents` (a deleted group's shares go), first among each so that skills are locked before agents (§10's lock order). Its API: `Skills` (`find`, `findAll`, `version`, `versions`, `content`, `sees`, `lockForPins`), `SkillInfo`, `SkillVersionInfo`, `SkillEvents`; its port `SkillUsage` (`agentCounts`, the live agents that pin each skill; `removeFromAgents`, a delete's removal) is implemented by the agent module (`AgentSkillUsage`). `agent` stands on `skill` (an agent's pins, an editor's sight of a skill); `run` stands on `skill` (the runner's download).
- **Who sees and edits a skill** (`SkillCallers.access`): as an agent (§15.5), its owner, a member of a group it is shared with, a member it is shared with on their own, and OWNER and ADMIN (`MANAGE_AGENTS`) see it; its owner and OWNER and ADMIN edit it (a new version, its shares, its delete), anyone else who sees it gets `403 SKILL_NOT_EDITABLE`; anyone else `404`. A deleted skill is `404` in the library, listed only for admins with `?deleted=true`.
- **An upload** (`SkillBundle`, `SkillService`): exactly one of a zip (`file`) and a `SKILL.md`'s text (`skillMd`), else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`. The zip is read with the limits counted on what inflates (200 files, 5 MiB in all, 5 MiB uploaded: `400 SKILL_TOO_LARGE` with `details.limit`), its directory entries, macOS's `__MACOSX/` and `.DS_Store` skipped, one top folder stripped, and every path checked (`400 SKILL_INVALID`, `details.rule` `PATH`, `details.path`); the top folder is a segment of every path as uploaded and passes a segment's rules before it goes, so `../SKILL.md`, `./x`, `/x`, `C:/x` and `a\b/x` are refused, never normalised (`SkillBundle.stripTopFolder`); a link in the zip is stored as the file it is. The front matter takes only the specification's keys (`name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`) and is read with SnakeYAML's safe constructor, no duplicate keys, few aliases. The files are written again as the canonical zip (sorted, deflated, one fixed time, no extra fields), whose SHA-256 is the version's; the same files as the latest version (paths and each file's SHA-256) store nothing and answer `200`. The canonical zip is scanned (`VaultService.scan`, inside zips): any match or unscannable content is `422 SKILL_CONTAINS_CREDENTIAL` with `credentialMatches`, never acknowledged, and nothing is stored or audited. At most 100 versions (`409 SKILL_VERSION_LIMIT`). A version is never changed or removed (`skill_version`: `SELECT, INSERT` only).
- **An agent's skills** (`AgentSkills`, `PUT /agents/{agentId}/skills`): at most 20, each once; a pinned skill without `version` keeps its pin, with one moves to it (a live skill only, `400 SKILL_DELETED`); a skill that joins must be one the editor sees (`Skills.sees`, else `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`) and live, at its latest or at the version named; two skills of one name are `400 SKILL_NAME_CONFLICT`. Every skill named is locked `FOR KEY SHARE` before the agent's row and read live only then (`Skills.lockForPins`, §10's lock order), so a delete never misses a pin made while it runs. A change makes a version of the agent that widens nothing (`AgentVersions.next`, `AgentChanged`), audited as `agent.skills_changed` (`added`, `removed`, `moved`, `version`). `Agent.skills` shows them to whoever sees the agent; `AgentSummary.skillUpdates` counts the pins behind their skill's latest version, for its editors only. `GET /agents/{agentId}/skills/{skillId}/content` gives whoever sees the agent the pinned version's zip.
- **Settings** take `skillsOff` (at most 20, each a skill of the agent, else `400 SKILL_NOT_ON_AGENT`), stored in `agent_settings_skill`, whose foreign key to `agent_skill` refuses a switch of a skill that left the agent meanwhile (`SETTINGS_WIDEN`) and goes with it when it leaves. `EffectiveSettings.skills()` is the agent's pins less those switched off, by name, each with its version's name, hash and size; `skillsOff()` the switches.
- **Runs** (`RunDelivery`, `RunSkillStore`, `RunnerSkillsController`): `run.assign.skills` carries `EffectiveSettings.skills()`; the assignment's transaction replaces the run's `run_skill` rows. The dispatcher (`HostSelection.select` with `needsSkills`) sends a run with skills only to a connected host whose `hello.features` lists `run.skills` (`RunnerMessages.FEATURE_RUN_SKILLS`), and otherwise the run waits with `NO_HOST_FOR_SKILLS`. The host downloads `GET /api/runner/v1/runs/{runId}/skills/{skillId}/versions/{version}?epoch=` with its token: the run assigned to it with that epoch and active, and the version in its `run_skill`, else `404`.
- **Deleting a skill** locks its row `FOR UPDATE`, which waits for every change of an agent's skills that names it (§10's lock order). It keeps it in the agents that pin it, unless `?removeFromAgents=true`: then `SkillUsage.removeFromAgents` (`AgentSkillUsage`) takes it from every live agent that pins it, each locked in the order of its id after the skill's row, each change a version that widens nothing (`AgentVersions.next`, announced with no editor's settings following), audited as `agent.skills_changed` with `cause` `skill.deleted`; the settings switches of it go with the pin.
- **Audit** (never a skill's content): `skill.created`, `skill.version_added`, `skill.shares_changed` (`cause` `principal.left`, `group.deleted`), `skill.deleted` (`agentCount`, `removedFromAgents`), `skill.passed_to_admins`, `agent.skills_changed`; `settings.changed` lists `skillsOff`.
- **Tests.** `SkillBundleTest` (paths, limits read and not claimed, the top folder, the front matter, the canonical zip), `SkillIntegrationTest` (who sees and edits a skill on every path, uploads and versions, the credential scan, an agent's pins and their updates, a deleted skill, settings, a leaving owner), `SkillPinDeleteRaceTest` (a change of an agent's skills and a delete of one of them, one after the other), `SkillDependentsLockOrderTest` (a leaving and a group's deletion against a delete with `removeFromAgents`), `ConversationSkillsIntegrationTest` (`run.assign.skills` at the pinned versions less the switched-off ones, the download per run, epoch, version and host, `NO_HOST_FOR_SKILLS`), `HostSelectionTest`, `TenantIsolationTest` (every skill path of company B) and `CrossTenantBodyTest` (B's skill in A's agent and shares, B's run's skills through A's host).

### 15.20 The MCP endpoint on triggers, systems, webhooks and spend

A person's AI client reads how the company's automation is set up and whether it fires, as far as the member may see it, and tests a system it manages; it changes nothing else (the package 6 contract's later work; its open point 15 stands: no tool creates, edits, pauses or runs a trigger). The endpoint, its scopes and its results stay as §14.11 says.

- **One path for the app and the client.** Each tool calls the public API over the service of one REST operation, with its inputs, its DTO and its authorization: `TriggerReads` (`listTriggers`, `getTrigger`, `getTriggerSettings`, `listTriggerFirings`; `TriggerClientReads` over `TriggerService`), `WebhookReads` (`listWebhookDeliveries`, `tryWebhookMapping`; `WebhookService`, which `WebhooksController` calls too), `SystemReads` (`listSystems`, `getSystem`, `listSystemTools`, `testSystem`; `SystemClientReads` over `SystemService` and `CatalogueService`), `Spend.report` (`getSpend`'s JSON, with `Spend.Query`; `SpendQueryService`, which `SpendController` calls too).
- **Visibility, as the API's.** A trigger is seen by its author, its team, OWNER and ADMIN (T15); anyone else gets `NOT_FOUND`, never forbidden. Every member sees the company's systems and their own personal MCP servers; what only whoever manages a system sees (its app, who tested it, a webhook's mapping, rate and secret's age) stays theirs. A webhook's deliveries and the try of its mapping are OWNER's and ADMIN's (`MANAGE_SYSTEMS`, `FORBIDDEN` otherwise); a system's test is whoever manages it. Spend is the caller's scope of §15.12 (G1).
