- **Tools.** `tinfirm:read`: `list_triggers` (`TriggerReads.Filter`: system, agent, author, team, status, account holder; paged), `get_trigger` (`{"trigger", "settings"}`, `settings` absent when the agent's settings cannot be read), `list_trigger_firings`, `list_systems`, `get_system`, `list_system_tools`, `list_webhook_deliveries` (10 a page, at most 20: a body may be 1 MiB and stays whole in `structuredContent`), `try_webhook_delivery` (the body as a JSON object; nothing stored or fired), `get_spend` (dates, zone, up to two dimensions, bucket, filters; the CSV, which the API audits, has no tool). `tinfirm:write`: `test_system`, not destructive: the system's last test is all it records.
- **Why a trigger would not fire.** `WebhookMapping.mismatch` is the one rule the receiver and the try ask: a trigger's events first (`EVENT`: the mapped event, the events it takes), then its conditions in their order (`CONDITION`: its index, pointer, op, values, and what the pointer reads, cut to 500 characters, `actual`); `matches` is its absence. The try's `WebhookMappedTrigger` adds the trigger's `status`, `pausedReason` and `waitingReason`: a trigger that is paused or waits takes no delivery, whatever it matches (the gate).
- **No secret.** No tool answers a webhook's secret, a token or an app's client secret: the DTOs never carry them, and `get_system` drops `webhook.secret` from its structured content whatever the DTO holds. The text says a secret is kept, never what it is.
- **Renderings** (`SetupRenderings`). Tinfirm's ids, kinds, states, outcomes, times and counts are plain lines; every other field is in a labelled block (§14.11). A delivery's body in the text is cut to 2,000 characters; `structuredContent` holds it whole.
- **Tests.** `McpSetupToolsIntegrationTest`: a webhook system, an MCP server with a catalogue and an unreachable one, a webhook trigger fired by one signed delivery; each tool with a personal access token and two with an OAuth grant, against the REST operation's answer; a member who is neither author nor team `NOT_FOUND` on the trigger and its firings; `FORBIDDEN` for a member on the deliveries, the try and a company system's test; the try's `mismatch` (`CONDITION` with `actual`, `EVENT`) equal to the REST answer's, nothing stored; a failed test that says why and is the system's last test; the webhook's secret in no text or structured content; the trigger's instruction, the bodies and the tools' descriptions inside labelled blocks. `McpConversationToolsIntegrationTest`: the new tools in `tools/list`, the write scope of `test_system`, and the tenant test over every one (B's trigger and system `NOT_FOUND` at B's endpoint and at A's). `WebhookVerifierTest`: the first condition that fails and what the body holds there.

### 15.21 Error reports (ADR-0070)

The installation's own errors, scrubbed when captured, grouped, shown to instance admins and delivered to the vendor's error tracker only while delivery is automatic ([ADR-0070](adr/0070-error-reports.md); the operator's guide is [operations/error-reports.md](operations/error-reports.md)).

- **The module `diagnostics`** stands on `shared` and `audit` and implements `shared.config.TelemetryStatus`, which `SystemInfo.telemetryEnabled` reads (true exactly while delivery is automatic with a destination). Nothing depends on it; its internals are in `diagnostics.internal`.
- **Capture.** `ErrorLogCapture` attaches a Logback appender to the root logger (started first, stopped last): every log event at `ERROR` of any logger but the module's own (`com.flowtivo.tinfirm.diagnostics.`) is captured on the logging thread, never throws, never logs, and ignores an error raised while it captures. `ServerErrors` keeps the logger, the log line's template masked (its `{}` placeholders unfilled, never its arguments), at most 5 exceptions of the cause chain with at most 40 frames each (`inApp` for `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.`), the Java version, and the request's route pattern (`GET /api/companies/{companyId}/...`, from `HandlerMapping.BEST_MATCHING_PATTERN_ATTRIBUTE`) when logged in a request. The web app's `ReportingErrorHandler` (Angular's `ErrorHandler`, which still logs to the console) posts each error to `POST /api/client-errors` (any session, CSRF), except an `HttpErrorResponse` and a stale chunk, at most 10 a page load and one per kind a minute, without the auth interceptor's redirect; the server takes at most `tinfirm.error-reports.per-user-minute` (20) a minute from one user, from their first, and answers `204` in every case, a dropped one and the mode `OFF` included. `ClientErrors` keeps a type of the shape of a JavaScript name (else `Error`), the masked message, a route pattern in the shape of the web app's routes, `/` or up to 12 segments each lowercase with dashes, a `:param` or `**` (else none), and frames as their file, line and column: a file is kept when it is one of the web app's own bundles on the instance's own origin (`TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL`; `/main-<hash>.js`, `/chunk-<hash>.js`, `/polyfills-…`, `/scripts-…`, the hash 8 characters, none in development; `inApp`), else `<external>`, and a frame's function is never kept (a minified name says nothing; the tracker restores names from source maps); the browser's name and major version from `User-Agent`. So the browser can put no text of its own where only code is expected, beyond the masked message.
- **Scrubbing** (`Scrubber`): in a message and a log line's template, in this order, URLs, emails, UUIDs, quoted strings (from a line's first quote to its last), IP addresses, file paths, hexadecimal values, runs of 16 or more letters and digits with a digit and a letter, or with a key's shape, letters alone included: a short prefix of up to three parts and `-` or `_` before 16 or more letters and digits (`sk-…`, `sk-proj-…`, `github_pat_…`), after an assignment too (`apiKey=sk-…`) (`<token>`; other runs of letters alone, as a snake_case constraint's or a kebab-case name, are code), and numbers of 4 digits or more are masked; control characters go, whitespace is folded, and the text is cut to 300 characters. Only what was scrubbed is stored: nothing of the request (URL, query, headers, cookies, body), no user, company or id of Tinfirm's objects, no IP address.
- **Grouping.** A fingerprint (SHA-256, hex): for the server, the source, the logger, the masked template and each exception's type with its first five frames of Tinfirm's code (of any code when none is), class and method without line numbers, a lambda's or a generated class's number dropped; for the web app, the type, the masked message and the route. `ErrorCapture` holds what a replica captured in memory, at most `max-pending` (200) kinds of error with their newest sample, count, first and last time; nothing in the mode `OFF`. `ErrorFlush` adds it to `error_report` every `flush-tick` (30 s) in one transaction, in the order of the fingerprints (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT (fingerprint) DO UPDATE`: the count added; the sample, what describes the error, the last version and commit only from occurrences at least as recent as the row's, the first version only from older ones, so that a replica's late flush does not put back an older sample); what it could not store waits for the next flush. A `worker` replica then deletes the reports not seen for `keep` (90 days) and the least recently seen beyond `max-reports` (1000). The sample is at most 64 KiB as written: a larger one keeps fewer frames.
- **The mode** (`ErrorReportingSettings`): `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS` when set, never read from the database; else `error_reporting.automatic` (`LOCAL` until an instance admin chooses), read again at most every 30 seconds. `PUT /api/admin/error-reporting` refuses a change while the operator set the mode (`409 ERROR_REPORTING_SET_BY_OPERATOR`) and `automatic: true` without a DSN (`409 ERROR_REPORTING_NO_DESTINATION`). The row is made at the first read, with the installation's random id (UUIDv7, never changed). A wrong DSN, label or proxy stops the server at startup; `automatic` without a DSN logs a warning and delivers nothing.
- **Delivery** (`ErrorDelivery`, `SentryTransport`, `SentryEvents`): on `worker` replicas, every `delivery-tick` (15 minutes, the first `delivery-initial-delay`, 2 minutes, after the start), while delivering, on a virtual thread of its own; one pass at a time across replicas, by the ShedLock lock `error-reports.delivery` (`shedlock`, `V047__shedlock.sql`; on the database's clock, held by the replica's host name): `delivery-lease` (10 minutes) from its take, extended at each report sent, so that a slow destination does not let a second pass in, the lock of a pass whose server crashed expires by itself, and a pass whose lock expired or another replica holds stops and releases nothing. A `429`'s `Retry-After` keeps every pass back (`retry_after`, at most 6 hours, a minute without one). A pass takes at most `delivery-batch` (100) reports with occurrences not yet sent, the longest waiting first, and sends each as one Sentry event in an envelope to `<DSN's origin and path>/api/<project>/envelope/` with `X-Sentry-Auth` (the DSN's public key), outside any transaction (the transport refuses one), without redirects, through `TINFIRM_ERROR_REPORTS_PROXY` when set. The event's id follows from the fingerprint and the count, so a resend is one event at the tracker; its `fingerprint` is the report's. Each sent report records its count (`reported_occurrences`) and time; the mode is read again before each send, so turning delivery off stops a running pass (at the latest once the replica's view of the admin's choice is fresh, 30 seconds); the first failure ends the pass, and the pass records its time and reason (`last_delivery_error`, cleared by the next pass that sends every report it took).
- **The event** holds the report's fields and nothing else: `platform` (`java`, `javascript`), `logger`, `release` (`tinfirm@<version>`), `transaction` (the route), the exceptions in Sentry's order (the outermost last, the innermost frame last) or the template as `message`, `tags` (`source`, `instance`, `label`, `commit`), `extra` (the counts, the first time and version, the template), the runtime's or browser's context, and `server_name` (the label, else the instance id). The download (`GET /api/admin/error-reports/export`, `ErrorReportExport`, `format` `tinfirm-error-reports/1`) holds these very events for every report, in every mode.
- **The instance admins' paths** (tag `ErrorReports`): `GET /api/admin/error-reports`, the reports, the most recently seen first, in pages (`limit`, `cursor`); `GET /api/admin/error-reports/{reportId}`, one report with its newest sample's exceptions and frames; `DELETE /api/admin/error-reports/{reportId}`, a report deleted once its error is understood, audited as `error_report.deleted` (the error starts a new report when it happens again); the download above; and `GET`, `PUT /api/admin/error-reporting`, the mode, its destination and its last delivery.
- **Problem codes:** `ERROR_REPORTING_SET_BY_OPERATOR` and `ERROR_REPORTING_NO_DESTINATION` (409). The paths under `/api/admin/` are instance admins' (the security chain and the services check it); `POST /api/client-errors` needs a session, nothing more.
- **Tests.** `ScrubberTest`, `DsnTest`, `ClientErrorsTest`, `ServerErrorsTest`, `SentryEventsTest`, `ErrorReportingSettingsTest`, and `ErrorReportsIntegrationTest` (a server error and a web error as scrubbed reports, the rate limit, who may read and post, automatic delivery to the stub tracker of `support/SentryStub` once per report, a failed delivery on the page, the download and a deletion). Web: `core/error-reports.spec` (stacks, routes, the limits, what is never reported, no redirect on `401`) and `pages/admin/error-reports/error-reports.spec`.

### 15.22 Automatic retries (ADR-0073)

A turn that failed for a reason that may pass is retried by itself when the settings' owner chose so ([ADR-0073](adr/0073-automatic-retries.md)): a few times in a row, with a growing wait, through the run queue.

- **The setting.** `agent_settings.auto_retry` (`AgentSettings.autoRetry` and `AgentSettingsRequest.autoRetry`, false by default and when absent; V051): a person's for their conversations with the agent, a trigger's author's for the trigger's; saved as any field of the settings (§15.5), audited in `settings.changed` or `trigger.settings_changed` as `autoRetry`, and back to false when the settings return to the defaults. `EffectiveSettings.autoRetry()`. The MCP endpoint's `get_trigger` says "Automatic retries: on" or "off" (§15.20); `get_agent`'s readiness carries it in the settings.
- **What is retried** (`TurnService.mayPass`). A turn whose run ended `FAILED` with the class `SERVER_LIMITED`, or `LOST` with the cause `HOST_RESTARTED` or `STALE_ON_RECONNECT`; never `LEASE_EXPIRED`, whose host may take the run back within `TINFIRM_RUNNER_LOST_RESUME_WINDOW` (`turnResumed` refuses while another run is active), nor any other class. Only when the conversation is open, has no open decision, no trigger's firing and no person's message waiting (the turn that takes it starts at once, as after any failed turn, §15.7), its trigger is not paused, and the settings in effect (`Settings.effective` of the conversation's owner) have `autoRetry`.
- **How often.** At most `TurnService.AUTO_RETRIES` (3) in a row; the `n`th waits `TurnService.waitBefore(n)`: 1, 2, 4 minutes from the failure. `conversation.auto_retries` is the latest turn's number in the row (`ConversationStore.turnStarted`): an automatic retry's, else 0, so any other turn (a person's message, retry or decision, a firing) counts from nothing again. Not configurable.
- **The retry.** In the transaction that ends the failed run (`TurnEnded.turnEnded`, under the conversation's lock), before the hooks and `TurnRecorded`: `TurnService` starts a `RETRY` turn through the one path of every turn (`request`), with `createdBy` null and a `NewTurn.Scheduled` (its time, its number, the limit). `Turns.start` inserts its run `QUEUED` with `dispatch_after` at that time and the queue reason `RETRY_SCHEDULED` (`QueueReason.retryScheduled`: `retryAt`, `used`, `limit`; "The previous turn failed for a reason that may pass; Tinfirm retries it by itself (automatic retry 1 of 3).") since its creation; `run_dispatch_queue` skips it until then, and the dispatcher treats it as any queued run after. `NewTurn` refuses a schedule on any turn but the system's `RETRY`. It takes the failed run's request (`ConversationStore.retake`), resumes the session and is prompted as a person's retry (§15.7). `run.started` is audited with the actor SYSTEM and `automatic`, `attempt`, `retryAt`. The conversation waits `RUNNER` with the reason `RETRY_SCHEDULED`; `/retry` answers `TURN_ACTIVE`, and **Cancel** ends the queued run `CANCELLED` as any queued turn (§15.6). A message written meanwhile goes with it when its host takes steers (ADR-0060), else waits for the next turn, as for any queued turn.
- **The note.** The failed turn's `SYSTEM` message ends "Tinfirm retries it by itself in 1 minute (automatic retry 1 of 3); cancel the turn to stop it." (2 minutes, 4 minutes for the next) instead of "Retry the turn, or say what to do next."; the failure that follows the last ends "Tinfirm retried it by itself 3 times in a row; retry the turn, or say what to do next."; it says so only once the retry's run is there. `TurnService.failureMessage`.
- **The place.** `ConversationEvents.TurnRecorded.retrying` is true for a failed turn whose retry started: the trigger module's `OutboxQueue` queues neither its `FAILURE` comment nor its `ON_FAILURE` actions, and the chat module's `ChatOutbox` posts no `FAILURE`; the open questions of its run are still written. The failure that ends the retries is written as any (its run's cause keys).
- **The runner** classes a provider's or gateway's unavailability as `SERVER_LIMITED` (`limits::classify_error`, after every other rule): an HTTP status 500, 502, 503 or 504 standing as a number of its own, the wordings of such an answer (`internal server error`, `bad gateway`, `service unavailable`, `gateway timeout`, `no available server`, `no healthy upstream`, `upstream connect error`), and Claude Code's error kind `server_error`.
- **Tests.** `AutomaticRetryIntegrationTest` (off by default; chosen and audited; a `SERVER_LIMITED` failure retried as a scheduled `RETRY` that waits for its time, after 1, 2 and 4 minutes, with its notes, queue reason and audit; spent after three; a person's retry counting from nothing; a cancel; a `PROTOCOL_ERROR` and a waiting message starting none), `AutomaticRetryOutboxIntegrationTest` (a trigger's settings through the API, audited; no failure comment or on-failure action while retried; both once a retry fails for a reason that does not pass), `TurnFailureMessageTest` (the notes, `mayPass`, the waits), the runner's `limits::tests::classifies_an_unavailable_provider_or_gateway`; the web app's settings form and queue reason specs.

### 15.23 Credentials on systems (ADR-0072)

The company's tokens and sign-ins on a company system, which the system holds and lends to groups and to service accounts ([ADR-0072](adr/0072-system-credentials-lent-to-groups-and-service-accounts.md), both its phases built together; `V049__system_credentials.sql`, `V050__triggers_follow_lendings.sql`; the operator's side is [operations/accounts.md](operations/accounts.md)). The account module keeps them (`Credentials`, `CredentialsController`, beside `AccountService`, through which they connect, sign in and go as a member's own account does). What they share with members' own accounts (I2, the binding rule and its check, a reveal, refresh, revocation) is §15.3; §15.1 holds service accounts, §15.15 a trigger's table, §15.16 a chat system's bot.

- **A credential** (decision 2). An `account` row that no principal holds (`principal_id` null, `principal_kind` `SYSTEM`), on a company system only, never a personal server (`account_ck`; the API's `400 PERSONAL_SYSTEM`). Its name is 1 to 100 characters without control characters, unique on its system ignoring case (`account_name_uq`; `409 DUPLICATE_CREDENTIAL_NAME`); its kind `TOKEN`, `OAUTH` or, on Jira Cloud, `CLIENT` (`account_holder_ck`; below); its secret a managed company secret (`SYSTEM_CREDENTIAL`, I3, §15.3); who it is at the system, from the system's "who am I" (`external_id`, `login`, `api_base_url`); its status `OK`, `RECONNECT` or `ERROR` with the last error; whether it is enabled; when it was connected and by whom (`connected_by`, the user who stored or signed it in last), and its last use. A system holds any number, and two may be one user (I2).
- **The API** (tag `Credentials`; a credential is shown with its status and identity, never its secret):

  | Endpoint | Permission | Does |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /systems/{systemId}/credentials` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` (OWNER, ADMIN) | The system's credentials by name, each with its status, `login`, `enabled`, `connectedByName`, last use, whether it is the chat system's bot (`bot`), the groups it is lent to with their access (`grants`) and the service accounts that borrow it, with their status, the access and the live triggers that act with it through each (`serviceAccounts`, counted by the port `CredentialUses.liveTriggers`, which the trigger module implements); and how many members hold their own account on the system, without who (`memberAccounts`: `ok`, `notOk`) |
  | `POST …/credentials` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | A token as a new credential, `{name, token}`, checked with the system's "who am I" as a member's token is (§15.3) and lent to nobody yet; `201`, audited as `account.connected` |
  | `POST …/credentials/oauth` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | A sign-in for a new credential, `{name, returnTo}`: the admin signs in at the system as the user the credential should be, with the system's app and the one callback (§15.3; `name_taken` when another credential took the name meanwhile) |
  | `POST …/credentials/client` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | An Atlassian service account's OAuth 2.0 credential as a new `CLIENT` credential on a Jira Cloud system, `{name, clientId, clientSecret}` (below); `201`, audited as `account.connected` |
  | `PUT …/credentials/{credentialId}/token`, `POST …/credentials/{credentialId}/oauth`, `PUT …/credentials/{credentialId}/client` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | A new token, a sign-in again, or new client credentials: the credential keeps its id, name, grants and lendings and is `OK` again (`account.reconnected`), so rotating a token changes no binding and no trigger |
  | `PATCH …/credentials/{credentialId}` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | Renames it, disables or enables it (`credential.changed`, with `systemId`, `name`, what `changed` and `enabled`); neither is `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, and a request that changes nothing is not audited |
  | `DELETE …/credentials/{credentialId}` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | Removes it: its grants (`grant.deleted`), then its lendings (`credential.withdrawn`), then the credential with its secret (`account.disconnected`), its OAuth grant revoked where the system offers revocation (§15.3); `204` |
  | `PUT …/credentials/{credentialId}/service-accounts/{serviceIdentityId}` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | Lends it to the service account, `{access}`, `READ` or `WRITE`, in place of what the service account borrowed on the system, or changes the access (below) |
  | `DELETE …/credentials/{credentialId}/service-accounts/{serviceIdentityId}` | `MANAGE_SYSTEMS` | Withdraws it (`credential.withdrawn`); `404` when the service account does not borrow it; `204` |
  | `GET /service-identities/{serviceIdentityId}/credentials` | `MANAGE_SERVICE_IDENTITIES` | What a service account borrows, by system name (§15.1) |

  The writes refuse as a member's connect does (`400 ACCOUNT_REFUSED`, `409 IDENTITY_IN_USE` with `details.holder` `MEMBER`, `409 SYSTEM_PAUSED`, `409 OAUTH_NOT_AVAILABLE`; `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID` on a chat or webhook system, but a Slack sign-in for `history`, §15.17), and every write on the chat system's bot is `409 BOT_ACCOUNT_IN_USE`: it changes only with the bot (§15.16). A personal server holds no credential: to its owner `400 PERSONAL_SYSTEM`, to anyone else `404`. A token is never in an answer, the audit or a log.
- **Client credentials** ([ADR-0093](adr/0093-jira-cloud-service-accounts-by-client-credentials.md), `V065`). On Jira Cloud only (`SystemKind.clientCredentials`; else `400 SYSTEM_KIND_INVALID`, as for a member's own account), a credential may be an OAuth client's own credentials (`CLIENT`): an Atlassian service account's, with the `client_credentials` grant at `https://auth.atlassian.com/oauth/token` (`audience` `api.atlassian.com`), without the system's app, a sign-in or a refresh token. Its secret holds `{"clientId","clientSecret"}` and the token minted last (`ClientCredentials`, its members those of `OAuthTokens`). Connecting mints a token, reads the site's cloud id from its public `/_edge/tenant_info` and asks `myself` through Atlassian's gateway (`SystemCalls.clientToken`, `clientIdentity`; `api_base_url` the gateway); a refusal of either is `400 ACCOUNT_REFUSED`. A reveal mints the next token under the refresh lock when the last expires within the margin (§15.3); a refused client turns it `RECONNECT`, a token minted while it was connected again or removed is never used, and an answer without `expires_in` is refused. Nothing is revoked on removal.
- **Disabling** (decision 8). A disabled credential acts for nobody, through groups and service accounts alike, from its next check until it is enabled again: the binding rule and the check read it as one an admin must fix (`RECONNECT_COMPANY`; a conversation's binding waits with `credentialEnabled` false and the admins' inbox names it, §15.7), a reveal answers nothing (§15.3), and a trigger that acts with it pauses `ACCOUNT_BROKEN` until its author resumes it (§15.15). Disabling and enabling are announced as `AccountEvents.CredentialChanged`, on which conversations' bindings, triggers and polls are looked at again after the commit. Pausing a system stops every credential on it (`SYSTEM_PAUSED`, §15.13); suspending a service account stops what acts through it, while groups' grants of the same credential go on (§15.1). The chat system's bot is disabled only by removing the bot.
- **Lending to service accounts** (decision 5, open point 1; `service_account_credential`, §7). A service account borrows at most one credential per system (the primary key is the service account and the system), `READ` or `WRITE`; a credential may be lent to several. A lending's access changes in place, and lending another credential on the system replaces the row (`Credentials.lendTo`; the same credential at the same access again does nothing): audited as `credential.lent` with `accountId`, `systemId`, `serviceAccountId`, `access` and, for a replacement, `replaced` (the credential it borrowed there before); announced as `CredentialChanged` for each credential, and for a replacement as `LendingReplaced` in the lending's transaction, on which the trigger module rewrites the rows of the triggers that acted with the replaced credential (§15.15). Withdrawing is `credential.withdrawn`, with a `cause` when nobody asked for it (`principal.deleted`, `account.disconnected` for a removed credential, `system.deleted`, `system.bot_removed`). A lending goes with its service account and with its credential (`ON DELETE CASCADE` below the explicit withdrawals). `READ` is enforced as a grant's `READ` is: a write tool is refused (`TOOL_DENIED`, §15.7), the workspace's repositories are read-only (`WorkspaceService`), and the trigger editor checks its open writes against it (`TriggerEditor`); `Accounts.fixedAccess` answers a row's access (`WRITE` for the author's own account; the lending's, `READ` once the service account no longer borrows it, which its next check stops).
- **Triggers** (decision 6, open point 3 as decided again; §15.15). A trigger's row names its author's own account, or a service account the author may use with the credential lent to it then (`TriggerAccountInput.holderPrincipalId`, which may be left out when exactly one service account the author may use borrows the credential); the database keeps a row to its holder's own account or the credential its service account borrows (`trigger_account_ck`). `Accounts.check` requires that the credential is still lent to that service account, `OK` and enabled. A replaced lending is followed: the row names the new credential in the lending's transaction (`trigger.accounts_changed` with `why` `lending.replaced`), its open conversations bind it at their next check (`conversation.binding.bound` with `followed`), and its author is told; a withdrawn credential pauses the trigger `NOT_ENTITLED` until its author names another. The service account's key and budget pay, as before. A credential's poll has no holder, so it serves the triggers of every service account that borrows it.
- **Chat bots** (decision 9, open point 2; §15.16). A chat system's bot is a credential on that system (a new one named `Bot`), lent `WRITE` to its service account and to no group, set, replaced and removed only with `PUT` and `DELETE /systems/{id}/bot`. I9: at most one per system, a credential lent to a service account.
- **Reading tools and testing** (decision 10; §15.2). `POST /systems/{id}/tools/refresh` and `POST /systems/{id}/test` take an optional `credentialId`; without it they use the caller's own `OK` account, else the system's only enabled `OK` credential, else none. A server that asks for a token when none was given was reached: the system stays `OK`, and the refresh answers `credentialRequired`.
- **The pages** (decision 11). Members keep connecting their own accounts on the Accounts page (§15.3). The system's page counts them (`memberAccounts`) and adds a credential with **Add credential** (a name, then "Paste a token", "Sign in to {system}" where the system's app offers it, or on Jira Cloud "Service account (OAuth 2.0)"), lent with **Lend to a group** and **Lend to a service account**; a service account's page lists what it borrows, read-only.
- **The migration** (decision 12, `V049`, one and lossless): every service identity's account became a credential on its system, named after the service identity (its name cut to 100 characters), keeping its secret (whose name stays `SERVICE_ACCOUNT`), identity, status and dates, lent `WRITE` to that service identity; its grants, a chat system's bot and the triggers' rows keep naming it; a binding through a grant lost its holder, and a credential's poll its holder; members' accounts did not move; pending sign-ins were dropped. `V050` made a live trigger's row whose service account borrowed another credential by then name that one, except where that one is a chat system's bot or a tracker trigger's source row would name a credential without an identity. Audit events written before keep their names.
- **The module API** (§10). `Accounts.lentTo` (the credential a service account borrows on a system, with its access), `borrowedBy`, `borrowers` (the service accounts that borrow a credential, oldest lending first), `fixedAccess`, `bot`, `credentialsConnectedBy` (the leaver's checklist, §15.13); the system module's port `SystemAccounts`, which the account module implements, adds `credentialName`, `credentialOf`, `onlyUsableCredential`, `revealCredential` and `holderOf` (the member whose own account it is, or the service account that borrows a credential); the port `CredentialUses`; the events `AccountEvents.CredentialChanged` and `LendingReplaced`.
- **Audit** (§8). A credential's `account.connected`, `account.reconnected`, `account.disconnected` and `account.refused` carry `credential` (its name) in place of `principalId`; `credential.changed`, `credential.lent` and `credential.withdrawn` as above; `grant.*` name the credential (`accountId`); `trigger.accounts_changed` with `why` `lending.replaced` (§15.15); `conversation.binding.bound` with `followed` (§15.7). Never a token.
- **Tests.** `AccountIntegrationTest` (credentials added, lent to groups and service accounts, I2 naming the credential, two credentials of one user, disabled, withdrawn, a deleted service account's lendings withdrawn with the credential kept, removed), `AccountConstraintTest` (I2 between members and credentials, I5, one lending per service account and system, no credential on a personal server, the bot a credential), `AccountBindingTest` (a trigger's row through a service account that borrows the credential), `CredentialRevealTest` (a credential revealed through a service account only while it is active and borrows it), `SignInIntegrationTest` (an admin signing a credential in; a member told the user is a shared credential), `CredentialMigrationTest` (V049), `LendingFollowMigrationTest` (V050), `TriggerIntegrationTest` (a row through a service account, `READ`, followed, withdrawn), `ConversationBindingTest` (a disabled credential), `SystemIntegrationTest` (a catalogue and a test with a credential), `ChatSystemsIntegrationTest` (the bot as a credential), `DepartureIntegrationTest` (the credentials a leaver connected), `InboxIntegrationTest` (the credential the admins fix), `ToolRulesTest` (a `READ` lending refusing a write), and `TenantIsolationTest` over every path.

### 15.24 The licence: editions, keys and seats (ADR-0077)

The licence of [ADR-0077](adr/0077-licence-editions-and-seats.md): one image runs as Community, Pro or Enterprise by the key it holds, and a seat follows the role: the key, the edition and its state, the seats and the role VIEWER (decisions 3, 5 and 6), the gates of decision 4 (`403 EDITION_REQUIRED`), expiry (decision 7) and Community's retention (decision 8). Also built: SCIM (§15.25) and SAML (§15.26). Planned: SIEM, high availability and air-gapped bundles, which do not exist yet.

- **Where it lives.** `ee/` (decision 1): `ee/src/main/java`, which the server's build compiles in as it does `libs/*` (build-helper; its tests in `ee/src/test/java`, its resources in `ee/src/main/resources`), so that every image holds it. The licence is the package `com.flowtivo.tinfirm.ee`, module `ee`. The core reads it through `shared.licence` only: the port `Licence`, which `ee` implements, and `Licensing`, which answers Community (nothing paid, no seat counted) without it; `ModularityTest` holds that no module depends on `ee`. The runner host's paid code is under `ee/runner/` ([ADR-0100](adr/0100-elastic-runners-in-pro.md)), its manifests in `runner/`, built into the one binary.
- **The paid features' code** (decision 1, amended 2026-10-21). The server code that only a paid feature runs, the planned Enterprise features' (`SAML`, `SCIM`, `SIEM`, `HA`, `AIR_GAPPED`) included when they are built, is under `ee/` too, in the package of the module it belongs to, so that it uses that module's internals as the module's own code does and Spring Modulith counts it in that module. The core reaches it only through an interface of its own in that package, found when used (`ObjectProvider`): without `ee/` there is no implementation, and the core answers as Community, `403 EDITION_REQUIRED` after the caller's access check. The implementation checks the licence in force (`Licensing`). The core checks the caller's access first, in its own transaction, and the implementation runs in that transaction; the Outlook add-in's manifest checks it itself, and the core checks it only to answer Community without `ee/`. `ModularityTest` holds that no class outside `ee/` names one under it, and that no class of the core names `Feature` but the limits that stay in the core and the fallbacks that answer Community without `ee/`, each listed in the test (`onlyTheCoresKnownGatesNameAFeature`): a new gate in the core fails the build until its code moves under `ee/`, or the list is extended on purpose. SQL that only a paid feature runs is under `ee/` too (`RetentionWrites`, `AuditToolsStore`); the stores of the core keep what every edition reads, sweeps, lists and lifts.

  | Core interface | Under `ee/` | The paid feature's code |
  |---|---|---|
  | `audit.internal.AuditTools` | `AuditToolsImpl`, `AuditFilteredEvents`, `AuditChainVerifier`, `AuditExport`, `AuditCheckResults`, and the SQL only they run, `AuditToolsStore` (an export's range by time, a check's acknowledgement) | `AUDIT_TOOLS`: the filters and their SQL, the verification on demand, the export and its stream, the checks' list, their acknowledgement and the open failure the inbox shows |
  | `governance.internal.RetentionChanges` | `RetentionChangesImpl`, and `RetentionWrites`, the SQL only it and `LegalHoldsImpl` run (the periods saved, a hold inserted) | `RETENTION_SETTINGS`: `PUT /retention`, `POST /retention/preview` |
  | `governance.internal.LegalHolds` | `LegalHoldsImpl` | `LEGAL_HOLDS`: `POST /holds`, and `GET /holds/conversations/{key}`, which finds a conversation to hold |
  | `mcp.internal.OutlookAddIn` | `OutlookManifests` | `OUTLOOK_ADDIN`: `GET /outlook/manifest` |
  | `mcp.internal.GmailAddOn`; `system.GmailMessages` | `GmailAddOnImpl`, `GmailCards`, `GmailEmails`, `GmailReplies`, `GmailDeployments`, `GmailLinking`; `GmailMessagesImpl` | `GMAIL_ADDON`: the cards that answer Google's verified requests, and all they do (the open email read with its attachments, conversations started and followed, drafts saved); `GET /gmail/deployment`; `POST /gmail/links/read` and `/confirm`; the Gmail API's calls (`GmailMessages`) |
  | `run.internal.SessionBundles` | `SessionBundlesImpl`, `SessionBundleRows` (a bundle found and stored), `RunnerSessionBundleController`, `BundleCipher` | `ELASTIC_RUNNERS`: `run.assign.sessionBundle`, the host's upload and download of a bundle; deleting bundles stays the core's (`SessionBundleCleanup`) |
  | `runner.internal.RunnerPools` | `RunnerPoolsImpl`, `PoolSchedule`, `RunnerPoolStore` (a pool's settings and the count sent) | `ELASTIC_RUNNERS`: a pool's settings in `PATCH /runner-hosts/{id}`, the schedule's evaluation and its minute's tick, a host's `pool`; what a host reports stays the core's |
  | `identity.internal.SamlLogin` | `identity.internal.saml`: `SamlLoginImpl`, `SamlRegistrations`, `SamlAuthnRequests`, `SamlResponseChecks`, `SamlDecryption`, `SamlAlgorithms`, `SamlPerson`, `SamlAssertionsSeen`, `SamlMetadataRefresh`, `SamlMetadataStore`, `SamlIdpMetadata`, `SamlGuardFilter`, `SamlSweep` | `SAML`: the start, the ACS and the SAML buttons of the login page (§15.26) |

  The rest of decision 4's gates are limits on what the core does in every edition, so they stay checks in the core: the audit's history (`AUDIT_FULL_HISTORY`, `AuditPages.withinHistory`), the declared companies (`MULTI_COMPANY`), the Outlook add-in's calls to the MCP endpoint (`McpRequestFilter`), the verification of Google's requests to the Gmail add-on (`GmailAddOnController`, `GoogleIdTokens`, with `GmailCalls.googleKeys`), which answers without `ee/` a card that says the add-on needs Pro, the retention periods in force (`RetentionPolicy`), and a runner host's `hello` that lists `sandbox.bridge` (`RunnerWebSocketHandler`, ADR-0100). So are the daily check of the audit chains, which runs in every edition, and the holds that exist, which the sweep and erasures keep and which are listed and lifted in every edition; and a person's links of Google accounts, which they list and end in every edition (`GmailLinks`). The web app is MIT, its pages of the paid features included (decision 1).
- **The key** (decision 6). `TINFIRM1.<base64url(payload)>.<base64url(signature)>`, the signature Ed25519 over the payload's bytes as the key carries them; the payload `{"v":2, "kid", "id", "rev", "customer", "edition":"PRO"|"ENTERPRISE", "seats": int|null, "companies": int|null, "startsAt":"YYYY-MM-DD", "endsAt":"YYYY-MM-DD"}`, unknown fields refused; version 1, without `kid`, was never issued and is refused. A Pro key holds a seat count and no count of companies; an Enterprise key may hold either. `kid` names the FLOWTIVO public key it is signed with, one of those the server ships with (`ee/src/main/resources/ee/licence-public-keys/<kid>.pub`, X.509 PEM; `kid` up to 64 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`): a key whose `kid` the server does not trust is refused, and the signature is verified against that key alone, offline, when a key is added, at the start and every hour (`TINFIRM_LICENCE_VERIFY_INTERVAL`, `tinfirm.licence.verify-interval`, `PT1H`); a stored key that no longer verifies puts nothing in force and is logged at `ERROR`. Two test fixtures add a trusted key, under key ids no bundled key may take, and the server refuses to start with either outside the profiles that name them: the server tests' bean (`TestLicenceTrust`, `kid` `test`, profile `test`), and `TINFIRM_LICENCE_TEST_PUBLIC_KEY` (`kid` `e2e`) (`tinfirm.licence.test-public-key`, the PEM) for the web app's end-to-end harness, which runs the real jar (profile `e2e` or `test`, `SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE`): it makes a key pair with `LicenceKeyTool keygen` at each run, signs an Enterprise key with `sign` and applies it with `PUT /api/admin/licence`. FLOWTIVO signs keys with `ee/tools/LicenceKeyTool.java` (`keygen`, `sign --key-id`), JDK only, which writes the private key encrypted with a passphrase it reads from the terminal (encrypted PKCS#8: PBES2, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, AES-256), or from standard input's first line when that is not a terminal, never from the command line; the private keys are never in the repository. Rotation and retiring a compromised key: `ee/README.md`.
- **One key in force** (decision 6; `Licences`). The instance holds the key in force and at most one next key. On today's date (UTC) the next key rules once it started (the hourly pass then moves it into the slot of the key in force, `licence.key.activated`), else the key in force: its edition, its seats (none counted for an Enterprise key without a seat count), its cap of companies (none when an Enterprise key holds none; Community and Pro run one). State: `NONE` without a key that started; `ACTIVE`; `EXPIRING` from 30 days before the end, unless the next key starts by the day after it; `GRACE` for the 30 days after the end, as in force; `EXPIRED` after, Community with SAML kept after Enterprise and the audit's export until 90 days after the grace (`auditExportUntil`), no seat counted. The features are decision 4's: Pro `OUTLOOK_ADDIN`, `GMAIL_ADDON`, `AUDIT_TOOLS`, `AUDIT_FULL_HISTORY`, `RETENTION_SETTINGS`, `ELASTIC_RUNNERS` (ADR-0100); Enterprise adds `LEGAL_HOLDS`, `SAML`, `SCIM`, `SIEM`, `MULTI_COMPANY`, `HA`, `AIR_GAPPED`.
- **The API** (tag `Licence`). `GET /api/licence`, any signed-in user: `{edition, state, customer, endsAt, graceEndsAt, auditExportUntil, seats: {licensed, used}, companiesLicensed, features}`. `GET /api/admin/licence` (instance admins, `getAdminLicence`): `AdminLicence {current, next}`, each a `LicenceKeyView` each `{licenceId, rev, customer, edition, seats, companies, startsAt, endsAt, appliedAt, appliedBy, appliedByName}` or absent, never the key's string. `PUT /api/admin/licence` (`putLicenceKey`) `LicenceKeyRequest {key}` (white space around it ignored) applies a key: one that starts by today replaces the key in force (seats bought during the year come as the same licence's next revision, with the new total and the same end; a next key of the same licence with a lower revision goes with it), a later one is held as the next key in place of any held; `200` with `{current, next}`, `licence.key.applied`. Refused: `400 LICENCE_KEY_INVALID` (not a key, or not signed by FLOWTIVO), `409 LICENCE_KEY_EXPIRED` (it ended), `409 LICENCE_KEY_SUPERSEDED` (a held key of the same licence has the same or a higher `rev`, so an older key cannot be pasted back), `409 LICENCE_SEATS_TOO_FEW` (the key counts fewer seats than are used on the day it comes into force: `details.required`, `details.licensed`). No key is removed: a licence ends by its date. Applying takes the seat lock.
- **Seats** (decision 5). A seat is a person's across the instance: the distinct active users holding OWNER, ADMIN or MEMBER in any company (`licence_seats_used()`); a Viewer, a service account, a deactivated user and a member who left hold none. Under a licence that counts seats, a role that holds one goes to a person without a seat only while one is free (`Licensing.requireSeat`, `409 SEATS_EXHAUSTED`): `POST /members`, `PATCH /members/{userId}`, and `PATCH /api/admin/users/{userId}` making a user with such a role active again. A person who holds a seat keeps it in another role and in another company. Every check takes a transaction-scoped advisory lock (`licence:seats`), so that two grants never take the last seat twice. The declared owner of a company without one (§5) becomes its OWNER whatever the seats: a company is never left without an owner. No role is taken away for want of a seat, and no action is refused for it.
- **A provider's new user** (decision 5, §5). A role that holds a seat becomes `VIEWER` when none is free (`ProvisionedMemberships.grant` answers the role given); `member.added` carries `seatFallback: true` and `providerRole`, and the company's `seat_fallback` row raises the `SEAT_FALLBACK` item of the inbox of OWNER and ADMIN (`InboxSeatFallback`: the user, their name, the provider's role and the provider), dismissible, while they are a Viewer still and for 30 days at most (`ee`'s `SeatFallbackInbox`, through the port `InboxItems`).
- **The gates** (decision 4). Each is the server's, after the access check (another company's path stays `404`), `403 EDITION_REQUIRED` with `details.feature` and `details.edition` (`Licensing.require`); the web app only hides what the server refuses:

  | Feature | Refused without it |
  |---|---|
  | `OUTLOOK_ADDIN` | `GET /outlook/manifest`; every call to the MCP endpoint that carries an `Origin`, which only the add-in's task pane sends (`McpRequestFilter`, a JSON-RPC error), once `McpMembers` found the caller an active member of the company, as a tool call's access check does: anyone else gets the endpoint's usual answer |
  | `GMAIL_ADDON` | `GET /gmail/deployment`; `POST /gmail/links/read` and `/confirm`, once the caller is an active member of the company; Google's requests to `/api/gmail/**`, verified as ever (`401` otherwise), answered with a card that says the add-on needs Pro. A person's links stay theirs to list and end (`GET /me/gmail/links`, `DELETE …/gmail/links/{linkId}`) |
  | `AUDIT_TOOLS` | `GET /audit-events` with any filter; `GET …/audit-events/verification`, `…/checks` and their acknowledgement, the company's and the instance's; the `AUDIT_CHECK` inbox item (the daily check itself runs in every edition); `GET …/audit-events/export`, which also stays for 90 days after a licence expired (`AuditExport`, under `ee/`) |
  | `AUDIT_FULL_HISTORY` | The audit shown beyond its last 90 days: `GET /audit-events` and `GET /api/admin/audit-events` read from 90 days ago without it (`AuditPages.withinHistory`) |
  | `RETENTION_SETTINGS` | `PUT /retention`, `POST /retention/preview` |
  | `ELASTIC_RUNNERS` | A runner host's `hello` that lists `sandbox.bridge` (a VM per run or `--sandbox-bridge`) or `sandbox.pool` (a VM per run's): `error` `EDITION_REQUIRED` and close 1008 (§14.10); `run.assign.sessionBundle`, and the host's `GET` and `POST …/session-bundle` (`403` once its token is checked); `pool` and `clearPool` in `PATCH /runner-hosts/{id}`, and the pool's minute's tick. Runner hosts with Docker, and the bundles that exist, which are deleted as ever, stay in every edition |
  | `LEGAL_HOLDS` | `POST /holds`, and `GET /holds/conversations/{key}`, which finds a conversation to hold; open holds stay in force, listed and lifted, in every edition |
  | `SAML` | `GET /saml2/authenticate/{id}` and `POST /login/saml2/sso/{id}` (`/login?error=EDITION_REQUIRED`, audited `edition_required`, `SamlGuardFilter`); the SAML providers of `GET /api/auth/providers`. Kept after an Enterprise key expired (§15.26) |
  | `SCIM` | Every `/scim/v2` request with a valid token (`403` with the detail `EDITION_REQUIRED`, in SCIM's form, `ScimDirectoryImpl.requireServed`); `POST /scim/tokens`; the tokens stay listed and revoked in every edition, and SCIM's roles are released to the app (§15.25) |
  | `MULTI_COMPANY` | A declared company beyond the first (§5), up to an Enterprise key's cap of companies (`companiesLicensed`): it waits, with a warning, and is created or adopted once a key holds it (`LicenceChanged`, after a key is applied or activated, applies the declarations again); one that exists keeps working under any licence, and the ones that exist (by their key, or a company from before keys that a declaration adopts, which adds none) count first |

- **Expiry** (decision 7). `EXPIRING` and `GRACE` keep everything working. The `LICENCE` inbox item of OWNER and ADMIN (`InboxLicence`: state, edition, `endsAt`, `graceEndsAt`, `auditExportUntil`), dismissible and back at each state, shows from 30 days before the end to the end of the audit's export; instance admins read the same on `GET /api/licence`. After the grace (`EXPIRED`) the instance is Community but: the audit's export for 90 days (`auditExportUntil`); the retention periods in force keep running, frozen (`RetentionPolicy` `FROZEN`, `PUT /retention` refused); open holds stay and none is set; SAML keeps working after Enterprise (`features` keeps `SAML`, §15.26); SCIM stops, its tokens kept and revocable (§15.25); declared companies beyond the first keep working; the roles stay and no seat is counted. A new key restores everything at once.
- **Community's retention** (decision 8; §15.11). `RetentionPolicy` gives the periods in force: under Pro and Enterprise the OWNER's, or today's defaults (run events 90 days, chats 30, content and files 365) until they set theirs, so that the defaults are the starting values once a paid key first comes into force; after expiry the periods it left, frozen; under Community, never licensed, every period keeps (`mode` `KEEP_EVERYTHING`, `GET /retention` answers the periods null but `memoryDays`): conversations, transcripts, files and run events are never purged by retention. The memory notes' period and the operational purges of `RetainedRecords` run in every edition. `V054` lets `run_events_days` and `chat_content_days` be NULL (keep), the chats' only while all content is kept.
- **Requests never wait on a Viewer** (decision 5). When a question or an approval is raised (`DecisionOps.approver`), and its only candidates are Viewers (a personal conversation's person; a trigger's author for its approval without a team, or for its question with no team or a team of Viewers only; a team whose members are all Viewers), it goes to the company's Owners and Admins: `approver_admins`, with no approver principal or group. They see it in their inbox and in `GET /decisions?mine=true`, and decide it where they see its conversation: a trigger's conversation, which OWNER and ADMIN see, or a personal one, which only OWNER sees. A Viewer's decider is none: they decide nothing, as the person, a team's member or a trigger's author. The approver follows the roles, the groups and the trigger's team and author as they are now (`DecisionOps.approverOf`): when a member becomes a Viewer or stops being one, or a group's members change (`MemberEvents.MemberRoleChanged`, `GroupEvents.GroupMembersChanged`, `DecisionRouting`, in the transaction of the change), each open decision the member or the group takes part in (as its approver, a personal conversation's person, or its trigger's author or team), and when a trigger's team or author changes, each of its open approvals, goes to who decides it now, the admins when its candidates are only Viewers and back to its approver once one holds a seat, audited `decision.approver_changed` with `fromAdmins` and `toAdmins`. `V055__decisions_of_viewers.sql` reconciled the open decisions raised before, both ways. Viewers still read the request and its outcome where they read the conversation.
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `LicenceKeyFormatTest` | A key signed by a trusted key verifies (its `rev` required, from 1); the end-to-end harness's public key trusted under `e2e` and refused at the start without it; another signer, a changed payload, what is not a key and each rule of the payload refused; an Enterprise key without counts; the bundled public key an Ed25519 key |
  | `LicencesTest` | Community without a key; a Pro key in force with its features and dates; the next key taking over on its start, Community while only a renewal is held; Enterprise's counts and an uncounted key; `EXPIRING` from 30 days before the end, not with a renewal that follows without a gap; `GRACE`, then `EXPIRED` with SAML kept after Enterprise and nothing after Pro |
  | `LicenceIntegrationTest` | Community without a key; a key applied by an instance admin, read by every member, shown and audited without its string; only an instance admin applies keys; `LICENCE_KEY_INVALID`, `_EXPIRED`, `_SUPERSEDED` (the same and a lower revision); seats bought during the year as the next revision, audited with what it replaced; a renewal held as the next key, a changed row putting nothing in force, and the hourly pass activating it (`licence.key.activated`); `LICENCE_SEATS_TOO_FEW` now and on a smaller renewal; `SEATS_EXHAUSTED` on adding and promoting, a seat kept in another role and company, a seat freed and given; a deactivated user not made active without a seat; an Enterprise key without a seat count |
  | `ViewerIntegrationTest` | A Viewer reads the company's pages; starts, edits and decides nothing (`VIEWER_READ_ONLY`); dismisses their own notices; a member made a Viewer and back |
  | `DecisionIntegrationTest` | A Viewer's question goes to the Owners and Admins: the Viewer cannot decide it, the OWNER sees it as theirs and decides it |
  | `DecisionRoutingIntegrationTest` | A decision goes to the Owners and Admins while its person is a Viewer and back after; an approval moved to a team of Viewers goes to them, and back to its author; a reassigned trigger's approval follows its author now; `V055` reconciles a decision both ways |
  | `OidcLoginIntegrationTest` | A provider's new user joins as VIEWER when every seat is taken, audited with `seatFallback`, and the admins' `SEAT_FALLBACK` item |
  | `EditionGatesIntegrationTest` | Each gate under Community after the access check (another company's path `404`); Enterprise serving them with today's periods as the start, a keep of every kind and `RETENTION_INVALID` for chats kept longer; an expired licence keeping the export for 90 days, freezing the retention, listing holds and setting none; the `LICENCE` notice for OWNER and ADMIN, never a member |
  | `DeclaredCompaniesIntegrationTest` | A declared company beyond the first waiting under Community, applied under Enterprise, both kept after |
  | `McpRequestFilterTest` | The Outlook add-in's calls (an `Origin`) refused under Community, an AI client's passing |
  | `ModularityTest` | No module depends on `ee`; no class outside `ee/` names one under it |

  The tests of the paid features run under an Enterprise key (`EnterpriseLicence`, a JUnit extension that holds one for the class and removes it after).

### 15.25 SCIM provisioning (ADR-0087)

SCIM 2.0 `Users` of [ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md), Enterprise, with SCIM's part of [ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md) (decision 8). The operator allows it per company (§4), its OWNER issues the token, and the identity provider creates, updates, deactivates and deletes the company's members.

- **Where it lives.** The core keeps the endpoint and its chain (`company.internal.ScimController`, `ScimSecurityConfiguration`, `ScimRequestFilter`, SCIM's errors in `ScimExceptionHandler`), the tokens' store, listing and revocation (`JdbcScimTokenStore`, `ScimTokenService`), the company's SCIM settings (`ScimSettingsImpl`), the memberships SCIM manages (`ScimMembersImpl`, which writes roles through ADR-0088's `MembershipRoles`), the release of SCIM's roles (`ScimRoleRelease`), the binding at sign-in (identity's `ExternalProvisioning`), SCIM's users (identity's `ScimUsers`) and the suspension's cause `SCIM` (principal's `Suspensions`). The company module exposes them to `ee` through its named interface `company.scim` (`ScimMembers`, `ScimSettings`, `ScimTokenStore`, and the ports `ScimDirectory` and `ScimTokens`). Enterprise's code is the `ee` module's (`ee.scim`): the directory (`ScimDirectoryImpl`: the resources, filters, `PUT` and `PatchOp`), token issuance (`ScimTokensImpl`) and the OWNER's `SCIM_LAST_OWNER` inbox item (`ScimLastOwnerInbox`). ADR-0087 decision 12 put the directory in the company module's package; it is the `ee` module's instead, since the company module may not depend on the principal and conversation modules that it needs (`ModularityTest`). Without `ee/`, `/scim/v2` answers `403 EDITION_REQUIRED` and no token is issued.
- **The endpoint** (decision 1). `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/scim/v2`: `GET`, `POST /Users`; `GET`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE /Users/{id}`; `GET /ServiceProviderConfig`, `/ResourceTypes`, `/ResourceTypes/{name}`, `/Schemas`, `/Schemas/{id}` (each resource type and schema at its `meta.location`, RFC 7644 §4). `application/scim+json` and `application/json`. The chain, in order: an `Origin` header `403`; a client address with 20 refused tokens in the last minute `429`; a missing, malformed, unknown, revoked or expired token, or a company the deployment no longer allows, `401` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"` (audited `scim.refused` in the instance chain with `reason` and `clientAddress`, at most once a minute per address and reason); the company's requests a minute `429` with `Retry-After`; a path not served `404`; then the request as the token, in its company's tenant context, `last_used_at` written at most once a minute. Then the licence: `403` with the detail `EDITION_REQUIRED` unless it holds `SCIM`. Filters `userName eq`, `externalId eq`, `id eq` (`400 invalidFilter` otherwise); pages of at most 100, `startIndex` at most 10 000 (`400 invalidValue`; §7). The members of every role, suspended ones included; a non-member is `404`.
- **The token** (decision 3). `POST /api/companies/{companyId}/scim/tokens {name, expiresAt?}` (OWNER, `MANAGE_PROVISIONING`, the licence's `SCIM`; `409 SCIM_NOT_ENABLED`, `409 SCIM_TOKEN_LIMIT` beyond two active) answers `ScimTokenCreated {token, secret}`; `GET …/scim/tokens` (OWNER, ADMIN) lists `ScimToken`s, newest first, revoked ones included; `DELETE …/scim/tokens/{tokenId}` (OWNER, every edition) revokes, idempotent. `GET /api/companies/{companyId}/scim` answers `ScimStatus {enabled, licensed, baseUrl, defaultRole, roleSource}`, which the web app's **Settings → Sign-in** shows. Audited `scim.token.created` and `scim.token.revoked` with the token's id and name.
- **The users and memberships** (decisions 4, 6, 7). `userName` is the email, in lower case; `externalId` is the membership's `scim_external_id` (required, unique in the company); the name is `displayName`, else `name.formatted`, else the given and family names, else the email's local part; `active` is whether the membership is in effect; the extension `urn:ietf:params:scim:schemas:extension:tinfirm:2.0:User` holds `role`. Every other attribute is ignored. A create of a `userName` a member holds is `409 uniqueness`; a user of that email who is not a member becomes one; else a user with `authSource` `SCIM` is created (`ScimUsers.create`, `user.created`). The email and name change only for a user whose `scim_company_id` is this company and who is a member of no other. An OWNER's role and `externalId` never change; a change of the `externalId` of a user who has not signed in is audited with both values' SHA-256.
- **Deactivation** (decision 7). SCIM's suspension is the membership's `scim_suspended_at`; the principal is suspended with the cause `SCIM` when it was active (`PrincipalSwitches.scimSuspended`), and SCIM's reactivation resumes only a principal suspended with that cause (`scimLifted`). An admin's suspension of a member SCIM suspended takes the cause `ADMIN`; an admin's resume of a `SCIM` suspension is `409 PRINCIPAL_SUSPENDED`; an admin's resume, or a user's reactivation, while SCIM's suspension holds leaves the principal suspended for `SCIM`. When no other company holds the member in effect, SCIM's deactivation of the user is set too (`app_user.scim_deactivated_at`, beside an instance admin's `admin_deactivated`; `active` is false while either holds, `app_user_active_ck`): their sessions end and they are suspended in every company. An instance admin is never deactivated by SCIM. Deactivating or deleting the last active OWNER is `400 mutability` with the detail `LAST_OWNER`, and that OWNER gets the `SCIM_LAST_OWNER` inbox item for 30 days (`company_membership.scim_last_owner_refused_at`, written in a transaction of its own). `DELETE` is a member who leaves (`MemberService.leave`), never an erasure; a user then a member of no company is deactivated.
- **Roles and seats** (decision 8; ADR-0088 decision 8). Under `_SCIM_ROLE_SOURCE=IDP` the role applies whenever a request carries it, through `MembershipRoles.apply` with the source `SCIM` (`409 ROLE_MANAGED` in the app; a sign-in's claim leaves it); a create's role source is `SCIM`. Under `APP` the role is given at creation only, the source `APP`. A seat's role without a free seat gives VIEWER, with `seatFallback` and `providerRole` on `member.added` or `member.role.changed` and the `SEAT_FALLBACK` item with the provider `scim`; never `SEATS_EXHAUSTED`. A user SCIM reactivates who would need a seat none is free for becomes VIEWER in this company. **Release** (`ScimRoleRelease`): at the start, and on `LicenceChanged`, every membership of source `SCIM` in a company whose SCIM no longer decides (not allowed, `APP`, or the licence without `SCIM`) becomes `APP`, audited `member.role_source.changed` by SYSTEM; the licence's hourly pass announces `LicenceChanged` when the features in force change by date (an Enterprise key past its grace).
- **Audit** (decision 9). By the token (`ActorType.SCIM`, the token's id; `actor.display` `SCIM token <name>` in its company's chain): `scim.user.created`, `scim.user.changed` (`attributes`, names only), `scim.user.deactivated`, `scim.user.reactivated`, `scim.user.deleted`, each with `userId`, beside the core's `user.created`, `user.updated`, `member.added`, `member.role.changed`, `member.removed`, `principal.suspended` and `principal.resumed` (cause `SCIM`) of the same transaction. A `GET` is not audited, and no event holds an email.
- **Tests.**

  | Test | Proves |
  |---|---|
  | `ScimIntegrationTest` | The discovery documents; the token issued once and hashed, at most two, revoked, expired, a company not allowed and one no longer allowed; the refusals without a token and from a browser, the limit per address; create, the existing user made a member, filters and pages; `PUT` and `PATCH` in Entra ID's and Okta's forms and their refusals; deactivation ending a session and a personal token at once and keeping the data; SCIM never lifting an admin's suspension, and the reverse; delete as a member who leaves; the OWNER's rules; the seat fallback; `APP` |
  | `ScimTenantIsolationTest` | A token of one company never reads, finds, changes or removes another's members; a person of two companies |
  | `ScimEntraIdCompatibilityTest`, `ScimOktaCompatibilityTest` | The documented sequences of Entra ID, with and without `aadOptscim062020`, and of Okta, replayed (`src/test/resources/scim/*.json`) |
  | `ScimEditionIntegrationTest` | `EDITION_REQUIRED` under Community, Pro and after the grace; served in `GRACE`; tokens listed and revoked in every edition |
  | `ScimRoleSourceIntegrationTest` | `409 ROLE_MANAGED` under `IDP`, an OWNER made in the app, SCIM taking an app member's role, `APP`, the release when the licence stops SCIM |
  | `ScimOidcBindingIntegrationTest` | A first OIDC sign-in bound by `externalId`, SCIM's role winning over the claim, a stranger refused under `_AUTO_PROVISION=false`, the binding by email, never an OWNER |
  | `EditionGatesIntegrationTest` | No token issued under Community; listed and revoked |

  Not yet: Microsoft's SCIM Validator and Okta's SCIM test suite against a public instance, and the guides against live tenants (decision 14), before the first release with SCIM. SAML's binding is built with ADR-0086 (§15.26).

### 15.26 SAML sign-in (ADR-0086)

SAML 2.0 sign-in of [ADR-0086](adr/0086-saml-sign-in.md), Enterprise, with SAML's part of [ADR-0088](adr/0088-roles-from-the-identity-provider.md) (decision 6) and of [ADR-0087](adr/0087-scim-provisioning.md) (the binding of decision 6). The deployment declares each provider (§4), bound to a declared company; Tinfirm is the service provider through Spring Security's `saml2Login` on OpenSAML 5.

- **Where it lives.** The core keeps the settings and their checks at the start (`identity.internal.SamlProperties`, `SamlSettings`, `SamlSpCredentials`), the provisioning (`ExternalProvisioning`, one path for OIDC and SAML), the audit of a refusal, the per-address throttle and the session (`SamlSignIns`), the company's sign-in page (`CompanySignInService`, `GET /api/companies/{companyId}/sign-in`: every provider that fills the company, `through` `COMPANY` as its own or `CLAIM` through its role claim, with the `roleMap` of this company, [ADR-0089](adr/0089-companies-from-the-role-claim.md)) and the port `SamlLogin`, which `SecurityConfiguration` finds when it builds the web app's chain. Enterprise's code is under `ee/`, in `identity.internal.saml` (§15.24's table). Without `ee/`, a declared provider logs a warning and is not offered.
- **The service provider** (decision 2). Per provider: entity ID and metadata `{TINFIRM_PUBLIC_URL}/saml2/service-provider-metadata/{id}` (`application/samlmetadata+xml`, public, `AuthnRequestsSigned` and `WantAssertionsSigned`, NameID formats persistent, emailAddress, unspecified, every credential as signing and encryption key, no `SingleLogoutService`), ACS `/login/saml2/sso/{id}` (HTTP-POST), start `/saml2/authenticate/{id}`. The SP metadata is served before the IdP's metadata is read.
- **The IdP's metadata** (decision 3). A `worker` reads it under a ShedLock lock (`SamlMetadataRefresh`): at the start, then every `_METADATA_REFRESH`, sooner for a `cacheDuration` or half the remaining `validUntil`, at least 5 minutes apart, 5 minutes after a failure. A URL over verified TLS, at most 1 MiB, a redirect only to the same host over https; a file read again each time. Parsed by OpenSAML's hardened parser pool (`SamlIdpMetadata`): an `EntityDescriptor`, or an `EntitiesDescriptor` with `_ENTITY_ID`; an IdP of SAML 2.0 with an https HTTP-Redirect `SingleSignOnService` (`http://localhost` and `*.localhost` aside) and a signing certificate; every `use="signing"` or unmarked key trusted. Stored in `saml_idp_metadata`; every replica builds its registration from the stored document (`SamlRegistrations`), checking its hash every 10 seconds. A failed read keeps the last good document; a document past its `validUntil` is not used (`metadata_expired`). At the start on a worker, a provider without any good document whose read fails stops the server under a licence that serves SAML.
- **The request** (decision 4). `OpenSaml5AuthenticationRequestResolver`, HTTP-Redirect, signed by the active credential, no `ForceAuthn`, `IsPassive` or `RequestedAuthnContext`. `SamlAuthnRequests` saves it in `saml_authn_request` with the SHA-256 of a random binding whose value is the `TINFIRM_SAML` cookie; the ACS loads it by that cookie and the path's provider, and deletes it only when a response has passed every check (`consume`, before provisioning), so that it answers one response and a refused one, such as a forged response that a page of another site posts from the browser, leaves it for the IdP's own; the cookie is expired once the person is signed in. A new start in the browser replaces it. In front of the ACS, `SamlGuardFilter` answers `403`, unparsed and uncounted, a post whose `Sec-Fetch-Dest` is not `document`: an IdP's response is always a top-level form post, so a hidden frame or a script of another site cannot use up the address's attempts.
- **The checks** (decision 8; `SamlResponseChecks`, `SamlAlgorithms`, `SamlDecryption`, `SamlGuardFilter`), each failure audited with its reason, the first that fails: the guard's throttle (`429` before parsing; SAML's failures in a bucket per address of their own, since the ACS takes cross-site posts), POST and 256 KiB, on the decoded path as Spring's matchers see it; the library's parse, signature (OpenSAML's profile and trust engine against the metadata's keys), issuer, audience, subject confirmation and conditions with a 60-second skew; Tinfirm's: the response or every assertion signed (`unsigned`), RSA-SHA256 or ECDSA with SHA-256 or stronger digests (`weak_algorithm`), status, issuer (`issuer_mismatch`, also for a signature the library refused for another issuer), exactly one assertion as it arrived (`multiple_assertions`), `Destination` the ACS and required on a signed response, `InResponseTo` present (`unsolicited`) and this browser's saved request (`browser_mismatch`, `in_response_to_mismatch`), named by the assertion's own bearer confirmation too, so that an assertion signed alone cannot be wrapped in an unsigned response that answers another request, an encrypted assertion when required (`not_encrypted`), RSA-OAEP key transport and AES-GCM or AES-CBC content (`weak_algorithm`; an encrypted assertion is decrypted only in a signed response), `IssueInstant` at most 5 minutes old, an `AuthnStatement`, and one use of the assertion's ID (`saml_assertion_seen`, `replayed`), last. Then the licence (`edition_required`).
- **The person** (decision 5; `SamlPerson`). The subject: the NameID (`transient_subject` refused) or the single value of `_SUBJECT`'s attribute, at most 255 characters. The email: the first of `_EMAIL_ATTRIBUTES` whose first value looks like an email, else an `emailAddress` NameID. The name: the first of `_NAME_ATTRIBUTES`, else the given name and surname, else the email's local part (core). The role's values: every value of `_ROLE_CLAIM`'s attribute. Attributes only from the verified assertion, decrypted when encrypted.
- **Audit** (decision 13). `auth.login.succeeded` and `auth.login.failed` with `method: saml` and `provider`; a refusal at most once a minute per address and reason, never the response, an attribute's value or an email; `user.created` with `authSource: SAML` and `emailAttribute`; `saml.idp_keys.changed`, `saml.metadata.failed` (§8).
- **The sweep** (`SamlSweep`, a worker every 10 minutes): the requests older than 5 minutes, the assertions past their expiry, the metadata of providers no longer declared.
- **Tests.**

  | Test | What it holds |
  |---|---|
  | `SamlKeycloakIntegrationTest` | The SP-initiated flow against Keycloak in Testcontainers without a browser, signed, then signed and encrypted; roles from groups, `ROLE_NOT_MAPPED`; Keycloak's response used once |
  | `SamlResponseChecksTest` | Each check of decision 8 with a response built by OpenSAML and changed in one way, with its audited reason |
  | `SamlAttackSamplesTest` | XSW1 to XSW8, the comment truncation of 2018 in the NameID and the email, XXE, an external DTD, a billion laughs, an XSLT transform, a duplicate ID, an empty reference |
  | `SamlEditionIntegrationTest` | Refused under Community and Pro; served under Enterprise, in its grace and after it expired; a new key restoring it |
  | `SamlSignInPageIntegrationTest` | The login page's SAML providers by licence, the company's sign-in page, the SP metadata |
  | `SamlProvisioningTest`, `SamlPropertiesTest`, `SamlSettingsTest`, `SamlSpCredentialsTest`, `SamlIdpMetadataTest`, `SamlPersonTest` | Provisioning through the shared path; the settings from the environment; the start's refusals; the keys; the metadata; the person |

  Not yet: the guides for Entra ID, Okta and AD FS against live tenants (decision 16), before the first release with SAML.

### 15.27 Session bundles (ADR-0097)

The runtime's session folders of a conversation's agent, which a company runner host uploads when a run ends and the next run's host restores, so that a turn resumes its session on any company host ([ADR-0097](adr/0097-session-bundles.md); the Kubernetes runner analysis §6.5, D3). The protocol's fields are in §14.10. Pro's and Enterprise's, part of elastic runners ([ADR-0100](adr/0100-elastic-runners-in-pro.md), `ELASTIC_RUNNERS`): keeping a bundle is under `ee/`; deleting one is the core's, in every edition.

- **Where** (`run` module). Under `ee/`: `SessionBundlesImpl` (the assignment's field, the upload, the download), which implements the core's `SessionBundles`, `SessionBundleRows` (a bundle found and stored), `RunnerSessionBundleController` and `BundleCipher`. The core's: `SessionBundleCleanup` (the end of a run, the purges and the sweeper), `SessionBundleStore` (the deletes and the sweeper's lookups), `BundleObjects` with `FileSystemBundleObjects` and `S3BundleObjects` (`BundleObjectsConfiguration` picks one by `tinfirm.session-bundles.store`; `SessionBundleProperties`). The vault's `DataKeys` creates and unwraps the data keys.
- **Which runs.** Only while the licence in force holds `ELASTIC_RUNNERS` and a store is configured, only for a company host (`HostScope.COMPANY`) whose `hello.features` lists `session.bundle` (`RunnerMessages.FEATURE_SESSION_BUNDLE`), read in the assignment's transaction (`RunDelivery`). The dispatcher does not wait for such a host: a run on another host keeps its home as before.
- **The upload** (`SessionBundlesImpl.upload`): without `ELASTIC_RUNNERS` the upload and the download are `403 EDITION_REQUIRED` once the host's token is checked; for a run `RUNNING` on the host with its epoch, in the host's company, else `404`; a session id of 1 to 255 characters without control characters; at most 32 MiB read (`413` when `Content-Length` says more), at most 4 uploads at once (another waits up to 30 seconds). The bundle is scanned with `VaultService.scan` in the company's transaction (the vault's secrets, the credential formats, through the gzip and the tar); a match or a bundle that cannot be scanned through is `REFUSED`, and the conversation agent's earlier bundle goes. Then a fresh data key (`DataKeys.create`, the context `session-bundle|company|conversation|agent|object`), the object sealed (`BundleCipher`) and put under a random UUID, and in one transaction, with the run checked again, the row replaced (`session_bundle`, one per conversation and agent); the replaced object, and the new one when the transaction fails, are deleted after it.
- **The download**: the bundle of the run's conversation and agent, of its runtime and of the session the run resumes (the one `run.assign` named), for a run assigned to the host with its epoch and active, else `404` (the host restores it only when it is the one `run.assign` named, by size and SHA-256); decrypted as it streams, with `Content-Length` and `X-Content-SHA256`. A missing object or one that cannot be opened is `404`, and the turn starts a new session.
- **The end of a run** (`SessionBundleCleanup.finished`, from `RunnerSessionHandler` after `RunCompletion`): a reported `sessionId` deletes the conversation agent's bundle unless the run uploaded it, whatever its `sessionBundle` says (a host's `STORED` is not trusted without the row).
- **Purges.** `RunPurge.purgeTextsOf` and `purgeEventsOf` (retention's `CONTENT` purge, an erasure of the whole conversation) delete the conversation's bundles, of every agent, in their transaction; the objects go after it. An erasure's `RUNNER_SESSIONS` remainder names the bundle of an open conversation with the hosts' homes.
- **The sweeper** (`SessionBundleCleanup.sweep`, on `worker` replicas every hour): the bundles whose conversation ended or took no turn for `tinfirm.session-bundles.retention` (`session_bundle_due`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` lookup of ids), each deleted again in its company's context; then the store's objects older than `tinfirm.session-bundles.orphan-age` (an hour) that no row names (`session_bundle_objects_known`).
- **Table** (`V066__session_bundles.sql`): `session_bundle` (`company_id`, `conversation_id`, `agent_id`, `runtime`, `session_id`, `object_name` unique, `size_bytes` 1 to 33554432, `sha256`, `key_id`, `wrapped_key`, `key_nonce`, `run_id`, `created_at`; key `(conversation_id, agent_id)`; foreign keys to the conversation and the run with `ON DELETE CASCADE`; RLS; the runtime role selects, inserts and deletes).
- **Not audited**: a bundle is the agent's own session, not the company's record; the run's log lines say what was stored, refused or restored, and the server logs each store, refusal and sweep.
- **Tests.** `BundleCipherTest` (every size around the chunk's back as it was; a changed byte, another object's name or key, a cut at a chunk's end, swapped chunks and another format fail), `DataKeysTest` (a key unwraps for its context only; a rotated master key still unwraps old keys, a retired one does not), `BundleObjectsTest` (the directory store; the S3 store against a fake S3 API: path-style requests with a SigV4 signature, put, get, list and delete), `SessionBundleIntegrationTest` (the upload and the restore on another host; another host's, another company's and no token's `404` and `401`; a refused bundle with a vault secret and the stale rule; a host without the feature; a purge; the sweeper's due bundles and orphans), and the runner host's `local_runs.rs` (§ the runner README).
